# Squad briefs, 2026-09-06

Every agent that returned before the session wound down at 93% of the daily usage limit. Analysts and
cross-reviewers are read-only by design; no lead had produced a commit when the waves were stopped, so
this document plus the packets is the entire work product of the six squads.

## Wave 1 — W3-03, W3-04, W3-07

26 agents reported.

### W3-03 · codebase

W3-03 touches four existing modules (`scout.py`, `daemon.py`, `rules.py`, `cli.py`), creates `src/sentinel/vendors.py` (which does not exist yet — W3-05 has not landed), and needs new config plumbing that `load_config` does not have. I re-ran the scout against the live home into a temp XDG dir: the qualitative claims all hold, but every number in the packet has drifted upward since 2026-09-04 (1242 entries / 138 roots / 56 dirs / 10.6 ms per event, not 940 / 134 / 54 / 6.8 ms). Three packet claims are wrong about the current tree, two pieces of the S5 block are already implemented and tested, and one S5 item (rate-limiting `refresh_watchlist`) will break an existing green test.

**Findings**

- /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/scout.py is the core surface: `WATCHLIST_FILENAME`, `DEFAULT_SEEDS`, `SETTINGS_NAMES`, `classify_kind(path)`, `_entry_for(path)`, `scout(home, seeds=None)`, `write_watchlist(result)` — `classify_kind` tries exact `SETTINGS_NAMES`, then substring `hook` in any lowered path part, then `auth`/`credentials` in the filename, then substring `mcp` in any part, else `other`; `_entry_for` emits only `path`/`kind`/`size`/`mtime` (no `vendor`) and the top-level dict has only `generated_at`/`home`/`paths` (no `version`).
- `scout()` walks `root.rglob("*")` with no pruning at all and dedupes on the resolved path string; it never opens a file (`_entry_for` calls `path.stat()` only), so the Forbidden rule about `~/.claude.json` bodies is already satisfied by construction and must stay that way.
- /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py: `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (which contains `watchlist.json`) is consulted in both `Daemon.handle_write` and `Daemon._on_fs_event`; `_on_fs_event` is the exact hook point for hot reload, and `Daemon._clock` (config key `clock`, default `time.monotonic`) already exists for an injectable 2 s debounce.
- Watch (re)building is all-or-nothing today: `Daemon._rebuild_watches` calls `_close_inotify()` and constructs a fresh `Inotify`, and the `Inotify` class exposes only `add_watch` — `_libc()` never binds `inotify_rm_watch`, so the packet's "diff against current roots, add or remove inotify watches" needs new ctypes plumbing, not just a diff.
- `watch_roots_from_entries(entries)` in daemon.py already filters on `WATCH_KINDS = {"hooks","settings","mcp","auth"}` and adds the parent dir for `hooks` entries, so dropping `other` entries from the file is schema-compatible; nothing else consumes the watchlist (`investigate.py` only emits `grep`/path strings for the evidence block, no parsing).
- /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/rules.py: `_path_under(path, roots)` is private and has exactly one caller, `evaluate_write`, which invokes it twice (self_paths, then watch_paths) and also calls `path.resolve()` a third time to build `paths`; `evaluate_write`'s only production caller is `Daemon.handle_write`, which passes the unresolved `self._watch_roots` and `self._self_paths()`.
- Measured on the live watchlist: 138 roots, `_path_under` costs 10.6 ms for one non-matching write under `~/.claude` — worse than the packet's 6.8 ms, so the pre-resolved-roots change is more valuable than stated.
- Live re-scout today (temp XDG, `.venv/bin/python` + `scout(Path.home())`): 1242 entries — other 1129, hooks 61, mcp 48, auth 3, settings 1; `watch_roots_from_entries` yields 138 roots collapsing to 56 inotify directories, 52 of them under `~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/claude-plugins-official/`, with 174 files sitting directly in watched dirs.
- `load_config` in daemon.py reads only the `[daemon]` table (falling back to the whole document) and only four keys — `sampler_interval`, `coalesce_window`, `extra_bypass_flags`, `known_basenames` — so `[scout] project_roots` needs a new reader; `cli.scout_main` calls `scout(home)` with no config load at all and /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/packaging/config.toml has only `[kill]` and `[notify]`.
- `write_watchlist` already writes atomically through `fsutil.write_private_atomic` (0600 tmp file + `os.replace`), so that bullet in the packet's Changes section is a no-op — it is already true.
- The S5 items 'track auto-added watch descriptors and drop them silently when they vanish' and 'raise the lost-watch alert only for a configured root' are already implemented via `Daemon._auto_wds` in `_drain_inotify`, and the exact requested regression test already exists and passes as `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent` (20x `history.jsonl.lock` create/remove, zero alerts, zero refreshes).
- Not implemented from S5: auto-watch is currently unconditional (any `IN_CREATE|IN_ISDIR` under any watched dir gets a watch, not just under hooks roots), and `refresh_watchlist` has no rate limit.
- `Daemon.sample_proc` does read each agent's cwd via `_readlink(pid_dir / "cwd")`, but it is a local passed straight into `handle_process` and discarded — the packet's phrasing implies the daemon already has a set of agent cwds it can hand to `scout()`; it does not, that is new state.
- Vendor-table ground truth on this machine: `~/.claude.json`, `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json` all exist; `~/.codex/config.toml` and `~/.cursor` do NOT. `~/.grok` additionally holds `auth.json`, `hooks/`, `hooks-paths`, `sandbox.toml`, `trusted_folders.toml`, `installed-plugins/`, `marketplace-cache/`; `~/.codex` holds sqlite state files plus `skills/`; `~/.gemini` holds `oauth_creds.json`, `google_accounts.json`, `trustedFolders.json`, `settings.json`.
- Project discovery finds exactly one hit here: `/home/tav/Work/.claude/settings.local.json`. Nothing else under `~/Work` at depth 5 matches `.claude`/`.codex`/`.cursor`/`.gemini` — this repo itself has no `.claude` dir but does carry `.grok/roles/*.toml` and `.grok/prompts/`, which the packet's `project_dirs` list omits.
- Existing tests that must change: all three in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_scout.py (they depend on the `seeds=` kwarg, on `.claude/hooks/x.sh` classifying via the substring rule, and on `.codex/auth.json` → `auth`), and the fixture tree /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/fixtures/fake_home contains only `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/hooks/x.sh`, `.codex/auth.json`.

**Risks**

- Confirmed live false positive that the hot-reload work will make hot: `watchlist.json.tmp` is not in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (only `alerts.jsonl.tmp` is), so a `sentinel-scout --refresh` — or the daemon's own `refresh_watchlist` — while the daemon watches `state_dir()` emits `('R-SELF', 'high', 'write to sentinel path by unknown')`. I reproduced this by feeding `state_dir()/'watchlist.json.tmp'` to `Daemon._on_fs_event`. The same gap exists for `health.json.tmp`, `allowlist.json.tmp`, `allowlist-session.json.tmp`, `notify-prefs.json.tmp`, `notify-burst.json.tmp` and `pause_until.tmp`, all written by `fsutil.write_private_atomic`.
- Rate-limiting `refresh_watchlist` to once per 60 s (the S5 note) directly breaks `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes`, which asserts `called == ['refresh', 'refresh']` after two back-to-back `handle_watch_lost()` calls. That test needs a clock injection or an explicit exemption; decide before implementing.
- The S5 paragraph is filed under `## Non-goals` but reads as required work, and it sits above the real non-goals bullets. An implementer following headings literally will skip it. Worth a Chief ruling.
- `Inotify` has no `rm_watch` and `_libc()` binds only `inotify_init1` and `inotify_add_watch`. Incremental watch removal is new C-ABI surface (and `inotify_rm_watch` needs the fd + wd, so `Inotify.wd_to_path` bookkeeping has to stay in sync with `_auto_wds`).
- A 2 s debounce will not fire on time: `Daemon._sample_timeout()` only accounts for `sampler_interval` (clamped 2-5 s) when computing the `select()` timeout, so a pending reload can sit up to a full sampler interval past its deadline unless `_sample_timeout` is min'ed with the debounce deadline.
- The new `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_watchlist_rewrite_reloads_without_alert` has to be careful with the `_daemon` helper: it passes a `self_paths` override, and `Daemon._dirs_to_watch` only ever watches `self._self_paths() + self._watch_roots`, so `state_dir()` is not watched unless the test includes it.
- A `slow` marker is unregistered — /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/pyproject.toml has no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, so the marker warns and, with no `addopts`, the acceptance command `.venv/bin/pytest -q` will still run the 300-root/1000-event perf test in the 210-test suite.
- Cross-packet collision with W3-05: its `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` change wants the last content hash stored per entry in `watchlist.json`, while W3-03 owns that schema and lists content hashing as a non-goal deferred to W6-03. `_entry_for` should leave the field open rather than lock the shape.
- Line-number citations in the packet are all stale by a few lines and one is plain wrong: `daemon.py:628` is inside `consume_launches`, not the `watchlist.json` ignore (that is `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` at the module top, consulted in `handle_write` and `_on_fs_event`). `classify_kind` is scout.py 27-40 (not 32-37), `DEFAULT_SEEDS` 14-22 (not 12-20), `_path_under` 149-160 (not 148-159).
- The packet's `exclude` list misses the dirs that actually generate the volume here — `~/.claude` currently contains `file-history`, `paste-cache`, `backups`, `cache`, `session-env`, `plans`, `themes`, `telemetry`, `skills` and `history.jsonl`, while `plugins/cache`, `todos`, `statsig` and `debug` do not exist. Since classification becomes an exact-match allowlist the exclude list only saves walk time, but it should be recomputed against the real tree rather than copied.
- The goal line 'watch root count drops from 134 to under 20' should read 138 — recheck the baseline in the report so the acceptance number is honest.
- The claim that `~/.grok/config.toml` holds `permission_mode`, hooks and plugins is UNVERIFIED by me: that file is scouted and the packet's Forbidden section bars reading its body.


### W3-03 · codebase:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- ALL FOUR AGENTS (and the packet) missed that the packet's own perf bound cannot be met by the packet's own change. I measured on this machine: current `_path_under` with the live 138 roots = 12.2 ms per non-matching event. Pre-resolving the roots exactly as the packet prescribes drops that to 3.05 ms - a 4x win, not a fix. At the packet's 300 roots a pre-resolved `relative_to` loop still costs 6.58 ms/event, i.e. 6.6 s for 1000 events, 13x over the stated 500 ms bound. The cost is `Path.relative_to` constructing a ValueError per root, NOT `resolve()` (`path.resolve()` is 0.064 ms). Comparing resolved *strings* - `s == root or s.startswith(root + os.sep)` - costs 0.075 ms/event at 300 roots (~75 ms for 1000 events, 6x under budget) and 0.045 ms at 138 roots. I verified string-prefix and the current loop agree on all 404 real paths I fed them, with one edge case: a root of `/` makes naive `root + os.sep` produce `//` and return False where the current code returns True, so the trailing separator must be stripped. Consequence: the tests agent's `test_evaluate_write_1000_events_under_500ms` would FAIL as designed if the implementer follows the packet's Root-resolution bullet literally, and R36/R37 as written by the spec agent are necessary but not sufficient.
- INTEGRATION agent: 'the entire genuine watch surface today is three files' is true of the on-disk file only, and is already out of date. I re-verified every number they gave against `/home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json` (generated_at 2026-09-05T07:30:03) and they are exactly right for that file: 1068 entries, 957 other / 59 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings, 134 roots, 54 inotify dirs, 131 marketplace roots, 52 marketplace dirs, 3 non-marketplace roots. But a fresh `scout(Path.home())` today gives 1299 entries (1186 other / 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings), 138 roots, 56 dirs, and SEVEN non-marketplace roots - the graphify skill has since added `~/.claude/skills/graphify/references/hooks.md` and `~/.codex/skills/graphify/references/hooks.md` plus their parent dirs. The substring false positives are actively growing, which strengthens the packet rather than weakening it.
- SPEC and TESTS agents: calling Context claim 1 'stale' and predicting 'Grok will mark them REFUTED-as-written' is the wrong verdict. There are three different snapshots in play, not one drifting number: the packet's 2026-09-04 scout (940/59/48/3/1), the on-disk watchlist written 2026-09-05 (1068/134/54), and a fresh scout today (1299/138/56 as of this run; the tests and spec agents each got 1242 earlier in this same session). A count that moves during a single afternoon is not refutable after the fact. The report should mark claim 1 CONFIRMED-directionally with today's re-run pasted verbatim and both the on-disk and fresh-scout figures labelled, not REFUTED.
- SECURITY agent: 'Python 3.12 `rglob` already declines to recurse into symlinked dirs' - the venv is Python 3.14.7 (`pyproject.toml` says `requires-python = ">=3.12"`, which is the floor, not the runtime). The conclusion survives - I confirmed `.claude/linkdir -> ../secret` is not traversed - but it survives because 3.13+ defaults `recurse_symlinks=False` on `**`, so citing 3.12 semantics is the wrong basis to rely on. Any hand-written `os.walk` replacement for project discovery must pass `followlinks=False` explicitly rather than inherit the behaviour.
- SECURITY agent's symlink escape is CONFIRMED and one notch worse than they stated. I reproduced it in a temp home: `.claude/hooks/hooks.json -> ../secret/id_ed25519` produces the entry `{"kind": "hooks", "path": ".../secret/id_ed25519", "size": 19}` - the resolved target path and the real key size leak into the watchlist. Their 'size 11' is just their fixture's byte count, immaterial. The addition: because `kind == "hooks"`, `watch_roots_from_entries` (daemon.py:196-213) also appends the resolved target's PARENT, so the daemon ends up adding an inotify watch on the secret directory itself, not merely recording metadata about the file.
- TESTS agent, BREAKS #4 (`test_move_self_triggers_watch_lost`, line 370): correct that it breaks, but only under one of the two implementations they themselves offer, and they do not connect the two. That test replaces `d.handle_watch_lost` with a stub, so a configured-root gate placed INSIDE `handle_watch_lost` leaves it green; only a gate in `_drain_inotify` - which is the option they recommend - makes `called == []`. State it as an either/or tied to the design choice, not as a flat 'the test must pass watch_paths'.
- SECURITY agent on the reload diff removing the self-watches: the hazard is real but the mechanism named is off. `_dirs_to_watch` (daemon.py:456-473) ALREADY unions `_self_paths()` with `_watch_roots` and returns a sorted set of resolved dirs, so the fix is to diff over `_dirs_to_watch()` rather than over `watch_roots_from_entries(...)` - one line, not a redesign. The genuinely hard part they under-weight: `_rebuild_watches` (daemon.py:480-494) closes the whole fd and resets `_auto_wds`, and I confirmed `_libc()` (daemon.py:96-109) binds only `inotify_init1` and `inotify_add_watch` while `Inotify` exposes only `add_watch`/`read_events`/`close`. Incremental removal is new C-ABI surface.
- PACKET Context claim 3 and every agent who repeated it: `~/.codex/config.toml` does not exist on this machine. `~/.codex/` holds only sqlite state files (`goals_1`, `logs_2`, `memories_1`, `queue_1`, `state_5` plus -shm/-wal), `installation_id`, `skills/`, `tmp/`, `.tmp`. There is no `auth.json` either, and `~/.cursor` does not exist at all. Confirmed present: `~/.claude.json` (52944 bytes, mode 0600, mtime 2026-09-06 02:49), `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.grok/auth.json`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json`. Codex and Cursor entries must ship marked UNVERIFIED per the packet's own Fact-check rule.
- PACKET's exclude list is wrong in both directions, and nobody costed the new vendor home. Four of its nine names do not exist under `~/.claude`: `todos`, `statsig`, `debug`, `plugins/cache` (`~/.claude/plugins` holds only `known_marketplaces.json` and `marketplaces/`). What exists and is unlisted, with file counts: plugins 447, projects 357, file-history 22, skills 10, backups 5, shell-snapshots 4, plans 3, sessions 2, telemetry 2, cache 1, paste-cache 1, themes 1. Critically, the packet ADDS `.grok` as a vendor home and nobody measured it: `~/.grok/sessions` 1265 files, `marketplace-cache` 562, `bundled` 422, `installed-plugins` 293, `docs` 27, `memtrace` 15, `logs` 3 - the packet's exclude list reaches only `sessions` and `logs` there, so adding `.grok` roughly TRIPLES the per-refresh walk unless the excludes are recomputed against the real tree. `~/.grok/hooks/` is empty (0 files), so the hooks_dirs entry for grok is correct but currently matches nothing.
- PROJECT DISCOVERY ground truth, correcting my own earlier report and refining INTEGRATION's depth ambiguity: under `~/Work`, pruning `.git`/`node_modules`/`.venv`, the only `project_dirs` hit is `~/Work/.claude` at depth 1. The `.grok` dirs - `~/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/.grok` and two nested `.worktrees/grok-*/.grok` - are hits only if `.grok` is added to `project_dirs`, which the packet's list omits. `~/Work` holds 411 files to depth 6, so the walk is cheap either way. Integration's depth-budget ambiguity is real but does not change the outcome for the only genuine hit, since depth 1 is reached under both readings.
- TMP-FILE R-SELF false positives - confirming CODEBASE/SECURITY/INTEGRATION and extending. Driving `Daemon._on_fs_event` directly: `watchlist.json.tmp`, `health.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp` and `allowlist.json.tmp` each emit `('R-SELF','high')`; `watchlist.json` and `alerts.jsonl.tmp` are correctly silent. The live `~/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl` (133 rows) contains exactly the one integration cited, at `2026-09-05T07:30:03.497268+00:00`, 8 ms after that watchlist's `generated_at` - note the date is 2026-09-05, not today, so it is one historical instance, not a running storm. It becomes a storm once refreshes get frequent.
- SPEC agent R28 vs spec section 7 - confirmed verbatim at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-omarchy-sentinel-design.md`: 'Record path, kind (`settings` / `hooks` / `mcp` / `auth` / `other`), size, mtime'. One consolidation: the same section lists `~/.config/sentinel/` and `~/.local/state/sentinel/` as seed candidates, so dropping `other` also drops the self seeds from the inventory. The spec agent filed these as two separate items (the `other`-metadata conflict and SELF-SEED VENDOR UNDEFINED); they are one conflict with one resolution.
- TESTS agent, minor and in their favour: `scout_main(["--refresh", "--home", str(home)])` works against the code as it stands - `scout_main` already has a `--home` argument (cli.py:43-64). No new CLI surface is needed for that test. The gap they should press instead is that `scout_main` calls `scout(home)` with NO config load whatsoever, so `[scout] project_roots` is invisible to the packet's own acceptance command unless `scout_main` gains a `load_config()` call.
- LINE NUMBERS, correcting the packet and the SPEC agent's restatements of it: `classify_kind` is scout.py:27-40 (packet and spec R9 say 32-37, which silently omits the mcp branch at 38-39 that R9 explicitly requires deleted); `DEFAULT_SEEDS` 14-22 (packet Context 4 and spec R16 say 12-20); `_entry_for` 43-50 (spec R27 says 41-49); `_path_under` 149-160 (spec R37 says 149-161, packet Context 6 says 148-159); `scout` 53-81; `write_watchlist` 84-87. The packet's `daemon.py:628` for the watchlist ignore is plain wrong - line 628 is inside `consume_launches`; the real sites are `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` at daemon.py:45-59, consulted at 532 (`handle_write`) and 701 (`_on_fs_event`). Every daemon line number the spec agent cited independently checks out within one line.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline is exactly 210 collected / 210 passed in 1.88 s in this worktree, on Python 3.14.7 in `.venv` (pyproject floor is >=3.12). Every post-change count must be quoted from pytest, not estimated.
- `src/sentinel/vendors.py` does not exist. W3-05 has not landed. W3-03 creates the file; whoever lands second must extend rather than replace, and the two schemas (home/settings/hooks_dirs/mcp/auth/project_dirs/exclude vs basenames/argv_bypass/config_bypass) do not overlap.
- The packet's 'writes atomically (tmp + os.replace)' bullet is already satisfied: `write_watchlist` (scout.py:84-87) calls `write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:44-60), which opens a 0600 tmp beside the target, fsyncs, and `os.replace`s. It is a no-op bullet; `write_private_atomic` must stay the only writer and nobody should reimplement it.
- Hot reload needs new ctypes surface. `_libc()` binds only `inotify_init1` and `inotify_add_watch`; `Inotify` exposes only `add_watch`, `read_events`, `close`; `_rebuild_watches` is all-or-nothing and resets `_auto_wds`. 'Diff and add/remove watches' is not a diff, it is `inotify_rm_watch` plumbing plus `wd_to_path`/`_auto_wds` bookkeeping - or an accepted full rebuild.
- The 2 s debounce has no timer to fire it. `run` blocks in `select.select(..., self._sample_timeout())` and `_sample_timeout` only accounts for `sampler_interval` (clamped 2-5 s); `tick()` only calls `maybe_sample()`. The debounce must use the injectable `self._clock` and `_sample_timeout` must be min'ed with the reload deadline, or the reload lands late or never on an idle machine.
- `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` contains `alerts.jsonl.tmp` but none of the other `.tmp` twins. Reproduced: `watchlist.json.tmp`, `health.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp` and `allowlist.json.tmp` each produce a bogus `R-SELF` high through `_on_fs_event`. W3-03 makes refreshes frequent, so the tmp names must be handled in this same change.
- S5 is half-landed. `_auto_wds` tracking (daemon.py:414) and silent transient-wd drop (daemon.py:731-735, 742) are implemented, and the exact regression test the packet asks for already exists and passes as `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent` (20x lock-dir churn, zero alerts, zero refreshes). Still missing: hooks-only auto-watch (daemon.py:722 is unconditional) and the 60 s rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` (daemon.py:216-218 has none).
- The 60 s rate limit breaks two currently-green tests: `test_overflow_emits_warning_and_refreshes` (asserts `called == ['refresh','refresh']` at line 175) and `test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes` (same assertion at line 468). Both call the handler twice back-to-back with `refresh_watchlist` stubbed as `lambda home=None: ...`. The packet demands both the rate limit and a green `pytest -q`; the Chief must amend one.
- The S5 paragraph sits under `## Non-goals`, above the two real non-goal bullets, while being phrased as mandatory work. An implementer following headings skips it. This needs an explicit Chief ruling, and the same defect exists in W3-05 and W3-07.
- The `seeds=` kwarg has a live consumer: `tests/test_scout.py:32` calls `scout(FIXTURES, seeds=['.claude', '.missing-agent', '.codex'])`. Dropping it for `project_roots` breaks that test, and `refresh_watchlist(home)` (daemon.py:216) is monkeypatched as `lambda home=None:` in five places, so any new positional parameter breaks those stubs too.
- `tests/fixtures/fake_home` contains exactly three files (`.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/hooks/x.sh`, `.codex/auth.json`) and is referenced only by the three tests in `tests/test_scout.py`. The richer fixture must be built in `tmp_path` because git cannot track files under a nested `Work/proj/.git/`, and the committed tree can be deleted in the same commit. Note `test_write_watchlist_under_state_dir` asserts the literal strings 'settings', 'hooks' and 'auth' appear in the output, so the vendor table must keep `.codex/auth.json` -> auth and `.claude/hooks/*` -> hooks for that test to survive unchanged.
- Config plumbing for `[scout] project_roots` does not exist anywhere. `load_config` (daemon.py:221-258) parses only the `[daemon]` table and only four keys; `packaging/config.toml` has only `[kill]` and `[notify]`; `cli.scout_main` calls `scout(home)` with no config load. Who reads the key, whether `~` is expanded, and whether the template gains the section (guarded by `tests/test_config_template.py`) all need stating.
- `pyproject.toml` has no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` and there is no `pytest.ini`/`setup.cfg`, so `@pytest.mark.slow` warns and the perf test still runs under the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command. Registering the marker is a pyproject edit the packet does not mention.
- Classifying `~/.claude.json` as `settings` puts an inotify watch on `$HOME`: `_dirs_to_watch` (daemon.py:456-473) watches `path.parent.resolve()` for every non-directory root. Combined with the unconditional auto-watch at daemon.py:722, one `git clone` in `$HOME` cascades watch descriptors. Scoping auto-watch to hooks roots is a prerequisite for adding `~/.claude.json`, not an optional S5 extra.
- `watch_roots_from_entries` does no validation - `KeyError: 'path'` on `[{'kind':'settings'}]`, `AttributeError` on `['nope']` or `[None]` - and `_load_watch_paths` catches only `OSError` and `json.JSONDecodeError`. Once a file write triggers reload, one malformed `watchlist.json` is a one-write kill of the daemon. Load-time validation is mandatory in this packet.
- `_entry_for` follows symlinks (`path.stat()` + `path.resolve()`) and has no `try/except OSError`. Both the credential-leak escape and the mid-walk-vanish crash are pre-existing, are not in the packet's Context, and must be called out to the implementer so they are not preserved verbatim while the surrounding function is rewritten.
- Merging code alone does not fix anything on the live machine: `_ensure_watchlist` (daemon.py:446-454) refreshes only when the file is ABSENT, so the daemon keeps reading the v1 file and its 131 marketplace roots until a manual `sentinel-scout --refresh` or the 03:30 timer. A `version != 2` -> force one refresh check at load is the cheap fix and is not in the packet.
- Report both snapshots and label them: the on-disk `watchlist.json` (1068 entries / 134 roots / 54 dirs / 3 non-marketplace roots, generated 2026-09-05T07:30) and a fresh scout at implementation time (1299 / 138 / 56 / 7 today). The Goal's '134 to under 20' should be restated against whichever number the acceptance snippet actually measures - `len(watch_roots_from_entries(...))`, which is 138 today.
- This run stays read-only on the live system: no systemctl, no omarchy, no sudo, no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local, no edits to tests/joint/, no new dependencies, no push/merge/rebase. The live daemon is pid 18419 running from `~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv` off an editable install pointing at `~/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel/src`, so merged code cannot reach the Owner in this run at all - reading those paths for evidence, as all five of us did, is fine.

**Final position.** "W3-03 is correctly motivated and the tree confirms its premise, but it cannot be implemented as written and it should not be implemented in one packet. From the code side there are three findings that change the plan.\n\nFirst, the packet's Root-resolution bullet does not achieve the packet's own perf target, and no analyst caught it. Pre-resolving roots takes `_path_under` from 12.2 ms to 3.05 ms at the live 138 roots - real, but at 300 roots the pre-resolved `relative_to` loop still costs 6.58 ms/event, i.e. 6.6 s for the packet's 1000 events against a 500 ms bound. The cost is ValueError construction in `Path.relative_to`, not `resolve()` (0.064 ms). Matching resolved paths as strings with `s == root or s.startswith(root + os.sep)` costs 0.075 ms/event at 300 roots and 0.045 ms at 138, is behaviourally identical across the 404 real paths I checked, and needs one guard for a root of `/`. R37 should be rewritten to require string comparison over pre-resolved roots; pre-resolution alone is necessary but not sufficient, and the tests agent's timed test would fail as designed.\n\nSecond, the security-critical work is not the precision work. `_entry_for` follows symlinks, so a planted `.claude/hooks/hooks.json -> ~/.ssh/id_ed25519` puts the resolved key path and its real size in the watchlist and makes the daemon watch the key's directory - I reproduced this. `watch_roots_from_entries` crashes on any malformed entry, and hot reload turns that latent crash into a one-write kill. `_entry_for`'s unguarded `stat()` becomes an availability bug the moment `~/Work` is in scope. None of these three appear in the packet's Context, and all three must be fixed in whatever lands, with load-time validation of watchlist paths against the vendor table plus a hard deny list.\n\nThird, the ground truth has moved and the packet's constants are wrong for this machine. Four of nine exclude names do not exist under `~/.claude`; the twelve directories that actually hold the volume are unlisted; `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.codex/auth.json` and `~/.cursor` do not exist at all; and `.grok`, which this packet newly adds as a vendor home, brings 2500+ files across `sessions`, `marketplace-cache`, `bundled` and `installed-plugins` that the exclude list does not reach - adding it roughly triples the walk. The exclude list must be recomputed against the real trees, and Codex and Cursor must ship marked UNVERIFIED.\n\nMy recommended split, for the Chief: land the vendor table, exact-match classification, `version: 2` plus `vendor`, and the symlink/stat/validation hardening as W3-03a - that alone takes 138 roots to single digits and is testable without touching the daemon loop. Land pre-resolved-plus-string root matching as W3-03b, a self-contained `rules.py` change with a deterministic call-count guard rather than a wall-clock one. Defer hot reload to W3-03c, after the Chief rules on the S5 scope question, the 60 s rate limit vs two green tests, and - the one I would not let slide - whether a foreign rewrite of `watchlist.json` reloads silently. The security agent is right that 'no alert' as literally specified makes `watchlist.json` the single file an agent running as the user can rewrite to `{\"paths\": []}` to blind the daemon within two seconds; silent must mean 'silent for a reload Sentinel itself caused', and that is a decision, not an implementation detail.\n\nProject discovery and agent-cwd forwarding I would drop from this packet entirely. The whole discoverable surface under `~/Work` is one directory, `~/Work/.claude`, at depth 1 out of 411 files; the feature's cost is entirely in unspecified config plumbing, an unstated depth semantics, and accepting attacker-controlled cwds as walk roots. It is a separate packet with its own threat model, and removing it also removes the `seeds=` signature conflict from W3-03's blast radius."

### W3-03 · integration

W3-03's premise checks out exactly against the Owner's live state: 1068 watchlist entries collapse to 134 watch roots, 131 of them inside ~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/, and only three files in the whole inventory are genuinely worth watching. The integration danger is not the precision work but its side effects -- vendors.py is claimed by both W3-03 and W3-05 with incompatible schemas, W3-07 rewrites the same OPERATIONAL_NAMES branch the hot reload hooks into, and classifying ~/.claude.json as `settings` silently puts an inotify watch on $HOME, where the existing auto-watch code will cascade into any new directory. On the live machine the change trades a one-off marketplace storm for a steady drip of high alerts from four config files that agents rewrite during ordinary use, and the fix does not take effect at all until someone re-runs the scout.

**Findings**

- Context claims 1 and 6 CONFIRMED against the live machine: /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json holds 1068 entries (957 `other`, 59 hooks, 48 mcp, 3 auth, 1 settings), `sentinel.daemon.watch_roots_from_entries` turns them into 134 roots / 54 inotify directories, and 131 roots and 52 directories sit inside ~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/.
- The entire genuine watch surface today is three files -- ~/.claude/settings.json, ~/.claude/.credentials.json, and one substring false positive ~/.codex/skills/.system/openai-docs/references/mcp-diagnostics.md -- so W3-03's precision win is real and large.
- Sharpest in-flight collision: both W3-03 and W3-05 create src/sentinel/vendors.py, both say "extend if it exists", and their schemas do not overlap (W3-03 wants home/settings/hooks_dirs/mcp/auth/project_dirs/exclude; W3-05 wants basenames/argv_bypass/config_bypass), so whichever lands second will rewrite the module rather than extend it.
- W3-05 also stores a per-file config hash "in watchlist.json" for its new R-BYPASS-CONFIG rule, which is the same scout output file W3-03 is versioning to `"version": 2` -- two packets changing one schema with no agreed field layout.
- W3-07 changes the meaning of `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:45-60) so operational writes are no longer ignored but ledger-classified, and it adds `pause_until` to that frozenset; W3-03's hot reload hooks into the exact same branch of `Daemon._on_fs_event` (daemon.py:697-703), so the two will conflict textually and semantically.
- W3-04 rewrites `Daemon.sample_proc` (daemon.py:644-674) into a single ProcSnapshot pass, while W3-03 asks the daemon to hand `sample_proc`'s agent cwds to `scout()`; W3-04's Changes section never mentions exposing cwds, so the dependency is unstated in both directions.
- UX-02a is already merged and `src/sentinel/investigate.py:242` prints `grep -n -F '"path": "<path>"' <state>/watchlist.json` as a user-facing verify step -- once `other` entries stop being written, that command returns nothing for the 125 historical marketplace alerts, so investigate's own "Verify it yourself" section will silently fail on existing rows.
- `watch_roots_from_entries` adds a parent directory only for kind `hooks`, but `Daemon._dirs_to_watch` (daemon.py:456-473) maps every non-directory root to its parent, so classifying ~/.claude.json as `settings` places an inotify watch on $HOME itself.
- With $HOME watched, `Daemon._drain_inotify` (daemon.py:720-724) auto-adds a watch for every directory created there and recursively for their children, so a single `git clone` or `npm install` in $HOME cascades watch descriptors without bound -- the S5 rule "auto-watch new directories only under hooks roots" is the required mitigation.
- The hot-reload feature writes into a directory the daemon already watches: `write_private_atomic` (src/sentinel/fsutil.py:42-48) creates `watchlist.json.tmp` beside the target, `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` lists `alerts.jsonl.tmp` but not `watchlist.json.tmp`, and `evaluate_write` treats state_dir as a self_path with an unknown writer -- the live alerts.jsonl already contains one R-SELF **high** on /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json.tmp, emitted at 07:30:03.497 in the same millisecond as the refresh that wrote it.
- Config plumbing for `[scout] project_roots` does not exist anywhere: `load_config` (daemon.py:221-259) parses only the `[daemon]` table and only four keys, `refresh_watchlist(home)` (daemon.py:216) accepts nothing else, and `cli.scout_main` (src/sentinel/cli.py:43-64) calls `scout(home)` with no config at all, so the nightly timer, the menu row, and the packet's own acceptance command would all ignore the configured roots.
- The live daemon is pid 18419 running /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin/sentinel daemon (up 6h37m) from an editable install whose .pth points at /home/tav/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel/src, so merged code reaches the Owner only via `omarchy plugin update` plus a service restart -- neither permitted in this run.
- Merging the code alone does not fix the storm: `Daemon._ensure_watchlist` (daemon.py:446-454) refreshes only when watchlist.json is absent, so an updated daemon keeps reading the v1 file and its 131 marketplace roots until `sentinel-scout --refresh` runs or the 03:30 sentinel-inventory.timer fires -- a `version < 2` check forcing one refresh at load is the cheap fix and is not in the packet.
- Four new alert sources all exist and are actively written on the Owner's machine right now: ~/.claude.json (52915 bytes, mtime 2026-09-06 02:36), ~/.grok/config.toml (mtime 02:22), ~/.gemini/settings.json, and ~/Work/.claude/settings.local.json; ~/.codex/config.toml and ~/.codex/auth.json are absent.
- The packet's `## Non-goals` section is malformed -- the S5 security-review paragraph (auto-watch scoping, transient-wd tracking, lost-watch alerts only for configured roots, 60 s rate limit on `refresh_watchlist`) is pasted above the two real non-goals, so hard requirements read as things not to do; W3-05 and W3-07 carry the identical defect.
- Half of S5 already landed as UX-00 (`Daemon._auto_wds` at daemon.py:414 and the transient-directory handling at daemon.py:731-735), but "auto-watch only under hooks roots", "lost-watch alert only for a configured root", and the 60 s rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` are all still missing.
- Baseline before any change: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` is 210 passed in 1.87 s; tests/fixtures/fake_home holds only three files (.claude/settings.json, .claude/hooks/x.sh, .codex/auth.json) and tests/test_scout.py asserts against them, so the packet's richer fixture tree replaces rather than extends that fixture.

**Risks**

- Biggest live regression risk: ~/.claude.json becomes a watched root, Claude Code rewrites it on project trust, MCP changes and session bookkeeping, and `coalesce_key` keys R-HOOK-WRITE on `rule|basename|path` with a 60 s window -- up to 60 high alerts an hour, with no content check because hashing is explicitly deferred to W6-03; measure its rewrite rate over an hour before merging, and consider severity medium or an mtime-only downgrade for it.
- Project discovery makes these very sessions an alert source: ~/Work/.claude/settings.local.json already exists and Claude Code rewrites <repo>/.claude/settings.local.json on every permission approval, so a Grok packet run under ~/Work would alert on its own approvals.
- Unbounded watch growth on $HOME via the IN_CREATE|IN_ISDIR auto-watch path is the one failure that could exhaust fs.inotify.max_user_watches (524288 here) or make the daemon a resource problem on the Owner's live machine; scoping auto-watch to hooks roots is not optional.
- The 2 s reload debounce has no timer to fire it: the main loop blocks in `select.select` with `_sample_timeout()` (daemon.py:757-771) bounded by the 2-5 s sampler interval, so unless the debounce shortens that timeout the reload lands late or, on an idle machine, not at all.
- Handing `scout()` the cwds of running agent processes is an unbounded scope expansion -- an agent started in $HOME or / would have the scout walk that tree; it needs a depth limit and a containment check against the configured project roots, neither of which the packet specifies.
- "Walk to depth 3 looking for project_dirs names, descend into .worktrees one level" is ambiguous about whether the depth budget counts the project or the project dir; under one reading ~/Work/.claude is found and this worktree's own config is not, under the other the nested .worktrees/grok-* checkouts and their .venvs are walked (6428 files, 92 MB under ~/Work).
- .grok is a vendor home in the packet's table but is not in its project_dirs list, and this repo has .grok/roles/*.toml at its root -- adding .grok to project_dirs would make the daemon alert on Grok's own role files mid-packet.
- The acceptance snippet sets XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/tmp/s3 (empty), so it exercises only in-code defaults and cannot catch the missing [scout] config plumbing; it also scouts the real $HOME, which is fine (stat only) but writes the Owner's full ~/Work path inventory into /tmp/s3.
- The live ~/.config/sentinel/config.toml is still the pre-UX-01 template with no [notify] section, and install-daemon.sh keeps an existing config, so a new [scout] section will never reach the Owner -- whatever ships as the in-code default is the behaviour they get.
- Must NOT be done in this run: no systemctl (the live sentinel.service, sentinel-inventory.timer and sentinel-unstable.service stay exactly as they are), no omarchy commands (`plugin update`, `notification send`), no sudo; no writes under ~/.config (which is where the live plugin source ~/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel/, the live config.toml, and the unit files live) or ~/.local (the live venv and ~/.local/state/sentinel/); reading those for evidence, as done here, is fine.
- Must NOT be done: no edits to tests/joint/ -- the harness test_adversarial.py, SCHEMA.md and cases/ are the Chief's, and SCHEMA.md's `requires` gate list currently knows only ux-01 and ux-02a, so a W3-03 gate is an ask, not an edit.
- Must NOT be done: no new dependencies, no reading the body of any scouted file (~/.claude.json holds an OAuth account block and may only be stat-ed), no scouting ~/.ssh, ~/.gnupg or ~/.aws, and no push, merge or rebase.
- Sequencing recommendation left open for the Chief: W3-03 and W3-05 should not run concurrently despite both claiming "Parallel-safe with" the other, and W3-07 should follow W3-03 so the OPERATIONAL_NAMES rework sees the hot-reload branch already in place.


### W3-03 · integration:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CORRECTING ALL FIVE OF US (spec R37/R39, codebase's 'the pre-resolved-roots change is more valuable than stated', tests' perf-test design, security's silence on it, and my own report): pre-resolving watch roots is necessary but nowhere near sufficient, and the packet's own perf budget is unreachable with the design as written. Measured in this worktree (Python 3.14.7), 300 already-resolved roots x 1000 events: CURRENT `_path_under` = 17.6 s; a `_path_under` changed exactly as the packet specifies (roots pre-resolved, rules.py:152 `Path(root).resolve()` deleted, `relative_to` loop kept) = 6.63 s, i.e. 13x over the packet's 500 ms budget. Per-event cost at 134 live roots decomposes as 0.014 ms for the one `path.resolve()`, 5.10 ms for the 134 `root.resolve()` calls the packet REMOVES, and 3.07 ms for the 134 ValueError-raising `relative_to` calls the packet KEEPS. `is_relative_to` is not a fix (5.86 s for the same 300k calls). A frozenset of root strings plus a parent walk gives 0.052 s / 1000 events and is O(path depth), not O(roots). Live-shaped (134 real roots, one write under ~/.claude): 12.12 ms now, 3.64 ms after the packet as written, 0.041 ms with set+parent-walk. R37 must widen from 'do not re-resolve roots' to 'containment must not scale with root count'.
- CORRECTING TESTS: `test_evaluate_write_does_not_resolve_roots_per_event` (counting `Path.resolve` calls, asserting 1 and independent of root count) pins the wrong contract. Under a packet-as-written implementation that test goes GREEN while `test_evaluate_write_1000_events_under_500ms` fails by 13x -- the resolve calls are gone but the ValueError loop still dominates. The deterministic guard must bound per-event work against root count (compare 3 roots vs 300 roots and require near-constant cost), not count resolve calls.
- CORRECTING MY OWN (integration) REPORT: I wrote that W3-07 'adds `pause_until` to that frozenset'. Wrong. `pause_until` is ALREADY in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` at src/sentinel/daemon.py:53 and is pinned by tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:645 `test_pause_until_write_is_operational`. The W3-07 packet's Context line 8 is stale on that point and I repeated it. The real W3-07 collision is semantic -- it changes what the `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` branch of `_on_fs_event` (daemon.py:697-703) MEANS, which is the exact branch W3-03's hot reload hooks into. That part stands.
- RECONCILING MY NUMBERS WITH SPEC/CODEBASE/TESTS: my '1068 entries / 134 roots / 54 dirs' described the ON-DISK /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json (verified just now: `version` is None, 1068 entries, 957 other / 59 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings). Their '1242 / 138 / 56' described a FRESH scout. Both were right about different objects and neither is a refutation of the other. A fresh scout right now gives 1322 entries (1209 other / 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings), 138 roots, 131 marketplace roots, 56 inotify dirs, 52 marketplace dirs. The entry count moved ~80 in three hours, so no report may pin it -- pin roots and marketplace roots, which are stable.
- CORRECTING TESTS on two details: (a) '~/.codex/ contains only sqlite files and installation_id' is incomplete -- it also holds `skills/`; codebase's wording is the accurate one. (b) BREAKS #2 overstates. `test_scout_classifies_settings_hooks_auth_without_reading` (tests/test_scout.py:8) and `test_write_watchlist_under_state_dir` (:40) both SURVIVE an exact-match vendor table so long as `.claude/hooks` is a `hooks_dirs` entry and `.codex` claims `auth.json`. Only `test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly` (tests/test_scout.py:32) hard-breaks, on the removed `seeds=` kwarg. One hard break, not three.
- SHARPENING TESTS' BREAKS #3 (the finding is right, the mechanism matters): `handle_overflow` calls `refresh_watchlist` UNCONDITIONALLY (daemon.py:585), while `handle_watch_lost` calls it only when `_watch_override is None` (daemon.py:607-608). Both failing tests (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:162 and :456) build the daemon with no `watch_paths`, so both reach the call and both assert `called == ['refresh','refresh']`. A rate limit therefore has to live inside `refresh_watchlist` itself or be gated per call site; it cannot be hidden behind the override check.
- CONFIRMING SECURITY's symlink escape -- I reproduced it independently. A symlink at `<vendor home>/hooks/hooks.json` pointing outside the tree produced the entry {'path': '<target>/id_ed25519', 'kind': 'hooks', 'size': 12, 'mtime': ...} and `watch_roots_from_entries` then emitted the SECRET'S PARENT DIRECTORY as a watch root. Also confirmed the mitigating half security stated: `rglob` does not descend a symlinked directory (a `.claude/linkdir -> secret` link produced zero entries), so only symlinked FILES leak today -- which means the new hand-written project walk must explicitly preserve that with `follow_symlinks=False`, or it opens a hole `rglob` currently closes.
- CONFIRMING SECURITY's malformed-watchlist crash: `watch_roots_from_entries` raises `KeyError: 'path'` for [{'kind':'settings'}], `AttributeError` for ['nope'], and `TypeError` for {'path': None}; `_load_watch_paths` (daemon.py:431-444) catches only `OSError` and `json.JSONDecodeError`. Hot reload converts that latent startup crash into a one-write denial of Sentinel on a live daemon.
- CONFIRMING the `watchlist.json.tmp` false positive on the LIVE machine, which security and I both flagged: /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl contains exactly one such row, id 1f822bee, ts 2026-09-05T07:30:03.497268+00:00, rule R-SELF, severity high, summary 'write to sentinel path by unknown', evidence.path = /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json.tmp. `write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:44) writes `<name>.tmp` beside the target and `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` lists `alerts.jsonl.tmp` but no other `.tmp`. W3-03 makes refreshes frequent, so this must be fixed in the same change.
- CONFIRMING my own $HOME claim by execution, since three reports treated it as inference: I constructed a Daemon with `_watch_roots = [~/.claude.json, ~/.claude/settings.json]` and `_dirs_to_watch()` returned `PosixPath('/home/tav')` alongside `/home/tav/.claude`. Classifying `~/.claude.json` as `settings` does put an inotify watch on $HOME itself.
- LINE-NUMBER HYGIENE -- codebase's set is the accurate one, spec's is off by small amounts. Verified: `_path_under` is rules.py:149-160 (spec says 149-161); `write_private_atomic` is fsutil.py:44-61 (spec says 43-59); `classify_kind` is scout.py:27-40; `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` is daemon.py:45-60 (the W3-03 packet's own citation of daemon.py:628 and W3-07's of daemon.py:42-50 are both wrong). Also verified against the machine: `~/.cursor` does not exist and `~/.codex/config.toml` does not exist, so Cursor and Codex entries must ship UNVERIFIED per the packet's own Fact-check rule.
- MINOR, CODEBASE: `load_config`'s defaults dict carries five keys, not four (`inotify` is the fifth); only four are read from the file section (daemon.py:243-252). That is the exact block a `[scout]` reader has to extend, and `packaging/config.toml` today has only `[kill]` and `[notify]` -- confirmed.

**Settled by the squad**

- The premise is real and the win is large. Fresh scout on the Chief's machine right now: 138 watch roots, 131 of them containing `marketplaces`, collapsing to 56 inotify directories of which 52 are under ~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/. Only three files in the whole inventory genuinely matter. Quote roots and marketplace-roots in the report, never the entry count -- it drifted 1068 (on-disk v1 file) -> 1242 (fresh scout 3h ago) -> 1322 (fresh scout now).
- The Goal line's '134' is the on-disk-file number; the acceptance snippet measures `len(watch_roots_from_entries(...))` on a FRESH scout, which is 138. The report and the acceptance line must say 138 -> under 20, and must say which number is bounded.
- The S5 paragraph is misfiled under `## Non-goals` but is phrased as mandatory work; all five of us flagged it. Needs an explicit Chief ruling on whether R40-R44 are in scope. Two of its four items already landed (`Daemon._auto_wds` at daemon.py:414 and the transient-wd handling at daemon.py:729-742), and the requested regression test already exists and passes as tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:422 `test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent`. Still missing: hooks-only auto-watch, configured-root-only lost-watch alert, 60 s refresh rate limit.
- The 60 s rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` breaks two currently-green tests (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:162 and :456, both asserting `called == ['refresh','refresh']`). The packet demands both the limit and a green `.venv/bin/pytest -q`; one must be amended before code is written.
- Dropping `seeds=` from `scout()` breaks exactly one test (tests/test_scout.py:32) and touches two production call sites, src/sentinel/cli.py:60 and src/sentinel/daemon.py:217. `refresh_watchlist(home)` needs a `project_roots` parameter or configured roots are silently ignored on every daemon-initiated refresh.
- Merging the code does not fix the storm on its own. `_ensure_watchlist` (daemon.py:446-454) refreshes only when watchlist.json is ABSENT, and `_load_watch_paths` ignores `version` entirely. A leftover v1 file keeps all 131 marketplace roots watched after the upgrade. A `version != 2 -> force one refresh` check at load is required and is not in the packet.
- `watchlist.json.tmp` must join `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` in this same change, along with the other `write_private_atomic` tmp names (health.json.tmp, allowlist.json.tmp, allowlist-session.json.tmp, notify-prefs.json.tmp, notify-burst.json.tmp, pause_until.tmp). One live false-positive R-SELF high already exists on disk from this gap.
- Incremental watch add/remove is new C-ABI surface: `Inotify` exposes only `add_watch`/`read_events`/`close` and `_libc()` binds only `inotify_init1` and `inotify_add_watch`. `_rebuild_watches` is all-or-nothing (it closes the fd and clears `_auto_wds`), so 'diff and add or remove watches' cannot reuse it.
- The 2 s debounce has no timer to fire it: `run()` blocks in `select.select(..., self._sample_timeout())` and `_sample_timeout` accounts only for `sampler_interval` (clamped 2-5 s). The debounce must use the injectable `self._clock` AND shorten the select timeout, or the reload lands late or not at all on an idle machine.
- `write_private_atomic` already satisfies the packet's atomic-write bullet (0600 tmp + `os.replace`, fsutil.py:44-61). That bullet is a no-op; `write_watchlist` must stay the only writer and no fresh `tempfile`/`write_text` implementation may replace it.
- Scout must keep never opening a body. Today that holds by construction (`_entry_for` calls only `path.stat()` and `path.resolve()`; scout.py imports json, datetime, pathlib, fsutil, paths -- no subprocess, no urllib). The read-guard test should be strengthened to poison `Path.open` and `Path.read_bytes` as well as `read_text`, and no `subprocess` may enter scout.py or vendors.py.
- Symlink hardening and watchlist input validation belong in this packet even though the packet does not name them: `os.lstat` / skip symlinked files, a post-resolve containment check against the vendor home or approved project dir, `try/except OSError` around every stat (a file vanishing mid-walk in ~/Work currently raises out of `scout()` into `refresh_watchlist`, which is called from `handle_overflow` with no handler), and load-time validation in `watch_roots_from_entries`/`_load_watch_paths` with a hard deny list and a root cap.
- 'No alert' on hot reload may only ever mean 'no alert for a reload Sentinel itself caused'. As literally written it makes watchlist.json the one attacker-writable file that silently reconfigures the daemon within 2 s, contradicting R-SELF as specced. Either land behind W3-07's ledger or emit a sticky-shaped R-SELF when a reload empties or shrinks the auth/settings root set.
- The reload diff must be taken over `_dirs_to_watch()`, not over `watch_roots_from_entries(...)` alone, and the self paths (config_dir, state_dir) must be unconditionally re-added and never removable by watchlist content. `_self_paths()` returns UNRESOLVED paths (daemon.py:426-429) and is passed to `_path_under` at daemon.py:538, so once roots stop being resolved per event, self paths must be pre-resolved too or R-SELF silently weakens across a symlinked XDG dir.
- `src/sentinel/vendors.py` is claimed by both W3-03 and W3-05 with non-overlapping schemas, and W3-05's R-BYPASS-CONFIG wants a content hash stored in watchlist.json -- the same v2 schema W3-03 owns, against W3-03's own Forbidden rule on body reads. The two should not run concurrently despite both declaring parallel-safety, and `_entry_for` should leave the hash field open rather than lock the shape.
- Baseline is 210 passed in 1.85 s in this worktree. pyproject.toml has no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, there is no pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini, and no test currently uses `mark.slow` -- so registering the marker (and deciding whether `.venv/bin/pytest -q` runs it) is a pyproject edit the packet does not mention.
- The fixture must be built in `tmp_path`, not committed, because git cannot track files under a nested `Work/proj/.git/`. `tests/fixtures/fake_home` is referenced only by tests/test_scout.py:5 (plus one historical plan doc), so it can be retired in the same commit.
- Hard rules for this run, unchanged: no systemctl, omarchy, or sudo; no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local; no edits to tests/joint/ (its SCHEMA.md `requires` gate list knows only ux-01 and ux-02a, so a W3-03 gate is an ask, not an edit); no new dependencies; no push, merge, or rebase.

**Final position.** W3-03's premise is confirmed and worth doing, but the packet cannot be implemented as written -- one of its two headline wins is mis-specified, and I would not let an implementer start until the Chief rules on four points.

THE BLOCKING TECHNICAL FINDING, which none of the five reports caught: pre-resolving watch roots does not deliver the performance goal. I measured the packet's own acceptance shape (300 pre-resolved roots, 1000 events) at 6.63 s after the change it specifies, against a stated 500 ms budget -- 13x over. The per-root `Path.resolve()` the packet deletes is only 5.1 ms of the 8.2 ms per-event cost at 134 roots; the 3.1 ms of ValueError-raising `relative_to` calls it keeps is what blows the budget at 300. Replacing the per-root loop with a frozenset of root strings plus a parent walk drops it to 0.052 s / 1000 events, and on the live 134-root shape from 12.12 ms to 0.041 ms per event. R37 must therefore be rewritten from "do not re-resolve roots" to "containment is O(path depth), not O(root count)", keeping R38's semantics (exact match, or under a root) which I verified the set+parent-walk preserves. Without that amendment the packet ships a perf test that fails on the Chief's own machine, and the tests report's resolve-call-counting guard would go green while it does.

FOUR DECISIONS I NEED FROM THE CHIEF BEFORE IMPLEMENTATION: (1) Are R40-R44, the S5 block misfiled under `## Non-goals`, in scope? Two of its four items already landed and its regression test already passes; the other two are the required mitigation for the $HOME watch this packet creates. (2) Does the 60 s rate limit apply to the overflow and watch-lost paths -- which breaks two green tests -- or only to the new hot-reload path? (3) Does hot reload stay silent unconditionally, or only for Sentinel's own writes with a sticky R-SELF otherwise? I will not implement the literal "no alert" reading; it makes watchlist.json the one file an agent can rewrite to blind the daemon in 2 s. (4) Does `other` metadata move to a separate inventory.json or is it lost? Dropping it contradicts spec section 7 and removes the basis for R-NEW-AGENT, and it silently breaks investigate's own "Verify it yourself" grep (src/sentinel/investigate.py:242) for the 125 historical marketplace alerts already on disk.

MY RECOMMENDED SEQUENCING: land W3-03 before W3-05 (shared vendors.py, colliding v2 schema) and before W3-07 (shared `_on_fs_event` branch). Fold in the four items the packet omits but that its own changes make load-bearing: the `version != 2 -> force one refresh` check, `watchlist.json.tmp` and its siblings into OPERATIONAL_NAMES, symlink refusal plus try/except OSError in the scout walk, and load-time validation of watchlist entries. Treat the entry count as unquotable -- it moved 1068 to 1322 across today -- and pin the report to 138 roots / 131 marketplace roots / 56 dirs, with the acceptance line reading 138 -> under 20.

### W3-03 · security

W3-03 rewrites the two most security-sensitive inputs Sentinel has: what the daemon decides to watch (`scout`/`classify_kind`) and how that decision can be changed at runtime (hot reload of `watchlist.json`). Eight invariants apply; the ones this packet can actually break are metadata-only/never-open-auth (symlinked entries already escape the vendor tree and leak a private key's size and mtime — reproduced), sticky self-tamper alerts bypassing pause and prefs (the packet's literal "no alert" hot reload turns `watchlist.json` into the one attacker-writable file that silently reconfigures the daemon), and 0600-in-0700 state (the atomic-write requirement must reuse `write_private_atomic`, not a new implementation). I made no edits; baseline is 210 passed in this worktree.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT 1 (runs as the user, never root): W3-03 needs no privilege, but the packet's goal of cutting watch roots creates pressure to raise `fs.inotify.max_user_watches`, and the new `[scout] project_roots` config lets an Owner point the walk at `/` or another user's home; the code must reject any project root that is not owned by `os.getuid()` and must never call `sysctl`, `sudo`, `systemctl`, or `omarchy` from `src/sentinel/scout.py` or the `sentinel-scout` entry point in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/cli.py:43`.
- INVARIANT 2 (metadata only, never bodies or credentials) — CONFIRMED BREAK TODAY: `_entry_for` at `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/scout.py:43-50` calls `path.stat()` (follows symlinks) and `path.resolve()`, so I reproduced a `~/.claude/hooks/hooks.json` symlink to a private key producing the watchlist entry `{"path": ".../secret/id_ed25519", "kind": "hooks", "size": 11, "mtime": ...}`; the fix is `os.lstat`, `if path.is_symlink(): continue`, and a post-`resolve()` containment check that the final path is still under the vendor home or an approved project dir.
- INVARIANT 2 continued — project discovery multiplies that exposure: any repository the user clones can ship `<repo>/.claude/settings.json` as a symlink to `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` or `~/.aws/credentials`, so the depth-3 walk described in the packet's Changes section must refuse symlinked files and symlinked directories (Python 3.12 `rglob` already declines to recurse into symlinked dirs — I verified `.claude/linkdir -> ../secret` was not traversed — but the new hand-written walk must preserve that by using `os.walk(..., followlinks=False)` and `entry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False)`).
- INVARIANT 3 (auth-kind files are never opened): the packet's move from substring matching to a vendor table invites classifying by content ("is this JSON a hooks file?") or by parsing `~/.claude.json` to find `mcpServers` and project trust entries, both of which the packet's Forbidden section rules out; `classify_kind(path, vendor)` must reach a verdict from `path.name` and the relative directory alone, `~/.claude/.credentials.json` must keep `kind == "auth"` and stay in the watchlist, and the existing `monkeypatch.setattr(Path, "read_text", boom)` guard in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_scout.py:8-19` should be extended to also poison `Path.open`, `Path.read_bytes`, and `builtins.open` for the new vendor and project-discovery paths.
- INVARIANT 4 (state files 0600 inside 0700 dirs): the packet says `sentinel-scout --refresh` "writes atomically (tmp + os.replace)", which is already true via `write_private_atomic` in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/fsutil.py:44-61`; a fresh implementation using `tempfile.mkstemp` in `/tmp`, `json.dump(open(path, "w"))`, or `Path.write_text` would produce a 0644 file (umask 022) holding the full inventory of the user's agent config and project layout, so `write_watchlist` at `scout.py:84-87` must remain the only writer.
- INVARIANT 5 (argv arrays only, never shell strings): nothing in W3-03 needs a subprocess, and the temptation is to speed the depth-3 project walk with `fd`, `find`, or `git rev-parse` — any of those built from a user-controlled project path is a shell-injection surface, so `scout.py` and the new `vendors.py` must import no `subprocess` at all; vendor and project names flowing into alert text stay safe only because `build_argv` in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/notify.py:222-235` passes them as list elements to `omarchy notification send`.
- INVARIANT 6 (no network from the daemon): the vendor table must be a static literal in `src/sentinel/vendors.py` with UNVERIFIED comments as the packet's Fact-check section requires, never fetched or auto-updated; `scout.py` currently imports only `json`, `datetime`, `pathlib`, and `sentinel.fsutil`/`sentinel.paths`, and `urllib` appears only in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/summarize.py:6-7`, which the daemon never imports — that separation must survive.
- INVARIANT 7 (no silent auto-kill): W3-03 touches no kill path, but its real analogue is silent blinding — dropping the 829 `other` entries, excluding `plugins/marketplaces`, and defaulting unknown vendors to `other` are all deliberate coverage reductions, so hooks that Claude Code actually executes from inside a marketplace plugin directory become invisible and that trade must be written down in `docs/collab/reports/W3-03-report.md` and the threat model rather than left implicit in a lower root count.
- INVARIANT 8 (sticky self-tamper alerts bypass pause and prefs) — THE PRIMARY RISK IN THIS PACKET: `is_sticky` at `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/notify.py:78-80` fires only for `R-SELF` with `evidence["event"]` in `{"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"}`, and the packet's hot reload is specified as "reload entries, diff against current roots, add or remove inotify watches, no alert", which makes `watchlist.json` the one file an agent running as the user can rewrite to `{"paths": []}` and unwatch everything within 2 seconds with no toast, no log line, and no sticky alert.
- INVARIANT 8 continued — the concrete requirement: the reload must be tamper-aware from day one, either by landing behind W3-07's `StateLedger` (`ours` reload silently, `foreign` emits `R-SELF` high with `evidence={"event": "foreign-write"}` so `Notifier.send` at `notify.py:420-423` takes the sticky branch before the `is_paused()` check) or, if W3-03 lands first, by emitting a sticky-shaped `R-SELF` whenever a reload shrinks the root set to zero or drops a previously watched `auth`/`settings` root — "no alert" may only ever mean "no alert for a reload Sentinel itself caused".
- CONFIRMED DEFECT that hot reload weaponizes: `watch_roots_from_entries` at `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py:196-213` does `entry.get("kind")` and `entry["path"]` with no validation — I ran it and got `KeyError: 'path'` for `[{"kind": "settings"}]` and `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'` for `["nope"]` — and `_load_watch_paths` at `daemon.py:431-444` catches only `OSError` and `json.JSONDecodeError`, so a malformed `watchlist.json` written by any process propagates out of `Daemon.run` and kills the daemon; with W3-03 that file becomes an event-triggered reload input, turning a latent crash into a one-write denial of Sentinel.
- CONFIRMED DEFECT the packet must not preserve: `watchlist.json` is in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (`daemon.py:45-60`) but `watchlist.json.tmp` is not, while `alerts.jsonl.tmp` is, so every `write_private_atomic` refresh creates a tmp file inside `state_dir()` — a self path per `Daemon._self_paths` (`daemon.py:426-429`) — and `handle_write` → `evaluate_write` (`/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/rules.py:211-226`) raises a bogus `R-SELF` high because inotify supplies no writer; W3-03 makes refreshes frequent, so the tmp name must join `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (or the ledger) in the same change.
- Reload must not unwatch Sentinel itself: `_dirs_to_watch` at `daemon.py:456-473` is the union of `_self_paths()` (config_dir and state_dir) and `_watch_roots`, so a reload diff computed only over `watch_roots_from_entries(...)` will treat the self-directory watches as "no longer in the root set" and remove them, silently killing the self-tamper tripwire that S1 and W3-07 depend on — the diff must be taken over `_dirs_to_watch()` and the self paths must be unconditionally re-added and never removable by watchlist content.
- Watchlist content must be re-validated at load, not only at scout time: because `_load_watch_paths` accepts whatever paths the file names, an attacker-written entry `{"kind": "hooks", "path": "/home/tav/.ssh/authorized_keys"}` makes the daemon add an inotify watch on `~/.ssh` (my symlink probe showed `watch_roots_from_entries` already emitting the secret's parent directory as a root because `kind == "hooks"` adds `path.parent`), so load-time filtering against the vendor table, the configured project roots, and a hard deny list (`~/.ssh`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.aws`, `/etc`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/run`, `/dev`) is mandatory, plus a cap on total roots so a flood of fabricated entries cannot exhaust the inotify budget and evict the self-watch.
- Agent cwds are attacker-controlled input to the scout: the packet has the daemon pass "the cwds of currently running agent processes it already reads in `sample_proc`" (`daemon.py:644-674`, `_readlink(pid_dir / "cwd")`), and a monitored agent can `chdir` anywhere before Sentinel samples it, so those cwds must be accepted only when they resolve under `$HOME` or a configured project root, must be checked for the `" (deleted)"`/`(unreachable)` forms `_exe_basename` already knows about at `daemon.py:324-328`, and must be scanned exactly one level for the vendor `project_dirs` names rather than walked.
- The S5 items the packet moved into Non-goals are still unimplemented and belong in this change: `_drain_inotify` at `daemon.py:720-724` auto-watches *any* new directory under *any* watched directory (not only under hooks roots as S5 requires), and `refresh_watchlist` at `daemon.py:216-218` has no rate limit, so the `handle_watch_lost` path (`daemon.py:591-611`) still lets a `mkdir`/`rmdir` loop drive full re-inventories — the packet's own regression test (create and remove `history.jsonl.lock` 20 times → zero alerts, at most one refresh) is the acceptance bar.
- Unguarded `stat()` is also an availability bug once project roots are in scope: `_entry_for` (`scout.py:43-50`) has no `try/except OSError`, so a file vanishing mid-walk in a busy `~/Work` build tree, or an EACCES directory, raises out of `scout()` into `refresh_watchlist`, which is called from `handle_overflow` and `handle_watch_lost` with no handler — every candidate must be wrapped, and unreadable paths skipped quietly rather than retried.
- Alert payload hygiene: adding `"vendor"` to watchlist entries is metadata and fine, but if any of it reaches `Alert.evidence` it must stay out of the cloud path — `build_redacted_bundle` in `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/summarize.py:56-70` filters through `ALLOWED_KEYS` and reduces `paths` to basenames via `_path_names`, so no new alert field may be added to that allowlist as part of W3-03.

**Risks**

- The packet as written says the hot reload emits "no alert", which directly contradicts S1/W3-07's requirement that a foreign write to `watchlist.json` be a sticky `R-SELF` high; if W3-03 lands first and hard-codes silent reload, the tamper tripwire for that file is gone and W3-07 will have to reopen this code — the Chief should decide the ordering explicitly rather than leaving it to whichever packet merges first.
- W3-03 and W3-05 both claim `src/sentinel/vendors.py`; two agents seeding the same table concurrently risks a merge where one side's `exclude` or `auth` lists are lost, which would silently restore substring-era over-watching or drop `.credentials.json` from coverage.
- Excluding `plugins/marketplaces` is a real detection blind spot, not just noise reduction: Claude Code can execute hooks declared inside marketplace plugin directories, so a planted plugin hook would go unwatched after this packet — accepted only if it is recorded in the report and the threat model.
- "Unknown names default to `other` and are not watched" means any agent CLI not in the vendor table is invisible until someone edits the table; a planted agent can simply choose a vendor Sentinel does not know.
- The symlink escape and the `stat()` crash are pre-existing in `scout.py` and are not named in the packet's Context claims, so an implementer following the packet literally may leave both in place while rewriting the surrounding function — they need to be called out to the implementing agent.
- Verifying each vendor's real config filenames (packet Fact-check item 2) means running `claude`, `codex`, `gemini`, and `grok` with `--help`; that is an execution of the very agents being monitored and should be done in a temp `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME` so it cannot mutate the Owner's live agent config.
- The acceptance block runs `sentinel-scout --refresh` against the Owner's real `$HOME` with `XDG_STATE_HOME=/tmp/s3`; `/tmp/s3` is a predictable path in a shared directory, so the watchlist of the Owner's whole agent and project layout lands somewhere another local user could pre-create or race — prefer `$(mktemp -d)`.
- A root cap or load-time filter that silently truncates the watch set trades one blind spot for another; whatever limit is chosen must raise a visible alert when it bites, otherwise the daemon looks healthy while watching less than it reports.


### W3-03 · security:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- INTEGRATION is measuring a stale artifact, not current behaviour. Its "1068 entries / 134 roots / 54 dirs" comes from the on-disk /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json, whose generated_at is 2026-09-05T07:30:03Z and whose `version` key is absent. A fresh scout(Path.home()) run just now gives 1322 entries (1209 other / 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings), 138 roots, 131 of them containing "marketplaces", collapsing to 56 inotify dirs of which 52 are marketplace. Both figures are real; they measure different things, and the report must say which.
- SPEC, CODEBASE and TESTS each state "1242 entries" as a fixed fact. I get 1322 for the same call a few hours later. The entry count is volatile (~/.claude/file-history, paste-cache, sessions, projects churn continuously), so it must not appear in any acceptance assertion. The root count is the stable number: 138 today, 131 marketplace. The packet's Goal line "134 to under 20" should read "138 to under 20", and the acceptance snippet already measures len(watch_roots_from_entries(...)), so that is the number the gate should name.
- INTEGRATION says W3-07 "adds `pause_until` to that frozenset". `pause_until` is already in OPERATIONAL_NAMES today (src/sentinel/daemon.py:45-60, read directly). That shrinks the W3-07 textual conflict estimate somewhat, though the semantic conflict on the same _on_fs_event branch stands.
- INTEGRATION says the 2 s debounce "lands late or, on an idle machine, not at all". Not at all is wrong. `_sample_timeout` (daemon.py:688-692) returns max(0.0, sampler_interval - elapsed) and `sampler_interval` is clamped to [SAMPLER_MIN=2.0, SAMPLER_MAX=5.0] (daemon.py:29-30, 92-93), so the select() timeout is never unbounded. Worst case the reload fires up to one sampler interval (<=5 s) past its 2 s deadline. Still needs `_sample_timeout` min'ed with the debounce deadline, but the failure is lateness, not starvation.
- I am correcting my own report: I described the symlink escape as leaking a private key's size and mtime. Re-running it just now, it is materially worse. With ~/.claude/hooks/hooks.json symlinked to an out-of-tree id_ed25519, scout emits {"path": ".../secret/id_ed25519", "kind": "hooks", "size": 14} because `_entry_for` (scout.py:43-50) calls path.resolve() and path.stat() through the link, and `watch_roots_from_entries` then returns BOTH the resolved secret file AND its parent directory (kind == "hooks" appends path.parent). `_dirs_to_watch` turns that into a live inotify watch on the secret directory. That is watch-surface redirection outside the vendor tree, not just metadata leakage. Critically, it SURVIVES W3-03's exact-match rewrite untouched, because the link is named exactly `hooks.json` inside an exact `hooks_dirs` entry. Fix must be lstat + `if path.is_symlink(): continue` + a post-resolve containment check, and it belongs in this packet since this packet rewrites that function.
- I also mis-stated the interpreter. `.venv/bin/python` is 3.14.7, not 3.12. The property I relied on (rglob not recursing into symlinked directories, verified: `.claude/linkdir -> secret` was not traversed) holds because `Path.glob`/`rglob` default `recurse_symlinks=False` from 3.13 onward. Any hand-written walk replacing rglob must reproduce that explicitly via os.walk(followlinks=False) / entry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False); it is a default, not a guarantee of the new code.
- TESTS proposes `test_path_under_uses_preresolved_roots` asserting `_path_under(real/"f.json", (link,)) is False`. I ran that against the tree: today it returns True. The proposed test is a correct pin of the intended post-change contract, but the report must record it as a deliberate behaviour change, not a refactor - it is precisely the direction that silently weakens containment for any caller that forgets to resolve.
- SPEC's R16 asserts the scout "now inventories ~/.codex/config.toml". That file does not exist on this machine (nor does ~/.codex/auth.json, nor ~/.cursor at all). Verified present: ~/.claude.json (0600, 52944 b), ~/.grok/config.toml (0644), ~/.gemini/settings.json (0644), ~/.claude/.credentials.json (0600), ~/Work/.claude/settings.local.json. R16 must be phrased as "the vendor table names them and the walk skips missing ones quietly", or the test derived from it fails on any machine without Codex installed.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline is 210 passed in ~2 s in this worktree (.venv/bin/pytest -q, run twice). No file was edited by me.
- Live scout ground truth, today: 1322 entries / 138 roots / 131 marketplace roots / 56 inotify dirs / 52 marketplace dirs. On-disk watchlist.json is 1068 entries / 134 roots, generated 2026-09-05T07:30:03Z, with no `version` key. Acceptance measures roots, not entries.
- CONFIRMED DEFECT, must land in this packet: `watchlist.json` is in OPERATIONAL_NAMES but `watchlist.json.tmp` is not, while `alerts.jsonl.tmp` is. Feeding state_dir()/<name> to Daemon._on_fs_event produces a bogus R-SELF high for watchlist.json.tmp, health.json.tmp, allowlist.json.tmp, pause_until.tmp and notify-prefs.json.tmp; watchlist.json and alerts.jsonl.tmp are correctly silent. The live alerts.jsonl already carries exactly one such alert, evidence {"path": "/home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json.tmp"}, ts 2026-09-05T07:30:03.497268Z - the same millisecond as the refresh that wrote it. W3-03 makes refreshes frequent, so the .tmp names must join OPERATIONAL_NAMES in this change.
- Nuance nobody stated, and it decides the alert wording: those R-SELF highs are NOT sticky. `is_sticky` (notify.py:78-80) requires evidence["event"] in STICKY_EVENTS = {"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"} (notify.py:40), and these carry only {"path": ...}. So today's noise is pause-suppressible - and equally, alerting on a foreign watchlist rewrite is worthless unless the reload path sets evidence={"event": "foreign-write"} explicitly, which is what makes Notifier.send take the sticky branch BEFORE the _is_paused() check (notify.py:420-423).
- CONFIRMED DEFECT that hot reload weaponizes: `watch_roots_from_entries` (daemon.py:196-213) validates nothing. [{"kind":"settings"}] raises KeyError 'path'; ["nope"] raises AttributeError; [{"kind":"hooks","path":123}] raises TypeError. `_load_watch_paths` (daemon.py:431-444) catches only OSError and json.JSONDecodeError. Making that file an event-triggered reload input converts a latent crash into a one-write denial of Sentinel.
- Symlink escape reproduced and it survives the packet's rewrite (see corrections). scout.py must gain lstat, symlink refusal, containment-after-resolve, and a try/except OSError around _entry_for (which has none today, so a file vanishing mid-walk in ~/Work propagates out of scout -> refresh_watchlist -> handle_overflow/handle_watch_lost with no handler).
- Self paths must be pre-resolved together with the watch roots. `config_dir()` and `state_dir()` (src/sentinel/paths.py) never call .resolve(), and `Daemon._self_paths()` (daemon.py:426-429) returns them raw. I demonstrated it: event <base>/real/sentinel/watchlist.json against root <base>/link/sentinel is True today and False once _path_under stops resolving roots.
