Dropping the per-root resolve (R37) without fixing this is a silent R-SELF bypass whenever an XDG dir traverses a symlink, not a perf detail. Add a daemon-side assertion that both tuples are resolved after load.
- Incremental watch removal is new C-ABI surface: `_libc()` (daemon.py:96-109) binds only inotify_init1 and inotify_add_watch, and `Inotify` exposes only add_watch/read_events/close. `_rebuild_watches` (daemon.py:480-494) closes the whole fd and clears `_auto_wds`, so "diff and add/remove" cannot reuse it.
- The reload diff must be taken over `_dirs_to_watch()`, not over watch_roots_from_entries alone: `_dirs_to_watch` (daemon.py:456-473) is the union of `_self_paths()` and `_watch_roots`, so a watchlist-only diff would compute the self-directory watches as removable and delete the tamper tripwire. Self paths must be unconditionally re-added and never removable by watchlist content.
- Classifying ~/.claude.json as `settings` puts an inotify watch on $HOME, because `_dirs_to_watch` maps every non-directory root to path.parent.resolve(). Combined with `_drain_inotify` (daemon.py:720-723), which auto-watches ANY new directory under ANY watched directory with no cap on `_auto_wds`, one git clone or npm install in $HOME cascades watch descriptors recursively against limits of 524288 watches / 1024 instances. Scoping auto-watch to hooks roots is mandatory, or ~/.claude.json must be watched some way other than watching its parent.
- Load-time re-validation of watchlist content is mandatory, independent of who wrote it: `_load_watch_paths` accepts whatever paths the file names, so {"kind":"hooks","path":"/home/tav/.ssh/authorized_keys"} yields an inotify watch on ~/.ssh via the same parent-adding path the symlink probe exercised. Filter at load against the vendor table, the configured project roots, and a hard deny list (~/.ssh, ~/.gnupg, ~/.aws, /etc, /proc, /sys, /run, /dev), plus a root cap - and make the cap raise a visible alert when it bites, or the daemon looks healthy while watching less than it reports.
- Agent cwds from `sample_proc` (daemon.py:644-675) are attacker-controlled: a monitored agent can chdir anywhere before sampling. Accept a cwd only if it resolves under $HOME or a configured project root, reject the " (deleted)"/"(unreachable)" forms `_exe_basename` already knows about, cap the count, and scan exactly one level. Note the daemon does not retain cwds today - `sample_proc` passes it into handle_process and drops it; that is new state.
- Test-suite facts, all verified by reading: tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:175 and :468 both assert called == ["refresh", "refresh"], so the 60 s rate limit breaks both; :370 test_move_self_triggers_watch_lost builds a Daemon with no watch_paths, so the "configured root only" rule breaks it; tests/test_scout.py:32 passes seeds=. All three tests in tests/test_scout.py reference tests/fixtures/fake_home (which holds exactly .claude/settings.json, .claude/hooks/x.sh, .codex/auth.json), including test_write_watchlist_under_state_dir. refresh_watchlist is monkeypatched as `lambda home=None:` at :167, :397, :428, :448, :461.
- pyproject.toml has no [tool.pytest.ini_options] and there is no pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini, so @pytest.mark.slow warns and the 300-root/1000-event timing test runs inside the plain `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command. The deterministic Path.resolve call-count test is the real gate; the wall-clock one is informational.
- Vendor ground truth on this machine (stat only): present - ~/.claude.json 0600, ~/.grok/config.toml 0644, ~/.gemini/settings.json 0644, ~/.claude/.credentials.json 0600, ~/Work/.claude/settings.local.json. Absent - ~/.codex/config.toml, ~/.codex/auth.json, ~/.cursor. Codex and Cursor entries must be annotated UNVERIFIED per the packet's own fact-check rule.
- Exclude-list reality under ~/.claude: of the packet's nine names only plugins/marketplaces, projects, shell-snapshots and sessions exist; plugins/cache, todos, statsig, debug and logs do not. Present and unexcluded: backups, cache, file-history, paste-cache, plans, session-env, skills, telemetry, themes, history.jsonl, CLAUDE.md. Exact-match classification keeps them out of the watchlist, so the exclude list only buys walk time - recompute it against the real tree rather than copying the packet.
- `write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:43-59) already does 0600 tmp + fsync + os.replace with cleanup on failure, so the packet's atomic-write bullet is already satisfied. It must stay the only writer of watchlist.json; a fresh tempfile/json.dump/write_text implementation would ship a 0644 inventory of the user's whole agent and project layout.
- The S5 block sits under `## Non-goals` but reads as mandatory work; half of it (`_auto_wds` tracking at daemon.py:414/729-742, and tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent) already landed. Auto-watch scoping, lost-watch-alert scoping and the refresh rate limit are the three still missing. The Chief must rule on scope before implementation, and on whether the rate limit applies to the overflow/watch-lost paths or only to the new reload path.
- Run constraints stand: no systemctl, omarchy or sudo; no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local (reads for evidence are fine and are all I did); no edits to tests/joint/; no new dependencies; no reading any scouted file's body; no push, merge or rebase. The acceptance block's XDG_STATE_HOME=/tmp/s3 should become $(mktemp -d) - /tmp/s3 is a predictable path in a shared directory and the file written there is the full inventory of the Owner's agent and project layout.

**Final position.** Ship the precision work, but three security items are non-negotiable additions to the packet and one is a scope call for the Chief.

Non-negotiable, in this packet, because this packet rewrites the exact code that contains them:

(1) Symlink hardening in scout.py. Reproduced live: a symlink at ~/.claude/hooks/hooks.json pointing outside the vendor tree makes scout emit the target's resolved path with its real size, and makes watch_roots_from_entries return the target's parent directory as a watch root, so the daemon puts an inotify watch on the secret's directory. It survives the exact-match rewrite untouched. Requires lstat, `if path.is_symlink(): continue`, a containment check that the post-resolve path is still under the vendor home or an approved project dir, and a try/except OSError around _entry_for (which has none, and which project discovery in a busy ~/Work will hit).

(2) Load-time validation and containment of watchlist entries. watch_roots_from_entries has three distinct crash shapes on malformed input and _load_watch_paths catches only OSError/JSONDecodeError, so hot reload turns a latent crash into a one-write daemon kill; and unvalidated paths let a fabricated entry point a watch at ~/.ssh. Filter at load against the vendor table, configured project roots and a hard deny list, cap the root count, and alert visibly when the cap bites.

(3) The reload must be tamper-aware from day one. "No alert" may only ever mean "no alert for a reload Sentinel itself caused". If W3-03 lands before W3-07's ledger, the minimum is: emit R-SELF high with evidence={"event": "foreign-write"} — the exact string, verified against notify.py:40 and 78-80 — whenever a reload shrinks the root set to zero or drops a previously watched auth/settings root. Without that literal event value the alert is not sticky and dies at the pause check, which defeats the point. Otherwise watchlist.json becomes the single file an agent running as the user can rewrite to {"paths": []} to unwatch everything within 2 seconds, with no toast, no sticky alert and no log line.

Also in this packet, cheap and already reproduced: add the .tmp siblings to OPERATIONAL_NAMES (watchlist.json.tmp, health.json.tmp, allowlist.json.tmp, allowlist-session.json.tmp, notify-prefs.json.tmp, notify-burst.json.tmp, pause_until.tmp). Five of them provably fire bogus R-SELF highs today and this packet multiplies refresh frequency.

Two design constraints I now hold more firmly than in my first pass. Pre-resolving roots (R37) must pre-resolve self paths in the same change — config_dir() and state_dir() are unresolved in paths.py, and I demonstrated the resulting R-SELF bypass under a symlinked XDG dir; a resolved-tuple assertion after _load_watch_paths is the guard, since the failure mode is silent under-detection rather than a test failure. And auto-watch must be scoped to hooks roots before ~/.claude.json becomes a settings root, because _dirs_to_watch watches a file root's parent and _drain_inotify then auto-watches every new directory under it recursively with no cap.

Chief decision I cannot resolve: excluding plugins/marketplaces wholesale is a real detection blind spot, not just noise reduction — Claude Code can execute hooks declared inside plugin directories, and on this machine ~/.claude/plugins holds only known_marketplaces.json and marketplaces/, so the installed-plugin case cannot be settled empirically here. Whichever way it goes, it and the "unknown vendor defaults to other, so a planted agent picks a name Sentinel does not know" gap must be written into docs/collab/reports/W3-03-report.md and the threat model rather than left implicit in a lower root count.

On numbers: use 138 roots / 131 marketplace as the baseline, never the entry count, which moved from 1242 to 1322 within hours today. And the report should state plainly that merging changes nothing on the Owner's machine until a scout refresh runs — _ensure_watchlist refreshes only when the file is absent, so the stale v1 file and its 131 marketplace roots persist; a version != 2 check forcing one refresh at load is the cheap fix and is not in the packet.

### W3-03 · spec

W3-03 asks for four coupled changes: a vendor table (`src/sentinel/vendors.py`) driving exact-match classification, project-dir discovery in `scout()`, hot reload of `watchlist.json` in the daemon, and pre-resolved watch roots in `rules._path_under`. I extracted 52 testable requirements plus the verbatim acceptance block, and re-ran the scout into a temp XDG dir: today's live numbers are 1242 entries / 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings and 138 watch roots of which 131 are marketplace, so Context claim 1's counts are stale though directionally right. Six items need a Chief decision before implementation: the S5 block filed under `## Non-goals` reads as mandatory work, its 60 s refresh rate-limit contradicts a currently-green test, the new `scout()` signature drops a parameter an existing test uses, and adding `~/.claude.json` as a root makes the daemon watch `$HOME` itself.

**Findings**

- ACCEPTANCE (verbatim from docs/collab/packets/W3-03-scout-v2-precise-watchlist.md:31-41):
```
.venv/bin/pytest -q
XDG_STATE_HOME=/tmp/s3 XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/tmp/s3 .venv/bin/sentinel-scout --refresh
.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'
import json,sys; sys.path.insert(0,"src")
from sentinel.daemon import watch_roots_from_entries
d=json.load(open("/tmp/s3/sentinel/watchlist.json")); print("version",d.get("version"),"entries",len(d["paths"]),"roots",len(watch_roots_from_entries(d["paths"])))
print("marketplace roots:", sum(1 for r in watch_roots_from_entries(d["paths"]) if "marketplaces" in str(r)))
PY
# expected on the Chief's machine: roots < 20, marketplace roots 0
```
- R1 (vendors): A module `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/vendors.py` exists and exports a table with one entry each for Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, Cursor and Grok Build.
- R2 (vendors): Each vendor entry carries the seven fields `home`, `settings`, `hooks_dirs`, `mcp`, `auth`, `project_dirs`, `exclude`, with `home` relative to `$HOME`.
- R3 (vendors): `settings`, `mcp` and `auth` hold exact filenames and `hooks_dirs` holds exact relative directories — no globs, no substrings.
- R4 (vendors): Every vendor's `exclude` covers at least `plugins/marketplaces`, `plugins/cache`, `projects`, `todos`, `shell-snapshots`, `statsig`, `debug`, `sessions`, `logs`, and files under those relative dirs are never scouted.
- R5 (vendors): `project_dirs` contains the relative names `.claude`, `.codex`, `.cursor`, `.gemini`.
- R6 (vendors): If W3-05 has already created `src/sentinel/vendors.py`, this packet extends that file (leaving `basenames`/`argv_bypass`/`config_bypass` intact) rather than replacing it.
- R7 (vendors): Any filename that could not be verified against the installed CLI's docs or `--help` is annotated `UNVERIFIED` in a comment inside `vendors.py`.
- R8 (classification): `classify_kind` takes a vendor argument — `classify_kind(path, vendor)` — replacing the one-argument form at `src/sentinel/scout.py:27`.
- R9 (classification): Substring matching is deleted, i.e. `src/sentinel/scout.py:32-37` (`any("hook" in part ...)`, `"auth" in name_lower`, `any("mcp" in part ...)`) no longer exists, and classification is exact filename or exact directory membership only.
- R10 (classification): A path classifies as `hooks` only when it lies inside one of that vendor's `hooks_dirs` entries.
- R11 (classification): `~/.claude.json` classifies as `settings`.
- R12 (classification): `~/.grok/config.toml` classifies as `settings`.
- R13 (classification): `~/.claude/.credentials.json` still classifies as `auth` and still appears in the watchlist (Context claim 7; the file exists on this machine, mode 0600).
- R14 (classification): A path belonging to no vendor in the table classifies as `other` and is never watched.
- R15 (classification): No file under `~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/**` classifies as `hooks` or `mcp` — specifically not `.../plugins/hookify/LICENSE`, `.../plugins/mcp-server-dev/README.md`, nor `.../plugins/y/hooks/hooks.json`.
- R16 (coverage): The scout now inventories `~/.claude.json`, `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.gemini/settings.json` and `~/.codex/config.toml`, none of which `DEFAULT_SEEDS` (`src/sentinel/scout.py:12-20`) reaches today.
- R17 (discovery): `scout` has signature `scout(home, project_roots=None)`.
- R18 (discovery): `project_roots` comes from config key `[scout] project_roots` and defaults to `["~/Work"]`.
- R19 (discovery): Each project root is walked to depth 3 looking for `project_dirs` names.
- R20 (discovery): The walk does not descend into the bodies of `.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv` or `.worktrees`, but does descend exactly one level into `.worktrees`.
- R21 (discovery): `scout` accepts a list of extra cwds and scouts the `project_dirs` found under each, and the daemon supplies the cwds of the running agent processes it already reads in `Daemon.sample_proc` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:644-675`).
- R22 (discovery): Project-level `<repo>/.claude/settings.json` and `<repo>/.claude/settings.local.json` appear in the watchlist with kind `settings` (spec section 7 names project `.claude/`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-omarchy-sentinel-design.md:173-186`).
- R23 (discovery): A missing seed, vendor home or project root is skipped without an alert or error (spec section 7: "Missing dirs = skip quietly").
- R24 (forbidden): No scouted file's body is opened — `~/.claude.json` may be stat-ed but never read — and the test enforcing this must keep passing (`tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_classifies_settings_hooks_auth_without_reading` monkeypatches `Path.read_text` to raise).
- R25 (forbidden): `~/.ssh`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.aws`, and anything outside the vendor table plus configured project roots are never scouted.
- R26 (output): `watchlist.json` contains the key `"version": 2`.
- R27 (output): Every watchlist entry carries a `"vendor"` key in addition to `path`, `kind`, `size`, `mtime` (`_entry_for`, `src/sentinel/scout.py:41-49`).
- R28 (output): Entries of kind `other` are not written to `watchlist.json` at all (they were 829 of 940 per Context; 1129 of 1242 when I re-ran it today).
- R29 (output): `sentinel-scout --refresh` (`src/sentinel/cli.py:43-64`) writes `watchlist.json` atomically via a temp file plus `os.replace` — already satisfied by `write_private_atomic` (`src/sentinel/fsutil.py:43-59`), which must stay in the path and keep mode 0600.
- R30 (hot reload): A write to `watchlist.json` observed in `Daemon._on_fs_event` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:697-703`) triggers `_reload_watchlist()` instead of being dropped by the `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` check at `src/sentinel/daemon.py:701` (set defined at `daemon.py:45-60`).
- R31 (hot reload): `_reload_watchlist()` is debounced to 2 seconds, so a burst of writes to `watchlist.json` produces at most one reload per 2 s window.
- R32 (hot reload): The reload re-reads the watchlist entries, diffs the resulting roots against the current ones, and adds or removes inotify watches accordingly.
- R33 (hot reload): The reload path emits no alert.
- R34 (hot reload): Every other name in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (`alerts.jsonl`, `health.json`, `allowlist.json`, `pause_until`, …) is still ignored by `_on_fs_event`.
- R35 (hot reload): After a rewrite of `watchlist.json` the running daemon's `_watch_roots` reflect the new file without a restart, making `sentinel-inventory.timer` (`track-b/systemd/sentinel-inventory.service`) effective on a live daemon.
- R36 (root resolution): Watch roots are resolved once at load into a `tuple[Path, ...]` of resolved paths (`Daemon._load_watch_paths`, `src/sentinel/daemon.py:431-444`; `watch_roots_from_entries`, `daemon.py:196-214`).
- R37 (root resolution): `_path_under` (`src/sentinel/rules.py:149-161`) no longer calls `Path(root).resolve()` per root per event (line 152), and still resolves the event path exactly once (line 150).
- R38 (root resolution): `_path_under` keeps its current semantics — an exact match with a root, or a successful `relative_to`, is inside; anything else is outside.
- R39 (perf): A write inside `~/.claude` with a realistic root count costs materially less than the measured 6.8 ms baseline, and the marked perf test bounds 300 roots x 1000 events at under 500 ms total.
- R40 (S5, filed under Non-goals): New directories are auto-watched only when they appear under a hooks root — today `src/sentinel/daemon.py:720-723` auto-watches any new directory under any watched directory.
- R41 (S5): Auto-added watch descriptors are tracked and dropped silently when they vanish (already implemented via `Daemon._auto_wds`, `src/sentinel/daemon.py:414, 729-735, 742`).
- R42 (S5): `handle_watch_lost` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:591-611`) raises its R-SELF alert only for a configured root, never for an auto-added descriptor.
- R43 (S5): `refresh_watchlist` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:216-219`) is rate-limited to at most once per 60 seconds.
- R44 (S5 regression test): Creating and removing `history.jsonl.lock` under a watched dir 20 times yields zero alerts and at most one refresh — the zero-alert half already exists as `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent`.
- R45 (test): `tests/test_scout.py` gains a temp-home fixture tree containing `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/.credentials.json`, `.claude/plugins/marketplaces/x/plugins/hookify/LICENSE`, `.claude/plugins/marketplaces/x/plugins/y/hooks/hooks.json`, `.claude.json`, `.grok/config.toml`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `Work/proj/.claude/settings.local.json` and `Work/proj/.git/`.
- R46 (test): That test asserts marketplace files are absent from the watchlist, `.claude.json` is kind `settings`, the project settings file is found, no `other` entries are present, and `version == 2`.
- R47 (test): `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_watchlist_rewrite_reloads_without_alert` writes a new watchlist into the state dir and asserts that after the debounce the daemon's roots changed and no alert was emitted.
- R48 (test): `tests/test_rules.py::test_path_under_uses_preresolved_roots` exists and pins the no-re-resolve contract.
- R49 (test): The 300-root/1000-event performance test carries a `slow` marker.
- R50 (report): `docs/collab/reports/W3-03-report.md` is written in PROTOCOL section 5 format, including the fact-check table for Context claims 1-7 and the pre-change kind counts from a scout run into a temp XDG dir.
- R51 (fact-check): Each vendor's real config filenames are verified against the installed CLIs (`claude`, `codex`, `gemini`, `grok` via mise) and anything unverifiable is marked UNVERIFIED.
- R52 (out of scope): Content hashing and diffing (W6-03) and the bypass-flag half of `vendors.py` (W3-05) are not implemented here.

**Risks**

- BLOCKING AMBIGUITY — the S5 block is in the wrong section: `docs/collab/packets/W3-03-scout-v2-precise-watchlist.md:48` sits under `## Non-goals` but is phrased as mandatory work ("auto-watch new directories only under hooks roots… rate-limit `refresh_watchlist`… Regression test: …"), while the actual non-goals are the two bullets at lines 51-52; the Chief must state whether R40-R44 are in scope or excluded.
- CONTRADICTION with a green test — the 60 s rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` (R43) breaks `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes`, which calls `handle_watch_lost()` twice back-to-back and asserts `called == ["refresh", "refresh"]` (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:455-470); the packet demands both the rate limit and a green `.venv/bin/pytest -q`, so one of them must be amended.
- SIGNATURE CONFLICT — the new `scout(home, project_roots=None)` (R17) drops the `seeds` parameter that `scout(FIXTURES, seeds=[".claude", ".missing-agent", ".codex"])` uses in `tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly` and that `DEFAULT_SEEDS` feeds; the packet never says whether `seeds` survives, is renamed, or that test is rewritten.
- UNSPECIFIED PLUMBING — `refresh_watchlist(home)` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:216`) has a single parameter and is monkeypatched as `lambda home=None: ...` in five places (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:167, 397, 428, 448, 461), so passing agent cwds positionally will break those stubs; the packet also never says where the daemon retains cwds (today `sample_proc` discards `cwd` after `handle_process`, daemon.py:668-673) nor caps how many are forwarded.
- SIDE EFFECT NOT ADDRESSED — adding `~/.claude.json` as a watched root makes the daemon add an inotify watch on `$HOME` itself, because `Daemon._dirs_to_watch` watches `path.parent.resolve()` for every file root (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:463-470`); that is the noisiest possible watch and partly re-imports the cost this packet removes, so the packet needs a rule to watch non-hooks files directly rather than their parent directory.
- CONTRADICTION with the spec — dropping `other` entries entirely (R28) conflicts with spec section 7, "Record path, kind (`settings` / `hooks` / `mcp` / `auth` / `other`), size, mtime" (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-omarchy-sentinel-design.md:186-194`), and the packet does not say whether that metadata moves to the separate `inventory.json` the spec lists in section 6 or is simply lost, which also removes the inventory basis for `R-NEW-AGENT` (spec section 8).
- SECURITY TENSION — "no alert" on watchlist reload (R33) conflicts with `R-SELF` as specced, "Edit/disable of Sentinel unit, config, allowlist, or watch list by a non-Sentinel writer" (spec section 8); an attacker who rewrites `watchlist.json` to empty it would silently blind the daemon, so the packet should require the silent path only when the writer is Sentinel/scout and an R-SELF alert otherwise.
- COVERAGE GAP — excluding `plugins/marketplaces` and `plugins/cache` wholesale also excludes hooks belonging to actually-installed Claude Code plugins, which live under `~/.claude/plugins/`; on this machine `~/.claude/plugins` holds only `known_marketplaces.json` and `marketplaces/`, so the distinction cannot be settled empirically here and needs a Chief decision.
- UNSPECIFIED CONFIG PATH — nothing today reads a `[scout]` section: `load_config` parses only the `daemon` section (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-260`) and `packaging/config.toml` has no `[scout]` key, so the packet must say who reads `[scout] project_roots` (scout, `scout_main`, or `Daemon`), whether `~` is expanded, and whether `packaging/config.toml` gains the key (guarded by `tests/test_config_template.py`).
- SELF-PATHS REGRESSION RISK — `_path_under` is also called with `self_paths` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:538` passing `Daemon._self_paths()`, which returns unresolved `config_dir()` / `state_dir()`, daemon.py:426-429); once `_path_under` stops resolving roots (R37), self paths must be pre-resolved too or `R-SELF` silently weakens whenever an XDG dir traverses a symlink — the packet only mentions watch roots.
- RELOAD MECHANISM UNDERSPECIFIED — "diff against current roots, add or remove inotify watches" cannot reuse `_rebuild_watches()` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:480-494`), which closes the whole inotify fd and clears `_auto_wds`; incremental removal needs an `inotify_rm_watch` ctypes binding that the `Inotify` class does not have today (it exposes only `add_watch`, `read_events`, `close`, daemon.py:153-193).
- DEBOUNCE TIMING UNSPECIFIED — `Daemon.run` blocks in `select.select(..., self._sample_timeout())` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:750-770`), so a pending 2 s reload will not fire unless the loop timeout accounts for it, and the packet does not say whether the debounce uses the injectable `self._clock` (needed for a deterministic `test_watchlist_rewrite_reloads_without_alert`).
- SELF-TRIGGERED RELOAD LOOP — `handle_overflow` and `handle_watch_lost` already call `refresh_watchlist` and then `_load_watch_paths` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:585-589, 606-611`); the resulting write to `watchlist.json` will now also trip the new reload path, so the packet should say whether Sentinel's own refresh suppresses the follow-on reload.
- STALE CONTEXT NUMBERS — re-running the scout today into a temp XDG dir gives 1242 entries / 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings, and `watch_roots_from_entries` yields 138 roots of which 131 contain `marketplaces`; Context claim 1 says 940 / 59 / 48 / 3 / 1 and 134 roots / 54 dirs, so the counts are stale (directionally correct) and Grok will mark them REFUTED-as-written.
- UNIT MISMATCH IN THE TARGET — the Goal says "watch root count drops from 134 to under 20" while Context claim 1 counts "54 inotify directories"; the acceptance snippet measures `len(watch_roots_from_entries(...))` (files plus hooks parents, 138 today), so the packet should state which number must be under 20.
- SCHEMA COLLISION WITH W3-05 — W3-05 (declared parallel-safe, same `src/sentinel/vendors.py`) requires `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` to hash vendor config files and store the hash in `watchlist.json` (`docs/collab/packets/W3-05-vendor-bypass-install-truth-click-path.md:19`), which collides with W3-03's Forbidden "Reading the body of any scouted file" and its Non-goal deferring content hashing to W6-03; whoever lands second must reconcile the v2 schema and the body-read rule.
- `slow` MARKER IS UNREGISTERED — `pyproject.toml` has no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` and there is no `pytest.ini`/`setup.cfg`, so a `slow` marker raises `PytestUnknownMarkWarning` and the 500 ms perf test still runs inside the plain `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command, turning a timing bound into a flaky gate on a loaded machine; the packet does not say whether to register the marker and add `-m "not slow"`.
- VERSION COMPATIBILITY UNSTATED — `Daemon._load_watch_paths` ignores the `version` field entirely (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:431-444`), and the packet does not say whether a leftover v1 `watchlist.json` must still load or should force a refresh.
- SELF-SEED VENDOR UNDEFINED — `DEFAULT_SEEDS` includes `.config/sentinel` and `.local/state/sentinel` (`src/sentinel/scout.py:18-19`), which belong to no vendor and would now be dropped as `other`; they are separately covered by `Daemon._self_paths()`, but the packet should say so explicitly rather than leave it implied.
- EXCLUDE LIST IS INCOMPLETE FOR THIS MACHINE — `~/.claude` also holds `backups`, `cache`, `file-history`, `paste-cache`, `plans`, `session-env`, `skills`, `telemetry` and `history.jsonl`, none of which are in the packet's `exclude` list; exact-match classification keeps them out of the watchlist, but the walk still stats ~1200 files per refresh.
- DEFAULT `["~/Work"]` IS OWNER-SPECIFIC — hardcoding this Owner's directory as the shipped default for an open-source package is a product decision the packet asserts without justification, and behaviour when the default does not exist is unstated (spec section 7's "skip quietly" is the only guidance).


### W3-03 · spec:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CORRECTING MYSELF, codebase and tests on "the packet's numbers are stale". I measured both baselines today. The live on-disk watchlist (/home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json, generated_at 2026-09-05T07:30:03Z) holds 1068 entries with hooks 59, mcp 48, auth 3, settings 1; watch_roots_from_entries gives 134 roots collapsing to 54 inotify directories, 52 of them under ~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/; 158 files sit directly in those dirs. Every one of the packet's Context claim 1 figures matches that file EXACTLY except the entry total (940 -> 1068, because `other` grows), and claim 6's "134 roots" / "157 files" match to within one file. The 138 roots / 56 dirs / 1242-1299 entries that the three of us quoted come from a FRESH scout, which is a different artifact, not a refutation. The fact-check table must record claim 1 as CONFIRMED-with-drift on the entry count only.
- CORRECTING tests (and myself) on a units error inside that: tests wrote "131 of them under plugins/marketplaces (packet says 52 of 54)" and I wrote "138 roots of which 131 are marketplace, so Context claim 1's counts are stale". The packet's "52 of the 54" counts inotify DIRECTORIES, not roots. Directories are 52-of-54 on the live file and 52-of-56 on a fresh scout; marketplace ROOTS are 131 in both. We compared roots against a directory count and called it stale. Nothing in claim 1 is refuted by that number.
- CORRECTING integration on S5 status. Integration says "lost-watch alert only for a configured root" is still missing; codebase says it is already implemented. Codebase is right, and the reason matters. Daemon._dirs_to_watch (daemon.py:456-473) only ever produces _self_paths() + _watch_roots, so every watch descriptor that is not in _auto_wds is by construction a configured root or a self path. The _auto_wds guard at daemon.py:731-735 therefore already satisfies that S5 clause in production. R42 needs no new code.
- CORRECTING tests on BREAKS #4. test_move_self_triggers_watch_lost (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:370) only breaks if the implementer replaces the _auto_wds guard with a new `path in self._watch_roots` membership check. That test drives a FakeIno whose wd_to_path holds tmp_path/"hooks" while _watch_roots is empty, a state _dirs_to_watch cannot produce. Keep the gate where it is and the test stays green with no edit.
- CORRECTING tests on the reload driver. Their new tests fire the debounce with d.tick(). Daemon.tick (daemon.py:694-695) is only `self.maybe_sample()`, and Daemon.run never calls tick() -- it calls self.maybe_sample() directly at daemon.py:771. A debounce hung off tick() would pass in tests and never fire in production. Drive it from maybe_sample() or an explicit check in the run loop, and min _sample_timeout() (daemon.py:688-692) with the pending reload deadline.
- REFINING tests on the R43 blast radius. I confirmed both breakages, but for different reasons: handle_overflow calls refresh_watchlist unconditionally (daemon.py:585) while handle_watch_lost refreshes only when _watch_override is None (daemon.py:606-608) -- and _daemon(tmp_path, monkeypatch) passes no watch_paths, so it does refresh twice. Also, their proposed test_refresh_watchlist_rate_limited_to_60s drives the limit through handle_overflow, which still calls _load_watch_paths and _rebuild_watches on every invocation; the test must assert only the refresh spy, not watch rebuilds.
- CORRECTING codebase's line-number nitpick. The packet's `scout.py:32-37` points at the substring block (actually 32-39), which is a near-miss, not "plain wrong"; classify_kind spans 27-40. The genuinely wrong citation is `daemon.py:628`, which sits inside consume_launches' line loop -- the real site is OPERATIONAL_NAMES at daemon.py:45-59, consulted at daemon.py:532 and daemon.py:701. DEFAULT_SEEDS is 14-22 (packet says 12-20). _path_under is 149-160 (packet says 148-159; my own R37 said 149-161 -- I was off by one too, and the per-root resolve is line 152).
- CORRECTING my own R28. I listed the spec-section-7 conflict but missed the concrete breakage integration found: investigate.py:242 emits `grep -n -F '\"path\": \"<path>\"' <state>/watchlist.json` as a user-facing verify step, and dropping `other` entries makes that command return nothing for every historical marketplace alert already in alerts.jsonl. Integration is right and this belongs in the report.
- CONFIRMING all three of security's defects by reproduction, so no one treats them as speculative. (1) Symlink escape: a scout of a temp home where .claude/hooks/hooks.json symlinks to a private key emitted {'path': '.../secret/id_ed25519', 'kind': 'hooks', 'size': 12, 'mtime': ...} -- the entry left the vendor tree and leaks the key's size and mtime; the symlinked DIRECTORY was correctly not traversed, so rglob's follow-symlinks=False behaviour is the thing the new hand-written walk must preserve. (2) watch_roots_from_entries raises KeyError('path') on [{'kind':'settings'}] and AttributeError on ['nope'], while _load_watch_paths (daemon.py:438-441) catches only OSError and json.JSONDecodeError -- once reload is event-driven, one malformed write is a one-shot daemon kill. (3) [{'kind':'hooks','path':'/home/tav/.ssh/authorized_keys'}] returns roots ['/home/tav/.ssh/authorized_keys', '/home/tav/.ssh'].
- CONFIRMING integration's live-alert claim precisely: /home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl contains exactly one row matching watchlist.json.tmp -- R-SELF high "write to sentinel path by unknown" at 2026-09-05T07:30:03.497268+00:00, eight milliseconds after the watchlist's own generated_at of 07:30:03.489507. watchlist.json.tmp is absent from OPERATIONAL_NAMES (daemon.py:45-59) while alerts.jsonl.tmp is present. This is a live, reproducing false positive that hot reload makes frequent.

**Settled by the squad**

- Two baselines, both real, and the report must carry both. Live on-disk watchlist: 1068 entries, 59 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings, 134 roots, 54 inotify dirs, 52 marketplace dirs, 131 marketplace roots, 158 files directly in watched dirs. Fresh scout today: 1299 entries, 61 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings, 138 roots, 56 dirs, 52 marketplace dirs, 131 marketplace roots. The Goal line's "134 to under 20" is honest against the live file and needs no amendment; the acceptance snippet measures len(watch_roots_from_entries(...)), so "under 20" is a ROOT count, not a directory count. Entry totals are volatile and must never be quoted as a fixed acceptance number.
- The whole genuine watch surface today is three files: ~/.claude/.credentials.json (auth), ~/.claude/settings.json (settings), and one substring false positive ~/.codex/skills/.system/openai-docs/references/mcp-diagnostics.md (mcp). The precision premise of the packet is correct and large.
- Vendor ground truth on this machine, for the UNVERIFIED annotations R7/R51 demand. EXISTS: ~/.claude.json (0600, 52944 B), ~/.claude/.credentials.json, ~/.claude/settings.json, ~/.grok/config.toml, ~/.grok/auth.json, ~/.grok/hooks/, ~/.grok/hooks-paths, ~/.grok/installed-plugins/, ~/.gemini/settings.json, ~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json, ~/.gemini/trustedFolders.json. ABSENT: ~/.codex/config.toml, ~/.codex/auth.json (~/.codex holds only sqlite state, installation_id, skills/, .tmp/) and ~/.cursor entirely. Codex and Cursor entries ship UNVERIFIED.
- The packet's exclude list is written against a tree that is not this one. ~/.claude actually contains backups, cache, file-history, paste-cache, plans, plugins, projects, session-env, sessions, shell-snapshots, skills, telemetry, themes, history.jsonl. Of the packet's list, todos, statsig, debug, logs and plugins/cache do not exist here. Since classification becomes an exact-match allowlist the list only saves walk time, but recompute it against the real tree rather than copying the packet's.
- vendors.py does not exist yet; W3-05 has not landed. W3-03 and W3-05 must not run concurrently -- same module, non-overlapping schemas, both saying "extend if it exists", and W3-05's R-BYPASS-CONFIG wants a content hash in the same watchlist.json v2 schema that W3-03's Forbidden section bars reading bodies to produce. _entry_for should leave the hash field open rather than lock the shape.
- Classifying ~/.claude.json as settings puts an inotify watch on $HOME, because _dirs_to_watch (daemon.py:463-470) adds path.parent.resolve() for every non-directory root. Combined with the unconditional auto-watch at daemon.py:719-723, one git clone or npm install in $HOME cascades watch descriptors without bound. Scoping auto-watch to hooks roots (R40) is not optional, and non-hooks file roots should be watched via their own path rather than by watching the parent.
- Incremental watch add/remove is new C-ABI surface. Inotify exposes only add_watch / read_events / close and _libc binds only inotify_init1 and inotify_add_watch; _rebuild_watches (daemon.py:480-494) closes the whole fd and clears _auto_wds. Either bind inotify_rm_watch and keep wd_to_path in sync with _auto_wds, or accept a full rebuild on reload and say so.
- The reload diff must be taken over _dirs_to_watch(), not over watch_roots_from_entries() alone, and the self paths (config_dir, state_dir) must be unconditionally re-added and never removable by watchlist content -- otherwise the reload silently deletes Sentinel's own tamper tripwire.
- Watchlist content must be validated at load, not only at scout time: wrap watch_roots_from_entries in a broad except, filter loaded roots against the vendor table plus configured project roots plus a hard deny list (~/.ssh, ~/.gnupg, ~/.aws, /etc, /proc, /sys, /run, /dev), and cap the total root count -- with a visible alert when the cap bites, so the daemon cannot look healthy while watching less than it reports.
- scout must stop following symlinks: os.lstat instead of stat, skip path.is_symlink(), containment-check the resolved path against the vendor home or approved project dir, use os.walk(followlinks=False) / entry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False) in the new hand-written walk, and wrap every stat in try/except OSError so a file vanishing mid-walk in ~/Work cannot raise out of refresh_watchlist into handle_overflow.
- write_private_atomic (fsutil.py:44-61) already gives 0600 + tmp + os.replace, so the packet's atomic-write bullet is a no-op. write_watchlist must stay the only writer; a fresh implementation with Path.write_text or tempfile.mkstemp would ship a 0644 inventory of the user's whole agent and project layout.
- watchlist.json.tmp must join OPERATIONAL_NAMES in this change. The same gap exists for health.json.tmp, allowlist.json.tmp, allowlist-session.json.tmp, notify-prefs.json.tmp, notify-burst.json.tmp and pause_until.tmp.
- Config plumbing for [scout] project_roots does not exist anywhere: load_config (daemon.py:221-258) reads only the [daemon] table and only four keys, refresh_watchlist(home) takes nothing else, cli.scout_main (cli.py:43-64) calls scout(home) with no config load at all, and packaging/config.toml has only [kill] and [notify]. tests/test_config_template.py asserts only that notify and kind are present, so adding a [scout] section does not break it. The packet's own acceptance command sets XDG_CONFIG_HOME to an empty dir and therefore cannot catch a missing reader -- whatever ships as the in-code default is the behaviour the Owner gets.
- The daemon does not have agent cwds today. sample_proc reads cwd at daemon.py:671 and passes it straight into handle_process, then discards it. Retaining cwds is new state, and those cwds are attacker-controlled (a monitored agent can chdir anywhere before sampling), so they need a containment check against $HOME or a configured project root, the " (deleted)"/(unreachable) filtering _exe_basename already knows about, a cap on how many are forwarded, and a one-level scan rather than a walk.
- Project discovery finds exactly two things under ~/Work today: /home/tav/Work/.claude (depth 1) and /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/.grok (depth 4). Under the packet's depth-3 budget and its project_dirs list of .claude/.codex/.cursor/.gemini, only the first is found -- .grok is not in project_dirs and sits past the depth budget even with the .worktrees exception. The depth budget is also ambiguous about whether it counts the project or the project dir; the packet must say.
- Merging the code does not fix the storm. _ensure_watchlist (daemon.py:446-454) refreshes only when watchlist.json is absent, and _load_watch_paths ignores `version` entirely, so an upgraded daemon keeps reading the v1 file and its 131 marketplace roots until a scout runs. A `version != 2 -> treat as stale, force one refresh` check at load is the cheap fix and is not in the packet.
- Test infrastructure facts: the suite collects 210; tests/fixtures/fake_home is referenced only by tests/test_scout.py:5 (plus two lines of historical plan docs) and holds only three files, so it is replaced rather than extended; the fixture must be built in tmp_path because git cannot track files under a nested .git; pyproject.toml has no [tool.pytest.ini_options], so a `slow` marker warns and the 500 ms perf test still runs under the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command.
- Excluding plugins/marketplaces is a real detection blind spot, not just noise reduction -- Claude Code can execute hooks declared inside marketplace plugin directories. Accepted only if written down in docs/collab/reports/W3-03-report.md and the threat model, alongside the fact that any agent CLI absent from the vendor table is now invisible.
- Run constraints, unchanged: no systemctl, omarchy or sudo; no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local; no edits to tests/joint/; no new dependencies; no reading any scouted file's body; no push, merge or rebase. Verifying vendor filenames via `claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`grok --help` must run under a temp XDG_CONFIG_HOME/XDG_STATE_HOME so it cannot mutate the Owner's live agent config. The acceptance block's /tmp/s3 should be $(mktemp -d).

**Final position.** W3-03 is implementable and its premise is confirmed, but six items need a Chief ruling before code is written, and two of my own earlier claims were wrong.

WHAT I RETRACT. The packet's numbers are not stale. Claim 1's 59 hooks / 48 mcp / 3 auth / 1 settings and "52 of the 54 inotify directories" match the live on-disk watchlist exactly, as does claim 6's 134 roots and (to one file) its 157 files-in-watched-dirs. Only the 940 entry total drifted, to 1068. Codebase, tests and I all compared a fresh scout against a live-file measurement and, in tests' and my case, compared a root count against a directory count. The Goal line's "134 to under 20" needs no amendment; record both baselines in the fact-check table and mark claim 1 CONFIRMED-with-drift on the entry total alone. My R37 citation should read rules.py:149-160, with the per-root resolve at line 152.

WHAT NEEDS A CHIEF DECISION.
1. Scope of the S5 block at packet line 48. It sits under `## Non-goals` above the two real non-goals but reads as mandatory. Ruling needed on R40-R44 in or out. Note R41 and R42 are already implemented via _auto_wds (daemon.py:731-735) and R44's zero-alert half already passes as test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent, so the live scope is really just R40 (hooks-only auto-watch) and R43 (the 60 s limit).
2. R43 versus two green tests. The 60 s rate limit breaks test_overflow_emits_warning_and_refreshes and test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes, both of which assert ["refresh","refresh"]. Either the limit applies only to the new reload path, or both tests gain a fake clock. The packet demands the limit and a green pytest; one must yield.
3. The `seeds` parameter. R17's scout(home, project_roots=None) drops the kwarg that test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly uses and DEFAULT_SEEDS feeds. Say whether seeds survives, and say explicitly that .config/sentinel and .local/state/sentinel move to Daemon._self_paths() rather than being silently dropped as `other`.
4. Silent reload versus R-SELF. "No alert" makes watchlist.json the one attacker-writable file that reconfigures the daemon within 2 seconds with no toast and no sticky alert. Security is right that this must mean "no alert for a reload Sentinel itself caused". If W3-03 lands before W3-07, require a sticky-shaped R-SELF whenever a reload empties the root set or drops a previously watched auth/settings root. Sequence W3-07 after W3-03 so its OPERATIONAL_NAMES rework sees the reload branch.
5. Dropping `other` entries. This conflicts with spec section 7's five-kind record and breaks investigate.py:242's verify command for every historical alert. Say whether that metadata moves to the spec's inventory.json or is lost, and what R-NEW-AGENT is then based on.
6. Whether installed-plugin hooks under ~/.claude/plugins/ are excluded along with marketplaces. Cannot be settled empirically here -- this machine's ~/.claude/plugins holds only known_marketplaces.json and marketplaces/.

WHAT I NOW TREAT AS SETTLED THAT I DID NOT BEFORE. Security's three defects are reproduced, not speculative: the symlink escape leaks a private key's size and mtime into the watchlist as kind `hooks`; watch_roots_from_entries raises on malformed input that _load_watch_paths does not catch, making one bad write a daemon kill once reload is event-driven; and a fabricated entry makes ~/.ssh a watch root. Integration's live R-SELF high on watchlist.json.tmp exists in alerts.jsonl at 07:30:03.497, eight milliseconds after the refresh that caused it. Codebase is right that R42 is already done; integration is wrong that it is missing. And tests' debounce driver must hang off maybe_sample(), not tick(), because run() never calls tick().

### W3-03 · tests

I read the packet, `src/sentinel/scout.py`, `src/sentinel/rules.py`, `src/sentinel/daemon.py`, `src/sentinel/cli.py` and all four affected test files, and reproduced the scout against the live home to check the Context claims. The design below is 31 tests across one new file (`tests/test_vendors.py`) and four existing ones, plus the fixture and marker infrastructure they need. Four currently-green tests break under the packet as written, two of them because of the S5 rate-limit and configured-root requirements, and those need a decision from the Chief before any code is written.

**Findings**

- FIXTURE (shared): add `fake_agent_home(tmp_path)` to /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/conftest.py, returning a tmp_path home built in code — NOT a committed tree — because the packet's fixture needs `Work/proj/.git/` and git cannot track files under a nested `.git` directory.
- FIXTURE contents (each file created with `write_text("{}")`): `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/.credentials.json`, `.claude/hooks/pre.sh`, `.claude/plugins/marketplaces/x/plugins/hookify/LICENSE`, `.claude/plugins/marketplaces/x/plugins/y/hooks/hooks.json`, `.claude/projects/foo/transcript.jsonl`, `.claude/shell-snapshots/snap.sh`, `.claude/history.jsonl`, `.claude.json`, `.grok/config.toml`, `.grok/auth.json`, `.grok/hooks/on-start.sh`, `.grok/logs/a.log`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.gemini/oauth_creds.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.codex/auth.json`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.ssh/id_ed25519`, `.gnupg/secring.gpg`, `.aws/credentials`, `Work/proj/.claude/settings.local.json`, `Work/proj/.git/HEAD`, `Work/proj/node_modules/pkg/.claude/settings.json`, `Work/proj/.venv/lib/.claude/settings.json`, `Work/sentinel/.worktrees/wt/.claude/settings.json`, `Work/a/b/c/d/.claude/settings.json` (depth 4), `Elsewhere/live-cwd/.claude/settings.json`.
- FIXTURE (migration): `tests/fixtures/fake_home` is referenced only by the three tests in tests/test_scout.py (grep-confirmed), so all three move to `fake_agent_home` and the committed tree can be deleted in the same commit.
- tests/test_vendors.py::test_vendor_table_covers_the_five_seeded_agents — asserts `set(sentinel.vendors.VENDORS) == {"claude", "codex", "gemini", "cursor", "grok"}`; fixture: none.
- tests/test_vendors.py::test_every_vendor_entry_is_well_formed — for each `Vendor` in `VENDORS`, asserts `home` is a non-empty relative str, and that `settings`, `hooks_dirs`, `mcp`, `auth`, `project_dirs`, `exclude` are each a frozenset of str where no member starts with `/` or contains a `..` component; fixture: none.
- tests/test_vendors.py::test_claude_exclude_lists_every_noise_dir — asserts `VENDORS["claude"].exclude >= frozenset({"plugins/marketplaces", "plugins/cache", "projects", "todos", "shell-snapshots", "statsig", "debug", "sessions", "logs"})`; fixture: none.
- tests/test_vendors.py::test_no_vendor_claims_a_secret_home — asserts no vendor's `home` and no member of any `project_dirs` is in `{".ssh", ".gnupg", ".aws"}`, enforcing the packet's Forbidden clause at the table level; fixture: none.
- tests/test_vendors.py::test_unknown_vendor_classifies_other — asserts `classify_kind(Path("/anywhere/whatever.json"), "unknown") == "other"`; fixture: none.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_marketplace_files_are_absent_from_watchlist — asserts `not any("marketplaces" in e["path"] for e in scout(home)["paths"])`, specifically that neither `plugins/hookify/LICENSE` nor `plugins/y/hooks/hooks.json` appears; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_never_descends_excluded_dirs — wraps `Path.iterdir` (or `os.scandir`) with a spy recording every directory visited and asserts `home/".claude/plugins/marketplaces"` and `home/".claude/projects"` are never in the visited set, proving exclusion happens during the walk rather than as an output filter; fixture: `fake_agent_home` + monkeypatch spy.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_claude_json_classifies_as_settings — asserts the entry whose path == `str(home/".claude.json")` exists and has `kind == "settings"` and `vendor == "claude"`; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_grok_config_toml_classifies_as_settings — asserts the `home/".grok/config.toml"` entry has `kind == "settings"`, `vendor == "grok"`; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_credentials_file_stays_auth — asserts `home/".claude/.credentials.json"` is present with `kind == "auth"`, pinning Context claim 7; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_hooks_kind_requires_a_listed_hooks_dir — asserts `home/".claude/hooks/pre.sh"` is `kind == "hooks"` while `home/".claude/plugins/marketplaces/x/plugins/hookify/LICENSE"` is absent entirely (not merely reclassified), proving substring matching is gone; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_no_other_entries_are_written — asserts `{e["kind"] for e in scout(home)["paths"]} <= {"settings", "hooks", "mcp", "auth"}`, i.e. `"other" not in kinds`; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_watchlist_is_version_2_with_vendor_on_every_entry — asserts `result["version"] == 2` and `all("vendor" in e for e in result["paths"])`; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_project_settings_are_discovered — asserts `home/"Work/proj/.claude/settings.local.json"` is present with `kind == "settings"` when called as `scout(home, project_roots=[home/"Work"])`; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_project_walk_skips_git_node_modules_and_venv — asserts none of `Work/proj/.git/HEAD`, `Work/proj/node_modules/pkg/.claude/settings.json`, `Work/proj/.venv/lib/.claude/settings.json` appear in the output paths; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_project_walk_descends_worktrees_one_level — asserts `Work/sentinel/.worktrees/wt/.claude/settings.json` IS present, the explicit exception in the packet; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_project_walk_stops_at_depth_3 — asserts `Work/a/b/c/d/.claude/settings.json` is absent; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_extra_cwds_are_scouted_outside_project_roots — calls `scout(home, project_roots=[home/"Work"], extra_cwds=[home/"Elsewhere/live-cwd"])` and asserts `Elsewhere/live-cwd/.claude/settings.json` is present, while a call without `extra_cwds` omits it; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_never_touches_ssh_gnupg_or_aws — asserts no output path contains `.ssh`, `.gnupg` or `.aws`, and (via the same iterdir spy as above) that those directories are never visited; fixture: `fake_agent_home`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_never_opens_a_file_body — monkeypatches `Path.read_text`, `Path.read_bytes` AND `Path.open` to raise `AssertionError`, then runs `scout(home, project_roots=[home/"Work"])` to completion; strengthens the existing `read_text`-only guard so `~/.claude.json` cannot be opened; fixture: `fake_agent_home` + monkeypatch.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_skips_missing_vendor_homes_quietly — replaces the deleted `test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly`: runs scout against a home containing only `.claude/settings.json` and asserts it returns without raising and yields exactly that one entry; fixture: bare `tmp_path`.
- tests/test_scout.py::test_scout_refresh_writes_atomically_and_leaves_no_tmp — calls `sentinel.cli.scout_main(["--refresh", "--home", str(home)])`, asserts return 0, `stat.S_IMODE(out.stat().st_mode) == 0o600`, `not (state_dir()/"watchlist.json.tmp").exists()`, and `json.loads(out.read_text())["version"] == 2`; fixture: `fake_agent_home` + the autouse XDG isolation already in tests/conftest.py.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_watchlist_rewrite_reloads_without_alert — builds a Daemon with `self_paths=[state]`, a fake `clock`, and NO `watch_paths` (critical: passing `watch_paths` sets `_watch_override` and makes `_load_watch_paths` a no-op), writes a v2 watchlist naming root A, calls `d._load_watch_paths()`, rewrites the file naming root B, calls `d._on_fs_event(state/"watchlist.json", IN_CLOSE_WRITE)` and asserts roots are still A and `list(iter_alerts()) == []`; then advances the fake clock 2.0 s, calls `d.tick()`, and asserts `d._watch_roots == (B_resolved,)` and still zero alerts.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_watchlist_reload_is_debounced_to_one_load — fires 10 `_on_fs_event` calls on `watchlist.json` inside a 1.9 s fake-clock window, then advances past 2.0 s and calls `d.tick()`, asserting a spy on `Daemon._load_watch_paths` recorded exactly 1 call.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_watchlist_reload_adds_and_removes_inotify_watches — same setup with `inotify=True` and real directories `A/` and `B/`; after the debounced reload asserts `B.resolve() in set(d._inotify.wd_to_path.values())` and `A.resolve() not in ...`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_refresh_watchlist_rate_limited_to_60s — with a fake clock, calls `d.handle_overflow()` twice at t=0 and t=30 and asserts the `sentinel.daemon.refresh_watchlist` spy recorded 1 call, then at t=61 asserts a 2nd; this is the test that supersedes the two existing `called == ["refresh", "refresh"]` assertions.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_auto_watch_only_under_hooks_roots — drives `_drain_inotify` with a `FakeIno` whose `wd_to_path` maps wd 1 to a settings root and wd 2 to a hooks root, feeding `IN_CREATE|IN_ISDIR` on each, and asserts `add_watch` was called for the hooks child only and `d._auto_wds` has exactly one member.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_v1_watchlist_without_version_still_loads — writes a watchlist with no `version` key and v1-shaped entries, calls `d._load_watch_paths()`, asserts no exception and that the hooks/settings roots are picked up (or, if the Chief chooses the stale-file route, that roots are empty and one refresh was scheduled — see risks).
- tests/test_rules.py::test_path_under_uses_preresolved_roots — creates `real = tmp_path/"real"` (a dir) and `link = tmp_path/"link"` symlinked to it, then asserts `_path_under(real/"f.json", (link,)) is False` and `_path_under(real/"f.json", resolve_roots([link])) is True`, pinning that `_path_under` no longer resolves roots and that `resolve_roots` is the caller's job.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_resolve_roots_returns_tuple_of_resolved_paths — asserts `resolve_roots([link, real])` is a `tuple` and every member equals its `.resolve()`.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_evaluate_write_resolves_the_event_path_once — wraps `Path.resolve` with a counting spy, calls `evaluate_write(p, 42, "/usr/bin/python3", self_paths=resolve_roots([a]), watch_paths=resolve_roots([b]))` and asserts the counter == 1, catching the current double-resolve at rules.py:209 and rules.py:150.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_evaluate_write_does_not_resolve_roots_per_event — 300 pre-resolved roots, one event, asserts the `Path.resolve` spy count is 1 and independent of root count (deterministic, unmarked, fast — this is the real regression guard for the 6.8 ms figure).
- tests/test_rules.py::test_evaluate_write_1000_events_under_500ms — `@pytest.mark.slow`; 300 pre-resolved roots, 1000 `evaluate_write` calls, asserts `time.perf_counter()` delta < 0.5; requires `[tool.pytest.ini_options] markers = ["slow: long-running performance guard"]` added to /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/pyproject.toml, which currently has no pytest section at all.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_load_config_reads_scout_project_roots — writes a config.toml containing `[scout]\nproject_roots = ["~/Elsewhere"]`, asserts `load_config(path)["project_roots"] == [str(Path.home()/"Elsewhere")]` (tilde expanded) and that omitting the section yields the expanded default of `["~/Work"]`; note `load_config` today only reads the `daemon` section (daemon.py:243-252), so a `scout` section is new plumbing.
- tests/test_config_template.py::test_config_template_declares_scout_project_roots — asserts `tomllib.load(packaging/config.toml)["scout"]["project_roots"] == sentinel.daemon.DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOTS`; the existing template test only checks for `notify` and `kill`, so adding the section does not break it.
- BREAKS #1 — /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_scout.py:32 `test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly` passes `seeds=[".claude", ".missing-agent", ".codex"]`, and the packet replaces that parameter with `project_roots`; delete and replace with `test_scout_skips_missing_vendor_homes_quietly` above.
- BREAKS #2 — tests/test_scout.py:8 `test_scout_classifies_settings_hooks_auth_without_reading` survives only by luck: it picks entries with `next(e for e in ... if e["kind"] == k)` over a sorted list, so it depends on the vendor table listing `.codex` → auth `auth.json` and `.claude` → hooks_dirs `hooks`; rewrite it to assert exact paths rather than first-match, and fold its `read_text` guard into the stronger `test_scout_never_opens_a_file_body`.
- BREAKS #3 — tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:456 `test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes` asserts `called == ["refresh", "refresh"]` for two back-to-back `handle_watch_lost()` calls, and tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:162 `test_overflow_emits_warning_and_refreshes` asserts the same for `handle_overflow()`; the S5 60-second rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` makes both `["refresh"]`, so both must gain a fake clock and advance past 60 s, or be replaced by the new rate-limit test.
- BREAKS #4 — tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:370 `test_move_self_triggers_watch_lost` builds the Daemon with no `watch_paths` (so `_watch_roots == []`) and feeds `IN_MOVE_SELF` for `tmp_path/"hooks"`; the S5 rule "raise the lost-watch alert only for a configured root" suppresses that call, so the test must pass `watch_paths=[tmp_path/"hooks"]`.
- STAYS GREEN (verified by reading, worth stating in the report) — tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:388 `test_transient_subdir_loss_is_silent` and :422 `test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent` still pass under hooks-only auto-watch, because the un-added wd falls through the `base is None` guard at daemon.py:716-718 and the ISDIR skip at daemon.py:725; tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:486 `test_load_watchlist_parents_and_kinds` is unaffected since `watch_roots_from_entries` filters on `WATCH_KINDS`; tests/test_investigate.py:386 and :505 only assert the literal string `watchlist.json` in generated shell text; tests/joint/ has no watchlist coupling.

**Risks**

- DECISION NEEDED (blocking): the S5 60-second rate limit on `refresh_watchlist` contradicts two green tests that assert two consecutive refreshes (`test_overflow_emits_warning_and_refreshes`, `test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes`). Does the limit apply to the overflow and watch-lost paths, or only to the new hot-reload/auto-add path? The answer changes whether those two tests are edited or left alone.
- DECISION NEEDED: "raise the lost-watch alert only for a configured root" — is the gate in `_drain_inotify` (my recommendation, keeps `handle_watch_lost` directly testable and leaves tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:444 green) or inside `handle_watch_lost` (which would also break :444)?
- DECISION NEEDED: no upgrade path is specified for an existing v1 `watchlist.json` on disk. `Daemon._load_watch_paths` (daemon.py:439-444) reads `data.get("paths")` and ignores `version`, so a stale v1 file would silently keep 138 marketplace roots watched after the upgrade. Recommend: `version != 2` is treated as stale, roots empty, one refresh scheduled — but that inverts my proposed `test_v1_watchlist_without_version_still_loads`.
- SECURITY REGRESSION SHAPE: making `_path_under` trust pre-resolved roots means any caller that forgets `resolve_roots` gets silent under-detection (writes stop matching) rather than a test failure. Mitigate with a daemon-side assertion that `Daemon._watch_roots` is a `tuple` of already-resolved paths after `_load_watch_paths`, in addition to the rules-level test.
- STALE PACKET NUMBERS: I reran the current scout against the live home today — 1242 entries (packet says 940), 61 hooks (59), 48 mcp (48), 3 auth (3), 1 settings (1), 138 watch roots (134), 131 of them under `plugins/marketplaces` (packet says 52 of 54). The qualitative claims 1, 2, 6 and 7 all hold, but the fact-check table must record the new figures and the acceptance line's "134 → under 20" should read "138 → under 20".
- UNVERIFIED VENDOR FILENAMES on this machine: `~/.codex/` contains only sqlite files and `installation_id` — no `config.toml` and no `auth.json` — and `~/.cursor/` does not exist at all, so Codex and Cursor entries cannot be confirmed empirically here and must be marked UNVERIFIED in the vendor table comments per the packet. `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.grok/auth.json`, `~/.grok/hooks/`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json` and `~/.claude.json` all exist and are confirmed.
- FLAKY-TEST RISK: the wall-clock 500 ms budget is load-sensitive and will fail intermittently on a busy machine. The deterministic `Path.resolve` call-count test is the guard that should gate the packet; the timed one should be `slow`-marked and treated as informational.
- MARKER PLUMBING: pyproject.toml has no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, so `@pytest.mark.slow` will emit `PytestUnknownMarkWarning` on every run until markers are registered — a pyproject edit the packet does not mention.
- FIXTURE MECHANICS: `Work/proj/.git/` cannot live in the committed `tests/fixtures/fake_home` (git treats a nested `.git` as a gitlink and will not track files under it), which is why the fixture must be constructed in `tmp_path`. Anyone who tries to extend the existing committed tree instead will get a silently incomplete fixture and a false-green skip test.
- API FAN-OUT: dropping `seeds=` from `scout()` also touches `src/sentinel/cli.py:60` and `src/sentinel/daemon.py:217`, both of which call `scout(home)` and must now thread `project_roots` from config; `refresh_watchlist(home)` needs a `project_roots` parameter or it will silently scout with the default `~/Work` regardless of the user's config.
- TEST COUNT: the suite is at 210 collected (confirmed with `.venv/bin/pytest -q --collect-only`). If the four broken tests are rewritten rather than deleted, the expected post-packet count is 210 + 31 new = 241; the report must quote the number actually produced by pytest, not this estimate.


### W3-03 · tests:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CORRECTING MYSELF (tests). My proposed `test_evaluate_write_resolves_the_event_path_once` asserting a resolve count of 1 is wrong and would fail forever. Measured with a `Path.resolve` spy on the real tree: `evaluate_write(p, 1, '/usr/bin/python3', self_paths=[a], watch_paths=[b])` makes 5 resolve calls (1 for `paths = [str(path.resolve())]` at rules.py:209, plus 2 per `_path_under` call x 2 calls = event path resolved 3 times total, plus 1 per root). The packet never asks to dedupe the three event-path resolves. Correct assertion: resolve count must be INDEPENDENT OF ROOT COUNT. Today it is 5 with 1 self + 1 watch root and 305 with 1 self + 301 watch roots; post-change both must be equal.
- CORRECTING ALL FIVE OF US, including the spec agent's R39 and my own `test_evaluate_write_1000_events_under_500ms`. The packet's performance acceptance (300 roots, 1000 events, under 500 ms) is UNACHIEVABLE by the change the packet actually specifies. I simulated the post-change `_path_under` (roots pre-resolved, no `Path(root).resolve()` in the loop) and measured 5623.8 ms for 1000 events x 300 roots -- 11x over budget. Pre-resolving roots removes only ~68% of the cost (17.7 ms/event -> 5.6 ms/event). The residue is the per-root `relative_to` scan: 1000 x 300 `relative_to` misses alone cost 5591.1 ms, i.e. 99% of the post-change total. Nobody costed the loop that survives the fix.
- FOLLOW-ON CORRECTION to the spec agent's R36 (`tuple[Path, ...]`) and to the packet's own wording. A tuple is the wrong data structure for the packet's own number. Replacing the O(roots) `relative_to` scan with a `frozenset` of resolved roots plus a walk over `resolved.parents` costs 46.4 ms for 1000 events (including the `path.resolve()`) -- 122x faster than the tuple version and comfortably inside 500 ms. It is exactly semantics-preserving for R38, because `resolved == root or resolved.relative_to(root) succeeds` is identical to `root in {resolved} | set(resolved.parents)`. So `_path_under` must take `frozenset[Path]`, and `Daemon._watch_roots` must become a frozenset of resolved paths, not a tuple.
- CORRECTING THE CODEBASE AGENT AND MYSELF on incremental watch removal. We both said the hot reload needs new `inotify_rm_watch` ctypes plumbing. The binding genuinely does not exist (`_libc()` at daemon.py:96-107 binds only `inotify_init1` and `inotify_add_watch`) -- but the conclusion does not follow. `_reload_watchlist()` can be `_load_watch_paths()` + `_rebuild_watches()`. After this packet `_dirs_to_watch()` returns under 20 directories, so a full fd teardown and rebuild is a handful of syscalls on a debounced path. Reading the packet's word "diff" as a required mechanism rather than a required outcome invents new C-ABI surface for no benefit. My `test_watchlist_reload_adds_and_removes_inotify_watches` still passes under a full rebuild, since it asserts on `wd_to_path.values()`.
- CORRECTING THE SPEC AGENT's R16 and confirming the codebase agent. R16 asserts the scout "now inventories ~/.claude.json, ~/.grok/config.toml, ~/.gemini/settings.json and ~/.codex/config.toml". `~/.codex/config.toml` does not exist on this machine and neither does `~/.codex/auth.json` -- `~/.codex` holds only sqlite files (goals_1, logs_2, memories_1, queue_1, state_5 plus -shm/-wal), `installation_id`, `skills/`, `.tmp/` and `tmp/`. `~/.cursor` does not exist at all. Verified as existing: `~/.claude.json` (0600, 52944 bytes), `~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.grok/auth.json`, `~/.grok/hooks/`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json`.
- CORRECTING THE ENTRY-COUNT DISPUTE between the integration agent (1068) and the spec/codebase agents and my own earlier run (1242). Nobody is wrong; we measured different artifacts, and the number is not stable. The live on-disk `/home/tav/.local/state/sentinel/watchlist.json` has 1068 entries (957 other, 59 hooks, 48 mcp, 3 auth, 1 settings, no `version` key) -- that is what integration measured. A fresh scout gave 1242 this morning and 1309 when I re-ran it minutes ago. What IS stable across every fresh run today: 138 roots, 131 marketplace, 56 inotify dirs, 52 marketplace dirs. The live file gives 134 roots / 131 marketplace / exactly 3 non-marketplace roots (`~/.claude/.credentials.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/skills/.system/openai-docs/references/mcp-diagnostics.md`). - CORRECTING THE INTEGRATION AGENT's reasoning (not its conclusion) on investigate.py:242. - REFINING MY OWN BREAKS #3 and the security/integration framing of the 60 s rate limit. `handle_overflow` calls `refresh_watchlist(self._home)` unconditionally (daemon.py:585) while `handle_watch_lost` calls it only `if self._watch_override is None` (daemon.py:607). Both existing tests use `_daemon(tmp_path, monkeypatch)` with no `watch_paths`, so `_watch_override is None` in both and both would go from `['refresh','refresh']` to `['refresh']` under the rate limit -- but for structurally different reasons, which matters when deciding whether to exempt one path.
- CONFIRMING THE SECURITY AGENT's symlink escape, reproduced verbatim. A fixture with `.claude/hooks/hooks.json -> secret/id_ed25519` produced the single watchlist entry `hooks /tmp/.../secret/id_ed25519 11` -- kind `hooks`, path outside the vendor tree, size of a private key leaked. Adding to that finding: a symlinked DIRECTORY (`.claude/linkdir -> secret/`) was NOT traversed, so today's exposure is symlinked files only; a hand-written `os.walk` for project discovery must use `followlinks=False` to keep that property. Also confirming the malformed-watchlist crash: `watch_roots_from_entries([{'kind':'settings'}])` raises `KeyError: 'path'` and `watch_roots_from_entries(['nope'])` raises `AttributeError`, neither caught by `_load_watch_paths`'s `except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError)`.
- CONFIRMING THE INTEGRATION AGENT's $HOME claim by direct execution: `Daemon({'watch_paths': [~/.claude.json, ~/.claude/settings.json], 'self_paths': []})._dirs_to_watch()` returns `[PosixPath('/home/tav'), PosixPath('/home/tav/.claude')]`. Classifying `~/.claude.json` as `settings` does put an inotify watch on $HOME, and the unconditional auto-watch at daemon.py:720-723 then cascades into every directory created there.
- CONFIRMING the `watchlist.json.tmp` false positive with a live count. `write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:44-59) names the temp file `path.name + '.tmp'`; `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` contains `alerts.jsonl.tmp` but not `watchlist.json.tmp`; the live alerts.jsonl contains exactly 1 R-SELF row on `watchlist.json.tmp`. The codebase, security and integration agents all called this and all three are right.
- PROVING MY OWN FIXTURE-MECHANICS CLAIM rather than asserting it. In a scratch repo, `mkdir -p f/proj/.git; echo x > f/proj/.git/HEAD; git init; git add -A` stages ZERO files and `git status --short` is empty -- git silently refuses to track anything under a nested `.git/`. The packet's requested `Work/proj/.git/` fixture therefore cannot live in the committed `tests/fixtures/fake_home` tree, and anyone extending that tree gets a silently incomplete fixture. `git ls-files tests/fixtures/` confirms the committed tree is exactly the three files the codebase and integration agents named.
- QUANTIFYING the marketplace blind spot that the security and integration agents raised qualitatively. `~/.claude/plugins/` contains only `known_marketplaces.json` and `marketplaces/`, and under `marketplaces/claude-plugins-official/plugins/` there are 6 real `hooks/hooks.json` files (explanatory-output-style, claude-security, hookify, learning-output-style, ralph-loop, security-guidance). Excluding `plugins/marketplaces` wholesale unwatches every installed Claude Code plugin hook on this machine -- 6 files, not a hypothetical.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline is 210 tests collected, 210 passed in 1.83 s in this worktree. Any post-packet count must be the number pytest actually prints, never an estimate.
- The fact-check table quotes ROOT counts, never entry counts, and names which artifact it measured. Fresh scout today: 138 roots, 131 marketplace, 56 inotify dirs, 52 marketplace dirs. Live on-disk file: 1068 entries, 134 roots, 131 marketplace, 3 non-marketplace roots. Entry counts drifted 1068 -> 1242 -> 1309 within one day and are not a stable fact. The Goal line's '134 to under 20' should read '138 to under 20' and should say it counts `len(watch_roots_from_entries(...))'.
- Pre-resolving roots is necessary but NOT sufficient for the packet's own 500 ms number. `_path_under` must also stop scanning roots linearly: a `frozenset` of resolved roots plus a walk over `resolved.parents` is semantics-identical to the current exact-match-or-`relative_to` rule and measures 46 ms vs 5624 ms for 1000 events x 300 roots. `Daemon._watch_roots` and `Daemon._self_paths()` both become frozensets of resolved paths; self paths must be pre-resolved too or R-SELF silently weakens.
- The deterministic guard is a root-count-independence test on the `Path.resolve` call count (5 today with 2 roots, 305 with 302 roots; equal after the change), plus an ancestor-set equivalence test. The wall-clock 500 ms test is `slow`-marked and informational only. Registering the marker requires adding `[tool.pytest.ini_options] markers = [...]` to pyproject.toml, which today has no pytest section, no pytest.ini, no setup.cfg and no tox.ini.
- `_reload_watchlist()` is `_load_watch_paths()` + `_rebuild_watches()` under a 2 s debounce on the injectable `self._clock`. No `inotify_rm_watch` binding is added in this packet. `_sample_timeout()` must be min'ed with the pending debounce deadline or the reload lands up to a full sampler interval late, and `tick()` must drive the pending reload so the test can be deterministic.
- The hot reload must NOT be unconditionally silent. 'No alert' means 'no alert for a reload Sentinel itself caused'. A reload that empties the root set or drops a previously watched `auth`/`settings` root emits a sticky-shaped R-SELF high. `_load_watch_paths` must also gain validation: `watch_roots_from_entries` currently raises `KeyError`/`AttributeError` on malformed entries and `_load_watch_paths` catches only `OSError` and `json.JSONDecodeError`, so one bad write kills the daemon on a path this packet makes event-triggered.
- The reload diff is taken over `_dirs_to_watch()`, and `_self_paths()` are unconditionally re-added and never removable by watchlist content, or the self-tamper tripwire dies on the first reload.
- `watchlist.json.tmp` joins `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` in this packet, along with the other `write_private_atomic` temp names (`health.json.tmp`, `allowlist.json.tmp`, `allowlist-session.json.tmp`, `notify-prefs.json.tmp`, `notify-burst.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp`). One live R-SELF false positive already exists and this packet makes refreshes frequent.
- `_entry_for` gains `os.lstat`, a `path.is_symlink()` skip, a post-resolve containment check against the vendor home or project root, and a `try/except OSError` so a file vanishing mid-walk cannot raise out of `scout()` into `refresh_watchlist`, which `handle_overflow` and `handle_watch_lost` call with no handler.
- The vendor table is a static literal. Codex and Cursor rows are UNVERIFIED (neither `~/.codex/config.toml` nor `~/.codex/auth.json` nor `~/.cursor` exists here). Verified rows: Claude (`~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json`), Grok (`~/.grok/config.toml`, `~/.grok/auth.json`, `~/.grok/hooks/`), Gemini (`~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json`). Any CLI `--help` probing runs under a temp XDG_CONFIG_HOME/XDG_STATE_HOME.
- The test fixture is built in `tmp_path` by a `fake_agent_home` conftest fixture, never committed, because git provably refuses to track files under a nested `.git/`. The committed `tests/fixtures/fake_home` tree is referenced only by the three tests in tests/test_scout.py and is deleted in the same commit.
- Four green tests change: `test_scout_skips_missing_seeds_quietly` (loses the `seeds=` kwarg), `test_scout_classifies_settings_hooks_auth_without_reading` (its `next(... if kind == k)` first-match picks become exact-path assertions and its `read_text` guard folds into a stronger guard poisoning `Path.open` and `Path.read_bytes` too), `test_overflow_emits_warning_and_refreshes` and `test_handle_watch_lost_refreshes` (both `['refresh','refresh']` under the 60 s limit). `test_move_self_triggers_watch_lost` changes only if the configured-root gate goes in `_drain_inotify` rather than in `handle_watch_lost`.
- `~/.claude.json` as a watched root puts inotify on $HOME -- confirmed by execution. Either the daemon watches non-hooks files without falling back to the parent directory, or `~/.claude.json` does not become a root. Scoping auto-watch to hooks roots is mandatory either way, not a non-goal.
- The S5 block sits under `## Non-goals` but is mandatory work; two of its four items already landed as `Daemon._auto_wds` and the exact requested regression test already exists and passes as `test_real_inotify_lock_dir_churn_is_silent`. Still missing: hooks-only auto-watch, configured-root-only lost-watch alert, and the 60 s rate limit. The Chief must rule on scope and on whether the rate limit applies to the overflow and watch-lost paths or only to the new reload path.
- `[scout] project_roots` is entirely new plumbing: `load_config` reads only the `[daemon]` table and four keys, `packaging/config.toml` has only `[kill]` and `[notify]`, and `cli.scout_main` calls `scout(home)` with no config load at all. The depth-3 walk under `~/Work` finds exactly one hit, `/home/tav/Work/.claude`. Agent cwds are attacker-controlled and must be containment-checked and scanned one level, not walked.
- A leftover v1 `watchlist.json` must not be trusted. `_load_watch_paths` ignores `version` entirely, so an upgraded daemon would keep the live file's 131 marketplace roots until the 03:30 timer fires. `version != 2` forces one refresh at load.
- Excluding `plugins/marketplaces` unwatches 6 real installed-plugin `hooks.json` files. That is a deliberate detection trade and goes in the report and the threat model in those words, not implied by a lower root count.
- The acceptance snippet's `XDG_STATE_HOME=/tmp/s3` is a predictable path in a shared directory and writes the Owner's full agent and project inventory there. Use `$(mktemp -d)`.
- vendors.py is claimed by both W3-03 and W3-05 with non-overlapping schemas, and W3-05 wants a content hash in the same `watchlist.json` this packet versions to 2 while W3-03 forbids reading file bodies. The two must not run concurrently despite both declaring parallel-safety; `_entry_for` leaves the hash field open rather than locking the shape.
- Run constraints hold: no systemctl, omarchy or sudo; no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local; no edits to tests/joint/ (grep confirms it has no watchlist coupling, so no edit is needed anyway); no new dependencies; no push, merge or rebase.

**Final position.** The test plan stands at roughly 31 tests across one new file and four existing ones, but two of my own designs were wrong and one shared assumption is wrong in a way that changes the implementation, not just the tests.
