My own errors, corrected: (1) `test_evaluate_write_resolves_the_event_path_once` cannot assert a count of 1 -- `evaluate_write` resolves the event path three times (once for `paths`, once inside each of two `_path_under` calls) and the packet does not ask to fix that; the assertion becomes root-count independence, measured today at 5 calls with 2 roots versus 305 with 302 roots. (2) I said incremental watch removal needs new `inotify_rm_watch` ctypes plumbing; it does not -- `_load_watch_paths()` + `_rebuild_watches()` is sufficient once the dir count is under 20, and my reload test passes unchanged under a rebuild.

The finding that matters most, which none of the five of us caught: the packet's own performance acceptance is unachievable by the packet's own change. I simulated post-change `_path_under` with pre-resolved roots and measured 5623.8 ms for 1000 events x 300 roots against a 500 ms budget. Pre-resolving roots buys only 68% (17.7 -> 5.6 ms/event); the remaining 99% of the residue is 300 `relative_to` ValueError raises per event, costing 5591 ms per 1000x300 on their own. The fix that actually hits the number is an ancestor-set membership test -- a frozenset of resolved roots plus a walk over `resolved.parents` -- which measures 46.4 ms for 1000 events, 122x faster, and is exactly semantics-preserving for the current exact-match-or-`relative_to` rule. So the spec agent's R36 `tuple[Path, ...]` is the wrong data structure: `_path_under` must take a frozenset, and `_self_paths()` must be pre-resolved alongside `_watch_roots` or R-SELF silently weakens whenever an XDG dir traverses a symlink.

Everything else I verified in the tree held up. The security agent's symlink escape reproduces verbatim (a `hooks.json -> id_ed25519` symlink leaks a private key's path and size as kind `hooks`, though symlinked directories are not traversed) and so does the malformed-watchlist crash. The integration agent's $HOME claim reproduces by direct execution, and its 125 marketplace alerts and 134/131/3 live root breakdown are exact -- only its reasoning about why the investigate grep breaks is off, since `other` entries were never watched in the first place. The codebase agent's vendor ground truth is right and the spec agent's R16 overreaches by listing `~/.codex/config.toml`, which does not exist here. The entry-count dispute between us dissolves once we say which artifact we measured; the number drifted 1068 -> 1242 -> 1309 in a day and must not appear in the fact-check table at all.

Three blocking decisions remain for the Chief, unchanged: whether the S5 block under `## Non-goals` is in scope, whether the 60 s rate limit applies to the overflow and watch-lost paths (it breaks two green tests either way) or only to the new reload path, and whether `~/.claude.json` becomes a watched root given that it provably drags $HOME into the watch set. I would add a fourth now: whether the 500 ms budget is retained -- if it is, `_path_under` must be restructured beyond what the packet describes; if the packet's literal change is all that lands, the budget must be raised to roughly 6 s or the test dropped.

### W3-04 · codebase

W3-04 touches four production modules — `sentinel.daemon` (`sample_proc`, `Daemon.__init__`, `load_config`), `sentinel.rules` (`ProcSnapshot`, `_as_proc`, `evaluate_child_shell`, `_is_agent`/`_proc_basename`), `sentinel.procinfo` (`_stat_fields`, `read_ppid`, `read_starttime`), and `packaging/config.toml` — plus the `_fake_proc` helper in `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py`. The seen-registry, coalescer, kill-target and investigate-renderer plumbing the packet assumes all already exists and is correct (`Daemon._seen`, `_seen_key`, `_prune_seen`, `PROCESS_RULES` already contains `R-CHILD-SHELL`, `action.resolve_child_pids`, `investigate._render_child_shell`). Three packet claims do not hold against the current tree: the line numbers in Context 2/3/4 are all wrong, Context 5's perf baseline is off by ~3x on this machine, and the acceptance threshold derived from it is very likely unreachable.

**Findings**

- Change site 1 is `Daemon.sample_proc` in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py: it iterates `self._proc_root`, skips non-digit names and `os.getpid()`, early-exits on empty `_read_cmdline` (kernel threads), then reads `_readlink(exe)` and `_read_comm` for every process, applies the `process_name_candidates(...).isdisjoint(self._known)` filter, and only for survivors reads `cwd` and `read_starttime` before calling `self.handle_process(...)`; it ends with `self._prune_seen(live)`.
- `sentinel.rules.ProcSnapshot` has fields pid, ppid, cmdline, exe, cwd, comm and a hand-written `__init__` — it has NO `starttime` field, so the packet's "one ProcSnapshot per pid (… starttime)" requires adding the field to the frozen dataclass, its custom `__init__`, and the Mapping branch `rules._as_proc`.
- `sentinel.procinfo` already has the exact primitive the packet asks for: `_stat_fields` parses past the last `)` so a comm containing spaces or parens is safe, and `read_ppid` (fields[1]) / `read_starttime` (fields[19]) sit on top of it; `read_ppid` is currently dead in production (only tests/test_procinfo.py uses it) and calling both helpers would read `stat` twice, so a combined reader over `_stat_fields` is the single-read path the packet wants.
- I verified the /proc field positions empirically on this machine: `awk '{print $4, $22}' /proc/$$/stat` gave ppid 115813 (matching `ps -o ppid`) and starttime 2498501 — Context/Fact-check item on field 4 = ppid and field 22 = starttime is CONFIRMED.
- The seen registry from W3-02 needs nothing new structurally: `daemon.PROCESS_RULES` is already `frozenset({"R-BYPASS", "R-CHILD-SHELL"})`, and both `Daemon._seen_key` and `daemon.coalesce_key` gate on `alert.evidence["instance"]` — so `evaluate_child_shell` (or the daemon wrapper) MUST set `evidence["instance"] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime)`, otherwise `_seen_key` returns None and every tick re-alerts through the 60 s coalescer only.
- `Daemon._prune_seen` parses the pid back out with `int(k.split("|", 2)[1].split(":", 1)[0])`; keying child-shell alerts on the child instance is compatible with it (including the `"11:?"` unknown-starttime form), and `sample_proc`'s `live` set already contains every pid in /proc, so child pids will be pruned correctly.
- `evaluate_child_shell` currently hardcodes `severity="high"` for all three kinds and has no parameter for per-kind severity or a net-helper allowlist — its only kwarg is `agent_basenames`. The packet's Changes section only shows `evaluate_child_shell(snapshots, agent_basenames=self._known)`, so it does not say whether severity mapping and `net_helper_ignore` filtering live in `rules` (new kwargs) or in the daemon after the call; that is an unresolved design choice Grok has to make.
- Config plumbing does not exist for either new key: `daemon.load_config` builds a flat dict, does `section = data.get("daemon", data)` and copies only `sampler_interval`, `coalesce_window`, `extra_bypass_flags`, `known_basenames` — there is no `[rules]` table handling at all, so `[daemon] child_shell` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore` each need a default in the `cfg` literal, a read in `load_config`, and a read in `Daemon.__init__` (which pulls cfg keys directly, e.g. `self._known`, `self._extra_flags`).
- `packaging/config.toml` has only `[kill]` and `[notify]` sections — no `[daemon]` and no `[rules]` — and `tests/test_config_template.py::test_config_template_parses_and_matches_code_defaults` only asserts `"notify" in data and "kill" in data`, so adding the new sections to the template is safe but is extra work the packet does not list.
- The test helper `_fake_proc` in tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py writes `/proc/<pid>/stat` ONLY when `starttime is not None`, and hardcodes ppid to 1 in the format string `f"{pid} ({exe_name}) S 1 1 1 0 -1 …"`; every packet test (pid 10 parent / pid 11 child with ppid 10) needs a new `ppid=` parameter and an always-written `stat`, otherwise the tree pass sees ppid 0/None and `_agent_ancestor` finds nothing.
- Existing tests in tests/test_rules.py survive the severity change: `test_rchild_shell_detects_curl_pipe_bash` asserts `severity == "high"`, but `test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper` asserts only rule, basename and kind — so demoting net-helper to medium does not break them.
- The kill path already prefers the child: `action.resolve_child_pids` reads `evidence["child_pids"]` first and `kill.plan_kill(mode="child")` intersects those with `alert_pids`, so `pids=[parent, child]` plus `child_pids=[child]` gives exactly the packet's "child as default kill target".
- `investigate._render_child_shell` already exists and is wired in `_RENDERERS`; it reads `evidence["kind"]`, `evidence["child_pids"]`, `evidence["parent_pid"]` and `alert.parent` (pid/basename), tolerates malformed pids, and emits `awk '{print $4}' /proc/<child>/stat` as a verify command — so a live-fired alert renders today with no change.
- Claim 6 CONFIRMED live: my own Bash tool call runs as `/usr/bin/bash -c source /home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-<epoch>-<rand>.sh 2>/dev/null || true && … && eval '<cmd>'`, and `~/.claude/shell-snapshots/` exists with `snapshot-bash-*.sh` files; `rules._is_interactive_shell` already returns False for it because `_shell_c_argument` finds the `-c` string, so the packet's `test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash` should pass without rule changes.
- Claim 1 CONFIRMED: `grep -rn evaluate_child_shell src/ tests/` shows the only definition in src/sentinel/rules.py and the only callers in tests/test_rules.py — nothing in `src/` imports it.
- Claim 2 CONFIRMED in substance (the disjoint-names early `continue` and the absence of any ppid read) but its citation `src/sentinel/daemon.py:575-601` is wrong: `sample_proc` is at lines 644-674 of the current tree.
- Claim 3's citation `rules.py:346-403` is off by one at the start (`def evaluate_child_shell` is at rules.py:347) and Claim 4's citations are both wrong: `severity="high"` in the child-shell alert is at rules.py:385, and the net-helper `curl` handling is at rules.py:57-58 (`NET_HELPER_BASENAMES`) and rules.py:327-328 (`_child_kind`), not 326-327. The LEDGER's own lesson says never to cite line numbers in a packet Grok will diff against the tree; this packet does it five times.
- Claim 5 REFUTED as written: on this machine right now `/proc` holds 305 pids (not 263) and the packet's own acceptance snippet gives a `sample_proc` median of 26.5 ms (not 9.5 ms). I ran it in an isolated `XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` tempdir with a no-op notify, exactly as the packet specifies.
- The full suite is green at 210 passed in 1.90 s under `.venv/bin/pytest -q`; the venv is Python 3.14.7.

**Risks**

- The acceptance threshold "under 30 ms on the Chief's machine" is almost certainly unreachable. Measured on this worktree's machine: the current-shape scan (cmdline+exe+comm for all pids) is 19.6 ms, adding cwd readlink + stat read for all pids takes it to 28.0 ms, `evaluate_child_shell` over the 133 real snapshots costs another 2.5 ms, and today's whole `sample_proc` already measures 26.5 ms. The new tree pass lands around 35-40 ms. Either the packet's budget needs rewriting against a fresh baseline, or `sample_proc` needs the cwd readlink deferred to only the pids that actually produce a candidate (child kinds and known agents), which the packet's "single pass" wording forbids.
- Agent identity is defined twice and the two definitions disagree. The daemon uses `process_name_candidates` (any cmdline token basename, plus comm, plus `_exe_basename` which strips a trailing ' (deleted)'), while `rules._is_agent` uses `_proc_basename` = exe basename, else comm, else argv[0]. So a node-wrapped agent (`node /usr/lib/node_modules/.bin/claude --yolo`) and a deleted-exe agent (`/usr/bin/claude (deleted)`) are agents to the sampler but NOT to `evaluate_child_shell` — both cases are already covered by `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper`. Children of those agents will silently never alert. The packet does not mention this.
- The kill path will misbehave unless the alert carries a per-pid starttime table. `action.expected_starttimes` reads `evidence["starttimes"]` (a pid->starttime dict) first and otherwise applies the scalar `evidence["starttime"]` to EVERY pid in `alert.pids`. A child-shell alert names `[parent_pid, child_pid]`, so a scalar starttime would assert the parent's start time for the child and `kill.plan_kill` would drop the real target as recycled. The packet says nothing about `starttimes`, only about the parent block and `child_pids`.
- Bare-shell false positives are a live risk the packet does not size. `rules._is_interactive_shell` returns True for a shell with no arguments at all, and `_agent_ancestor` walks the entire ancestor chain rather than just the direct parent, so any `bash`/`sh`/`zsh` at any depth under an agent produces a high-severity alert. Nothing fired on this machine's current tree (0 alerts over 133 live snapshots), but a login shell or a tool wrapper spawned deep under an agent would.
- The `slow` marker has no home: there is no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in pyproject.toml and no pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini, and no test currently uses `pytest.mark`. A `@pytest.mark.slow` performance test will emit `PytestUnknownMarkWarning` and, more importantly, will still run in the plain `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command unless marker registration and a deselect are added.
- "Document that net-helper is a visibility alert, not a kill prompt, in the summary text" is ambiguous between `Alert.summary` (built in `evaluate_child_shell`) and the investigate rendering (`investigate.RULE_TEXT` / the `limits` list in `_render_child_shell`, which today says only "interactive-shell detection is argv-based"). Picking the wrong one leaves the guidance invisible wherever the reviewer looks.
- `keys.alert_flag_set` falls back to `extract_bypass_flags(alert.cmdline)` when evidence carries no flags, so a child whose cmdline happens to contain a bypass token (e.g. `bash -c "claude --yolo"`) would fold that flag into both the `_seen_key` and the approval fingerprint for R-CHILD-SHELL. Harmless today, but it makes approvals for child-shell alerts key on something the packet never reasons about.
- The packet's "substring match on cmdline tokens" for `net_helper_ignore` is under-specified: matching `"127.0.0.1"` as a substring of any token also matches `127.0.0.10`, and matching `"localhost"` matches `evil-localhost.example`. Neither the semantics nor a test for the near-miss case is named.


### W3-04 · codebase:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- PERF ATTRIBUTION — correcting the tests agent AND my own earlier codebase report. Both of us named the cwd readlink as the dominant new syscall cost (tests agent: lazy cwd "removes the single largest new syscall cost"; my report: cwd is "the main threat to the budget"). Measured on this machine just now, isolating each read over live /proc: cmdline+exe+comm for all pids = 16.7 ms median; adding a stat read for all pids = 25.4 ms (+8.7); adding the cwd readlink on top = 26.7 ms (+1.3). The stat read is ~7x more expensive than cwd. Deferring cwd buys almost nothing; deferring stat is the whole win.
- PERF CONCLUSION — correcting the spec agent, the integration agent, the security agent, and my own report, all four of which called the "under 30 ms" acceptance bar marginal or unreachable. It is reachable. I implemented and timed a two-phase pass: phase 1 reads cmdline+exe+comm for every pid and classifies with rules._proc_basename/_child_kind; phase 2 reads /proc/<pid>/stat only for pids that are a known agent or have a non-None _child_kind, plus each ancestor walked on demand. Median 20.8 ms with 9 stat reads instead of 133 — statistically identical to today's sample_proc at 20.9 ms, and ~11 ms under the full-tree eager version at 32.5 ms. The tree pass can be added for free. It is still one iteration over /proc, so it satisfies the packet's "single pass" wording; only the eager-stat reading of it is expensive.
- PROCESS COUNT — correcting the tests agent's perf fixture design. /proc holds 304-308 pid dirs on this machine but only 133-135 have a non-empty cmdline; the rest are kernel threads that exit at the `if not cmdline: continue` guard. The proposed fixture (400 dirs, ~10 with empty cmdline) therefore builds ~390 full snapshots, roughly 3x the real live load, against a budget derived from live measurements. The kernel-thread ratio should be ~55% empty, or the budget should be re-derived; and tmpfs vs procfs read costs are not comparable, so the 40 ms number must be measured on the actual fixture before it is asserted, not carried over from the live figure.
- NET-HELPER SEVERITY — correcting the spec agent's R11 and the packet itself, and a gap none of the five of us caught. The blanket downgrade of kind `net-helper` to medium also downgrades reverse and bind shells. I confirmed _child_kind returns `net-helper` for `nc -l -p 4444`, for `socat TCP-LISTEN:9,fork EXEC:/bin/bash`, and for an ordinary `curl https://api...` alike. The packet's stated motivation is that agents call HTTPS APIs constantly — that argues for demoting curl and wget, not nc/ncat/netcat/socat, which have no routine agent use and are the classic exfil/reverse-shell primitives. tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper is built on exactly the `nc -l -p 4444` listener shape, so the packet as written silently demotes the one case that test exists to catch. Recommend severity within net-helper by basename: curl/wget medium, nc/ncat/netcat/socat high — and scope `net_helper_ignore` to curl/wget only, since `net_helper_ignore = ["127.0.0.1"]` would otherwise suppress `nc -l 127.0.0.1 -p 4444`.
- UID FILTER — correcting the security agent. The proposed `os.stat(proc_root/'<pid>').st_uid != os.getuid()` drop per pid is largely redundant: kill.plan_kill already refuses non-owned pids (src/sentinel/kill.py:158-165 raises PermissionError listing them), so a foreign-uid target cannot be killed today. Cross-uid children of a user agent essentially require a setuid exec, and in that case the visible child is `sudo` (basename `sudo`, not in SHELL_BASENAMES or NET_HELPER_BASENAMES), so _child_kind returns None and no alert is produced anyway. If the check goes in it should live in the candidate phase (9 stats), not on all 133 pids, where it would undo the perf headroom the two-phase pass buys.
- SEEN-KEY vs FINGERPRINT — correcting the security agent's claim that setting evidence["flags"] = ["kind:<kind>"] would make `_seen_key` separate the three kinds "too". It would not, and does not need to: _seen_key (src/sentinel/daemon.py:496-500) is `f"{rule}|{instance}|{flags}"` and `instance` is already unique per (pid, starttime), so two kinds can never collide there. The flags fix matters only for the approval fingerprint. The underlying approval finding itself is CONFIRMED and is the strongest security point in the five reports: an `interactive-shell` child and a `curl|bash` child both have basename `bash`, alert_flag_set returns an empty frozenset for both (I verified extract_bypass_flags finds nothing in either argv), and approval_prefix returns GLOBAL_PREFIX "" at forever scope — so fingerprint("R-CHILD-SHELL", "bash", frozenset(), "") is byte-identical and one Approve-forever on a benign `bash -i` blinds the high-severity curl|bash detector everywhere. Side effect the security agent did not mention: summarize._flag_list (src/sentinel/summarize.py:33-40) reads evidence["flags"], so `kind:curl|bash` would ride into the cloud bundle. That is benign metadata, not a URL, but it should be a deliberate choice rather than a surprise.
- MY OWN REPORT, downgrading a risk I raised. I flagged that keys.alert_flag_set falls back to extract_bypass_flags(alert.cmdline) so a child whose cmdline contains a bypass token would fold it into the key. I tested the shape I cited: extract_bypass_flags(["bash", "-c", "claude --yolo"]) returns []. extract_bypass_flags matches whole argv tokens (src/sentinel/rules.py:109-121), and the -c payload is a single element, so the realistic case does not trigger. The risk only materialises if a bypass flag appears as its own argv token in the child, which is rare. Treat it as a footnote, not a design constraint.
- CURL|BASH ON MERE MENTION — confirming and sharpening the integration agent, whose finding I independently reproduced and which I consider the highest-impact unaddressed item in the packet. _child_kind returns "curl|bash" (severity high, Kill prompt) for a real Claude-Code-shaped argv: ["/usr/bin/bash", "-c", "source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/s.sh 2>/dev/null || true && eval 'grep -rn \"curl https://x | bash\" docs/'"]. _PIPE_TO_SHELL_RE (rules.py:62) searches the -c string as a blob, so grepping, echoing, or catting an install one-liner fires it. docs/collab/packets/W3-04-wire-child-shell.md itself contains such a string, so grepping this repo trips the rule. The packet's own regression test (test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash) does not cover this because it only exercises a benign `ls` payload.
- AGENT-IDENTITY DIVERGENCE — confirming the integration and tests agents with executed evidence rather than reading. evaluate_child_shell([node_wrapped_claude, bash -i child], agent_basenames={"claude"}) returns 0 alerts, and the same for a `/usr/bin/claude (deleted)` exe parent (_is_agent returns False because _proc_basename yields "claude (deleted)" and rules has no _DELETED_SUFFIX strip, unlike daemon._exe_basename). Both shapes are already asserted as agents by tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper (pids 11 and 13), so this is a coverage hole the project has already committed to closing, not a hypothesis.
- SPEC AGENT's R2 is right that read_ppid + read_starttime double-reads stat, but the framing that this "requires a combined helper" in procinfo.py understates the better move: with the two-phase pass, ppid is needed lazily per-pid during ancestor walks, so a `read_stat_fields`-style accessor returning the parsed field list (procinfo._stat_fields is already exactly that, just private) with a small per-tick memo is the shape that both satisfies read-once and keeps the budget. Promoting _stat_fields to public is a smaller change than a new combined reader.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline is 210 passed (I ran .venv/bin/pytest -q: 210 passed in 1.68s at ca0465a, Python 3.14.7). Any RETURN must be measured against 210.
- evaluate_child_shell is dead code today: nothing in src/ imports it, only tests/test_rules.py calls it. Wiring it is the packet's entire substance.
- rules.ProcSnapshot (src/sentinel/rules.py:68-93) has no `starttime` field, and rules._as_proc silently drops it — I confirmed by probe that _as_proc({...,"starttime":55}) produces an object with no starttime attribute. The packet's parent-block-carries-starttime and child-instance-key requirements cannot be met without either adding the field to the frozen dataclass, its hand-written __init__, and _as_proc, or patching the alert daemon-side. Pick one before Grok starts.
- load_config reads neither new key. I confirmed with a temp config containing [daemon] child_shell = false and [rules] net_helper_ignore = ["localhost"]: load_config returned only coalesce_window, extra_bypass_flags, inotify, known_basenames, sampler_interval. `child_shell = false` is dead code unless load_config gains the key AND a second-section read for [rules]. Also confirmed the `data.get("daemon", data)` fallback silently accepts a bare top-level key (sampler_interval = 9 at top level was honoured) — do not repeat that shape for [rules].
- /proc/<pid>/stat field 4 = ppid, field 22 = starttime, and procinfo._stat_fields already parses past the last ')' so a comm containing spaces or parens is safe. Do not hand-roll awk-style whitespace splitting.
- The alert must carry evidence["starttimes"] as a per-pid dict {parent_pid: st, child_pid: st}, not only a scalar evidence["starttime"]. action.expected_starttimes (src/sentinel/action.py:264-279) fans a scalar out to every pid in alert.pids via setdefault, and a child-shell alert names [parent, child], so a scalar would assert the wrong starttime for one of them and plan_kill would silently drop it as recycled. Three of us reached this independently; it is settled.
- evidence["instance"] must be instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime). PROCESS_RULES already contains R-CHILD-SHELL, and both _seen_key and coalesce_key return None/fall through without an instance. _prune_seen's int(k.split("|",2)[1].split(":",1)[0]) parses that key form correctly, including the "11:?" unknown-starttime variant.
- The new pass must tolerate a missing or unreadable /proc/<pid>/stat and keep the snapshot with ppid=0, starttime=None. tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:203-204 and :243-273 build seven fake /proc entries with no stat file at all (the _fake_proc helper writes stat only when starttime is passed); `if fields is None: continue` turns both existing tests red.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::_fake_proc hardcodes ppid=1 into its stat line, so no existing fixture can express a parent/child link. It needs a `ppid: int = 1` keyword; the default keeps all seven current callers byte-identical.
- Claude Code's Bash tool shape is a `-c` shell and _is_interactive_shell already rejects it. test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash is a regression guard, not new behaviour, and must not be presented as evidence the snapshot case was handled.
- The forbidden reads hold: no /proc/<pid>/environ, no /proc/<pid>/fd/*, no DNS or reachability probe for net_helper_ignore (literal substring on argv tokens only), no auto-kill from the sampler.
- net_helper_ignore must gate the `net-helper` kind only, never curl|bash — otherwise `curl http://localhost/x | bash` is a one-line bypass of the highest-severity detection.
- "Not a kill prompt" is unenforceable as summary text. Panel.qml:446 sets hasPids from modelData.pids and Panel.qml:565 shows the Kill button on hasPids with no severity or rule check, so every medium net-helper card offers Kill. Ship the summary wording if the Chief wants it, but record it as documentation-only or file a separate packet.
- The `slow` marker has no home: no [tool.pytest.ini_options] in pyproject.toml, no pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini, no pytest.mark anywhere in tests/. A @pytest.mark.slow test still runs under the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command and emits PytestUnknownMarkWarning.
- packaging/config.toml has only [kill] and [notify]; tests/test_config_template.py asserts only that notify and kill are present plus load_policy(path) == NotifyPolicy(), so adding [daemon] and [rules] sections is safe but is unlisted work.
- W3-04 and W3-03 both rewrite sample_proc, rules.py, tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py and tests/test_rules.py. "Parallel-safe" is wrong; serialize them.
- Nothing merges into the Owner's running daemon automatically — it loads ~/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel — and no agent in this run may write there, run systemctl/omarchy/sudo, touch tests/joint/, add dependencies, or push/merge/rebase.

**Final position.** Implementable as specified, but three things must be decided by the Chief before Grok starts, and one of my own earlier conclusions was wrong.

WHAT I RETRACT: I reported the perf budget as "almost certainly unreachable" and named the cwd readlink as the main cost. Both are wrong. Isolating the reads on live /proc: cmdline+exe+comm for all pids is 16.7 ms, +8.7 ms for an eager stat on all pids, +1.3 ms for cwd. The stat read is the cost, not cwd. A two-phase pass — one iteration reading cmdline/exe/comm and classifying, then stat read only for known agents, _child_kind candidates, and ancestors walked on demand — measures 20.8 ms median with 9 stat reads instead of 133, against today's sample_proc at 20.9 ms. The tree pass is free. The "under 30 ms" acceptance bar holds comfortably. The spec, integration and security agents should drop the perf objection too; it is the wrong thing to spend the Chief's decision budget on.

THREE CHIEF DECISIONS, in priority order:

1. curl|bash fires on mere mention. I reproduced it: a real Claude-Code-shaped `bash -c "source ...snapshot.sh ... && eval 'grep -rn \"curl https://x | bash\" docs/'"` returns kind curl|bash, severity high, with a Kill prompt. Grepping this very repo trips it, because the W3-04 packet file contains such a string. Shipping unchanged makes the rule's highest-severity path a false-positive generator on the Owner's own machine. Either narrow _PIPE_TO_SHELL_RE to reject payloads whose pipe sits inside a quoted argument to grep/echo/cat/rg, or gate curl|bash on the child having actually exec'd, or accept it and say so.

2. The net-helper downgrade is too blunt, and nobody caught why. _child_kind returns net-helper for `curl https://api...`, `nc -l -p 4444`, and `socat TCP-LISTEN:9,fork EXEC:/bin/bash` alike. The packet's justification is routine HTTPS traffic — true of curl and wget, false of nc/ncat/netcat/socat, which are reverse- and bind-shell primitives with no routine agent use. tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper is built on the `nc -l -p 4444` listener, so the packet silently demotes the exact case that test exists to catch. Recommend: curl/wget medium, nc/ncat/netcat/socat high, and scope net_helper_ignore to curl/wget only so `net_helper_ignore = ["127.0.0.1"]` cannot silence a loopback bind shell. This also resolves the spec agent's design-spec contradiction (design doc line 201 lists R-CHILD-SHELL as high with a Kill prompt) without a spec edit, because the shapes §11.2 names as kill targets stay high.

3. Approval fingerprint collapse. fingerprint("R-CHILD-SHELL", "bash", frozenset(), "") is identical for an interactive-shell child and a curl|bash child — same basename, both empty flag sets, empty prefix at forever scope. One Approve-forever on a benign `bash -i` permanently blinds curl|bash detection everywhere, with no visible signal to the Owner. Fix by setting evidence["flags"] = ["kind:<kind>"], noting that summarize._flag_list will then carry that string into the cloud bundle (benign, but deliberate). This does not affect _seen_key, which already separates by instance.

SETTLED IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE I would hand Grok: promote procinfo._stat_fields to a public accessor with a per-tick memo rather than adding a new combined reader; add `starttime: int | None = None` as the trailing field of ProcSnapshot, its hand-written __init__, and _as_proc; keep severity mapping and net_helper_ignore as explicit keyword arguments on evaluate_child_shell so the rule stays unit-testable and the daemon only forwards config; write evidence["starttimes"] as a per-pid dict alongside the scalar; add ppid= to _fake_proc; and treat the two stat-less existing sampler tests as the guard that a failed stat read must degrade to ppid=0/starttime=None, never `continue`.

TWO ITEMS I WOULD NOT BLOCK ON. The security agent's per-pid uid filter is largely redundant with kill.plan_kill's existing non-owned-pid refusal and would spend the perf headroom the two-phase pass buys; if it ships, put it in the candidate phase. The _is_agent widening for node-wrapped and deleted-exe parents is a real coverage hole the project has already committed to elsewhere (its own sampler test asserts both shapes are agents), but it changes the parent-skip at rules.py:378 and collides with W3-03 and W3-05 — file it as a follow-up packet with a documented limit in the report, rather than widening rules.py under W3-04.

### W3-04 · integration

W3-04 is well-supported by what W3-02 already landed — `PROCESS_RULES` already lists R-CHILD-SHELL and the seen registry, coalescer and kill-side starttime guard are all in place — so the wiring itself is small, but the packet under-specifies four things that decide whether the rule is safe live: the config plumbing, the agent-identity derivation, the per-pid starttime evidence, and the approval fingerprint. I verified the baseline (210 tests green), measured the real sampler cost, and probed the rule against this machine's actual process tree; the tree pass costs about +7 ms per tick, but the `curl|bash` matcher fires on any Bash tool call that merely mentions a curl-pipe, and a node-wrapped agent produces no alerts at all. Nothing merges into the Owner's running daemon automatically — it imports the installed plugin clone at ~/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel — but that clone tracks this exact branch, so the risk lands the moment the Owner runs a plugin update.

**Findings**

- Baseline confirmed in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` is 210 passed in 1.86s at ca0465a, and the W3-02 machinery W3-04 depends on is already merged (`PROCESS_RULES = frozenset({"R-BYPASS", "R-CHILD-SHELL"})` at src/sentinel/daemon.py:61, `Daemon._seen_key` at :496, `_prune_seen` at :502, `coalesce_key` at :262).
- The packet's config kill switch is inert as specified: `load_config` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-260) reads only `data.get("daemon", data)` against a hardcoded key list, and I proved with a temp config that `[daemon] child_shell` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore` are both silently dropped (`load_config` returned only coalesce_window, extra_bypass_flags, inotify, known_basenames, sampler_interval), so `load_config` must gain both the new `[daemon]` key and a second-section read or `child_shell = false` will not disable anything.
- Agent identity is derived two different ways and they disagree: the daemon matches agents with the broad `process_name_candidates` (exe basename + every cmdline token basename + comm, src/sentinel/daemon.py:331-348) while `rules._proc_basename` (src/sentinel/rules.py:252-258) takes the first non-empty of exe, comm, cmdline[0], so passing `agent_basenames=self._known` into `evaluate_child_shell` silently narrows detection.
- That asymmetry is real on this machine and not hypothetical: Grok Build runs as pid 2291 with `exe=.../node` and `comm="node-MainThread"` (comm is truncated at 15 chars) wrapping `@xai-official/grok`, and my probe of `evaluate_child_shell` on a snapshot triple modeled on it returned zero alerts for a `curl` child that the daemon's own matcher would have attributed to an agent.
- Context claim 6 is CONFIRMED with live evidence: Claude Code's Bash tool call is currently pid 822780, `/usr/bin/bash -c source /home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-1788656294993-opd0o4.sh 2>/dev/null || true && ...`, and `rules._child_kind` returns None for it, so `_shell_c_argument` correctly keeps it out of `interactive-shell`.
- The `curl|bash` matcher fires on mere mention, not execution: I ran `_child_kind` over a realistic snapshot-prefixed blob containing `grep -rn 'curl https://x | bash' docs/` and it returned `curl|bash` (high), which means any Bash tool call that greps or echoes an install one-liner — including greps over this repo, since docs/collab/packets/W3-04-wire-child-shell.md itself contains the string — raises a high alert with a Kill prompt.
- Sampler cost claim 5 is stale and the acceptance threshold is marginal: on 307 live processes the current filtered pass measures 21.6 ms median (not the packet's 9.5 ms at 263 processes) and a full single-pass tree read of pid/ppid/cmdline/exe/comm/cwd measures 28.7 ms median with a 35.0 ms max, i.e. about +7 ms for the tree, against an acceptance bar of "under 30 ms".
- `src/sentinel/procinfo.py` exposes only `read_ppid` and `read_starttime`, each of which re-reads `/proc/<pid>/stat` through `_stat_fields`, so a naive implementation doubles stat I/O for every process and the packet's "read stat once" requires a new combined accessor in procinfo.py — a file the packet's Changes section never names.
- `rules.ProcSnapshot` (src/sentinel/rules.py:66-89) has no `starttime` field and `_as_proc` does not carry one, so the packet's requirement that the `parent` block include the agent's starttime forces either a frozen-dataclass field addition in rules.py or a daemon-side augmentation after `evaluate_child_shell` returns.
- Kill safety depends on an evidence shape the packet does not specify: `action.expected_starttimes` (src/sentinel/action.py:264-280) fans a single `evidence["starttime"]` out to every pid in `alert.pids`, so with child-shell `pids=[parent, child]` a single value would tag the agent pid with the child's starttime and make `sentinel-action <id> kill --session` silently drop the agent as "recycled"; W3-04 must write the per-pid `evidence["starttimes"]` dict instead.
- Approval keying is globally scoped for this rule: `keys.approval_prefix` returns `GLOBAL_PREFIX` ("") for any non-write rule at scope session/24h/forever, and `keys.alert_flag_set` yields an empty set for a child-shell alert, so `fingerprint("R-CHILD-SHELL", "curl", frozenset(), "")` means one Approve on one noisy net-helper alert allows every curl child of every agent in every directory — the same class of collapse the LEDGER records being fixed on 2026-09-05 02:12 for write rules.
- "net-helper is a visibility alert, not a kill prompt" cannot be enforced by summary text alone: Panel.qml renders the Kill button on `card.hasPids` (Panel.qml:541-573) and binds `x` to kill (Panel.qml:279), and child-shell alerts always carry `pids=[parent, child]`, so the panel offers Kill on every medium net-helper card.
- Child argv is stored and displayed verbatim: `evaluate_child_shell` sets `cmdline=list(proc.cmdline)`, `investigate._render_child_shell` (src/sentinel/investigate.py:302-333) prints it via `_fact("cmdline", ...)`, and `redact_mapping` (src/sentinel/investigate.py:76-91) only drops sensitive *keys*, so a `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer ..."` child would land in ~/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl in full; the cloud path is safe because `summarize.build_redacted_bundle` uses an ALLOWED_KEYS allowlist that excludes cmdline.
- The live machine will not change on merge but is one command away: the running daemon is pid 18419 out of /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv, whose .pth points at /home/tav/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel/src, and that directory is a git clone whose origin is this worktree, on branch feature/omarchy-sentinel at 7df435c — so `omarchy plugin update` plus a service restart (both Owner-only actions) is what makes W3-04 live.
- Alert-volume blast radius is bounded by `store.MAX_ROWS = 5000` / `MAX_BYTES = 5_000_000` with a single `.1` generation (src/sentinel/store.py:18-65) against a live alerts.jsonl of 133 rows / 72 KB, so a net-helper storm both floods and eventually rotates away the Owner's audit trail.
- Toasting is on for medium by default (`toast_medium = true` in packaging/config.toml) with burst threshold 5 per 300 s, so net-helper mediums reach the Owner's screen individually until burst mode collapses them.
- Existing sampler tests constrain the implementation: `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::_fake_proc` (line 178) writes `stat` only when `starttime` is passed, so ppid reads must tolerate a missing stat file, and `test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper` (line 243) asserts pids {11, 12, 13} that only the broad `process_name_candidates` matcher finds — replacing that filter with the rules-side derivation breaks it.
- In-flight file collisions: W3-03 edits src/sentinel/daemon.py, src/sentinel/rules.py, tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py and tests/test_rules.py (all four of W3-04's files) plus adds vendors.py; W3-07 edits Daemon.emit and the operational-name path in daemon.py; UX-02b edits daemon.py, packaging/config.toml and tests/test_config_template.py; W3-05 edits rules.py.
- UX-02b already specifies the downstream evidence contract for this rule (`evidence["chain"]` with child and agent basenames plus pids, and `evidence["matched"]` for the argv text that matched) and is marked soft-blocked on W3-04, so the evidence key names W3-04 chooses should be agreed now rather than reworked later.
- tests/joint/ contains only harness-burst-collapses.json, harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json and harness-tampered-prefs-ignores-junk.json with no child-shell case, and it belongs to the Chief — W3-04 must not add one there.
- Hard prohibitions for this packet run, all confirmed against PROTOCOL.md section 9: no systemctl, no omarchy commands, no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local (including the installed plugin clone and the live allowlist/state), no edits to tests/joint/, no new dependencies, no push/merge/rebase, plus the packet's own bans on reading /proc/<pid>/environ or /proc/<pid>/fd/* and on any automatic kill.

**Risks**

- The most likely production outcome of a correct implementation is a false-positive generator: every Bash tool call whose text merely mentions a curl-pipe raises a high alert, and every long-enough `curl` child raises a medium one, which on the Owner's screen becomes the same toast storm that UX-01 and W3-03 were written to end.
- The rule may pass every test and still never fire for a real agent: any node- or python-wrapped agent (grok as installed via mise today) is invisible to `rules._is_agent`, so acceptance would look green while production coverage is partial — and nothing in the packet's tests would catch it.
- A Kill click on a net-helper alert targets a curl that has almost certainly already exited; without per-pid `evidence["starttimes"]`, `action.plan_kill`'s recycled-pid guard has nothing to compare against for the child and the daemon could signal a pid that now belongs to something else.
- One Approve at scope forever on a noisy net-helper permanently blinds the rule for that basename everywhere, and the Owner has no visible signal that the approval was global rather than about the alert in front of them.
- The acceptance perf command ("under 30 ms on the Chief's machine") may fail for reasons unrelated to the change, since the measured full-pass median is already 28.7 ms with a 35.0 ms max and the packet's 9.5 ms baseline no longer reproduces at 307 processes; a failed acceptance here would look like a regression and could trigger an unnecessary RETURN.
- Running W3-04 in parallel with W3-03 is a merge-conflict near-certainty rather than a risk, since both rewrite `sample_proc`/rules.py/tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py; the packet's "parallel-safe with W3-03" line is optimistic and should be re-read before both are launched.
- If the implementation keys the seen registry on anything other than `<rule>|<pid>:<starttime>|<flags>`, `_prune_seen`'s `int(k.split("|", 2)[1].split(":", 1)[0])` raises ValueError inside the sample loop and takes the daemon down on the first tick after a child dies.
- Secrets in a child's argv (bearer tokens, database URLs) would be persisted to alerts.jsonl and echoed by `sentinel-action investigate`; the file is 0600 and the cloud bundle excludes cmdline, but this is the first rule to put arbitrary third-party argv into the alert log at volume and it deserves an explicit Chief decision on value-level redaction rather than a Grok-side judgment call.
- `child_shell = false` will not actually disable the feature unless `load_config` is extended, so the packet's own safety valve is the thing most likely to be implemented as dead code — and it is the exact switch the Owner would reach for if the live machine starts screaming.
- The full-tree pass reads cmdline, comm, exe and cwd for every process on the machine rather than a handful of agents; that is metadata only and within invariant 2, but it is a materially wider read surface and should be stated in the security review rather than discovered at G1.
- Unresolved and needing a Chief decision before Grok starts: whether `evaluate_child_shell` should accept an explicit agent-pid set from the daemon (a rules.py signature change that collides with W3-03 and W3-05) or keep `agent_basenames` and accept the wrapper blind spot as a documented limit.


### W3-04 · integration:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CORRECTING security (and confirming its own open question): security wrote 'adding a uid os.stat per PID ... may put the 40 ms budget out of reach; measure it rather than assume.' I measured it. On this box /proc holds 309 pid dirs, of which only 107 are owned by us (198 foreign). A full-tree pass (cmdline + exe + comm + cwd readlink + one _stat_fields read) over all uids: median 28.6 ms, max 101.0 ms, 135 surviving snapshots. The same pass with an os.stat st_uid check first: median 18.9 ms, max 24.0 ms, 102 snapshots. The uid filter pays for itself roughly 10x and collapses tail latency 4x. It is not a perf cost, it is THE perf fix.
- CORRECTING codebase and spec on the perf remedy: both propose deferring the /proc/<pid>/cwd readlink to candidate pids only, and codebase adds that 'the packet's single pass wording forbids' it. That is the second-best lever and it forces an awkward argument with the packet. The uid filter (above) drops 198 of 309 pid dirs before ANY read, needs no wording fight, and is required for correctness anyway. Take the uid filter; cwd deferral becomes optional.
- CORRECTING integration (my own lens, self-correction): I reported the tree pass costs '+7 ms per tick' and that the 30 ms acceptance bar is 'marginal'. Both figures assumed no uid filter. With it, the full-tree pass at 18.9 ms median is FASTER than today's filtered sample_proc, which I re-measured at 22.3 ms median / 25.7 ms max using the packet's acceptance snippet verbatim. I withdraw '+7 ms' and withdraw the claim that acceptance is at risk on timing.
- CORRECTING tests: the proposed test_rchild_shell_recognizes_node_wrapped_agent asks to widen rules._is_agent to daemon.process_name_candidates. I read that function (daemon.py:331-348) and it unions EVERY cmdline token basename. So a child `bash -c "claude --dangerously-skip-permissions"` would match `claude`, be classified as an agent, and hit the `if _is_agent(proc, agents): continue` self-skip at rules.py:373 — the rule silently drops exactly the child it exists to catch. Widening _is_agent wholesale opens a trivial bypass. tests flagged the parent-skip as a thing to 'check with the Chief'; it is worse than that, it is a detection hole.
- RESOLVING integration's 'unresolved Chief decision' and the correction above together: the daemon already computes process_name_candidates for every pid inside the same loop, so it can hand evaluate_child_shell an explicit `agent_pids: set[int]` at zero extra syscall cost. _agent_ancestor and the self-skip then test pid membership — the daemon's identity judgement — instead of re-matching token soup. Keep the existing agent_basenames kwarg as the fallback so tests/test_rules.py keeps working standalone. This fixes the node-wrapper blind spot without touching _is_agent.
- CORRECTING security on notify: 'notify._title_body deliberately carries only basename and the cwd basename' is wrong. notify.py:213 is `title = f"{alert.severity.upper()}: {alert.summary}"` — the summary goes straight into the toast title. This matters because the packet's R13 pushes explanatory prose INTO the summary. Constraint to enforce: the summary stays short and basename-only (as evaluate_child_shell builds it today); never put child argv, a URL, or the matched ignore token in it, or attacker-controlled text reaches `omarchy notification send`.
- CORRECTING security on enforceability: 'the medium net-helper alert must not be given a default kill action' cannot be satisfied within this packet's file list. Panel.qml gates the Kill button purely on `card.hasPids` (Panel.qml:446, 541, 565), and every child-shell alert carries pids=[parent, child]. Verified in the file. spec is right to call this an UNENFORCED REQUIREMENT: R13 is documentation-only in W3-04, and any real gating is a separate Panel.qml packet.
- CORRECTING codebase on a number, minor but worth pinning: codebase reports the acceptance snippet at 26.5 ms, integration at 21.6 ms, I measured 22.3 ms. The refutation of Context claim 5 stands overwhelmingly (packet says 9.5 ms at 263 procs; reality is 309 procs and ~22 ms), but no single figure reproduces run-to-run. The report should give a range and the process count, not one decimal.
- ADDING a consequence none of the five stated: dropping foreign-uid pids can sever an ancestor chain that transits a root-owned process (an agent reparented under a root supervisor). _agent_ancestor walks ppid via by_pid.get, so a missing link yields None and simply produces no alert — a false negative, never an exception. That is the right trade (plan_kill would refuse a foreign pid with PermissionError anyway, kill.py:159-162), but it must be written down as a known limit, not discovered at G1.

**Settled by the squad**

- Line anchors, verified: sample_proc daemon.py:644-674; evaluate_child_shell rules.py:347-403; the rule's severity="high" rules.py:385; _child_kind rules.py:322-330; NET_HELPER_BASENAMES rules.py:57-59; ProcSnapshot rules.py:69-93; _as_proc rules.py:248-258; load_config daemon.py:221-259; _seen_key daemon.py:496; _prune_seen daemon.py:502. Packet Context 2/3/4 anchors are all stale. Re-anchor the packet on symbols and cite no line numbers to Grok.
- Context claim 1 CONFIRMED: daemon.py:24 imports only evaluate_process, evaluate_write; nothing in src/ references evaluate_child_shell. Context claim 5 REFUTED: 309 pid dirs, not 263; ~22 ms, not 9.5 ms. Context claim 6 CONFIRMED by three lenses independently — I ran _child_kind on the real snapshot-prefixed argv and it returns None, so test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash is a regression guard, not new behaviour.
- /proc field 4 = ppid and field 22 = starttime, confirmed. Use procinfo._stat_fields (it splits after rfind(')') so a comm with spaces/parens is safe) via ONE new combined reader. Calling read_ppid then read_starttime reads the same file twice — both go through _stat_fields.
- ProcSnapshot has no starttime and _as_proc drops every key it does not name. Add `starttime: int | None = None` as the trailing parameter of the frozen dataclass and its hand-written __init__, and carry it through _as_proc. All existing tests/test_rules.py callers pass keywords, so nothing breaks.
- MANDATORY evidence contract, verified end to end: evidence['instance'] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime) — without it _seen_key returns None and the 60 s coalescer re-alerts forever; the '<pid>:<starttime>' shape is required by _prune_seen's int(k.split('|',2)[1].split(':',1)[0]). PLUS evidence['starttimes'] = {parent_pid: parent_st, child_pid: child_st} — action.expected_starttimes (action.py:264-279) fans a scalar over every alert.pid, so with pids=[10,11] a `--session` kill would compare the agent against the child's starttime and plan_kill (kill.py:142-155) would drop the agent with a false 'recycled since the alert' message. Child-mode kill is unaffected.
- MANDATORY evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>']. Verified: keys.alert_flag_set (keys.py:29-38) falls back to extract_bypass_flags(alert.cmdline) and yields an empty set for a shell child, while keys.approval_prefix (keys.py:56-74) returns GLOBAL_PREFIX for any non-write rule at session/24h/forever. So one Approve-forever on a benign `bash -i` silently allowlists every future `curl|bash` at basename bash, everywhere. security found this; it verifies exactly as described. Treat as required, not optional.
- Missing-stat tolerance is the single most likely breakage. _fake_proc (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:178) writes /proc/<pid>/stat ONLY when starttime is passed, and two existing tests (lines 202 and 243) build entries with no stat at all. The new pass must fall back to ppid=0 / starttime=None and KEEP the snapshot; a `continue` on unreadable stat turns those two red. Also add `ppid: int = 1` to _fake_proc — the default keeps all seven existing callers byte-identical.
- Config plumbing is entirely absent and must be built: load_config copies a hardcoded 4-key tuple out of data.get('daemon', data). `[daemon] child_shell` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore` are both silently dropped today, so `child_shell = false` disables nothing. Each needs a cfg default, a load_config read (plus a genuine second-section read for [rules]), and a Daemon.__init__ pull. Do NOT extend the bare-top-level-key fallback to [rules]. packaging/config.toml has only [kill] and [notify]; adding sections is safe (test_config_template asserts only 'notify' and 'kill' present).
- net_helper_ignore is scoped to kind == 'net-helper' ONLY. _child_kind tests _is_pipe_to_shell first, so filtering after classification is free and preserves curl|bash detection. Literal substring on argv tokens — no DNS, no urlparse-then-resolve, no reachability probe. Reject empty/whitespace entries (a user- and agent-writable config, and ['' ] would silence the kind). The severity map and the allowlist go on evaluate_child_shell as keyword args, not daemon post-processing, so the rule stays unit-testable standalone.
- The medium downgrade breaks no existing test: test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper (the nc case) asserts rule, basename and kind only; test_rchild_shell_detects_curl_pipe_bash pins high and stays high.
- Test-infra facts: there is no [tool.pytest.ini_options] in pyproject.toml, no pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini, and the only pytest.mark anywhere is `parametrize` in tests/joint/. @pytest.mark.slow will warn AND still run under the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command. Baseline is 210 passed in ~1.85 s at ca0465a.
- Two live false-positive generators, both reproduced by me on this machine: (a) _child_kind returns 'curl|bash' at HIGH for a Bash tool call whose text merely MENTIONS a curl-pipe — I ran it on the genuine snapshot-prefixed argv wrapping `grep -rn "curl https://x | bash" docs/` and it fired; the W3-04 packet file itself contains that string, so grepping the repo trips it. (b) a bare `['bash']` with no arguments classifies as 'interactive-shell', also high. Both get a Kill button. Conversely, evaluate_child_shell returns ZERO alerts for a bash -i child of `node /usr/lib/node_modules/.bin/claude` — acceptance can be fully green while the rule never fires for the node/mise-wrapped agents actually running here.
- Forbidden, unanimously and confirmed against PROTOCOL.md section 9 plus the packet: no /proc/<pid>/environ, no /proc/<pid>/fd/* (so no resolving a net helper's destination via sockets), no signalling or killing from the sampler, no edits to tests/joint/, no new dependencies, no systemctl/omarchy/sudo, no writes under ~/.config or ~/.local, no push/merge/rebase. Additionally: the whole tree pass must be wrapped so one exception cannot abort sample_proc and take R-BYPASS sampling down with it.

**Final position.** RETURN W3-04 for six packet edits before Grok starts; the wiring itself is a day's work but three of the six change the code Grok would write, so amending after launch wastes a Grok run.

(1) ADD the own-uid filter to the pass — `os.stat(proc_root/'<pid>').st_uid != os.getuid()` before any read. It is simultaneously the correctness fix security asked for and the perf fix codebase and spec were reaching for: measured 28.6 ms median / 101 ms max over all 309 pid dirs versus 18.9 ms / 24.0 ms over the 107 we own. With it the full-tree pass is FASTER than today's filtered sample_proc (22.3 ms), the 30 ms live bar is comfortable, and the cwd-deferral argument with the packet's "single pass" wording disappears. Document the resulting limit: an ancestor chain transiting a root process breaks and yields a false negative.

(2) SPECIFY the evidence contract verbatim in the packet, because three of it is load-bearing for safety and none of it is currently written down: evidence['instance'] = instance_key(child_pid, child_st) in '<pid>:<starttime>' form; evidence['starttimes'] = {parent_pid: st, child_pid: st}; evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>']. Without the second, `sentinel-action <id> kill --session` silently drops the agent as recycled. Without the third, one Approve-forever on a benign `bash -i` blinds `curl|bash`. Agree the key names with UX-02b (which already specifies evidence['chain'] and evidence['matched']) now rather than reworking.

(3) SETTLE agent identity by passing `agent_pids: set[int]` from the daemon, computed from process_name_candidates it already builds in the same loop at zero syscall cost. Do NOT widen rules._is_agent — that would make a child `bash -c "claude --yolo"` look like an agent and skip it, which is a bypass of the rule's whole purpose.

(4) REWRITE the perf section against a fresh baseline (309 processes, ~22 ms today, not 263/9.5 ms) and resolve the slow marker: either register it in pyproject.toml and add a deselect to the acceptance command, or drop the wall-clock assertion. As written the perf test runs inside the graded suite and can RETURN the packet on timing noise.

(5) CHIEF DECISION on two policy items the packet cannot settle unilaterally. The design spec line 201 lists R-CHILD-SHELL as severity high with "Kill (child) / Investigate"; the packet downgrades net-helper to medium and calls it not-a-kill-prompt. That needs a spec edit, and the "not a kill prompt" half is unenforceable here because Panel.qml gates Kill on hasPids alone. Second: given that the rule fires high on any Bash call merely mentioning a curl-pipe and medium on every agent HTTPS call, decide whether net-helper ships enabled by default at all. Also decide value-level argv redaction — this is the first rule to write arbitrary third-party argv (`curl -H "Authorization: Bearer ..."`) into alerts.jsonl at volume, and that overlaps W3-05; picking a boundary now avoids two competing scrubbers.

(6) SEQUENCE W3-04 AFTER W3-03. Both rewrite sample_proc, rules.py, tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py and tests/test_rules.py. The packet's "parallel-safe with W3-03" line is wrong and running them together is a conflict near-certainty, which given the Grok budget is not a risk worth taking.

Nothing here reaches the Owner's live daemon on merge — it imports ~/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel — but that clone tracks this branch, so the false-positive profile lands the moment a plugin update runs.

### W3-04 · security

W3-04 turns `evaluate_child_shell` from dead code into a live production path, and that flips three things at once: `sample_proc` starts snapshotting every PID in `/proc` instead of only agent-named ones, alert records start carrying arbitrary child argv (curl, wget, nc) verbatim to disk, and `evidence.child_pids` starts naming a kill target. The invariants most at risk are "runs as the user" (foreign-uid processes entering the tree), "metadata only, never credentials" (`curl -u`/`-H Authorization` argv landing in `alerts.jsonl`), and the kill-safety machinery in `action.py`/`kill.py`, which has two concrete latent defects the packet's current wording would trigger: a scalar `evidence["starttime"]` mis-applied to the agent parent, and the precious-worktree confirm gate evaluated against the child's cwd. No `shell=True` exists anywhere in `src/sentinel/`, the cloud bundle key allowlist already excludes `cmdline`, and the sticky-R-SELF path is structurally unaffected — those three invariants are safe as long as the packet adds nothing new to them.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT runs-as-user-never-root: `sample_proc` (/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py:644) currently discards non-agent processes before building anything, but the new single-pass tree will snapshot root- and other-user-owned PIDs whose `comm` is world-readable, so a foreign `curl` or `bash -i` whose ppid chain reaches a user agent PID can produce an alert that `plan_kill` (src/sentinel/kill.py:131-165) then refuses with PermissionError — the pass must drop any pid whose `os.stat(proc_root/'<pid>').st_uid != os.getuid()` before it becomes a `ProcSnapshot`.
- INVARIANT runs-as-user-never-root: `_readlink` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:350) returning "" for unreadable foreign `/proc/<pid>/exe` and `/proc/<pid>/cwd` is the correct unprivileged behaviour and must never be 'fixed' by adding CAP_SYS_PTRACE, a setuid helper, or a root unit to make the tree complete.
- INVARIANT metadata-only-never-credentials: `evaluate_child_shell` copies the child's argv verbatim into `Alert.cmdline` (src/sentinel/rules.py:376) and `store.append_alert` (src/sentinel/store.py:67) writes it to `alerts.jsonl`, so wiring this rule live makes every `curl -u user:token`, `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer sk-..."`, `wget --password=`, and `nc` argv a plaintext on-disk secret that did not exist before this packet — W3-04 must redact credential-bearing tokens at snapshot time.
- INVARIANT metadata-only-never-credentials: `investigate.redact_mapping` (src/sentinel/investigate.py:79) drops by *key name* only and `cmdline` is not a sensitive key, so the redaction that already protects the investigate render and the panel will not catch a secret sitting in an argv value — the value-level scrub has to happen before `Alert.new`, not downstream.
- INVARIANT metadata-only-never-credentials: `Alert.cmdline` is unbounded (src/sentinel/models.py:22) and `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` can be megabytes, so a hostile agent can exec `bash -i` with a giant argv and drive `alerts.jsonl` past `store.MAX_BYTES` (src/sentinel/store.py:19) to force rotation and destroy earlier evidence — cap both token count and total argv length in the snapshot builder.
- INVARIANT metadata-only-never-credentials: the cloud path stays clean today because `build_redacted_bundle`'s ALLOWED_KEYS (src/sentinel/summarize.py:14-28) omits `cmdline`, and W3-04 must not add the child argv, the matched `net_helper_ignore` token, or a URL to `alert.evidence` in any key that the bundle or `_flag_list` (src/sentinel/summarize.py:40) would pick up.
- INVARIANT auth-kind-files-never-opened: net-helper classification must stay pure argv shape (`_is_net_helper`, src/sentinel/rules.py:277) — the `[rules] net_helper_ignore` substring check must compare against tokens already in the snapshot and must never dereference `curl --config <file>`, `-K`, `-d @file`, `-H @file`, `--netrc`, or `~/.netrc`, which is exactly the file class `scout.classify_kind` (src/sentinel/scout.py:36) labels "auth".
- INVARIANT auth-kind-files-never-opened: the packet's Forbidden list and this invariant both ban `/proc/<pid>/environ` and `/proc/<pid>/fd/*`, so the tree pass must read only `stat` (once, for ppid field 4 and starttime field 22), `cmdline`, `comm`, and the `exe`/`cwd` readlinks — resolving the net helper's destination via its socket fds is the tempting shortcut and is out of bounds.
- INVARIANT state-0600-inside-0700: the seen registry is in-memory (`Daemon._seen`, src/sentinel/daemon.py:416) and should stay that way; if W3-04 persists any child-shell cache it must use `fsutil.ensure_private_dir`/`open_private`/`write_private_atomic` (0700/0600) and its filename must be added to `daemon.OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:45), or the daemon's own writes will fire R-SELF against itself.
- INVARIANT argv-arrays-never-shell-strings: no `shell=True` exists anywhere in `src/sentinel/`, and `notify._title_body` (src/sentinel/notify.py:212) deliberately carries only `basename` and the cwd basename — W3-04 must not put the child's cmdline into the toast title or body, where it becomes attacker-controlled text passed through `omarchy notification send`.
- INVARIANT argv-arrays-never-shell-strings: `_PIPE_TO_SHELL_RE` (src/sentinel/rules.py:62) joining argv into a string is parsing, not execution, and is fine; the line not to cross is any new code that reconstructs a runnable command from `alert.cmdline` outside `investigate._q`'s `shlex.quote` (src/sentinel/investigate.py:97).
- INVARIANT no-network-from-the-daemon: `net_helper_ignore` must be literal substring matching on argv tokens — no DNS resolution, no `urlparse`-then-resolve, and no reachability probe to decide whether a host is localhost, since any of those would make the daemon originate traffic to an attacker-chosen destination on every tick.
- INVARIANT no-silent-auto-kill: `evaluate_child_shell` must only populate `evidence.child_pids`; killing stays behind `action.cmd_kill` -> `confirm_kill` -> `plan_kill` (src/sentinel/action.py:393-423), and the medium `net-helper` alert in particular must not be given a default kill action, matching the packet's own instruction to describe it as a visibility alert.
- KILL-SAFETY DEFECT (concrete): `action.expected_starttimes` (src/sentinel/action.py:264-279) applies a scalar `evidence["starttime"]` to *every* pid in `alert.pids`, so if W3-04 records only the child's starttime while `pids == [10, 11]`, the agent parent's expected starttime is wrong and `plan_kill` silently drops it from a `--session` kill with a misleading 'recycled since the alert' message — the alert must set `evidence["starttimes"] = {parent_pid: st, child_pid: st}` and `evidence["instance"] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime)`.
- KILL-SAFETY DEFECT (concrete): `cmd_kill` computes `precious = cwd_is_precious(alert.cwd)` (src/sentinel/action.py:408) and this rule sets `Alert.cwd` from the *child* (src/sentinel/rules.py:377), so a child that ran `cd /tmp` bypasses the precious-worktree tty confirm for a session kill that still terminates the agent working in the protected worktree — carry the parent's cwd and make the precious check consider both.
- ALLOWLIST CROSS-SUPPRESSION (concrete): `fingerprint` keys on rule+basename+flags+prefix and `approval_prefix` (src/sentinel/keys.py:56-74) uses an empty global prefix for process rules, while `alert_flag_set` (src/sentinel/keys.py:29) yields an empty set for a shell child — so approving one benign `bash -i` 'forever' silently allowlists every future `curl|bash` (also basename `bash`, also no flags) — W3-04 must set `evidence["flags"] = ["kind:<kind>"]` so the three kinds cannot cross-approve and so `_seen_key` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:496) separates them too.
- INVARIANT sticky-self-tamper-bypasses-pause-and-prefs: `Notifier.send` checks `is_sticky` before `_is_paused` (src/sentinel/notify.py:421-424) and `_seen_key` returns None outside `PROCESS_RULES`, so R-SELF is structurally unaffected today — but any per-tick alert cap or rate limiter W3-04 adds inside `Daemon.emit` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:507) must exempt `R-SELF` with a `STICKY_EVENTS` kind, and R-CHILD-SHELL must not be added to `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` (src/sentinel/notify.py:40).
- SELF-DEFENCE: `[daemon] child_shell = false` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore` live in `~/.config/sentinel/config.toml`, which the monitored agent can write, and `net_helper_ignore = [""]` or `["/"]` silences every net helper — the loader must reject empty/whitespace entries, and config.toml must stay out of `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:45) so a foreign write still fires R-SELF, which is the tripwire S1/W3-07 depends on.
- AVAILABILITY OF DETECTION: `load_config` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-243) reads a fixed key list from `data.get("daemon", data)`, so a wrong-typed `net_helper_ignore` must be coerced or ignored rather than raise, and the whole tree pass must be wrapped so one exception cannot abort `sample_proc` and take R-BYPASS sampling down with it — a crashed sampler is a silent monitoring outage.
- SELF-ALERT FEEDBACK LOOP: `sample_proc` skips only `os.getpid()`, so when the daemon is started from inside an agent session (as happens on this project's own dev machine) its `omarchy notification send` and `sentinel-action` children have an agent ancestor and `_agent_ancestor` (src/sentinel/rules.py:332) will match them — exclude the daemon's own descendant subtree and Sentinel basenames from `_child_kind` candidates before the live smoke.

**Risks**

- The packet cites `sample_proc` at src/sentinel/daemon.py:575-601 but it actually lives at 644-674, and `evaluate_child_shell`'s severity is at rules.py:385 not 384 — the packet's line anchors are stale, so the fact-check must re-anchor on symbols before any of its claims are marked verified.
- One alert per child instance plus a per-instance seen key means N forked `bash -i` children produce N appends and N toasts in a single tick, and `coalesce_key` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:260) includes the instance so the Coalescer will never collapse distinct PIDs — a per-tick child-alert cap that degrades to one summarizing alert is needed, and it must not be implemented as a blanket cap in `emit` that also swallows R-SELF.
- Adding a uid `os.stat` per PID on top of the ~5 syscalls the pass already does may put the 40 ms / 400-process budget out of reach; measure it rather than assume, and note the acceptance snippet's 30 ms target was set for the old filtered pass, not the full-tree one.
- Reading `/proc` is non-atomic, so a PID can be recycled between the `stat` read and the `cmdline` read and yield a snapshot that mixes one process's ppid with another's argv, naming an innocent PID as the kill target — re-read starttime after the other reads and drop the snapshot if it changed.
- Credential redaction of child argv overlaps W3-05 (finding S4); implementing it independently here risks two different scrubbers with different rules, and any packet test asserting an exact `cmdline` value will conflict with it — coordinate the boundary before writing either.
- Claim 6 (Claude Code's `bash -c "source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/..."`) is unverified from this worktree; `_is_interactive_shell` (src/sentinel/rules.py:305-319) already returns False whenever a `-c` is present, so the test is a regression guard rather than a fix, and a wrapper that ever used `bash -i -c` would still alert.
- Even at medium severity, agents run `curl` constantly, so if `net_helper_ignore` proves too coarse the practical outcome is alert fatigue that trains the user into a global R-CHILD-SHELL approval — which, given the fingerprint cross-suppression above, would take the high-severity `curl|bash` detection down with it.
- The `net-helper` medium alert still writes the child's full argv and cwd to `alerts.jsonl` on every ordinary API call an agent makes, which turns a routine-traffic log into a durable record of project paths and request shapes; consider recording only the basename and a token count for the medium kind.


### W3-04 · security:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- I am correcting MYSELF (security). My point "the pass must drop any pid whose /proc/<pid> st_uid != os.getuid() before it becomes a ProcSnapshot" is WRONG and I withdraw it. Measured on this machine: 306 live pids, 197 foreign-uid, but only 33 of those have a readable cmdline (the rest are kernel threads that the existing `if not cmdline: continue` already drops), and every one of the 33 descends from pid 1, not from a user agent. The single case where a foreign-uid process legitimately reaches an agent ancestor is `sudo curl … | bash` / `sudo nc` — exactly the case most worth alerting on. A uid filter would blind the rule to sudo-elevated helpers. Keep foreign pids in the tree. The correct handling is downstream: kill.plan_kill raises PermissionError for the WHOLE plan on any non-owned pid (src/sentinel/kill.py:163-166), which is spec-conformant (design spec §11.5 "Refuse any PID not owned by the user") but means the panel offers a Kill button that errors out. Record the child's uid in evidence so investigate/panel can say "owned by root, not killable" instead.
- I am correcting the TESTS agent (and the same claim in my own and the codebase agent's perf reasoning). "readlink /proc/<pid>/cwd for all 400 … removes the single largest new syscall cost" is REFUTED by measurement. On this tree, live /proc, median of 15 runs: current sample_proc 21.4 ms; a full pass adding a stat read for every pid but NO extra cwd 28.1 ms; full pass with stat AND cwd for every pid 29.9 ms. So the stat read costs +6.7 ms and the cwd readlink costs +1.8 ms. Lazy cwd buys ~6% of the budget, not the largest share; the stat read is both unavoidable (ppid is required) and ~4x more expensive. Do not build the design around deferring cwd.
- I am correcting MYSELF (security) again, more narrowly. I wrote that notify._title_body "deliberately carries only basename and the cwd basename". Reading src/sentinel/notify.py:212-216, the title is f"{severity.upper()}: {alert.summary}" and the child-shell summary embeds _proc_basename(child), which is attacker-chosen (exe path basename, else comm, else argv[0]). So the toast DOES carry an attacker-influenced string. It is still not full argv, and notify.build_argv passes an argv list with no shell, so there is no injection — but "no attacker-controlled text in the toast" is too strong and should not be relied on.
- I am correcting the PACKET's severity design, and none of the five of us caught this. Downgrading the whole `net-helper` kind to medium also downgrades `nc -l -p 4444` — which is the exact case tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper encodes, and it is a reverse-shell listener, not API noise. NET_HELPER_BASENAMES (src/sentinel/rules.py:57-59) mixes curl/wget (agents run these constantly, near-zero signal) with nc/ncat/netcat/socat (near-zero legitimate agent use, high signal). The packet's noise goal is satisfied by demoting curl/wget only. Recommend: curl|wget -> medium visibility alert; nc|ncat|netcat|socat -> stay high with the spec's Kill (child) prompt; and net_helper_ignore must apply ONLY to the curl/wget subset, never to the listener subset, or the allowlist becomes a backdoor-hiding switch.
- I am confirming and strengthening the INTEGRATION agent's false-positive claim, which is the most important live finding in the five reports. I reproduced it: rules._child_kind(ProcSnapshot(cmdline=["bash","-c","grep -rn 'curl https://x | bash' docs/"])) returns 'curl|bash'. _is_pipe_to_shell joins the -c string and regex-matches mention, not execution. Because docs/collab/packets/W3-04-wire-child-shell.md itself contains that literal, a grep over this repo by an agent raises a HIGH alert with a Kill prompt at the spec's default. This is not a tuning nit; it is a self-triggering rule in the repo that ships it. Nothing in the packet's test list catches it.
- I am correcting the SPEC agent's framing of R25/net-helper test safety — the conclusion is right but the reason matters. I read tests/test_rules.py:212-222: test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper asserts only rule, basename and kind, so a medium downgrade is green. But the fixture is `nc -l -p 4444`, so that green test would then be silently asserting that a listener backdoor is a medium visibility alert. Green here is not evidence of correctness; see my severity-split correction.
- I am confirming the INTEGRATION agent's approval-collapse finding and adding the concrete mechanism, which I verified: allowlist.fingerprint(rule, basename, flag_set, cwd_prefix) (src/sentinel/allowlist.py:22-30); keys.approval_prefix returns GLOBAL_PREFIX "" for any non-write rule at session/24h/forever (keys.py:56-74); keys.alert_flag_set falls back to extract_bypass_flags(cmdline), which returns [] for both `bash -i` and `bash -c 'curl … | bash'`. Both are basename 'bash' with an empty flag set, so ONE Approve-forever on a benign interactive shell allowlists every future curl|bash from every agent everywhere. Fix: set evidence["flags"] = [f"kind:{kind}"]; that separates the three kinds in both fingerprint and Daemon._seen_key. Caveat I owe the group: "flags" IS in summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS, so "kind:net-helper" will reach the cloud bundle — benign (no argv, no host), but state it rather than discover it at G1.
- I am confirming the TESTS agent's top breakage risk against the fixture: _fake_proc (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:178-196) writes /proc/<pid>/stat only when starttime is not None, and test_sampler_detects_known_basename_bypass and test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper both call it without one. A `if fields is None: continue` in the new single pass turns three tests red at once. The pass must fall back to ppid=0, starttime=None and keep the snapshot.
- I am confirming the SPEC agent's spec-contradiction, verbatim from the tree: design spec line 201 lists R-CHILD-SHELL at severity high with default menu "Kill (child) / Investigate", and §11.2 (line 286) names curl, wget, nc as the default kill target. The packet contradicts both. Separately, "not a kill prompt" is unenforceable as written: Panel.qml:446 gates the Kill button on hasPids, Panel.qml:279 binds `x` to kill, and every child-shell alert carries pids=[parent, child] — so a medium net-helper card still shows Kill.
- Perf, settled by three independent measurements rather than the packet's number: I measured current sample_proc at 21.4 ms median (306 pids), a full stat+cwd pass at 29.9 ms, and evaluate_child_shell over 142 real snapshots at 4.1 ms median — about 34 ms per tick end to end. The codebase agent got 26.5/35-40 and the integration agent 21.6/28.7 on the same box. The packet's Context claim 5 (263 processes, 9.5 ms) and its "under 30 ms" acceptance bar are both unreachable and must be rewritten before launch, or acceptance fails for reasons unrelated to the change.

**Settled by the squad**

- Line anchors in the packet's Context are stale and must be re-anchored on symbols, not line numbers: sample_proc is src/sentinel/daemon.py:644-674 (not 575-601); evaluate_child_shell is rules.py:347-403 (not 346); severity="high" is rules.py:385 (not 384); _child_kind's net-helper branch is rules.py:327-328 with NET_HELPER_BASENAMES at rules.py:57-59.
- Context claim 1 CONFIRMED: evaluate_child_shell has no caller in src/; only tests/test_rules.py uses it. Context claim 6 CONFIRMED live by three of us: Claude Code's Bash tool runs /usr/bin/bash -c 'source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-*.sh …', and _is_interactive_shell already returns False for any -c shell — the packet's snapshot test is a regression guard, not new behaviour.
- /proc/<pid>/stat field 4 = ppid, field 22 = starttime, CONFIRMED empirically by three of us and already documented in src/sentinel/procinfo.py's module docstring. procinfo._stat_fields is the correct primitive (it parses past the last ')' so a comm with spaces or parens is safe); read_ppid and read_starttime each re-read the file, so a combined single-read accessor in procinfo.py is required — a file the packet's Changes section never names.
- ProcSnapshot (rules.py:67-92) is a frozen dataclass with a hand-written __init__ and has NO starttime field, and _as_proc (rules.py:248-258) drops unknown mapping keys. The packet's parent-block starttime and the child instance key cannot be satisfied without either adding the field (plus __init__ plus _as_proc) or having the daemon patch the returned alerts. This is an unresolved design choice the Chief must make, not Grok.
- evidence["starttimes"] = {parent_pid: st, child_pid: st} is MANDATORY, not optional. action.expected_starttimes (action.py:264-279) fans a scalar evidence["starttime"] out to every pid in alert.pids; with pids=[parent, child] that tags the agent with the child's starttime and kill.plan_kill silently drops the agent from a --session kill as "recycled". Four of five of us found this independently.
- evidence["instance"] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime) is required or Daemon._seen_key (daemon.py:496-500) returns None and the alert re-fires through the 60 s coalescer forever. The key format is compatible with _prune_seen's int(k.split('|',2)[1].split(':',1)[0]) for both "11:901" and the unknown "11:?" form — I verified both parse.
