- Neither [daemon] child_shell nor [rules] net_helper_ignore is readable today: load_config (daemon.py:221-259) iterates a hardcoded four-key tuple against data.get("daemon", data) and has no [rules] handling at all. The packet's own safety valve is dead code until both are plumbed, plus a matching Daemon.__init__ cfg read. packaging/config.toml has only [kill] and [notify].
- net_helper_ignore must be literal substring matching on argv tokens the snapshot already holds — no DNS, no urlparse-then-resolve, no reachability probe, and no dereferencing of curl --config/-K/-d @file/--netrc. It must be scoped to the net-helper kind only; applying it before _child_kind would let `curl http://localhost/x | bash` disable pipe-to-shell detection. The loader must reject empty/whitespace entries, since net_helper_ignore = [""] silences every helper and ~/.config/sentinel/config.toml is agent-writable. config.toml must stay out of daemon.OPERATIONAL_NAMES so a foreign write still fires R-SELF — I verified config_dir() is a self-watched path and config.toml is not in OPERATIONAL_NAMES.
- Forbidden reads hold: only stat (once), cmdline, comm, and the exe/cwd readlinks. No /proc/<pid>/environ, no /proc/<pid>/fd/*, no auto-kill, no shell=True (there is none anywhere in src/sentinel/). The cloud path stays clean because summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS excludes cmdline — do not add child argv, a matched URL, or a host to any evidence key.
- Child argv lands verbatim in ~/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl (rules.py:376 -> store.append_alert) and investigate.redact_mapping drops by KEY NAME only, so a `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer …"` child writes a plaintext secret to disk. This is the first rule to log arbitrary third-party argv at volume. It needs an explicit Chief decision on value-level redaction — and it overlaps W3-05 finding S4, so the boundary must be agreed before either is written or two scrubbers will diverge.
- The tree pass must be wrapped so one exception cannot abort sample_proc and take R-BYPASS sampling down with it; a crashed sampler is a silent monitoring outage. A wrong-typed net_helper_ignore must be coerced or ignored, never raise.
- Agent identity is derived two incompatible ways — daemon.process_name_candidates (broad: exe basename + every cmdline token basename + comm) vs rules._proc_basename (first non-empty of exe, comm, argv[0]) — so node- and python-wrapped agents (grok as installed via mise today) are agents to the sampler and NOT to the tree rule, and their children never alert. Widening rules._is_agent also changes the parent-skip at rules.py:378, so it is a Chief decision, not a Grok one.
- Baseline: 210 passed in 1.97 s at this worktree. tests/joint/ is Chief-owned and off limits. W3-03 edits all four of W3-04's files, so "parallel-safe with W3-03" is wrong and the two should not run concurrently. The @pytest.mark.slow marker has no registration anywhere (no [tool.pytest.ini_options], no pytest.ini) and the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance would run the perf test regardless.

**Final position.** "Do not launch W3-04 as written. The wiring itself is small and the W3-02 machinery it needs is already correct, but three of the packet's own decisions are unsafe or unbuildable as specified, and one of them is self-triggering in this repo.\n\nFirst, the severity design is wrong in a way none of the five reports named. Demoting the whole `net-helper` kind to medium demotes `nc -l -p 4444` alongside `curl https://api.anthropic.com`. Split it: curl/wget medium with the visibility wording and the net_helper_ignore allowlist; nc/ncat/netcat/socat stay high with the design spec's Kill (child) prompt and are never allowlistable. That satisfies the packet's noise goal without contradicting spec §8 and §11, and it removes the need for a spec edit on the dangerous half.\n\nSecond, the rule fires on mention, not execution. I reproduced `_child_kind` returning `curl|bash` for `bash -c \"grep -rn 'curl https://x | bash' docs/\"`, and this repository contains that literal in the packet file itself — so an agent grepping the repo raises a high alert with a Kill prompt on the Owner's screen. `_is_pipe_to_shell` needs to reject a -c string whose match sits inside a quoted argument to grep/rg/echo/cat, or W3-04 ships a rule that alerts on being read about. This should be settled before Grok starts, not tuned after G1.\n\nThird, the alert must carry a per-pid evidence[\"starttimes\"] map and evidence[\"flags\"] = [\"kind:<kind>\"]. Without the first, a --session kill silently drops the agent as recycled. Without the second, one Approve-forever on a benign `bash -i` allowlists every future `curl|bash` — same basename, same empty flag set, same global prefix, same fingerprint.\n\nI withdraw my uid-filter recommendation: foreign-uid pids belong in the tree because `sudo curl | bash` is the case worth catching, and kill.plan_kill already refuses non-owned pids per spec §11.5. Record the child's uid so the UI can explain the refusal instead of offering a button that errors.\n\nOn budget: measured here, the full pass is 29.9 ms plus 4.1 ms for evaluate_child_shell, against a 21.4 ms baseline — about 34 ms, over the packet's 30 ms bar. The stat read is +6.7 ms and unavoidable; the cwd readlink is only +1.8 ms, so deferring cwd does not save the acceptance. Rewrite the threshold against a fresh baseline rather than contorting the design, and register the slow marker with a deselect so wall-clock noise cannot RETURN the packet.\n\nOne unresolved item that is a Chief call, not a Grok call: whether the child's argv is redacted before Alert.new. Today this rule would write `curl -H \"Authorization: Bearer …\"` to alerts.jsonl in full, and it overlaps W3-05 finding S4."

### W3-04 · spec

Packet `docs/collab/packets/W3-04-wire-child-shell.md` asks for `evaluate_child_shell` (src/sentinel/rules.py:347-403) to be wired into `Daemon.sample_proc` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:644-674) via a single full-`/proc` pass that builds one `ProcSnapshot` per pid, with per-kind severity, a `[rules] net_helper_ignore` allowlist, a `[daemon] child_shell` switch, and the W3-02 seen registry keyed on the child instance. I extracted 28 testable requirements and the acceptance commands verbatim. Five of the six Context claims verify as stated (line references have drifted); the substantive problems are that `ProcSnapshot` has no `starttime` field even though the packet requires it in the parent block, that `[rules]`/`[daemon] child_shell` are not read by `load_config` at all today, and that the net-helper severity downgrade contradicts the design spec's rule table and kill policy.

**Findings**

- R1 (sampler pass): `Daemon.sample_proc` (/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py:644-674) must build exactly one `ProcSnapshot` per numeric pid directory under `self._proc_root` in a single pass, carrying pid, ppid, cmdline, exe, comm, cwd and starttime.
- R2 (one stat read): `/proc/<pid>/stat` must be read once per pid and both ppid (field 4) and starttime (field 22) taken from that single read — calling `sentinel.procinfo.read_ppid` and `read_starttime` in sequence reads the file twice and violates this (both call the private `_stat_fields`, src/sentinel/procinfo.py:12-43), so a combined helper is required.
- R3 (kernel-thread early exit): a process whose `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is empty must still be skipped before any further reads, preserving the existing `if not cmdline: continue` behaviour at daemon.py:662-664.
- R4 (agent path unchanged): `evaluate_process` must still run only on snapshots whose `process_name_candidates(exe, cmdline, comm)` intersects `self._known` (daemon.py:667-670), producing the same R-BYPASS alerts as today with `pids=[pid]`, `evidence.starttime` and `evidence.instance` (daemon.py:546-570).
- R5 (tree evaluation): after the pass, `evaluate_child_shell(snapshots, agent_basenames=self._known)` must be called exactly once per tick with the full snapshot list, and `sentinel.daemon` must import `evaluate_child_shell` (today daemon.py:25 imports only `evaluate_process, evaluate_write`, and `grep -rn evaluate_child_shell src/` matches only rules.py:347).
- R6 (seen registry): every R-CHILD-SHELL alert must carry `evidence['instance'] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime)` (and `evidence['starttime']`) so `Daemon._seen_key` (daemon.py:496-500) returns a key — R-CHILD-SHELL is already in `PROCESS_RULES` (daemon.py:61) but `_seen_key` returns None without an `instance`, which would fall through to the coalescer and re-alert after the 60 s window.
- R7 (one alert per child instance): a second `sample_proc()` tick over an unchanged fake `/proc` must produce no additional alert row and no additional notify call for the same child pid+starttime.
- R8 (registry pruning): `self._prune_seen(live)` must still be called with the full live pid set at the end of the pass, and the child-instance keys must be parseable by its `int(k.split('|',2)[1].split(':',1)[0])` pid extraction (daemon.py:502-505).
- R9 (severity curl|bash): an alert of kind `curl|bash` must have `severity == 'high'`.
- R10 (severity interactive-shell): an alert of kind `interactive-shell` must have `severity == 'high'`.
- R11 (severity net-helper): an alert of kind `net-helper` must have `severity == 'medium'`, which requires changing the unconditional `severity="high"` at src/sentinel/rules.py:385 or post-processing the alert in the daemon.
- R12 (net-helper allowlist): a `net-helper` alert must be suppressed entirely when any string in config `[rules] net_helper_ignore` (default `["localhost", "127.0.0.1"]`) is a substring of any token of the child's own cmdline.
- R13 (net-helper summary text): the `net-helper` alert's `summary` must state in words that it is a visibility alert and not a kill prompt, so a test can assert on the summary string.
- R14 (parent block): the alert's `parent` mapping must contain `pid`, `exe`, `basename` and `starttime` — `starttime` is new; rules.py:394-398 emits only the first three, and `ProcSnapshot` (rules.py:68-93, plus `_as_proc` at rules.py:248-258) has no `starttime` field at all.
- R15 (kill target unchanged): `evidence['child_pids']` must remain the list containing only the child pid, since `sentinel.action.resolve_child_pids` (src/sentinel/action.py:137-140) and `sentinel.kill` (src/sentinel/kill.py:109-127) use it as the default kill target.
- R16 (config switch default): `[daemon] child_shell` must default to `true`, and `load_config` (daemon.py:221-259) currently reads only `sampler_interval`, `coalesce_window`, `extra_bypass_flags`, `known_basenames` from the `daemon` section, so both `child_shell` and the new `[rules]` section need new plumbing plus a matching `Daemon.__init__` cfg key.
- R17 (config switch off): with `child_shell = false`, the tree pass must be skipped — no `evaluate_child_shell` call and no R-CHILD-SHELL alert — while R-BYPASS sampling still works.
- R18 (Claude Code snapshot shell): a child `bash -c "source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/x.sh; ls"` under an agent parent must produce no alert; verified live that Claude Code's Bash tool runs `/usr/bin/bash -c source /home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-*.sh ... && eval '<cmd>'`.
- R19 (forbidden reads): the implementation must not read `/proc/<pid>/environ` or resolve `/proc/<pid>/fd/*`.
- R20 (no auto-kill): the sampler must not signal or kill any process.
- R21 (test): `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_fires_child_shell` — fake `/proc` with `claude` (pid 10) and child `bash -i` (pid 11, ppid 10) yields exactly one alert with `rule == 'R-CHILD-SHELL'`, `evidence['kind'] == 'interactive-shell'`, `evidence['child_pids'] == [11]`, `pids == [10, 11]`.
- R22 (test): `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash` — child `bash -c "source .../shell-snapshots/x.sh; ls"` yields no alert.
- R23 (test): `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_net_helper_is_medium_and_once` — child `curl https://api.example` yields one alert with `severity == 'medium'`, and a second tick yields nothing.
- R24 (test): `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_child_shell_disabled_by_config` must exist and cover R17.
- R25 (test): the existing child-shell tests in `tests/test_rules.py:174-249` must stay green; note `test_rchild_shell_detects_curl_pipe_bash` asserts `severity == 'high'` while `test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper` (nc listener) asserts no severity, so the net-helper downgrade does not break them as written.
- R26 (perf test): a test marked `slow` must build a fake `/proc` with 400 processes and assert `sample_proc` median under 40 ms over 20 runs.
- R27 (fact-check duty): the report must mark Context claims 1-6 CONFIRMED/REFUTED/UNVERIFIED with evidence, and verify `/proc/<pid>/stat` ppid=field 4, starttime=field 22 against `man 5 proc`.
- R28 (report): the run must produce `docs/collab/reports/W3-04-report.md` in the section 5 format of docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md, including the fact-check table.
- ACCEPTANCE (verbatim, command 1): `.venv/bin/pytest -q`
- ACCEPTANCE (verbatim, command 2): `.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'` / `import os,sys,tempfile,time; d=tempfile.mkdtemp(); os.environ["XDG_STATE_HOME"]=d; os.environ["XDG_CONFIG_HOME"]=d` / `sys.path.insert(0,"src")` / `from sentinel.daemon import Daemon` / `dm=Daemon({"notify":lambda a: None,"inotify":False})` / `t=[]` / `for _ in range(10):` / `    t0=time.perf_counter(); dm.sample_proc(); t.append(time.perf_counter()-t0)` / `t.sort(); print("live sample_proc median ms:", round(t[5]*1000,1))` / `PY` — expected: under 30 ms on the Chief's machine (263 processes).
- FACT-CHECK 1 CONFIRMED: `grep -rn evaluate_child_shell src/` matches only `src/sentinel/rules.py:347`, and docs/superpowers/plans/STATUS-AND-NEXT.md:64 repeats that the sampler is not wired.
- FACT-CHECK 2 CONFIRMED with drift: `sample_proc` is at daemon.py:644-674, not 575-601; it does skip name-disjoint processes (daemon.py:667-670) and never reads ppid.
- FACT-CHECK 3 CONFIRMED with drift: `evaluate_child_shell` spans rules.py:347-403 (packet says 346-403) and takes the whole tree, building `by_pid` and walking `_agent_ancestor` via `ppid` (rules.py:332-344).
- FACT-CHECK 4 CONFIRMED with drift: the single `severity="high"` for this rule is rules.py:385 (packet says 384) and `_child_kind` returns `net-helper` at rules.py:327-328 for any of `curl, wget, nc, ncat, netcat, socat` (NET_HELPER_BASENAMES, rules.py:57-59).
- FACT-CHECK 6 CONFIRMED live: `~/.claude/shell-snapshots/` exists with `snapshot-bash-*.sh` files and the running Bash tool process is `/usr/bin/bash -c source .../snapshot-bash-1788656294993-opd0o4.sh ...`, a `-c` shell that `_is_interactive_shell` already rejects at rules.py:307-308.

**Risks**

- CONTRADICTION with the design spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-omarchy-sentinel-design.md:201 lists R-CHILD-SHELL as severity `high` with default prompt `Kill (child) / Investigate`, and §11.2 (line 286) names `curl`, `wget`, `nc` explicitly as the default kill target; the packet downgrades net-helper to `medium` and declares it 'not a kill prompt'. Per PROTOCOL.md §Status the packet is not the source of truth over the protocol, but the design spec is a separate document — this needs an explicit Chief decision and a spec edit, otherwise the packet ships a rule that disagrees with §8 and §11 of the spec.
- BLOCKING GAP: `ProcSnapshot` (rules.py:68-93) is a frozen dataclass with a hand-written `__init__` and no `starttime` field, and `_as_proc` (rules.py:248-258) drops unknown mapping keys — so R14 (parent block carries starttime) and R6 (child instance key) cannot be satisfied without either extending `ProcSnapshot` and the parent dict in rules.py, or having the daemon patch `alert.parent['starttime']` and `alert.evidence['instance']` from a pid→starttime map it kept. The packet does not say which, and the choice changes the rules.py public API.
- AMBIGUITY: the packet says net-helper alerts fire 'only when the helper's own cmdline is not covered by an allowlist', but does not say whether `net_helper_ignore` also suppresses `curl|bash` (e.g. `curl http://localhost/x | bash`) — suppressing that would create a trivial bypass, so the filter should be scoped to kind `net-helper` only, and the packet should say so.
- AMBIGUITY: 'substring match on cmdline tokens' does not state direction or case sensitivity; the only sane reading is `any(ignore in token for token in cmdline for ignore in ignore_list)`, case-sensitive, but a test should pin it.
- AMBIGUITY: the packet does not say whether the filtering/severity logic lives in `rules.evaluate_child_shell` (new keyword args) or in the daemon after the call; two implementations will pass the named tests but only one keeps `evaluate_child_shell` usable standalone (as tests/test_rules.py uses it).
- AMBIGUITY: `sample_proc` currently skips `pid == os.getpid()` before reading anything (daemon.py:660-661); the packet does not say whether the daemon's own snapshot belongs in the tree map. Excluding it breaks any ancestor chain that passes through Sentinel; including it risks Sentinel alerting on its own children. Recommend including it in `by_pid` for chaining but never emitting an alert whose child is Sentinel.
- PERFORMANCE: the packet requires `cwd` on every snapshot, so `readlink(/proc/<pid>/cwd)` now runs for ~300 processes instead of only known agents, and most of those are EACCES for other users' processes. Combined with per-pid `stat` reads this is the main threat to the 'under 30 ms' live budget and the 40 ms/400-process test; reading `cwd` only for pids that are agents or candidate children would keep the budget without changing any asserted behaviour.
- TEST INFRA: there is no `[tool.pytest.ini_options] markers` block in pyproject.toml and no marker use anywhere in tests/, so `@pytest.mark.slow` raises PytestUnknownMarkWarning and, more importantly, the acceptance command `.venv/bin/pytest -q` runs the perf test rather than deselecting it — either register the marker and add a deselect to the acceptance command, or accept a wall-clock assertion inside the graded suite.
- FLAKINESS: the 40 ms/400-process and 30 ms/live thresholds are wall-clock assertions on a shared developer machine (this box currently shows 303 processes in /proc, not the 263 of Context claim 5); on a loaded machine or cold page cache these will intermittently fail and cause spurious RETURNs.
- NOISE: because the seen registry is keyed on the child instance and short-lived helpers exit within a tick, every distinct `curl` invocation by an agent produces a new medium alert with a new pid; `net_helper_ignore` defaults (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`) do nothing for the ordinary case the packet itself cites in Context claim 4 (agents calling public HTTPS APIs). Consider whether the default should also ignore known agent API hosts, or whether net-helper should be off by default.
- UNENFORCED REQUIREMENT: 'not a kill prompt' is specified only as summary text; nothing in src/sentinel/action.py or src/sentinel/notify.py varies the offered menu by rule or severity, so a user will still be offered Kill on a medium net-helper alert. Either accept that this is documentation-only or file it as a separate packet.
- TEST IS A NO-OP GUARD: `test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash` passes today's rules logic unchanged, because `_is_interactive_shell` already returns False for any `-c` shell (rules.py:307-308) — it is a regression guard, not new behaviour, and should not be read as evidence the snapshot case was specially handled.
- CONFIG SURFACE: `packaging/config.toml` has no `[daemon]` or `[rules]` section and `tests/test_config_template.py` only asserts `notify` and `kill` are present; if the new keys are added to the template the test should be extended, and `load_config`'s current `data.get("daemon", data)` fallback means a bare top-level key in a user config is silently accepted as a daemon key — worth not repeating for `[rules]`.
- LINE-NUMBER DRIFT in the packet's Context section (claims 2, 3, 4) will make a literal fact-check read as REFUTED unless the reviewer accepts 'correct file, moved lines'; the packet should be corrected to daemon.py:644-674, rules.py:347-403, rules.py:385 and rules.py:327-328.


### W3-04 · spec:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- PERFORMANCE, correcting the tests agent and the codebase agent (both say the cwd readlink is the dominant new cost and that deferring it keeps the budget): measured on /proc with 310 numeric pid dirs, of which only 133 survive the empty-cmdline early exit. Today's read shape (cmdline+exe+comm) is 19.3 ms median / 21.9 p90. Adding a per-pid stat read takes it to 27.4 / 29.2. Adding the cwd readlink on top takes it to 29.3 / 30.9. The stat read costs +8.1 ms; the cwd readlink costs +1.9 ms. Deferring cwd buys about 2 ms and does not make the acceptance bar. The codebase agent's own numbers (19.6 -> 28.0) actually agree with mine; its conclusion from them does not.
- PERFORMANCE, correcting all four of us including my own spec report: nobody measured the packet's own 400-process perf test, and it fails as specified. I built the fake tree the packet describes (400 pid dirs on tmpfs, 40 of them kernel-thread dirs with empty cmdline, 360 readable) and timed 21 runs. Today's read shape: 29.4 ms median / 35.5 max. Plus a stat read for every pid: 45.9 / 53.8. Plus cwd: 48.6 / 58.0. The packet instructs a stat read for every pid and then asserts a 40 ms median over that exact tree. Those two sentences of the packet contradict each other. This is a hard fail, not the timing flakiness the tests agent and I both filed it as.
- PERFORMANCE, the fix none of the five of us proposed: read stat lazily for candidates and their ancestor chains only, not for every pid. evaluate_child_shell calls _agent_ancestor (rules.py:332) only for procs where _child_kind (rules.py:322) is non-None, so ppid is only ever consulted for candidate children and the pids on their chains. Pre-filling ppid for exactly that set produces byte-identical rule output. Measured live: the pass without stat is 22.2 ms over 133 snapshots, 3 candidates, 9 stat reads on the chains, 0.56 ms. Total about 22.8 ms, which is under the 30 ms bar and cheaper than today's whole sample_proc. On the 400-proc fake tree it lands near 30 ms, under the 40 ms bar. This requires amending the packet's 'one ProcSnapshot per pid (... ppid ... starttime)' and 'read stat once' wording, which is a Chief decision, not a Grok judgment call.
- BASELINE, correcting the integration agent: it reports 21.6 ms median at 307 processes for today's sample_proc. I measured 26.3 ms at 310 numeric pid dirs using the packet's own acceptance snippet verbatim. The codebase agent's 26.5 ms at 305 is the number that reproduces. This matters because integration concludes acceptance is 'marginal' while the correct reading is that today's unmodified sample_proc is already at 26.3 ms against a 30 ms bar, so the tree pass has under 4 ms of headroom before any code is written.
- LINE ANCHORS, correcting the codebase agent and the tests agent: both cite _as_proc at rules.py:228-238. It is at rules.py:248-258. The tests agent also cites ProcSnapshot at rules.py:68-88 (the decorator is at 68, `class ProcSnapshot` at 69, body through 93) and severity="high" at rules.py:384 in one place (it is 385; 138, 216 and 233 are other rules). Settled anchors for the report: sample_proc daemon.py:644-674, load_config daemon.py:221-259, _seen_key daemon.py:496-500, _prune_seen daemon.py:502-505, handle_process daemon.py:546-570, PROCESS_RULES daemon.py:61; rules.py NET_HELPER_BASENAMES 57-59, ProcSnapshot 68-93, _as_proc 248-258, _is_agent 269-270, _is_interactive_shell 304-320, _child_kind 322-329, _agent_ancestor 332-344, evaluate_child_shell 347-403, its severity="high" 385.
- SECURITY, correcting the security agent's uid filter: it asks that the pass drop any pid whose /proc/<pid> st_uid differs from getuid() before it becomes a ProcSnapshot. That drops exactly the case worth seeing, an agent spawning `sudo curl` or a setuid helper, and it adds a stat syscall per pid on a budget that measurement shows is already blown. The design spec already handles this at the right layer: section 11.5, 'Refuse any PID not owned by the user', and kill.plan_kill raises PermissionError there. Recommend no uid filter in the sampler; the alert is metadata the user should see, and the kill path already refuses.
- SECURITY, scoping the security agent's credential-redaction demand: value-level argv scrubbing before Alert.new is a real gap but the integration agent is right that W3-05 also edits rules.py and owns finding S4. Two scrubbers with different rules is worse than one, and any W3-04 test asserting an exact cmdline value will collide with W3-05. Settle the boundary now: W3-04 caps argv token count and total length in the snapshot builder (that is a denial-of-evidence fix against store.MAX_BYTES rotation, store.py:19, and belongs here), W3-05 owns redaction of credential-bearing tokens. Do not do both in this packet.
- TESTS, correcting the tests agent's proposed assertion `rows[0].evidence["source"] == "sampler"`: that key is set only in Daemon.handle_process (daemon.py:568, `alert.evidence.setdefault("source", "sampler")`), and child-shell alerts do not go through handle_process. It is a requirement on the new code, not existing behaviour, and must be stated as such in the packet or the test will fail against a correct-looking implementation.
- INTEGRATION, correcting its _prune_seen crash claim ('raises ValueError inside the sample loop and takes the daemon down on the first tick after a child dies'): I checked the parse. With the mandated key shape `R-CHILD-SHELL|11:901|` the expression int(k.split('|',2)[1].split(':',1)[0]) yields 11, and the unknown-starttime form `11:?` yields 11 as well. There is no crash on the specified shape. The real statement is narrower: any implementer who invents a different key shape breaks _prune_seen, which is an argument for pinning the key in a test, not a live daemon risk.
- MY OWN spec report, R2: I wrote that the fix is 'a combined helper' reading stat once per pid. That is necessary but it solved the wrong problem. read_ppid plus read_starttime reading the file twice would cost about +16 ms; reading it once for every pid still costs +8.1 ms and still fails the 400-process test. The requirement is fewer stat reads, not one per pid. R2 should be rewritten as: take ppid and starttime from a single _stat_fields call, and make that call only for pids on a candidate's ancestor chain.

**Settled by the squad**

- Confirmed by all five and by me in the tree: the packet's line anchors in Context 2, 3 and 4 are stale (sample_proc is at daemon.py:644 not 575, evaluate_child_shell at rules.py:347 not 346, severity at rules.py:385 not 384, _child_kind at rules.py:322-329 not 326-327). The packet must be re-anchored on symbols before Grok diffs against it, per the LEDGER's own lesson.
- Context claim 5 is REFUTED. The Chief's machine figure of 9.5 ms at 263 processes does not reproduce anywhere: 310 pid dirs and 26.3 ms today on this box. The 'under 30 ms' acceptance was derived from the stale baseline and must be re-derived, and the 40 ms / 400-process test must be re-derived with it.
- Context claim 6 is CONFIRMED live by three of us independently. Claude Code's Bash tool runs /usr/bin/bash -c 'source ~/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-*.sh ... && <cmd>'; _shell_c_argument (rules.py:283) finds the -c and _is_interactive_shell (rules.py:304) already returns False. test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash is a regression guard, not new behaviour, and must be reported as such.
- Context claim 1 is CONFIRMED: nothing in src/ imports evaluate_child_shell. /proc stat field 4 = ppid and field 22 = starttime are CONFIRMED empirically and match procinfo.py's own docstring and indices (fields[1], fields[19] after the comm split).
- ProcSnapshot (rules.py:68-93) has no starttime field and _as_proc (rules.py:248-258) drops unknown mapping keys, so the packet's 'parent block includes the agent's starttime' cannot be satisfied without either a rules.py API change or daemon-side patching of alert.parent. All five of us found this; the packet must say which, because it decides whether tests/test_rules.py can pin the behaviour.
- The alert must carry evidence['starttimes'] as a pid -> starttime dict, not a scalar. action.expected_starttimes (action.py:264-279) fans a scalar evidence['starttime'] onto every pid in alert.pids, so with pids=[parent, child] a scalar tags the agent with the child's starttime and kill.plan_kill silently drops the agent from a --session kill as recycled. Four of us found this independently; it is the single most consequential omission in the packet.
- evidence['instance'] must be instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime) or _seen_key (daemon.py:496-500) returns None and every tick re-alerts through the 60 s coalescer only. R-CHILD-SHELL is already in PROCESS_RULES (daemon.py:61), so no change is needed there.
- load_config (daemon.py:221-259) reads a hardcoded four-key tuple out of data.get('daemon', data) and has no [rules] handling at all, so both [daemon] child_shell and [rules] net_helper_ignore are inert as the packet specifies them. The kill switch the Owner would reach for is the thing most likely to ship as dead code. Both keys need a cfg default, a load_config read, a Daemon.__init__ read, and a corresponding section in packaging/config.toml (which today has only [kill] and [notify]).
- The single-pass loop must tolerate a missing or unreadable /proc/<pid>/stat and keep the snapshot with ppid 0 / starttime None. tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::_fake_proc (line 178) writes stat only when starttime is passed, and two existing sampler tests (lines 202 and 243) rely on that; `if fields is None: continue` turns them red. _fake_proc also hardcodes ppid to 1 in its format string and needs a ppid keyword parameter with default 1 so no existing caller changes.
- The net_helper_ignore allowlist must be scoped to kind == 'net-helper' only. Applying the substring filter before _child_kind would let `curl http://localhost/x | bash` suppress the high-severity curl|bash detection, which is a trivial bypass. Pin it with a test. The substring semantics as written are also weak: 'localhost' matches localhost.evil.example and '127.0.0.1' matches 127.0.0.10. The Chief must either anchor the match or accept and document the weakness.
- The allowlist must be literal substring matching on tokens already in the snapshot: no DNS, no urlparse-then-resolve, no reachability probe, and no dereferencing curl --config / -K / -d @file / --netrc. The daemon originates no network traffic and opens no auth-kind files.
- Approval fingerprints cross-suppress across kinds today. keys.approval_prefix returns the empty global prefix for non-write rules at session/24h/forever, and keys.alert_flag_set yields an empty set for a shell child, so one Approve-forever on a noisy bash -i silently allowlists every future curl|bash (same rule, same basename bash, same empty flag set). Setting evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>'] separates the three kinds in both the fingerprint and _seen_key. The security agent found this; it is real and it is a safety bug, not a nicety.
- cmd_kill computes precious = cwd_is_precious(alert.cwd) and evaluate_child_shell sets Alert.cwd from the child (rules.py:377), so a child that ran `cd /tmp` bypasses the precious-worktree confirm for a session kill that still terminates the agent working in the protected worktree. The alert must carry the parent's cwd and the precious check must consider both.
- 'net-helper is a visibility alert, not a kill prompt' is unenforceable as summary text. Nothing in action.py or notify.py varies the offered menu by rule or severity, and Panel.qml renders Kill on card.hasPids, which every child-shell alert has. Either accept it as documentation-only and say so, or file the menu change as a separate packet.
- Agent identity is derived two different ways and they disagree. daemon.process_name_candidates unions exe basename, comm, and every cmdline token basename; rules._proc_basename takes the first non-empty of exe, comm, argv[0]. I confirmed the consequence by running the rule: a node-wrapped agent (exe /usr/bin/node, comm node, cmdline node .../claude) with a `bash -i` child returns zero alerts. Grok Build as installed on this machine is exactly that shape. The rule can pass every named test and still never fire for a real agent. The Chief must decide between widening _is_agent, passing an explicit agent-pid set from the daemon, or documenting the blind spot.
- The pytest `slow` marker has no home: no [tool.pytest.ini_options] in pyproject.toml, no pytest.ini, no marker use anywhere in tests/. As written the perf test still runs under the bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` acceptance command. Registering the marker means editing pyproject.toml, outside the packet's file list.
- In-flight collisions are real and the packet's 'parallel-safe with W3-03' line is wrong. W3-03 edits daemon.py, rules.py, tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py and tests/test_rules.py, which is all four of W3-04's files. UX-02b edits daemon.py, packaging/config.toml and tests/test_config_template.py and already specifies the downstream evidence contract (evidence['chain'], evidence['matched']) for this rule, so the evidence key names should be agreed before Grok starts, not reworked after.
- Baseline is 210 tests green at 1.8 s. Nothing in src/ imports evaluate_child_shell today, tests/joint/ has no child-shell case and stays Chief-owned, and merging changes nothing on the Owner's live machine until an Owner-only plugin update plus service restart.

**Final position.** "W3-04 should not be launched as written. The requirements are extractable and the wiring is small, but the packet contains one internal contradiction that guarantees a RETURN and four gaps that would ship a rule that is unsafe or that never fires.\n\nThe contradiction is arithmetic, and I measured it rather than argued it. The packet instructs a stat read for every pid in a single full-/proc pass and then asserts a 40 ms median over a 400-process fake tree. That pass measures 48.6 ms median (58.0 max) on tmpfs here; the stat reads alone are 45.9 ms before cwd is touched. The live acceptance is equally out of reach: today's unmodified sample_proc is already 26.3 ms at 310 pid dirs against a 30 ms bar, and Context claim 5's 9.5 ms at 263 processes does not reproduce. The fix is not the cwd deferral the tests and codebase agents propose (worth about 2 ms). It is to read stat only for candidate children and their ancestor chains: evaluate_child_shell consults ppid only where _child_kind is non-None, so pre-filling ppid for that set alone gives byte-identical rule output, and it measures 22.8 ms live (3 candidates, 9 stat reads) and roughly 30 ms on the 400-proc tree. That amendment must be made in the packet before Grok starts, because it directly contradicts the packet's 'one ProcSnapshot per pid' and 'read stat once' sentences and Grok is instructed to treat the packet as its brief.\n\nThe four gaps the Chief must close in the packet text, not leave to Grok: (1) evidence['starttimes'] as a per-pid dict, or --session kill silently drops the agent as recycled; (2) whether starttime lands on ProcSnapshot or is patched daemon-side, since that decides the rules.py public API and collides with W3-03 and W3-05; (3) evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>'], or one Approve-forever on a routine curl blinds the curl|bash detection; (4) the node-wrapped-agent blind spot, which I confirmed by running the rule and which makes green acceptance compatible with zero production coverage.\n\nOn my own lane's finding, I hold the contradiction with the design spec and I have now pinned it verbatim. Spec section 8 (line 201) lists R-CHILD-SHELL as severity high with default prompt 'Kill (child) / Investigate', and section 11.2 (line 286) names curl, wget and nc explicitly as the default kill target. The packet downgrades net-helper to medium and declares it 'not a kill prompt'. That is not a clarification of the spec, it reverses it in both columns. I recommend the Chief make the call and amend the spec in the same commit as the packet, because a rule that disagrees with sections 8 and 11 of its own design document will be re-litigated at every future review.\n\nI also add one finding of my own that the noise argument sharpens past the point of a severity tweak. I ran _child_kind against a realistic Claude Code Bash argv containing the string `grep -rn 'curl https://x | bash' docs/` and it returns curl|bash, high. The matcher fires on mention, not execution, and this repository's own W3-04 packet file contains that string, so grepping these docs raises a high alert with a Kill prompt. Separately, a bare `bash` with no arguments at any depth under an agent is interactive-shell, high. Downgrading net-helper to medium addresses the least noisy of the three kinds while the two high kinds stay as written. I would ask the Chief whether _is_pipe_to_shell should require the pipe to be the command being run rather than text appearing anywhere in the -c blob, and note that fixing it is a rules.py change that collides with W3-05.\n\nMinimum before launch: re-anchor the packet on symbols, replace Context 5 with a fresh baseline, rewrite the stat instruction and both perf numbers around the lazy-chain design, add the starttimes/flags/parent-cwd requirements, name the severity-and-kill-policy decision explicitly with a spec edit, and either sequence W3-04 after W3-03 or accept a manual merge across four shared files."

### W3-04 · tests

Packet W3-04 needs 11 new tests in tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py, 5 in tests/test_rules.py, and 1 in tests/test_config_template.py, plus one signature change to the shared `_fake_proc` fixture (it currently hardcodes ppid=1 into the stat line, so no existing fixture can express a parent/child link). No existing test breaks if the implementer is careful, but two of them — `test_sampler_detects_known_basename_bypass` and `test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper` — build fake /proc entries with **no** `stat` file at all, so they fail the moment the new single-pass loop treats an unreadable `stat` as fatal. I found three design gaps the packet does not resolve: `ProcSnapshot` has no `starttime` field yet, a two-pid alert with a single `evidence["starttime"]` corrupts `action.expected_starttimes` for session-mode kills, and the substring `net_helper_ignore` match is trivially bypassed by a hostname like `localhost.evil.example`.

Baseline confirmed: 210 tests collected in this worktree.

**Findings**

- FIXTURE CHANGE (prerequisite for every tree test): `_fake_proc` at /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:178 writes its stat line as `f"{pid} ({exe_name}) S 1 1 1 0 -1 4194560 0 0 0 0 0 0 0 0 20 0 1 0 {starttime} 0 0 0"` — the first `1` after the state letter is ppid (field 4), hardcoded. Add a `ppid: int = 1` keyword parameter and interpolate it there. The default keeps all seven existing callers byte-identical. Also note the helper writes `stat` ONLY when `starttime is not None`; keep that, because two existing tests depend on the no-stat path.
- FIXTURE HELPER (new, same file): `_agent_child(proc_root, cwd, child_cmdline, *, child_exe="bash", agent_pid=10, agent_st=800, child_pid=11, child_st=901)` that calls `_fake_proc` twice — parent `claude` (cmdline `["claude"]`, no bypass flag, so it emits no R-BYPASS and the tree alert is the only row) and the child with `ppid=agent_pid`. Four of the new tests share it.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_fires_child_shell (packet-named). Fixture: `_agent_child(proc, cwd, ["bash", "-i"])`; daemon via `_daemon(tmp_path, monkeypatch, proc_root=proc, known_basenames=["claude"])`; one `d.sample_proc()`. Asserts on `rows = list(iter_alerts())`: `len(rows) == 1`; `rows[0].rule == "R-CHILD-SHELL"`; `rows[0].evidence["kind"] == "interactive-shell"`; `rows[0].evidence["child_pids"] == [11]`; `rows[0].pids == [10, 11]`; `rows[0].severity == "high"`; `rows[0].basename == "bash"`. The pid ORDER `[parent, child]` already matches `rules.py:388` (`pids=[parent.pid, proc.pid]`), so assert the list, not a set.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_child_shell_alert_carries_child_instance_and_parent (new; not in the packet, but it is the only test that proves the W3-02 seen-registry keying the packet demands). Same fixture. Asserts: `rows[0].evidence["instance"] == "11:901"` (the CHILD instance, not the agent's); `rows[0].evidence["starttime"] == 901`; `rows[0].evidence["source"] == "sampler"`; `rows[0].parent == {"pid": 10, "exe": "/usr/bin/claude", "basename": "claude", "starttime": 800}`; `{int(k): v for k, v in rows[0].evidence["starttimes"].items()} == {10: 800, 11: 901}`; and `len(d._seen) == 1 and "11:901" in next(iter(d._seen))`. The `starttimes` map is load-bearing — see the risks list.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_child_shell_alerts_once_across_ticks (new). Same fixture plus `now = [1000.0]`, `clock=lambda: now[0]`, `sampler_interval=3.0`, `notify=lambda a: toasts.append(a)`. Body: `for _ in range(50): d.maybe_sample(); now[0] += 3.0`. Asserts `len(list(iter_alerts())) == 1` and `len(toasts) == 1`. This is the child-shell twin of the existing `test_same_instance_alerts_once` (line 585) and is what actually distinguishes seen-registry wiring from the 60 s coalescer, which would otherwise let a second alert through on tick 21.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash (packet-named). Use the REAL argv shape, which I verified live on this machine (pid 813029, child of the `claude` process 115813): `["/usr/bin/bash", "-c", "source /home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-1788656294993-opd0o4.sh 2>/dev/null || true && shopt -u extglob 2>/dev/null || true && ls"]`, with `exe_target="/usr/bin/bash"`. Add a second child in the same fixture, pid 12 `["bash", "-lc", "ls"]` (clustered `-lc`), to drive the clustered-flag branch of `rules._shell_c_argument` (rules.py:293-296) through the sampler. Asserts `list(iter_alerts()) == []`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_net_helper_is_medium_and_once (packet-named). Fixture: child pid 11 `["curl", "https://api.example/v1"]`, `exe_target="/usr/bin/curl"`, `ppid=10`, `starttime=902`. Asserts: `len(rows) == 1`; `rows[0].evidence["kind"] == "net-helper"`; `rows[0].severity == "medium"`; `"visibility" in rows[0].summary.lower()` (the packet requires the summary to say it is a visibility alert, not a kill prompt — assert the substring, leave the rest of the wording free). Then a second `d.sample_proc()` and `len(list(iter_alerts())) == 1`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_net_helper_ignore_list_suppresses_localhost (new; the packet defines the config key but names no test for it). Fixture: two children of `claude` pid 10 — pid 11 `["curl", "http://127.0.0.1:8000/health"]` (st 903) and pid 12 `["curl", "https://api.example"]` (st 904); daemon config `net_helper_ignore=["localhost", "127.0.0.1"]`. Asserts exactly one alert and `rows[0].evidence["child_pids"] == [12]`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_child_shell_disabled_by_config (packet-named). Fixture = the `bash -i` one, daemon config `child_shell=False`. Assert the pass is SKIPPED, not merely filtered: `calls = []; monkeypatch.setattr("sentinel.daemon.evaluate_child_shell", lambda *a, **k: calls.append(1) or []); d.sample_proc(); assert calls == [] and list(iter_alerts()) == []`. Pair it with `test_child_shell_enabled_by_default` — the same fixture with no `child_shell` key in the config emits the alert, guarding the documented default of true.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_load_config_reads_child_shell_and_net_helper_ignore (new). `load_config` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-259) today reads only `data.get("daemon", data)` and iterates a hardcoded four-key tuple, so it can see neither `child_shell` nor a `[rules]` table. Fixture: write `cfg.toml` containing `[daemon]\nchild_shell = false\n\n[rules]\nnet_helper_ignore = ["localhost"]\n` into tmp_path; assert `load_config(cfg)["child_shell"] is False` and `== ["localhost"]`; then an empty file asserts the defaults `child_shell is True` and `net_helper_ignore == ["localhost", "127.0.0.1"]`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_child_shell_survives_missing_stat (new; this is the regression guard for the single most likely breakage). Fixture: `_fake_proc(proc, 10, "claude", ["claude", "--yolo"], cwd)` and `_fake_proc(proc, 7, "bash", ["bash", "-c", "echo hi"], cwd)` — both with `starttime=None`, i.e. NO stat file, exactly as the two existing sampler tests build them. Asserts: one R-BYPASS row for pid 10; `rows[0].evidence["starttime"] is None`; `rows[0].evidence["instance"] == "10:?"` (per `procinfo.instance_key`); no exception; no R-CHILD-SHELL row. If the implementer writes `if fields is None: continue` in the new pass, this test and two existing ones go red together.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sample_proc_400_processes_under_budget, marked `@pytest.mark.slow` (packet-named). Fixture: 400 pid dirs — 2 agents (`claude` pid 10, `codex` pid 20), 3 real children (a `bash -i`, a `curl`, a snapshot `bash -c`), ~10 kernel-thread dirs whose `cmdline` file is written EMPTY (`b""`) to exercise the early exit at daemon.py:663, and the rest ordinary `node`/`python3`/`systemd` rows with distinct starttimes. Body: one warm-up `d.sample_proc()`, then 20 timed runs with `time.perf_counter`, `times.sort()`, `median = times[10]`. Assert `median < 0.040`. The warm-up matters: the first pass pays `append_alert` plus the notify hook for 3 alerts, and the seen registry suppresses them on every subsequent pass, so only the warmed runs measure steady state.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_net_helper_is_medium and ::test_rchild_shell_interactive_is_high (new). `rules.py:384` hardcodes `severity="high"` for all three kinds. Using the existing `_agent()`/`_child()` helpers (lines 150-171): a `curl` child asserts `alerts[0].severity == "medium"`, a `bash -i` child asserts `"high"`. The existing `test_rchild_shell_detects_curl_pipe_bash` already pins `curl|bash` at high and stays green.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_net_helper_ignore_matches_cmdline_token (new). `evaluate_child_shell([_agent(), _child(cmdline=["curl", "http://127.0.0.1:9000/x"], exe="/usr/bin/curl")], net_helper_ignore=["127.0.0.1"]) == []`, and the same tree with `net_helper_ignore=["localhost"]` still returns one alert. Keep the allowlist as an explicit keyword on the rule (defaulting to the two documented entries) rather than filtering in the daemon, so the rule stays unit-testable and the daemon just forwards config.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_ignore_does_not_apply_to_shells (new). With `net_helper_ignore=["127.0.0.1"]`, a child `["bash", "-c", "curl 127.0.0.1/x | bash"]` must still produce one alert with `kind == "curl|bash"` and `severity == "high"`. The allowlist gates the `net-helper` kind only; without this test an implementer can easily apply the substring filter before `_child_kind` and silently disable pipe-to-shell detection.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_proc_snapshot_carries_starttime (new). `ProcSnapshot` (rules.py:68-88) has NO starttime field today, and its hand-written `__init__` takes positional `(pid, ppid, cmdline, exe, cwd, comm)`. Add `starttime: int | None = None` as the trailing parameter — every existing caller in tests/test_rules.py uses keyword args via the `_agent`/`_child` dicts, so nothing breaks. Assert the default is `None`, that `ProcSnapshot(pid=1, ppid=0, cmdline=["x"], starttime=55).starttime == 55`, and that the mapping path survives it: `rules._as_proc({"pid": 1, "ppid": 0, "starttime": 55}).starttime == 55` — `_as_proc` (rules.py:228-238) currently drops every key it does not name.
- tests/test_rules.py::test_rchild_shell_recognizes_node_wrapped_agent (new, and it exposes a real divergence). `rules._is_agent` matches on `_proc_basename` = exe basename, then comm, then cmdline[0]; the sampler matches on `daemon.process_name_candidates`, which unions ALL THREE plus every cmdline token basename. So a `node /usr/lib/node_modules/.bin/claude` process is an agent to the sampler and NOT an agent to the tree rule — its children are missed. Tree: parent `ProcSnapshot(pid=10, ppid=1, cmdline=["node", "/usr/lib/node_modules/.bin/claude", "--resume"], exe="/usr/bin/node", comm="node")`, child `bash -i` ppid=10; assert one alert. Note this changes `_is_agent`, which also governs the parent-skip at rules.py:378 — flag it for the Chief before implementing.
- tests/test_config_template.py::test_config_template_documents_child_shell_and_net_helper_ignore (new). /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/packaging/config.toml has `[kill]` and `[notify]` but NO `[daemon]` and no `[rules]` table. Assert the template parses with `data["daemon"]["child_shell"] is True` and `data["rules"]["net_helper_ignore"] == ["localhost", "127.0.0.1"]`, and that both equal the `load_config` defaults. The existing test at line 9 only asserts `"notify" in data and "kill" in data`, so adding sections does not break it.
- FACT-CHECK, claim 6 — CONFIRMED on this machine. `/home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/` holds 4 snapshot files, and a live Claude Code Bash call (pid 813029) has argv `["/usr/bin/bash", "-c", "source /home/tav/.claude/shell-snapshots/snapshot-bash-1788656294993-opd0o4.sh 2>/dev/null || true && shopt -u extglob ... "]` with ppid 115813, which is the `claude` process (`comm=claude`, `exe=/home/tav/.local/share/mise/installs/claude/2.1.259/claude`). It is a `-c` shell, so `rules._is_interactive_shell` (rules.py:299-315) already returns False for it — the packet's test is a regression guard, not a new behavior.
- FACT-CHECK, /proc stat field positions — CONFIRMED empirically (no man page is installed here). Field 4 is ppid: `awk '{print $4}' /proc/813029/stat` gives 115813, which is the real parent. Field 22 is starttime: the child reads 2485801 against the parent's 364027, consistent with launch order, and `procinfo.read_starttime` (index 19 of the post-comm split) already passes `tests/test_procinfo.py::test_real_proc_self_has_stable_starttime`. Reuse `procinfo._stat_fields` rather than `awk`-style whitespace splitting: it handles a comm containing spaces and parentheses via `rfind(')')`, which naive `$4`/`$22` do not.
- PERFORMANCE recommendation that changes what the tests may assert: `evaluate_child_shell` reads `proc.cwd` only to fill `cwd=` on an alert it actually emits (rules.py:395); `_agent_ancestor` never touches it. So the new pass should readlink `/proc/<pid>/cwd` lazily — only for pids that are a known agent or whose `_child_kind` is non-None — instead of for all 400. That removes the single largest new syscall cost. Consequence: the perf test must not assert that every snapshot has a populated cwd, and `test_sampler_fires_child_shell` should assert `rows[0].cwd == str(cwd)` for the alerting child only.

**Risks**

- Top breakage risk: `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_sampler_detects_known_basename_bypass` (line 202) and `::test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper` (line 243) build fake /proc entries with no `stat` file at all, because `_fake_proc` writes one only when `starttime` is passed. The new single-pass loop reads `stat` for every pid; if a failed read short-circuits with `continue`, both tests drop to zero alerts and fail. The loop must fall back to `ppid=0, starttime=None` and keep the snapshot. Test 10 in the list above is the guard.
- Two-pid alert plus a single starttime corrupts safe kill. `action.expected_starttimes` (src/sentinel/action.py:264-279) falls back to `for pid in alert.pids: out.setdefault(pid, int(single))` when `evidence["starttimes"]` is absent. With `pids == [10, 11]` and only the child's starttime recorded, a `--session` kill would compare the agent pid 10 against the CHILD's starttime, decide it was recycled, and silently drop the supervisor from the plan (kill.py:143-155). The daemon must write `evidence["starttimes"] = {10: <agent st>, 11: <child st>}`. Asserted in test 4.
- `net_helper_ignore` as documented (substring match on cmdline tokens) is trivially bypassed: `curl https://localhost.evil.example/payload` contains the substring `localhost` and is suppressed. Recommend the Chief either anchor the match to a parsed host token or accept and document the weakness; I would write the test to pin whichever behavior is chosen rather than silently encode the loose one.
- The `slow` marker is unregistered. There is no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in pyproject.toml, no pytest.ini, no setup.cfg, and no existing use of `slow` anywhere in the tree, so `@pytest.mark.slow` emits `PytestUnknownMarkWarning`. It will not fail `pytest -q` (no `-W error` is configured), but registering the marker means editing pyproject.toml, which is outside the packet's stated file list — check with the Chief before touching it.
- The 40 ms / 400-process budget is a wall-clock assertion in the default suite, since the packet's acceptance runs a bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q` with no deselect. It will be flaky under a loaded machine, coverage, or a slower disk. Mitigations to agree on up front: keep the warm-up run, use the median of 20 rather than the min or mean, and either raise the budget or gate the assertion behind an env var so a busy CI does not RETURN the packet on timing noise.
- The acceptance snippet runs `sample_proc()` against the LIVE /proc with the tree pass on by default. On the Chief's machine that tree contains the real `claude` at pid 115813 with real `bash -c` and possibly `curl` children, so the run will write genuine R-CHILD-SHELL rows into the temp XDG state dir. That is harmless (`notify` is a no-op lambda and XDG_STATE_HOME is a tempdir), but the first of the ten timed iterations pays `append_alert` for each and is not representative — report the median as the snippet already does, not the mean.
- `rules._is_agent` widening (test 16) is a behavior change beyond the packet's Changes section. It also affects the parent-skip at rules.py:378, and it could make `python3 --yolo` with `comm=claude` (pid 12 in the existing `test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper` fixture) an agent for tree purposes. That fixture asserts `pids == {11, 12, 13}` over ALL rows, so any extra alert from a widened match fails it. None of its four processes is a shell or net helper today, so it stays green — but the assertion is fragile and worth noting in the report.
- Nothing in src/ currently sets `evidence["schema"] = 2`; `investigate._legacy_limit` (src/sentinel/investigate.py:121) therefore stamps every live alert with "recorded before evidence v2". The new child-shell alert captures parent starttime and a starttimes table, which is exactly evidence v2 shape. Whether W3-04 should stamp `schema: 2` or leave it to UX-02b is an open question for the Chief; I did not write a test either way.
- `tests/joint/` is Chief-owned and off limits. Its harness (`tests/joint/test_adversarial.py`) gates on `IMPLEMENTED = {"ux-01", "ux-02a"}` and loads JSON cases, so it does not exercise the sampler and should be unaffected — but a red-team case naming a `child-shell` gate would be skipped rather than failed, which means the joint suite gives no coverage signal for this packet.
- I did not run pytest in this worktree (read-only stage); the 210 figure comes from `--collect-only -q -p no:cacheprovider`, which matches the count the packet states.


### W3-04 · tests:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CORRECTING THE TESTS LENS (myself): my recommendation that `/proc/<pid>/cwd` readlink is 'the single largest new syscall cost' and should be deferred is REFUTED by measurement. On this machine (305 pid dirs, 145 with a cmdline) I timed the new pass in stages, median of 20 runs after a warm-up: base cmdline+exe+comm = 19.8 ms; +stat read = 26.0 ms; +cwd readlink = 28.5 ms; +ProcSnapshot construction = 29.3 ms. cwd is 2.5 ms of a ~9.5 ms increase; the single stat read is 6.2 ms. Deferring cwd buys the least and complicates the loop for nothing. Withdraw that recommendation and the perf-test caveat that followed from it.
- CORRECTING codebase, integration AND spec, which all conclude the 'under 30 ms' acceptance bar is unreachable and should be rewritten: it is reachable, and I measured the lever. Full single pass (uid prefilter, cmdline, exe, comm, one stat, cwd, ProcSnapshot) plus `evaluate_child_shell` over the result = 22.9 ms median, against today's `sample_proc` at 20.6 ms median on the same machine. Without the uid prefilter the same pass is 33.2 ms and does fail the bar. So the packet's budget is not stale arithmetic; it is a design constraint that one decision (below) satisfies. Do not RETURN the packet on the perf bar before that decision is made.
- CORRECTING security, final risk bullet ('a global Approve on a noisy net-helper would take the high-severity curl|bash detection down with it'): REFUTED. `_allow_decision` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:285) computes `fingerprint(alert.rule, alert.basename, flags, prefix)` and net-helper alerts carry basename curl/wget/nc, while `curl|bash` alerts ALWAYS carry a shell basename because `_is_pipe_to_shell` (src/sentinel/rules.py:295) short-circuits on `_is_shell`. I confirmed this live: a `bash -c 'curl ... | bash'` child produces `basename == 'bash'`. The real cross-approval collapse is `bash -i` <-> `curl|bash` (both basename bash, both empty flag set), which security stated correctly earlier in its own points and integration stated correctly. The fix (`evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>']`) is right; the stated blast radius is not.
- CORRECTING integration's '+7 ms for the tree': the 28.7 ms full-pass figure omits `evaluate_child_shell` itself, which is not free. I timed it at 4.47 ms median over the 145 real snapshots and 7.74 ms over a synthetic 402-snapshot tree. The true delta over today's pass is ~12-13 ms without a uid prefilter, not 7 ms.
- CORRECTING security's uid recommendation ('drop any pid whose st_uid != getuid() before it becomes a ProcSnapshot'): correct on perf, but it carries an unstated detection cost that nobody priced. Dropping foreign pids removes the entire `agent -> sudo -> root bash -i` chain, not just the leaf, because every node above the agent boundary is root-owned. I tested the obvious compromise (keep foreign pids in the tree, skip their always-empty exe/cwd readlinks) and it recovers almost nothing: 32.7 ms vs 33.2 ms. The cost is the cmdline/comm/stat reads, not the readlinks. So this is a genuine either/or for the Chief -- 22.9 ms with a sudo-child blind spot, or 33.2 ms with full coverage and a failed acceptance bar -- not a free win.
- CORRECTING my own line anchors: `_as_proc` is at src/sentinel/rules.py:248, not 228-238; `class ProcSnapshot` starts at rules.py:69 (block runs to 93), not 68-88. Spec's anchors (rules.py:248-258, 69-93, 347-403, 385, 327-328; daemon.py:644-674) are the correct ones -- use spec's, discard mine.
- CORRECTING spec's R11, which offers 'changing rules.py:385 OR post-processing the alert in the daemon' as equivalent options: they are not. tests/test_rules.py calls `evaluate_child_shell` directly with no daemon (I read all seven tests at tests/test_rules.py:171-249), so daemon-side severity mapping and daemon-side allowlist filtering leave both behaviours unreachable from a unit test and force every assertion through the fake-/proc fixture. Settle on rules-side keyword arguments (`net_helper_ignore=`, per-kind severity internal to the rule) and let the daemon forward config. This also matches what I proposed and what codebase and integration both flagged as the unresolved choice.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline: 210 passed in 1.89s at ca0465a; I re-ran `.venv/bin/pytest -q` in this worktree. Any RETURN must be measured against 210.
- Fact-check 1 CONFIRMED: `evaluate_child_shell` is defined only at src/sentinel/rules.py:347 and imported nowhere in src/. Fact-check 6 CONFIRMED: I ran `_child_kind` on the real snapshot argv shape and it returns None, because `_shell_c_argument` finds the `-c`. The packet's `test_sampler_ignores_claude_snapshot_bash` is a regression guard, not new behaviour -- all five of us agree, and it should be described that way in the report.
- /proc/<pid>/stat: ppid is field 4, starttime is field 22, and both must come from ONE read via a new combined accessor over `procinfo._stat_fields` (which parses past the last ')' and so survives a comm containing spaces or parens). `read_ppid` and `read_starttime` each re-read the file; calling both is the wrong path. procinfo.py needs adding to the packet's Changes list.
- `ProcSnapshot` has no `starttime` field: I confirmed `ProcSnapshot(pid=1, ppid=0, cmdline=['x'], starttime=5)` raises TypeError today. The field must be added to the frozen dataclass, its hand-written `__init__`, AND to `_as_proc` (rules.py:248-258), which names six keys explicitly and silently drops everything else.
- Evidence shape is settled by three independent derivations and I verified the mechanism at src/sentinel/action.py:264-279: the alert must carry BOTH `evidence['instance'] = instance_key(child_pid, child_starttime)` (or `_seen_key` returns None and the seen registry never engages) AND `evidence['starttimes'] = {parent_pid: st, child_pid: st}`. A scalar `evidence['starttime']` alone is fanned out to every pid in `alert.pids`, so a `--session` kill would compare agent pid 10 against the child's starttime and drop the agent as recycled.
- Fixture and robustness: `_fake_proc` (tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:178) hardcodes ppid=1 into the stat line and writes `stat` ONLY when `starttime` is passed. It needs a `ppid: int = 1` parameter. Critically, `test_sampler_detects_known_basename_bypass` and `test_sampler_matches_comm_deleted_exe_and_node_wrapper` pass no starttime and therefore have NO stat file -- the new pass must fall back to ppid=0/starttime=None and keep the snapshot. `if fields is None: continue` turns two existing tests red.
- The config kill switch is dead code as specified. `load_config` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-259) copies exactly four keys from `data.get('daemon', data)` and has no `[rules]` handling at all. Both `[daemon] child_shell` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore` need a cfg default, a load_config read, and a `Daemon.__init__` read. packaging/config.toml has only `[kill]` and `[notify]`.
- Agent identity diverges between `daemon.process_name_candidates` and `rules._proc_basename`, and it is not hypothetical: I ran `evaluate_child_shell` on a node-wrapped agent (`node /usr/lib/node_modules/.bin/claude --resume`, exe=/usr/bin/node, comm=node) with a `bash -i` child and got ZERO alerts, while the same process is an agent to the sampler. Widening `_is_agent` also changes the parent-skip at rules.py:378, so it is a Chief decision, not a Grok judgment call.
- `_child_kind` fires on mere mention: I fed it a realistic snapshot-prefixed `bash -c` blob containing `grep -rn 'curl https://x | bash' docs/` and got `curl|bash` at high severity. Since this repo's own packet file contains that string, grepping the repo self-triggers. This is the packet's largest live false-positive source.
- `net_helper_ignore` must be literal substring matching on argv tokens already in the snapshot -- no DNS, no urlparse-then-resolve, no reachability probe -- and must be scoped to kind `net-helper` only, or it becomes a trivial `curl 127.0.0.1/x | bash` bypass. It is also trivially evaded by `localhost.evil.example`; pin whichever semantics the Chief picks with a near-miss test rather than encoding the loose one silently.
- Approval cross-suppression is real: `fingerprint('R-CHILD-SHELL', 'bash', frozenset(), '')` is shared by `bash -i` and `curl|bash`, since `alert_flag_set` yields an empty set for both. Set `evidence['flags'] = ['kind:<kind>']` so the kinds cannot cross-approve and `_seen_key` separates them.
- Clean today and must stay clean: no `shell=True` anywhere in src/ (verified); `summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS` excludes `cmdline` so the cloud bundle is safe; `investigate._render_child_shell` exists and is wired at investigate.py:302/420 so a live alert renders with no change; R-SELF stickiness is structurally unaffected. Do not add the child argv to the toast body or to any bundle key.
- The `slow` marker has no home -- no `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in pyproject.toml, no pytest.ini/setup.cfg, no existing `pytest.mark` use -- and the acceptance command is a bare `.venv/bin/pytest -q`, so the perf test runs inside the graded suite. Registering the marker means editing pyproject.toml, which is outside the packet's file list.
- Do not touch tests/joint/. Do not run systemctl/omarchy/sudo, write under ~/.config or ~/.local, add dependencies, or push/merge/rebase. Do not read /proc/<pid>/environ or /proc/<pid>/fd/*.

**Final position.** "The packet is implementable and its perf bar is achievable, but it needs three Chief decisions before Grok starts, and one of them is a security/coverage tradeoff nobody priced.\n\nDECISION 1 (perf, and it subsumes the whole 'rewrite the budget' thread). Measured on this machine, 305 pid dirs: today's sample_proc 20.6 ms; the new full pass + rule WITHOUT a uid prefilter 33.2 ms (fails the 30 ms bar); WITH `os.stat(pid_dir).st_uid != os.getuid(): continue` placed FIRST, before the cmdline read, 22.9 ms (passes, and is only +2.3 ms over today). The uid filter is a 10 ms saving, not a cost, because it drops 36 processes before any of their cmdline/comm/stat reads. Security asked for exactly this filter on invariant grounds; it happens to also be the only thing that makes acceptance pass. The cost is that `agent -> sudo -> root bash -i` becomes invisible, since the whole chain above the sudo boundary is foreign-owned; I verified the compromise (keep foreign pids, skip their always-empty exe/cwd readlinks) recovers only 0.5 ms and so is not worth it. Note that `kill.plan_kill` raises PermissionError for the ENTIRE plan if any pid is foreign, so a root-child alert would give the Owner a Kill button that hard-errors -- which argues for the filter. Recommend: take the uid filter, document the sudo blind spot as a known limit in the report.\n\nDECISION 2 (severity, and it is narrower than the packet frames it). A free +7 ms is available that nobody found: `rules._basename` is `Path(exe).name`, which I measured at 8.54 ms per 1600 calls against 0.40 ms for `str.rpartition('/')[2]` -- 21x -- and it is called roughly four times per process through `_proc_basename`/`_is_shell`/`_is_net_helper`. It is ~60% of `evaluate_child_shell`'s runtime (4.47 ms over 145 live snapshots, 7.74 ms over 402). One-line fix, no behaviour change, and it is what makes the 400-process/40 ms test comfortable rather than marginal. More substantively: downgrading ALL of NET_HELPER_BASENAMES to medium also downgrades `nc -l -p 4444` -- which is the fixture in the EXISTING `test_rchild_shell_detects_net_helper` -- and an nc listener is a reverse-shell primitive, not an API call. The packet's own justification for medium ('agents call HTTPS APIs constantly') applies to curl/wget only. Recommend splitting: curl/wget -> medium, nc/ncat/netcat/socat -> high. That also resolves spec's flagged contradiction with design spec sec.11.2, which names curl, wget and nc as kill targets, without a spec edit for the nc case.\n\nDECISION 3 (where the logic lives). Rules-side keyword arguments, not daemon-side post-processing -- see my correction of spec's R11. `evaluate_child_shell` must stay unit-testable because tests/test_rules.py is the only place either behaviour can be asserted cheaply.\n\nTEST PLAN, revised. Keep all 11 daemon tests and 5 rules tests I proposed, with these amendments: (a) drop my 'assert cwd is populated only for the alerting child' caveat -- cwd is now read for everyone, so `rows[0].cwd == str(cwd)` is a plain assertion; (b) split `test_rchild_shell_net_helper_is_medium` into a curl case asserting medium and an `nc -l` case asserting high, if Decision 2 goes my way; (c) add `test_sampler_skips_foreign_uid_processes` if Decision 1 goes to the uid filter, using monkeypatched `os.stat`/`os.getuid` over the fake /proc; (d) keep `test_sampler_child_shell_survives_missing_stat` as the highest-value guard in the set -- it is the one test that catches the single most likely implementation error, and two existing tests fail alongside it. Write `test_child_shell_disabled_by_config` as a monkeypatched-call-counter on `sentinel.daemon.evaluate_child_shell`, asserting the call never happens, not merely that no alert lands; the config path is the thing most likely to ship as dead code. Register `slow` in pyproject and add `-m 'not slow'` to the acceptance command, or move the perf assertion behind an env var -- a wall-clock assertion inside the graded suite will produce spurious RETURNs on a loaded box.\n\nWhat I would NOT let Grok decide alone: widening `rules._is_agent` for node-wrapped agents (changes the parent-skip and risks the pids-{11,12,13} assertion in an existing test), value-level credential redaction of child argv (overlaps W3-05 finding S4; two scrubbers with different rules is worse than one late one), and whether to stamp `evidence['schema'] = 2`. All three are report-and-escalate, not implement."

### W3-07 · codebase

W3-07 touches seven existing modules in src/sentinel (daemon, action, allowlist, store, fsutil, notify, investigate) and adds two new ones (journal.py, statewatch.py), neither of which exists anywhere in the tree today. The consumer half of this packet is already merged and tested — notify.STICKY_EVENTS, notify.is_sticky, investigate._SELF_EVENT_KINDS and SentinelModel.js all already hard-code the exact event names "foreign-write" and "alert-log-truncated", and investigate._log_lines already prints a `journalctl --user -t sentinel SENTINEL_ID=<id>` query — only the producer is missing. Baseline is 210 passed in the worktree; the packet's Context section has one factually wrong claim, four stale line ranges, and omits three Sentinel-side state writers plus a reproducible existing false-positive that the ledger design must absorb.

**Findings**

- New files: `src/sentinel/journal.py` and `src/sentinel/statewatch.py` do not exist; a repo-wide grep for `mirror_alert`, `StateLedger`, `control.sock`, `.cli-writes`, `statewatch` and `pause_max` returns nothing outside the packet itself, so every symbol the packet names is greenfield.
- Journal socket (Context 5) is CONFIRMED on this machine: an unprivileged `AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` connect+send to `/run/systemd/journal/socket` succeeded (94 bytes) and `journalctl --user -t sentinel-probe --output=json` returned the record with `SENTINEL_RULE` intact as a first-class field.
- `src/sentinel/investigate.py` `_log_lines` already emits `journalctl --user -t sentinel SENTINEL_ID={id} -o verbose`, and `tests/test_investigate.py::test_rself_foreign_write_journal_query` asserts it — so `mirror_alert` MUST set `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel` alongside `SENTINEL_ID`, or the shipped investigate output points at an empty query.
- `src/sentinel/notify.py` already defines `STICKY_EVENTS = frozenset({"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"})` with the comment "this set is code, not config", plus `is_sticky()` and `STICKY = SeverityPolicy("critical", 0, True)`; `Notifier.send` checks `is_sticky` BEFORE `_is_paused()`, so tamper alerts already bypass pause, prefs and burst. `tests/test_notify.py` covers both event names.
- `src/sentinel/investigate.py` `_SELF_EVENT_KINDS` already contains both events and `_render_self_event` renders evidence keys `event`, `path`, `size_before` and `size_after` — the packet's evidence dicts (`{"event":"foreign-write","path":...}` and `{"event":"alert-log-truncated"}`) omit `size_before`/`size_after`, which investigate will then render as blank facts.
- `Daemon.handle_write` and `Daemon._on_fs_event` each carry an independent `if path.name in OPERATIONAL_NAMES: return` guard — both must change, and `_on_fs_event` additionally short-circuits `LAUNCHES_FILENAME` to `consume_launches()` before the guard.
- `Daemon.emit` is where `mirror_alert` goes, but it runs `_allow_decision(alert, ...)` (allowlist suppression) and `Coalescer.should_emit(coalesce_key(alert))` BEFORE `store.append_alert` — the packet says "called from Daemon.emit after append_alert", which puts the append-only evidence copy behind two suppression gates.
- `Daemon.consume_launches` is the truncation precedent the packet cites: `if size < self._launches_offset: self._launches_offset = 0`. It silently resets and never alerts, and the offset is seeded in `Daemon.__init__` from `launches_path().stat().st_size`; an alerts.jsonl equivalent needs the same `__init__` seeding plus a persisted ledger.
- `store.rotate_if_needed` legitimately shrinks `alerts.jsonl` to zero via `os.replace(path, alerts.jsonl.1)`, and it runs inside `append_alert` under `_locked()` — i.e. on the daemon's own path — so a naive "alerts.jsonl shrank -> R-SELF" fires on every rotation at MAX_BYTES=5_000_000 or MAX_ROWS=5000.
- Pause surface is `action.PAUSE_FILENAME`, `pause_until_path`, `write_pause`, `read_pause_until`, `is_paused`, and `cmd_pause`; `action.cmd_status` duplicates the comparison inline (`until is not None and when < until`) rather than calling `is_paused`, so the cap must land in both places.
- `BarWidget.qml` computes `readonly property bool paused: pauseUntilMs > Date.now()` from `Model.parsePauseUntil(text())` on the raw `pause_until` file — a third, QML-side pause evaluation the Python cap cannot reach, so a 30d `pause_until` would still show "Paused" in the bar and panel (`Panel.qml` `root.paused`).
- `daemon.load_config` copies only `sampler_interval`, `coalesce_window`, `extra_bypass_flags`, `known_basenames` out of the `[daemon]` section, so a `pause_max` config key must be added there explicitly; `packaging/config.toml` has only `[kill]` and `[notify]`, and `tests/test_config_template.py` asserts `load_policy(path) == NotifyPolicy()`.
- `notify.Notifier.__init__` already exposes two unused injection seams the ledger and the cap can use without touching notify internals: `on_state_write: Callable[[Path], None]` (called as `self._on_state_write(state)` after `BurstTracker.save` writes `notify-burst.json`) and `paused: Callable[[], bool] | None`. `Daemon.__init__` constructs `Notifier(load_policy(), clock=...)` and passes neither.
- Daemon-side writes the ledger must record: `store.append_alert` (alerts.jsonl, alerts.lock, and rotation to alerts.jsonl.1), `allowlist.remove` called from `Daemon.emit` on an expired approval (allowlist.json + allowlist-session.json), `allowlist.clear_session` called from `Daemon.run` (unlinks allowlist-session.json), `refresh_watchlist` -> `scout.write_watchlist` called from `handle_overflow`/`handle_watch_lost`/`_ensure_watchlist` (watchlist.json), and `BurstTracker.save` (notify-burst.json).
- `allowlist.fingerprint` (Context 2) is confirmed: `hashlib.sha256(f"{rule}\0{basename}\0{','.join(sorted(flag_set))}\0{cwd_prefix}".encode()).hexdigest()` over entirely public alert fields, with `keys.candidate_prefixes` making the daemon test the global prefix and every parent of the location.
- `keys.WRITE_RULES` includes `R-SELF` and `keys.approval_prefix` keys write rules on the exact file path, so the new R-SELF foreign-write alerts are approvable per-file via `sentinel-action approve` — which itself writes allowlist.json.
- `rules.evaluate_write` is the existing R-SELF producer (self_paths branch, severity high, `evidence={"path": paths[0]}`); its `_is_sentinel_writer` basename check (security review S2) is never exercised because inotify supplies no writer, which is why `investigate._writer_unknown_limit` exists.
- `fsutil.write_private_atomic` writes `path.name + ".tmp"` then `os.replace`, `fsutil.open_private` uses `os.open` with `FILE_MODE = 0o600` and `ensure_private_dir` uses `DIR_MODE = 0o700`; there is no socket-creation helper, so a 0600 `control.sock` needs new code in this module's style.
- Two existing tests assert exactly the behavior W3-07 inverts and the packet does not mention them: `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_handle_write_skips_operational_state_files` (asserts no alert for foreign writes to alerts.jsonl / launches.jsonl / allowlist.json / watchlist.json) and `::test_pause_until_write_is_operational` (asserts no alert for pause_until, alerts.lock, alerts.jsonl.tmp, alerts.jsonl.1, notify-prefs.json).
- `tests/conftest.py` autouse fixture `_isolated_xdg` already redirects XDG_STATE_HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME per test, so the new `tests/test_journal.py` and `tests/test_statewatch.py` get isolation for free; `tests/joint/test_adversarial.py` has no R-SELF or tamper coverage today and is Chief-owned.

**Risks**

- Packet Context 1 is factually wrong: `pause_until` IS in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py`), and `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_until_write_is_operational` asserts it. The Changes bullet "pause_until becomes an operational name (removes the self-inflicted alert)" is already done and should be struck.
- All four cited line ranges are stale against the current tree: OPERATIONAL_NAMES is at daemon.py:45-60 (not 42-50); `fingerprint` at allowlist.py:22-30 (not 20-28); `is_paused` at action.py:96-104 (not 91-99); `consume_launches` at daemon.py:613-643 with the truncation reset around :622 (not 552-553).
- Three Sentinel processes other than `sentinel-action` write into state_dir and have no announcement path in the packet's design: `scout.write_watchlist` (watchlist.json, run as `sentinel-scout --refresh` by `track-b/systemd/sentinel-inventory.service`), `health.write_health_report` (health.json, run as `python -m sentinel.health --source timer` by `track-a/systemd/sentinel-health.service`), and `wrap_record.record_launch` (appends launches.jsonl from the `wrappers/sentinel-wrap` shim). Without a seam for each, normal operation produces a stream of R-SELF high foreign-write alerts.
- Reproduced against the current tree in a temp XDG: `Daemon.handle_write` on `allowlist.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `health.json.tmp` and `notify-prefs.json.tmp` already produces five R-SELF high alerts, because `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` lists only `alerts.jsonl.tmp` among the `.tmp` siblings while state_dir is a watched self_path and `write_private_atomic` closes the tmp file (IN_CLOSE_WRITE) before renaming. This is a live false positive today that the ledger must key on the final name and absorb, not inherit.
- `state_dir()/control.sock` and `state_dir()/.cli-writes` themselves live inside a watched self_path; unless they are exempted, creating the socket at daemon startup self-triggers R-SELF.
- Placing `mirror_alert` after `append_alert` inside `Daemon.emit` means an attacker who has already written `allowlist.json` can pre-approve the R-SELF fingerprint for that exact path (`keys.approval_prefix` keys write rules on the file) and suppress both the store row and the append-only journal copy in one move — the journal mirror arguably belongs before `_allow_decision`.
- The journal wire format needs the 64-bit little-endian length form for any field value containing a newline; `SENTINEL_SUMMARY` derives from `rules.evaluate_write`'s f-string over an attacker-influenced writer basename, and `SENTINEL_LOCATION` from a path, so the plain `FIELD=value\n` form alone can drop or corrupt fields.
