- The packet gives "the daemon raises R-SELF medium once" for an over-cap pause no home in the code: `is_paused` is reached only inside `Notifier._is_paused` during `Daemon.emit`'s own `self._notify(alert)` call, which would make the alert re-entrant with emit. `Daemon.tick`/`maybe_sample` is the only periodic hook, and the "once" latch needs `__init__` state like `self._seen`.
- The packet specifies no persistence for `StateLedger`, so after any daemon restart every operational file has no recorded `(size, mtime_ns, sha256)`; a cold start must either seed a baseline or every first write classifies as `foreign`.
- Adding `statewatch` or `journal` imports to `src/sentinel/action.py` risks an import cycle: `notify.py` does `from sentinel.action import is_paused` at module scope, and `action.py` already imports `sentinel.store`, `sentinel.investigate`, `sentinel.kill` and `sentinel.allowlist`.
- `Coalescer` (60 s default window, key `R-SELF||<path>`) means the packet's "foreign write -> one R-SELF high" assertion holds only inside that window; repeated tampering after 60 s produces a second alert, which the test should pin explicitly.


### W3-07 · integration

W3-07 is implementable but it converts a currently-silent code path into the loudest alert class Sentinel has, and it does so on a machine where the daemon, the inventory timer and the bar plugin are already live. The two blocking integration problems are that the 24 h pause cap breaks a Chief-owned joint harness case the packet is forbidden to touch (tests/joint/cases/harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json), and that at least three legitimate writers of "operational" state files (the scout timer, the health timer, the launch wrapper), plus atomic .tmp files, log rotation and every panel-driven status rewrite, are not covered by the packet's `sentinel-action`-only announcement path. UX-02b rewrites the same function (`Daemon.handle_write`) and already carries a W3-07 addendum, so W3-07 must land first.

**Findings**

- Baseline confirmed at HEAD ca0465a: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` gives 210 passed in 1.84s, and W3-07's only dependency W3-02 is merged (72031f6), so the packet is unblocked.
- Packet Context claim 1 is stale and partly refuted: `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` now lives at /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py:45-58 (not 42-50) and already contains `"pause_until"` (added by W3-02, 72031f6), so the Changes bullet "pause_until becomes an operational name" is already done and the claim as written invites the same wasted-turn failure the LEDGER records for UX-01.
- Claim 5 is confirmed on this machine: `/run/systemd/journal/socket` exists as `srw-rw-rw- root root`, so an unprivileged datagram send works and the `logger -t sentinel` fallback is dead code here that still needs a test double.
- The operational-ignore exists in two places, daemon.py:532 (`Daemon.handle_write`) and daemon.py:701 (`Daemon._on_fs_event`), so removing it must change both, and the `LAUNCHES_FILENAME` early return at daemon.py:698 must stay ahead of any ledger classification.
- inotify supplies no writer pid: `_on_fs_event` calls `self.handle_write(path)` with `writer_pid=None`, so `rules._is_sentinel_writer` (src/sentinel/rules.py:170) cannot exonerate anyone and the ledger hash is the only discriminator a foreign-write alert will ever have.
- Three legitimate non-daemon writers of operational files are outside the packet's announcement path, which only covers `sentinel-action`: `sentinel-scout --refresh` writes watchlist.json from the enabled daily timer (track-b/systemd/sentinel-inventory.timer, OnCalendar 03:30), `python -m sentinel.health --source timer` writes health.json (track-a/systemd/sentinel-health.service, src/sentinel/health.py:154), and the wrapper's `record_launch` appends launches.jsonl (src/sentinel/wrap_record.py:51).
- `fsutil.write_private_atomic` (src/sentinel/fsutil.py:44) writes `<name>.tmp` then renames, so allowlist.json.tmp, watchlist.json.tmp, notify-prefs.json.tmp, health.json.tmp and pause_until.tmp all raise their own events while only `alerts.jsonl.tmp` appears in OPERATIONAL_NAMES today.
- Sentinel's own rotation is indistinguishable from the truncation tripwire: `store.rotate_if_needed` (src/sentinel/store.py:47-63) does `os.replace(alerts.jsonl, alerts.jsonl.1)` at 5 MB or 5000 rows, after which the live log is size 0.
- Every panel action rewrites the whole alert log from another process: `store.update_alert_status` (src/sentinel/store.py:111-127) is called by approve, dismiss, investigate and kill (src/sentinel/action.py:166, 177, 189, 424), so each click needs an announcement round trip and can shrink the file.
- The tri-state `unchanged | ours | foreign` has no value for "no baseline recorded yet", which matters because the Owner's live state dir already holds an alerts.jsonl of 133 rows and an allowlist.json of 95 entries written long before any ledger existed.
- Every false positive is maximally loud: `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` (src/sentinel/notify.py:40) already contains `foreign-write` and `alert-log-truncated`, and `Notifier.send` (src/sentinel/notify.py:420-425) dispatches STICKY (urgency critical, timeout 0) before the pause, prefs and burst checks, so a wrong classification is an unclosable critical toast that `pause` cannot silence.
- Hard conflict with the Chief-owned joint harness: tests/joint/cases/harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json sets `pause_until` to 2099-01-01 and expects `toasts 1`, `urgencies ["critical"]`, `timeouts []`, so a 24 h pause cap makes `is_paused` false, lets the second high alert through under the default `SeverityPolicy("normal", 30_000, True)` (src/sentinel/notify.py:59) and fails three assertions in a file W3-07 is forbidden to edit.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:645 `test_pause_until_write_is_operational` writes pause_until, alerts.lock, alerts.jsonl.tmp, alerts.jsonl.1 and notify-prefs.json directly and asserts zero alerts, which is precisely the "foreign" pattern, so this packet must rewrite that test or the classifier must never classify lock, tmp and rotated names.
- UX-02b is the real collision: its Changes block rewrites `daemon.handle_write` (schema 2, fs_event, watch_root, watch_kind, stat, hashes) and it carries an explicit "W3-07 addendum" adding `size_before`/`size_after` to self-tamper alerts, so W3-07 should land first and its evidence key names are a contract UX-02b will consume.
- W3-03 and W3-04 also edit src/sentinel/daemon.py but in the watchlist-load and `sample_proc` regions rather than the write-classification path, so they are merge-noise not semantic conflict; W3-05 edits src/sentinel/action.py near `is_paused` (action.py:96-104), W3-06 is docs-only, and W6-01a adds src/sentinel/scan.py and is disjoint.
- Wave 5 exposure is live, not hypothetical: the plugin `tav.sentinel` is installed and running, BarWidget.qml:115 tails `~/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl`, and BarWidget.qml:36 shells out to `sentinel-action` for every action, so a daemon updated ahead of the CLI and the plugin turns every panel click into an unannounced write; W5-02 packaging must version daemon and `sentinel-action` together.
- tests/conftest.py isolates only XDG_STATE_HOME and XDG_CONFIG_HOME, so `mirror_alert`'s absolute `/run/systemd/journal/socket` (and the `logger` fallback subprocess) escapes the suite's isolation and every `Daemon.emit` test would write into the Owner's real journal unless the socket path is injectable and the autouse fixture neutralizes it.
- The mirror widens the read audience on purpose and must stay minimal: alerts.jsonl is 0600 by the S3 rationale in src/sentinel/fsutil.py, while /var/log/journal/be711d81.../user-1000.journal is 0640 root:systemd-journal, so SENTINEL_SUMMARY and SENTINEL_LOCATION move project paths and agent basenames into a wider-readable, longer-lived store and cmdline must never be added.
- `control.sock` is a new same-user IPC surface inside the state dir: any process running as the user can pre-announce the sha256 of a file it is about to write and be classified `ours`, so this closes S1 only against unaware agents, and the review's S1 item 4 (freeze approvals while a tripwire is pending, docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md) is not in the packet's Changes.
- The run loop at src/sentinel/daemon.py:750-773 selects on the inotify fd alone, so the control socket must join that same `select` rather than arrive on a reader thread, which would be the daemon's first shared-state concurrency against `_seen`, the coalescer and the ledger.
- Cost matters here: hashing after every write means re-reading alerts.jsonl on every `append_alert` (src/sentinel/store.py:66) against a file capped at 5 MB, which is quadratic across a burst on a daemon that already spent 1 min 8 s CPU on 132 alerts in 81 minutes, so size and mtime_ns must gate the sha256.
- scripts/collab/grok-run.sh allows `Bash(python*)` and `Bash(.venv/bin/*)` but neither `journalctl` nor `logger` (lines 80-90), so the claim-5 fact-check and the `journalctl --user -t sentinel` acceptance line cannot run inside the sandbox and should stay Chief- or Owner-run.
- `scout.DEFAULT_SEEDS` includes `.local/state/sentinel` (src/sentinel/scout.py:21), so `control.sock` and `.cli-writes` will be inventoried into watchlist.json and re-watched unless the new names are excluded from the scout inventory as well.

**Risks**

- A false-positive storm on the Owner's live machine is the headline risk, because a wrong `foreign` verdict produces a sticky critical toast with no timeout that `sentinel-action pause` cannot suppress; the enabled 03:30 inventory timer writing watchlist.json is the concrete nightly case.
- A feedback loop is possible once operational writes stop being ignored: emit writes alerts.jsonl and then the Notifier writes notify-burst.json (src/sentinel/notify.py:332) from inside the same call, each of which raises an inotify event that can classify as foreign and emit again.
- A stale control.sock left by an unclean daemon exit makes bind fail; the CLI then falls back to the `.cli-writes` nonce file, which the packet says the daemon consumes only at startup, so every approval for the life of that daemon is unannounced and therefore foreign.
- There is an inherent race between the CLI's post-write announcement and the daemon's inotify read: an announcement that lands after the daemon hashed the file yields an R-SELF for a legitimate Approve click.
- The daemon would hash alerts.jsonl without holding `store._locked()` (src/sentinel/store.py:26-38), so a concurrent append gives a hash of an intermediate state and a spurious foreign verdict.
- The joint harness case cannot be fixed by this packet and will go red on merge, so the Chief must decide up front: either the Chief updates harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json, or `pause_max` becomes config the case can set, or the cap ships behind a default that leaves the case passing.
- Unit tests could write into the Owner's real systemd journal and spawn `logger` subprocesses, an isolation break that today's tests/conftest.py does not catch, and the same call from inside the Grok workspace sandbox would be a write outside the worktree.
- Merging W3-07 after UX-02b would force a manual reconciliation inside `Daemon.handle_write`, and UX-02b's `size_before`/`size_after` addendum silently depends on evidence key names this packet has not frozen.
- Version skew on the live machine is unhandled: the installed daemon, the installed `sentinel-action`, and the installed plugin `tav.sentinel` update through different commands, and any daemon-first update makes the older CLI look like an attacker.
- The pause cap changes documented behavior (the module docstring at src/sentinel/notify.py:10-13, plus README and docs claims about pause), and W3-06 docs-truth is in flight, so the same sentence has to change in two packets.
- Two design points are unresolved in the packet and should be settled before Grok starts: what `check()` returns for a path with no ledger entry (first write after a daemon restart on a pre-existing state dir), and whether `mirror_alert` runs synchronously inside `emit`, where a slow or full journal socket would stall the alert path.
- Nothing in this run may run `systemctl`, `omarchy` or `sudo`, write under ~/.config, ~/.local/bin or ~/.local/state, edit tests/joint/, add a dependency, or push, merge or rebase; the acceptance line `journalctl --user -t sentinel` and any live restart of sentinel.service to pick up the change are Owner-run only, and the live daemon at ~/Work/sentinel must not be redeployed as part of the packet.


### W3-07 · security

W3-07 is the right fix for S1, but as written it adds three new attack surfaces to the daemon — a hashing ledger over Sentinel's own state, a unix control socket any same-uid process can speak to, and an external journal writer — each of which can break an existing invariant if implemented naively. The single most important finding is not in the packet at all: `Daemon.emit` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:507) consults the allowlist *before* it appends or notifies, so one attacker-written allowlist entry silences every R-SELF alert the tripwire will ever raise, which I confirmed by execution; W3-07 must exempt self-tamper alerts from `_allow_decision` or the whole packet is a no-op against a competent agent. Secondary but certain: the packet will manufacture daily sticky-critical false positives from `sentinel-scout --refresh`, `health.json`, log rotation, and the `*.tmp` files of `fsutil.write_private_atomic`, and alert fatigue on a never-expiring critical toast is itself a way to defeat the monitor.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT "no silent auto-mute of tamper alerts" is already broken and W3-07 must fix it first: `Daemon.emit` (/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/daemon.py:507-509) calls `_allow_decision(alert)` and returns before `append_alert` and `self._notify`, and `_allow_decision` tests `candidate_prefixes(location)` which begins with the global empty prefix, so writing one entry `{sha256("R-SELF\0\0\0"): {"scope":"forever",...}}` into `allowlist.json` mutes every R-SELF forever — I verified this in an isolated XDG dir: fingerprint `bfec340cf0555b2b4bac4ea4e1a7299a0c925724048d94bde515c90271c78a04`, second foreign write produced zero alerts — so `emit` must skip the allowlist entirely when `notify.is_sticky(alert)` is true (or when `alert.rule == "R-SELF"`), and `keys.approval_prefix` must refuse to key R-SELF approvals on the global prefix.
- INVARIANT "runs as the user, never root": the temptation in a tamper-evidence packet is a privileged append-only sink, so `src/sentinel/journal.py` must `sendto` `/run/systemd/journal/socket` (verified `srw-rw-rw- root root` on this machine, reachable by uid 1000) as the user and must never shell out to `sudo`, `pkexec`, `systemd-cat --namespace`, `chattr +a`, or write under `/var/log`, and `src/sentinel/statewatch.py` must place `control.sock` under `state_dir()` (0700) rather than `/run/sentinel` or `/tmp`, matching the packet's own Forbidden list.
- INVARIANT "metadata only, never file bodies or credentials" is at risk from the journal field set: `SENTINEL_SUMMARY` must be `alert.summary` only — today always `"{basename} started with {flag}"` (rules.evaluate_process) or `"write to sentinel path by {writer_base}"` (rules.evaluate_write) — and must never be composed from `alert.cmdline`, `alert.evidence`, or the matched-argv text W3-04/UX-02b add, because security review S4 records that agent CLIs accept API keys as flags and the journal is a log the user cannot delete.
- INVARIANT "metadata only" is also at risk from the ledger: if `StateLedger` computes sha256 by walking `Daemon._self_paths()` (daemon.py:409-412, i.e. `config_dir()` and `state_dir()`) it will `open()` and read `config_dir()/api_key`, the 0600 xAI credential file `summarize.resolve_api_key` (src/sentinel/summarize.py:73-88) reads, so the ledger must iterate a hard-coded closed tuple of filenames under `state_dir()` only — never a `rglob`, never `config_dir()` — and must store only `(size, mtime_ns, ino, digest)`, never any file bytes, in the alert or on disk.
- INVARIANT "auth-kind files are never opened" (UX-02b's Forbidden clause, `scout.classify_kind` returning `"auth"` for any name containing `auth`/`credentials`, e.g. the live `~/.claude/.credentials.json` at alerts.jsonl line 131) can be defeated through the new control socket: if the daemon hashes whatever `path` a datagram names, an agent can send `{"path":"/home/tav/.claude/.credentials.json"}` and make the daemon open it, so the announcement handler must `Path(p).resolve()` and then require exact membership in the closed operational-name set under `state_dir()` — a membership test, never `startswith`, never a `..`-tolerant prefix check — and note that `classify_kind` returns `"other"` for `api_key`, so the closed list, not the kind heuristic, is what protects the credential.
- INVARIANT "state files 0600 inside 0700 dirs" (fsutil.DIR_MODE/FILE_MODE, review S3): `bind()` creates an AF_UNIX socket with `0777 & ~umask` — 0755 under the daemon's inherited umask 022 — so `statewatch` must `fsutil.ensure_private_dir(state_dir())` first and then either wrap the bind in `os.umask(0o177)` or `os.chmod(sock, 0o600)` before any client can connect, and the `.cli-writes` nonce file must be created through `fsutil.open_private` (0600) rather than `Path.write_text`.
- The socket bind must not become a file-deletion primitive: an attacker who pre-creates `state_dir()/control.sock` as a symlink to `alerts.jsonl` would have a naive `unlink-then-bind` daemon delete the alert log, so the daemon must `os.lstat` the path and unlink only when `stat.S_ISSOCK(st.st_mode)`, and must treat any other file type there as evidence (R-SELF) rather than clearing it.
- An attacker who binds `control.sock` before the daemon starts either absorbs every `sentinel-action` announcement or forces the daemon's bind to fail, and in both cases every legitimate CLI write then classifies as `foreign`; the daemon must therefore raise a sticky R-SELF on bind failure instead of degrading silently, since a flood of never-expiring critical toasts is itself a way to make the Owner mute Sentinel.
- INVARIANT "argv arrays only, never shell strings": the `logger -t sentinel` fallback is the packet's only new external process and its message is attacker-influenced (basename comes from the monitored process's exe), so a summary beginning with `-` becomes option injection — the call must be `subprocess.run([shutil.which("logger"), "-t", "sentinel", "--", text], shell=False, stdin=DEVNULL, timeout=...)` or pass the text on stdin, and never `os.system`, `shell=True`, or an f-string command.
- The journal native protocol needs the binary framing, not `FIELD=value\n`: Linux filenames may legally contain `\n`, so a crafted watched path flowing into `SENTINEL_LOCATION` would let an agent forge `PRIORITY=`/`MESSAGE=` fields in the append-only evidence copy — `journal.mirror_alert` must emit `FIELD\n<le64 len><raw bytes>` for every value, or reject values containing `\n`/`\0`, and must truncate each field to a fixed bound (~2 KiB) so a long path cannot push the datagram past the socket's size limit.
- INVARIANT "no network from the daemon": `journal.py` and `statewatch.py` must use `socket.AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` exclusively and the daemon must gain no import path to `sentinel.summarize` (the only module importing `urllib`), which is worth pinning with a test asserting the daemon's transitive imports exclude `urllib`/`http`.
- Both new sockets must be non-blocking or carry `SO_SNDTIMEO`/`SO_RCVTIMEO`, because `mirror_alert` is called from `Daemon.emit` inside the `select` loop (daemon.py:749-772) and a blocking `sendto` to a backed-up journald would stall inotify draining and the sampler — exactly the failure class review S7 flagged for `notify.py`.
- INVARIANT "no silent auto-kill" (spec lines 37, 110, 341): the S1 item-4 idea of ignoring approvals "while a tripwire is pending" must only suppress approvals and raise alerts — the tripwire state must never reach `kill.plan_kill`/`kill.execute_kill`, and must not auto-`allowlist.remove()` beyond the existing expiry path already in `emit`.
- INVARIANT "sticky self-tamper alerts bypass pause and prefs" is satisfied for two of the three new alerts and silently broken for the third: `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` is exactly `{"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"}` and `notify.is_sticky` also requires `rule == "R-SELF"`, so the daemon must spell those evidence keys verbatim, but the packet's pause-cap alert (line 18) has no sticky event, so an agent that writes `notify-prefs.json` with `{"medium": false}` and a 30-day `pause_until` gets a completely silent tamper signal — add a third event (e.g. `"pause-cap-exceeded"`) to `STICKY_EVENTS` or raise it as high and sticky.
- The pause cap must fail safe and stay daemon-side: `action.parse_duration`'s `^(\d+)([smhd])$` accepts `999999999d`, which overflows `timedelta` and would raise out of `is_paused` into `Notifier.send`, so `pause_max` must be clamped before the `timedelta` is built and every parse failure must return "not paused" (fail open to notifying); and the one-shot R-SELF medium must be emitted by the daemon through `Daemon.emit`/`Coalescer`, never by `sentinel-action`, since a CLI that appends to `alerts.jsonl` is both a new foreign-write source and a log-flooding primitive.
- Three legitimate non-daemon writers will be classified `foreign` unless the packet handles them: `sentinel-scout --refresh` writes `watchlist.json` from `sentinel-inventory.service` (track-b/systemd/sentinel-inventory.service, OnCalendar 03:30, Persistent=true) every night; `health.write_health_report` writes `health.json` from its own timer context; and `Daemon.__init__` (daemon.py:381-387) constructs `Notifier(load_policy(), clock=...)` without passing the `on_state_write` hook that notify.py:376 already exposes, so the daemon's own `notify-burst.json` writes are unrecorded — all three must go through the ledger or the control socket.
- `store.rotate_if_needed` (src/sentinel/store.py:46-63) does `os.replace(alerts.jsonl, alerts.jsonl.1)` at 5 MB, after which `alerts.jsonl` legitimately restarts at 0 bytes, so a naive "size shrank → alert-log-truncated" check fires a sticky critical false alarm on every rotation; the check must compare `(st_ino, st_size)` against the ledger and treat an inode change accompanied by an `alerts.jsonl.1` of the recorded size as rotation.
- `fsutil.write_private_atomic` creates `<name>.tmp` beside every state file but only `alerts.jsonl.tmp` is in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (daemon.py:45-58), so `allowlist.json.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `health.json.tmp`, `notify-burst.json.tmp` and `pause_until.tmp` already fall through to `evaluate_write` and each produced an R-SELF high in my isolated run; W3-07 must cover them by suffix rule through the ledger, and must not let them become the new sticky criticals.
- Ordering is a correctness requirement, not a detail: the daemon must record its own digest into the ledger *before* the corresponding inotify event can be drained (inside `store._locked()` for `append_alert`, and before returning from any `write_private_atomic` call site), and `sentinel-action` must announce inside the same lock, or Sentinel will alert on its own writes under load.
- Fact-check corrections for the packet's Context: the line numbers are stale (`OPERATIONAL_NAMES` is daemon.py:45-58, `fingerprint` is allowlist.py:22-30, `is_paused` is action.py:96-104, `consume_launches` is daemon.py:613-637) and claim 1 is wrong that `pause_until` "is not even listed" — it is already in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` and pinned by `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:645-655`, so packet line 19 is a no-op; claim 5 is confirmed on this machine (the journal socket is world-writable and `logger` is at /usr/bin/logger).

**Risks**

- The journal mirror widens who can read alert metadata: on this machine `/var/log/journal/<id>/user-1000.journal` is `root:systemd-journal 0640` with POSIX ACLs granting `wheel` and `adm` read (the user is in `wheel`), so anything mirrored is readable by more principals than the 0600 `alerts.jsonl` that review S3 deliberately created — acceptable only if the field set stays at the five named fields and never grows to `cmdline` or `evidence`.
- journald applies per-service rate limiting (RateLimitIntervalSec/RateLimitBurst) and vacuums under SystemMaxUse, so the mirror is best-effort evidence rather than a guaranteed complete ledger; the packet and the eventual threat-model doc (W5-05) should say so instead of letting "append-only" imply "complete", and an agent that deliberately floods alerts could push older mirrored records out.
- The control socket is same-uid by construction, so any process the agent controls can announce a write as `ours` and defeat the tripwire directly — this is inside the packet's stated non-goal, but it means the honest ceiling of W3-07 is "catches unsophisticated tampering and accidents", and the report must not claim more.
- The `.cli-writes` nonce fallback is trusted on the word of a file any process can forge while the daemon is down, letting an attacker pre-write nonces for their own tampering before the daemon starts; bound it by requiring each nonce to carry the digest it claims and honouring it only when the file's current digest still matches, size-capping the file, and truncating it atomically after consumption.
- A ledger held only in daemon memory loses its baseline on every restart, and neither choice is free — treating "no baseline" as `foreign` gives an alert storm at every `systemctl --user restart`, treating it as `ours` gives a blind window; the packet should choose "re-baseline silently at startup" and record the blind window as a known limit, and must not persist the ledger to a state file an attacker can rewrite alongside the file it is meant to protect.
- `AF_UNIX` `sun_path` is 108 bytes, so a long `XDG_STATE_HOME` (the acceptance command uses `mktemp -d`, and tests use `tmp_path`) can make the bind fail with ENAMETOOLONG; the daemon must catch `OSError` and fall back to the nonce path rather than crash, and the tests must cover a long-path state dir.
- Draining the control socket must be bounded per loop iteration and capped at a small `recv` size, or an agent can replace review S5's `mkdir`/`rmdir` thrash with a datagram flood that starves the sampler; the socket fd also has to be added to the `select` set in both branches of `Daemon.run` (daemon.py:753-766), since with `inotify = false` in config there is no `select` on any fd today and announcements would never be read.
- Unresolved for the Chief: whether the pause-cap alert joins `STICKY_EVENTS` (I recommend yes) and whether `Daemon.emit` bypassing the allowlist for all R-SELF, or only for sticky R-SELF, is the right scope — the narrower choice leaves `IN_DELETE_SELF`/`IN_MOVE_SELF` R-SELF alerts approvable, which is desirable for noise but keeps a small mute surface.


### W3-07 · spec

Packet W3-07 asks for three things: a journald mirror of every appended alert (new `src/sentinel/journal.py`), a `StateLedger` tripwire that classifies writes to Sentinel's own state files as `unchanged | ours | foreign` (new `src/sentinel/statewatch.py`) with a control socket for `sentinel-action` to announce its writes, and a cap on `pause_until`. I extracted 30 testable requirements and the three acceptance commands verbatim. Context claim 1 is factually refuted (`pause_until` IS already in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` since W3-02) and all four line references are wrong; more seriously, removing the `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` early-return without exempting `sentinel-wrap`, `sentinel-scout` and `sentinel.health` turns every agent launch into an R-SELF high, and the packet's own journal field list cannot satisfy its own `journalctl -t sentinel` acceptance command.

**Findings**

- ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 1 (verbatim): `.venv/bin/pytest -q`
- ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 2 (verbatim): `XDG_STATE_HOME=$(mktemp -d) .venv/bin/python -c "..." # simulate foreign allowlist write while a Daemon instance runs handle_write; expect R-SELF`
- ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 3 (verbatim): `journalctl --user -t sentinel -n 1 --output=json | jq .SENTINEL_RULE   # after a real emit in a temp XDG (Owner-run)`
- R1 — A new module `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/journal.py` exposes `mirror_alert(alert)` that writes exactly one structured journal record per call.
- R2 — The mirrored record carries exactly the fields `SENTINEL_RULE`, `SENTINEL_ID`, `SENTINEL_SEVERITY`, `SENTINEL_SUMMARY`, `SENTINEL_LOCATION`, sourced from `Alert.rule`, `Alert.id`, `Alert.severity`, `Alert.summary` (`src/sentinel/models.py:14-29`) and `sentinel.keys.alert_location(alert)` (`src/sentinel/keys.py:20-26`).
- R3 — Transport is a stdlib `socket.AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` `sendto` against `/run/systemd/journal/socket` in the native journal wire format (newline-separated `KEY=value`, 8-byte little-endian length framing for any value containing a newline), adding no runtime dependency.
- R4 — When the journal socket is absent or the send fails, `mirror_alert` falls back to invoking `logger -t sentinel`.
- R5 — `mirror_alert` never raises and never blocks: every `OSError`, `socket.error` and subprocess failure is swallowed so alert delivery continues.
- R6 — `Daemon.emit` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:503-519`) calls `mirror_alert(alert)` immediately after `append_alert(alert)`, so exactly the alerts that were actually appended (not allowlist-suppressed, not `_seen`-suppressed, not coalesced) are mirrored — one journal record per `alerts.jsonl` line.
- R7 — No data beyond the five listed fields enters the journal: no `cmdline`, no `evidence` dict, no `hashes`, no file bodies (packet Forbidden; PROTOCOL.md security invariant 2).
- R8 — The socket path and the `logger` runner are injectable so `tests/test_journal.py` can bind a fake datagram socket under `tmp_path` and exercise the fallback without writing to the live journal or spawning a real `logger`.
- R9 — A new module `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/statewatch.py` defines `StateLedger`, which records `(size, mtime_ns, sha256)` for a path after each write Sentinel makes.
- R10 — `StateLedger.check(path)` returns exactly one of the string values `unchanged`, `ours`, `foreign`.
- R11 — The daemon updates the ledger on every write it makes itself: `store.append_alert` and `store.rotate_if_needed` (`src/sentinel/store.py:63-71`), the `alerts.lock` open/close (`src/sentinel/store.py:29-37`), `scout.write_watchlist` via `daemon.refresh_watchlist` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:215-217`), and the burst-state write in `src/sentinel/notify.py:332`.
- R12 — `sentinel-action` announces each write it makes by sending `{"path":..., "sha256":...}` to `state_dir()/control.sock` both before and after writing — covering `write_pause` (`src/sentinel/action.py:73`), `allowlist._save_file` (`src/sentinel/allowlist.py:58`), `store.update_alert_status` (`src/sentinel/store.py:127`) and the prefs write (`src/sentinel/notify.py:167`).
- R13 — The daemon creates and owns `state_dir()/control.sock` as an `AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` socket and chmods it to 0600 after bind (umask alone will not guarantee the mode).
- R14 — When the control socket cannot be reached, the CLI instead writes a nonce file `state_dir()/.cli-writes`, which the daemon consumes at startup.
- R15 — The daemon must drain pending control-socket announcements before classifying an inotify event; today `Daemon.run` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:750-762`) selects only on the inotify fd, so the control socket fd has to be added to that `select.select` set.
- R16 — Both `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` early-returns are removed and replaced by ledger classification: `Daemon.handle_write` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:531-533`) and `Daemon._on_fs_event` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:701-702`).
- R17 — A `foreign` classification emits one R-SELF alert of severity `high` with `evidence={"event": "foreign-write", "path": <path>}`; that event name is already in `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` (`src/sentinel/notify.py:40`), so the toast is critical, untimed and ignores pause.
- R18 — `alerts.jsonl` shrinking below the ledger's recorded size emits one R-SELF `high` with `evidence` event `alert-log-truncated`, mirroring the size check `consume_launches` already does for `launches.jsonl` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:621-622`).
- R19 — Classifications of `ours` and `unchanged` emit no alert at all.
- R20 — Legitimate rotation, `os.replace(alerts.jsonl, alerts.jsonl.1)` at `src/sentinel/store.py:63`, is recorded as ours and must not trip `alert-log-truncated`.
- R21 — `action.is_paused()` (`src/sentinel/action.py:96-104`) returns `False` when `pause_until` is more than `pause_max` in the future, instead of honouring any future timestamp.
- R22 — `pause_max` is a configuration value with a default of 24 hours.
- R23 — The daemon emits R-SELF severity `medium` exactly once when it observes an over-cap `pause_until`.
- R24 — `pause_until` is classified through the ledger like every other state file (it is already in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` at `src/sentinel/daemon.py:53`, so the packet's 'becomes an operational name' clause is already satisfied and is a no-op).
- R25 — `tests/test_journal.py` asserts the mirror writes to a fake socket path and that the `logger` fallback path is used when the socket is absent.
- R26 — `tests/test_statewatch.py` asserts `ours` vs `foreign` classification, CLI announcement via the socket, and the `.cli-writes` nonce fallback.
- R27 — `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py` gains four cases: foreign write to `allowlist.json` → exactly one R-SELF high; truncated `alerts.jsonl` → R-SELF; a CLI-announced write → no alert; a 30-day pause → `is_paused()` False and exactly one R-SELF medium.
- R28 — Every new test sets `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to temp paths (AGENTS.md engineering standard) and none of them touches the live journal, the live home, or `tests/joint/` (Chief-owned).
- R29 — The existing test `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_until_write_is_operational` (line 645) asserts `list(iter_alerts()) == []` after writing five operational-named files and must be rewritten, because R16 changes exactly that behaviour.
- R30 — The full suite stays green; baseline is 210 collected tests, confirmed by `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --collect-only` in this worktree.

**Risks**

- REFUTED, Context claim 1: `pause_until` IS already listed in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:53`), added by W3-02 along with `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_until_write_is_operational`. The claim's clause 'pause_until is not even listed' and the Changes bullet 'pause_until becomes an operational name' are both stale; W3-07 is describing the pre-W3-02 tree.
- All four line references are wrong: `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` is at daemon.py:45-60 (not 42-50), `fingerprint()` at allowlist.py:22-30 (not 20-28), `is_paused()` at action.py:96-104 (not 91-99), and `consume_launches()` at daemon.py:613-643 with the truncation check at 621-622 (not 552-553).
- BLOCKING GAP: three Sentinel writers other than the daemon and `sentinel-action` write into `state_dir()` and have no announcement path — `wrappers/sentinel-wrap` appends `launches.jsonl` on every agent launch, `sentinel-scout --refresh` writes `watchlist.json` (track-b/systemd/sentinel-inventory.service:12), and `python -m sentinel.health` writes `health.json` (track-a/systemd/sentinel-health.service:8). Once R16 removes the early return, each becomes a `foreign` R-SELF high, so every wrapped agent launch raises a sticky critical tamper toast. The packet must either extend announcement to these three or state an explicit exemption.
- `fsutil.write_private_atomic` (`src/sentinel/fsutil.py:47-56`) writes `<name>.tmp` then `os.replace`, so every state write fires IN_CLOSE_WRITE on `allowlist.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp`, `health.json.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `notify-prefs.json.tmp` plus IN_MOVED_TO on the final name. Only `alerts.jsonl.tmp` is in `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` today; the packet never says how the ledger keys tmp sidecars, so they will classify as foreign.
- `alerts.lock` is opened `O_RDWR|O_CREAT` and closed on every `append_alert` (`src/sentinel/store.py:29-37`), producing an IN_CLOSE_WRITE per alert on a zero-byte file whose sha256 never changes; unless `check()` maps 'content identical to ledger' to `unchanged`, every alert emission triggers a second alert.
- `control.sock` and `.cli-writes` are new files created inside the watched `state_dir()` and generate their own inotify events; the packet does not say how they are classified, and a self-referential foreign-write on `control.sock` would be an infinite tamper loop.
- AMBIGUOUS: `check()` has only three return values with no `unknown`, but the ledger as described is in-memory. Every path is unrecorded at daemon start. If unknown maps to `foreign`, each restart storms; if it maps to `unchanged`, an agent tampers freely in the pre-start window. The packet must state the startup seeding rule (hash existing state files at start) — and if the ledger is persisted to survive restarts, that ledger file is itself agent-writable state needing its own handling.
- AMBIGUOUS: what `sha256` the 'before' announcement carries is undefined — the current on-disk content, the intended new content, or nothing when the file does not yet exist. `ours` vs `unchanged` is likewise never defined in terms of the announcement.
- DESIGN LIMIT worth stating publicly: `control.sock` at mode 0600 is writable by the same uid as the agent being watched, so any local agent can announce its own write and be classified `ours`. This is consistent with Non-goal 1 (same-user tampering is out of scope) but means `foreign` is evidence, not a control, and the report and user docs should say so rather than implying prevention.
- CONTRADICTION: `logger(1)` cannot set structured journal fields, so on the fallback path `journalctl --user -t sentinel -n 1 --output=json | jq .SENTINEL_RULE` returns `null`. Acceptance command 3 is only satisfiable on the native-socket path; either scope it explicitly or require the fallback to encode the five fields into the message text.
- CONTRADICTION: the Changes field list omits `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel`, `MESSAGE=` and `PRIORITY=`. `journalctl -t` matches on `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER`, so acceptance command 3 cannot match any record written with only the five `SENTINEL_*` fields, and a record with no `MESSAGE` renders as nothing in `journalctl`. These three fields must be added to the required record.
- UNSPECIFIED: where the pause-cap R-SELF medium is raised. `is_paused()` lives in `action.py` and is called from `notify.Notifier._is_paused` (`src/sentinel/notify.py:400-401`) inside the daemon; returning `False` carries no reason, so the daemon gets no signal. This needs either a second function returning a reason or a daemon-side `read_pause_until()` check, plus a definition of 'once' — the 60s `Coalescer` window is not 'once', and `_seen` only covers `PROCESS_RULES`.
- UNSPECIFIED: `pause_max` config plumbing. `daemon.load_config` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:221-258`) forwards only four keys from `[daemon]`, and `action.is_paused()` reads no config at all (`load_precious_worktrees` reads `[kill]` directly). The section, key name and unit (seconds vs a `24h` duration string) are all undefined, and `packaging/config.toml` plus `tests/test_config_template.py` need the new key or the template test drifts.
- GAP: the cap is read-time only, so `sentinel-action pause 30d` still writes a 30-day `pause_until` and `cmd_status` (`src/sentinel/action.py:236-248`) still prints the machine as paused for 30 days while the daemon ignores it — the CLI will report a state that is not in effect.
- Acceptance command 2 is literally `.venv/bin/python -c "..."` with the script elided, so it is not reproducible as written and Chief and Grok can verify different things; the packet needs the actual one-liner.
- Acceptance command 3 is marked Owner-run and needs a live `--user` journal, so it cannot pass inside a sandboxed packet run and must be recorded as deferred-to-Owner in the report rather than claimed.
- Fact-check 5 is not fully verifiable read-only. Evidence I gathered: `/run/systemd/journal/socket` exists as `srw-rw-rw- 1 root root`, world-writable, and both `/usr/bin/logger` and `/usr/bin/systemd-cat` are present — strong but not conclusive. Proving an unprivileged datagram is accepted requires actually sending one, which writes a record to the live journal; I did not do that.
- UNCONSIDERED HOLE: `Daemon.emit` runs `_allow_decision` before appending (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:503-504`), and `keys.approval_prefix` keys `WRITE_RULES` (which includes R-SELF) on the exact file path (`src/sentinel/keys.py:63-70`). A user who clicks Approve `forever` on one `foreign-write` alert for `allowlist.json` permanently disables that exact tripwire. The packet does not say whether tamper alerts should be exempt from the allowlist the way `is_sticky` exempts them from pause.


### W3-07 · spec:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- SECURITY is wrong on the fix location for its own headline finding. It says "keys.approval_prefix must refuse to key R-SELF approvals on the global prefix." I read src/sentinel/keys.py:57-74: approval_prefix already returns the exact file location for every rule in WRITE_RULES (which contains R-SELF), for all four scopes. I executed it — session/24h/forever/this-repo all return the alert's file path, never GLOBAL_PREFIX. Changing approval_prefix is a no-op. The hole is entirely one-sided, in daemon._allow_decision (daemon.py:284), which iterates keys.candidate_prefixes(location) whose first element is GLOBAL_PREFIX = "". sentinel-action can never write the muting entry; only a direct write to allowlist.json can. The fix must be in _allow_decision/candidate_prefixes (do not test the global prefix for WRITE_RULES) and/or in Daemon.emit (skip _allow_decision when notify.is_sticky(alert)).
- SECURITY understates the blast radius of that same finding, and the understatement changes the priority. It describes the entry as muting "every R-SELF forever," then illustrates with one path. I confirmed it is worse and confirmed the exact fingerprint it cites: with XDG_STATE_HOME in a temp dir, evaluate_write on a self path returns basename='' and no flags, so fingerprint("R-SELF", "", frozenset(), "") = bfec340cf0555b2b4bac4ea4e1a7299a0c925724048d94bde515c90271c78a04 for EVERY self path. One entry returned ('allowed', bfec340c...) for both allowlist.json and alerts.jsonl. It is a single constant, path-independent, computable offline with no knowledge of the victim's paths — an attacker does not need to see an alert first. This is the one item that can make the whole packet a no-op, and it belongs in the packet's Changes block, not in a risk list.
- SECURITY's rotation heuristic is unsound. It proposes treating an inode change on alerts.jsonl plus an alerts.jsonl.1 of the recorded size as rotation. store.update_alert_status (src/sentinel/store.py:111-127) calls write_private_atomic on alerts.jsonl, i.e. tmp + os.replace, so it ALSO changes the inode — and action.py calls it on every approve, dismiss, investigate and kill from the panel. An inode-change heuristic therefore either misses real truncation or fires on every panel click. Rotation and status rewrites must be recognised because the daemon or the CLI RECORDED them (ledger update inside store._locked(), announcement for the CLI), never inferred from stat.
- INTEGRATION is half wrong that "control.sock and .cli-writes will be inventoried into watchlist.json unless the new names are excluded from the scout inventory." scout.scout (src/sentinel/scout.py:67) collects `[p for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file()]`. I bound a real AF_UNIX socket and checked: Path.is_file() is False for a socket, True for the nonce file. So control.sock is filtered out for free; only .cli-writes needs excluding. The related real finding INTEGRATION missed: DEFAULT_SEEDS already rglobs `.config/sentinel` (scout.py:20), which is where summarize.resolve_api_key reads the 0600 `config_dir()/api_key` (summarize.py:78) — a pre-existing scout exposure, out of scope for W3-07, but it is the reason SECURITY's rule "the ledger iterates a closed tuple under state_dir() only, never rglob, never config_dir()" must be honoured rather than copied from scout's pattern.
- TESTS, CODEBASE, SECURITY and INTEGRATION all enumerate the .tmp false positive incompletely, each listing four or five names. I enumerated write_private_atomic's callers (scout.py:86, notify.py:167, notify.py:332, action.py:73, store.py:127, allowlist.py:58, health.py:163) and executed handle_write's classifier against each sibling. There are SIX tmp siblings plus two new files, and all eight raise R-SELF high today: allowlist.json.tmp, allowlist-session.json.tmp, watchlist.json.tmp, pause_until.tmp, health.json.tmp, notify-prefs.json.tmp, notify-burst.json.tmp, control.sock, .cli-writes. `allowlist-session.json.tmp` appears in no agent's list — allowlist._save_file (allowlist.py:57-58) is called for both the persisted and the session file, and clear_session runs on every daemon start. The ignore rule must be the `.tmp` suffix, not an enumerated set, or the next write_private_atomic caller reopens the hole.
- MY OWN R2 is wrong and TESTS is right to contradict it. I specified the journal record as "exactly the fields SENTINEL_RULE, SENTINEL_ID, SENTINEL_SEVERITY, SENTINEL_SUMMARY, SENTINEL_LOCATION." That record cannot satisfy the packet's own acceptance command: journalctl -t matches SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER, and a record with no MESSAGE renders as nothing. It also breaks a shipped contract — investigate._log_lines (investigate.py:463) emits `journalctl --user -t sentinel SENTINEL_ID=<id> -o verbose` and tests/test_investigate.py:401 asserts it. The record is EIGHT fields: MESSAGE, PRIORITY, SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel, plus the five SENTINEL_*. Read my R7 as "nothing beyond these eight," not five.
- MY OWN R3 is too weak on the wire format; SECURITY is right. I wrote that 8-byte little-endian framing is needed "for any value containing a newline." Since SENTINEL_SUMMARY is an f-string over an attacker-influenced writer basename (rules.py:217) and SENTINEL_LOCATION is a path, and Linux filenames may legally contain \n, a per-value decision is itself the injection surface. Length-frame every value unconditionally, or reject \n and \0 outright, and bound each field.
- MY OWN R11 is wrong to list alerts.lock as something the ledger records. store._locked (store.py:26-38) opens it O_RDWR|O_CREAT and closes it on every append_alert and every update_alert_status; it is zero-byte and its sha256 never changes. Recording it means a ledger write per alert for no signal. It belongs in the ignore set, which is where TESTS put it.
- CODEBASE's import-cycle warning is overstated but its conclusion should still be adopted. daemon.py's imports (daemon.py:18-27) do not include sentinel.action, so action -> statewatch -> daemon does not actually close a cycle today. The real constraint is narrower: if statewatch imports OPERATIONAL_NAMES from daemon, then action.py (already importing allowlist, investigate, kill, store) pulls the entire daemon module in on every CLI invocation, and notify.py:28's module-scope `from sentinel.action import is_paused` makes that graph fragile to the next edit. Keep the closed operational-name set and the ignore rule in statewatch.py itself and have daemon.py import from statewatch, not the reverse.
- A correction to my own earlier reading, for the record, so nobody repeats it: TESTS' citation of `_daemon(tmp_path, monkeypatch, self_paths=[...], watch_paths=[])` is valid. Daemon.__init__ (daemon.py:375) takes a single config Mapping and rejects self_paths as a direct keyword, but the helper at tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py:32-43 funnels **cfg into that mapping and daemon.py:407 reads cfg["self_paths"]. TESTS is right; the constructor signature is not a blocker for the new cases.

**Settled by the squad**

- Baseline is 210 passed, at HEAD ca0465a. Confirmed by `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --collect-only` in this worktree. Any report claiming a different starting number is measuring a different tree.
- Packet Context claim 1 is factually refuted and the Changes bullet "pause_until becomes an operational name" is a no-op. `pause_until` is at src/sentinel/daemon.py:53 inside OPERATIONAL_NAMES (frozenset spanning 45-60), added by W3-02, and pinned by tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_until_write_is_operational. All five of us reached this independently; strike the bullet before Grok launches.
- All four of the packet's line citations are stale, verbatim as follows: OPERATIONAL_NAMES is daemon.py:45-60 (not 42-50); allowlist.fingerprint is allowlist.py:22-30 (not 20-28); action.is_paused is action.py:96-104 (not 91-99); daemon.consume_launches is daemon.py:613-643 with the truncation reset at 621-622 (not 552-553). Rewrite them in the packet rather than letting Grok burn a turn rediscovering this.
- BLOCKER 1 — the allowlist mutes the tripwire. Daemon.emit (daemon.py:507-509) runs _allow_decision before append_alert and before _notify, and candidate_prefixes tests the empty global prefix first. Verified by execution: one crafted allowlist.json entry under the constant fingerprint bfec340cf0555b2b4bac4ea4e1a7299a0c925724048d94bde515c90271c78a04 silences R-SELF for every path, forever. W3-07 must fix this or it ships nothing. This is SECURITY's find and it outranks everything else in the packet.
- BLOCKER 2 — the Chief-owned joint case breaks, and the packet may not touch it. tests/joint/cases/harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json sets state.pause_until to 2099-01-01 and expects toasts 1 / urgencies ["critical"] / timeouts []. The harness at tests/joint/test_adversarial.py:98 builds Notifier(load_policy(...), runner=recorder, clock=...) with no `paused=` injection, so notify.py:401 falls through to the real action.is_paused(). I executed the cap: uncapped is_paused() is True, capped it is False — so the second ordinary high alert toasts under SeverityPolicy("normal", 30000, True) and three assertions fail. The Chief must settle this BEFORE launch (rewrite the case, make pause_max config the case can set, or ship the cap defaulted off); it cannot be resolved inside the packet without crossing an ownership line.
- BLOCKER 3 — three legitimate non-daemon writers have no announcement path, and the packet's design only covers sentinel-action: wrappers/sentinel-wrap via wrap_record.record_launch (launches.jsonl, every agent launch), sentinel-scout --refresh via scout.write_watchlist (watchlist.json, from the enabled 03:30 inventory timer), and python -m sentinel.health via health.write_health_report (health.json, from its timer). Once the OPERATIONAL_NAMES early-return goes, each becomes a sticky critical R-SELF that pause cannot silence. The packet must extend announcement to all three or state an explicit exemption.
- The .tmp problem is a live pre-existing bug, not new work W3-07 creates. Reproduced against HEAD in a temp XDG: eight names under state_dir() already produce R-SELF high through evaluate_write because OPERATIONAL_NAMES lists only alerts.jsonl.tmp among the siblings. The ledger must key on the final name and ignore by `.tmp` SUFFIX. This is a regression fix the packet inherits, and its test is a fix, not just coverage.
- The consumer half is already merged and its names are a frozen contract. notify.STICKY_EVENTS is exactly {"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"} (notify.py:40); notify.is_sticky additionally requires rule == "R-SELF" (notify.py:78-80); Notifier.send dispatches STICKY before the pause check (notify.py:421-425); investigate._SELF_EVENT_KINDS holds both names (investigate.py:57-59) and _render_self_event renders evidence keys event, path, size_before, size_after (investigate.py:278-281). The daemon must spell those two event strings verbatim and populate size_before/size_after, or shipped UI renders blank facts.
- The journal record is eight fields and SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel is mandatory, not optional. It is required by the packet's own acceptance command and by the already-shipped investigate._log_lines query (investigate.py:463, asserted at tests/test_investigate.py:401). The `logger` fallback must be `logger --journald` reading NAME=value lines on stdin (confirmed present on this machine, `--journald[=<file>]  write journald entry`), because plain `logger -t` carries no structured fields and would make the acceptance command print null. Never build that call as a shell string — the message is attacker-influenced.
- Context claim 5 is confirmed: /run/systemd/journal/socket is srw-rw-rw- root root, and both /usr/bin/logger and /usr/bin/systemd-cat exist. CODEBASE went further and got a real record back with SENTINEL_RULE intact as a first-class field. I did not repeat that probe — it writes to the Owner's live journal.
- Two acceptance commands cannot pass inside a sandboxed packet run and must be recorded as deferred-to-Owner rather than claimed: command 3 needs a live --user journal, and scripts/collab/grok-run.sh allows neither journalctl nor logger. Command 2 is literally `.venv/bin/python -c "..."` with the script elided and is not reproducible as written — the packet needs the actual one-liner or Chief and Grok will verify different things.
- The socket needs an explicit chmod; umask is not enough. I bound a real AF_UNIX socket and measured mode 0755 under the default umask. SECURITY is right: ensure_private_dir(state_dir()) first, then chmod 0600 (or bind under os.umask(0o177)) before any client can connect, and create .cli-writes through fsutil.open_private, not Path.write_text.
- The socket fd must join the select set in BOTH branches of Daemon.run. daemon.py:757 selects on the inotify fd alone, and with inotify=false the elif branch (daemon.py:766) does self._stop.wait(timeout) with no fd select at all, so announcements would never be read in that configuration. No reader thread — the daemon has no shared-state concurrency today and this packet must not introduce the first.
- The control socket must never let a datagram choose which file the daemon opens. Resolve the announced path and require exact membership in the closed operational-name set under state_dir() — a membership test, never a startswith or a ..-tolerant prefix check — or an agent announces ~/.claude/.credentials.json and makes the daemon read it. Likewise the ledger iterates a closed tuple under state_dir() only, never rglob, never config_dir(), which holds the 0600 api_key that summarize.resolve_api_key reads (summarize.py:78).
- An announcement must not launder content it did not name: honour it only when the landed bytes hash to the sha256 the announcement carried. This is the single property that keeps a same-uid 0600 socket from being blanket amnesty, and TESTS is right to pin it with a dedicated case.
- Ledger design is settled as in-memory plus a record_all() baseline at startup, with the pre-start blind window written down as a known limit. Persisting it to state_dir() makes the ledger itself agent-writable alongside the file it protects. Ordering is a correctness requirement: the daemon records its own digest inside store._locked() before the inotify event can be drained, and the CLI announces inside the same lock.
- Deletion is not a write. rm alerts.jsonl — the packet's own headline attack — arrives as IN_DELETE / IN_MOVED_FROM, not IN_CLOSE_WRITE, so Daemon._drain_inotify's routing must cover it, not just check(). daemon.py:698's LAUNCHES_FILENAME short-circuit must stay ahead of any ledger classification, and both OPERATIONAL_NAMES guards (daemon.py:532 and daemon.py:701) must change together.
- The pause cap is daemon-side and fails open to notifying. action.parse_duration accepts 999999999d, which overflows timedelta, so clamp before the timedelta is built and treat every parse failure as not-paused. The one-shot R-SELF medium is emitted by the daemon through emit/Coalescer, never by sentinel-action — a CLI that appends to alerts.jsonl is both a new foreign-write source and a log-flooding primitive. The "once" latch needs __init__ state; the 60s Coalescer window is not "once", and _seen only covers PROCESS_RULES (daemon.py:496-500).
- pause_max needs explicit plumbing that does not exist. daemon.load_config forwards only four keys out of [daemon] (daemon.py:245-250) and action.is_paused reads no config at all; packaging/config.toml has only [kill] and [notify], so [daemon] is a new section and tests/test_config_template.py must keep passing alongside the new key. Reuse action.parse_duration's grammar so config and CLI speak the same units.
- The cap is read-time only, which leaves the CLI reporting a state that is not in effect: action.cmd_status duplicates the comparison inline rather than calling is_paused, and BarWidget.qml computes paused independently from the raw pause_until file. Decide one answer for `sentinel-action pause 30d` — I recommend refusing it with exit 1 and naming the cap, so the Owner cannot trip their own tamper alarm by typing a long pause.
- Honest ceiling, and it goes in the report rather than being implied away: control.sock at 0600 is reachable by any process running as the same uid, so a deliberate agent can announce its own write and be classified `ours`. W3-07 catches unsophisticated tampering and accidents. `foreign` is evidence, not prevention. That is consistent with the packet's Non-goal 1, and the journal mirror is best-effort (journald rate-limits and vacuums) rather than a complete ledger.
- Sequencing: W3-07 lands before UX-02b, which rewrites the same Daemon.handle_write and already carries a W3-07 addendum depending on the size_before/size_after evidence key names this packet freezes.

**Final position.** W3-07 is implementable but the packet as written is not launch-ready, and the reason is not the work it describes — it is that the packet's stated Changes do not include the one fix that makes any of it function. Three items must be settled by the Chief before Grok starts.

First, add the allowlist bypass to the packet's Changes block. Daemon.emit consults _allow_decision before it appends or notifies, and candidate_prefixes tests the empty global prefix first. I verified by execution that a single allowlist.json entry under the path-independent constant fingerprint bfec340cf0555b2b4bac4ea4e1a7299a0c925724048d94bde515c90271c78a04 silences R-SELF for every path forever. An attacker computes that offline with no knowledge of the victim. Without this fix W3-07 builds a tripwire whose wire is already cut, and the fix belongs in _allow_decision/candidate_prefixes plus an is_sticky bypass in emit — NOT in keys.approval_prefix, which as I verified already keys write rules on the exact file and cannot produce the muting entry.

Second, the Chief must decide the joint-case question before launch, not during. tests/joint/cases/harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json pins a 2099 pause_until against a harness that calls the real is_paused(); I executed the cap and confirmed it flips is_paused() to False, breaking three assertions in a file AGENTS.md forbids this packet to edit. Leaving this open either fails the gate or tempts Grok across an ownership line.

Third, the announcement design must cover sentinel-wrap, sentinel-scout and sentinel.health, or explicitly exempt them. As written, removing the OPERATIONAL_NAMES early-return turns every agent launch and every 03:30 timer tick on the Owner's live machine into a sticky critical toast that pause cannot silence — and alert fatigue on an unclosable toast is itself a way to defeat the monitor.

Beyond those, my own requirement list needs three amendments I am making against myself: the journal record is eight fields, not five (SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel and MESSAGE are load-bearing for the packet's own acceptance command and for the already-shipped investigate query at investigate.py:463); every field value should be length-framed unconditionally rather than only when it contains a newline, because the per-value decision is itself the injection surface; and alerts.lock belongs in the ignore set, not the ledger. I also correct the whole group on scope: there are six .tmp siblings, not four or five — allowlist-session.json.tmp appears in nobody's list — so the ignore rule must be the .tmp suffix, not an enumerated set, or the next write_private_atomic caller reopens the hole.

Finally, the report must state the honest ceiling rather than let the packet's language imply prevention. A 0600 socket reachable by the same uid means a deliberate agent can announce its own write and be classified ours; `foreign` is evidence, not a control. That is exactly the packet's Non-goal 1, and saying so plainly is more useful to the Owner than a claim the design cannot support.

### W3-07 · tests

I read W3-07 in full plus daemon.py, action.py, rules.py, store.py, notify.py, investigate.py, keys.py and every existing test that touches the affected surfaces, and confirmed the baseline at 210 passed in this worktree. The test list below is 47 new tests across six files (two of them new), each with a named fixture and an exact assertion, plus two existing tests that must be rewritten. Two things need a decision before Grok is launched: a Chief-owned joint case that the pause cap will break and that Grok is forbidden to touch, and a pre-existing false-positive R-SELF on `.tmp` siblings that I reproduced and that W3-07 must close or it will make worse.

**Findings**

- PROPOSED API for `src/sentinel/journal.py`, needed so the tests are writable: `JOURNAL_SOCKET = Path("/run/systemd/journal/socket")`, `alert_fields(alert) -> dict[str,str]`, `encode(fields) -> bytes` (native journal protocol), and `mirror_alert(alert, *, socket_path=None, runner=None) -> Literal["socket","logger","none"]` — the injectable `socket_path` and `runner` are what make every journal test hermetic with no journald.
- PROPOSED API for `src/sentinel/statewatch.py`: `Snapshot(size, mtime_ns, sha256)`, `StateLedger.record(path)`, `.record_all()`, `.announce(path, sha256|None)`, `.check(path) -> Literal["unchanged","ours","foreign"]`, `.open_control()`, `.poll_control() -> int`, `.consume_nonce() -> int`, plus module-level `announce_write(path, *, phase)` for the CLI side and `is_ignored(path)` for the lock/tmp/socket/nonce names.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_sends_one_datagram_to_the_socket — fixture `fake_journal` binds an `AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` socket at `tmp_path/"journal.sock"` with `settimeout(0.5)`; assert `mirror_alert(a, socket_path=p) == "socket"`, one `recv(65536)` succeeds, and a second `recv` raises `socket.timeout`.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_carries_the_five_sentinel_fields — same fixture plus a local independent decoder `parse(datagram)`; assert `parse(d)["SENTINEL_RULE"] == a.rule`, `["SENTINEL_ID"] == a.id`, `["SENTINEL_SEVERITY"] == a.severity`, `["SENTINEL_SUMMARY"] == a.summary`, and `["SENTINEL_LOCATION"] == keys.alert_location(a)`.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_field_names_are_exactly_the_allowlist — assert `set(parse(d)) == {"MESSAGE","PRIORITY","SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER","SENTINEL_RULE","SENTINEL_ID","SENTINEL_SEVERITY","SENTINEL_SUMMARY","SENTINEL_LOCATION"}`, which is the packet's Forbidden clause (no alert bodies beyond the listed fields) turned into an assertion.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_never_carries_cmdline_evidence_or_hashes — fixture builds an alert with `cmdline=["claude","--token","SECRET"]`, `evidence={"flags":[...]}`, `hashes={"/x":"deadbeef"}`; assert `b"SECRET" not in d and b"deadbeef" not in d` (security invariant 2).
- tests/test_journal.py::test_multiline_summary_uses_the_length_prefixed_form — alert summary `"first\nsecond"`; assert the datagram contains `b"SENTINEL_SUMMARY\n" + struct.pack("<Q", 12) + b"first\nsecond\n"` and the independent parser round-trips it, because a naive `KEY=value\n` encoder makes journald silently drop the whole record.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_syslog_identifier_is_sentinel — assert `parse(d)["SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER"] == "sentinel"`, which is exactly what the packet's Acceptance command `journalctl --user -t sentinel` selects on.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_priority_maps_severity — parametrized over `("high","3"), ("medium","4"), ("low","5")`; assert `parse(d)["PRIORITY"]` equals the mapped value so `journalctl -p err` surfaces high alerts.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_fallback_runs_logger_when_socket_missing — `socket_path=tmp_path/"absent.sock"` and `runner=lambda argv, payload: calls.append((argv, payload))`; assert the return is `"logger"`, `"--journald" in calls[0][0]`, and `b"SENTINEL_ID=" + a.id.encode() in calls[0][1]`.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_fallback_when_the_path_is_not_a_socket — write a plain file at the socket path so `connect()` raises `ENOTSOCK`; assert the logger fallback is taken and nothing propagates.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_returns_none_and_never_raises_when_both_paths_fail — absent socket plus a runner raising `FileNotFoundError`; assert `mirror_alert(...) == "none"` with no exception, because tamper evidence must never be able to kill the daemon.
- tests/test_journal.py::test_mirror_alert_uses_only_af_unix — monkeypatch `socket.socket` to record `(family, type)`; assert `family is socket.AF_UNIX and type is socket.SOCK_DGRAM` and that `AF_INET` is never constructed (PROTOCOL section 9 invariant 3, the daemon makes no network call).
- tests/test_journal.py::test_oversized_summary_is_truncated_not_dropped — a 64 KiB summary; assert `len(d) <= 65536` and `parse(d)["SENTINEL_ID"] == a.id`, since an over-size datagram fails with EMSGSIZE and would lose the record entirely.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_record_then_check_is_unchanged — autouse `_isolated_xdg` from tests/conftest.py plus a local `_ledger()` helper; write `state_dir()/"allowlist.json"`, `led.record(p)`, assert `led.check(p) == "unchanged"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_foreign_write_after_record_is_foreign — record, then append bytes with a plain `open(p,"a")`; assert `led.check(p) == "foreign"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_check_is_foreign_for_a_file_the_ledger_never_saw — assert an unrecorded existing file classifies `"foreign"`, pinning that the default is suspicious rather than safe (and forcing a `record_all()` baseline at daemon startup).
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_record_all_baselines_every_operational_file — helper writes one byte to each name in `daemon.OPERATIONAL_NAMES` minus `is_ignored` names, then `led.record_all()`; assert every `check` is `"unchanged"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_announced_write_is_ours_and_rebaselines — `led.record(p)`, `led.announce(p, sha256_of_new)`, write the new content; assert the first `check` is `"ours"` and an immediately following `check` is `"unchanged"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_an_announcement_cannot_launder_content_it_did_not_name — announce the sha256 of content A but land content B; assert `led.check(p) == "foreign"`, which is the one property that keeps the 0600 control socket from being a free amnesty for any process running as the user.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_deleting_a_recorded_file_is_foreign — record `alerts.jsonl`, `p.unlink()`, assert `led.check(p) == "foreign"` (erasing the alert log is the headline attack, and it arrives as IN_DELETE, not a write).
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_announced_deletion_is_ours — `led.announce(p, None)` then unlink; assert `"ours"`, which is the path `allowlist.clear_session()` and `store.rotate_if_needed()` take.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_mtime_only_change_with_identical_bytes_is_not_foreign — record, then `os.utime(p, ns=(t, t + 10**9))` with byte-identical content; assert `led.check(p) in {"unchanged","ours"}`, i.e. the sha256 decides and a bare touch is not tamper evidence.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_control_socket_is_created_private — `led.open_control()`; assert the path is `state_dir()/"control.sock"`, `stat.S_ISSOCK(...)` is true and `stat.S_IMODE(...) == 0o600` (fsutil's FILE_MODE, matching test_store.py::test_state_files_are_private).
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_cli_announcement_over_the_socket_is_consumed — `led.open_control()`, call `statewatch.announce_write(p)` in-process, assert `led.poll_control() == 1` (non-blocking recv loop, no threads) and that writing the announced content then classifies `"ours"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_announce_write_falls_back_to_the_nonce_file_when_no_socket — with no `control.sock` present, assert `announce_write(p) is False`, `state_dir()/".cli-writes"` exists at mode 0600, and its single line is JSON carrying `path` and `sha256`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_consume_nonce_at_startup_folds_and_clears — pre-seed `.cli-writes` with one announcement; assert `led.consume_nonce() == 1`, the file is empty or gone afterwards, and `check` on that path is `"ours"`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_junk_in_the_nonce_file_is_skipped_line_by_line — a file holding a torn line, a non-JSON line and one valid line; assert `consume_nonce() == 1` with no exception, matching the per-line tolerance rule tests/joint/SCHEMA.md already states for prefs.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_ignored_names_and_tmp_siblings_are_never_classified — assert `is_ignored` is True for `alerts.lock`, `control.sock`, `.cli-writes`, `alerts.jsonl.tmp`, `allowlist.json.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp`, `health.json.tmp` and False for `alerts.jsonl`, `allowlist.json`, `pause_until`.
- tests/test_statewatch.py::test_an_announcement_is_confined_to_the_state_dir — assert `announce_write(Path("/etc/passwd"))` returns False (or raises ValueError) and writes no nonce line, so the socket cannot be used to describe files outside `state_dir()`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_foreign_write_to_allowlist_raises_one_rself_high — existing `_daemon(tmp_path, monkeypatch, self_paths=[str(state_dir())], watch_paths=[])` helper at line 91, ledger baselined first; write `allowlist.json` from outside and call `d.handle_write(p)` twice; assert exactly one row with `rule == "R-SELF"`, `severity == "high"`, `evidence["event"] == "foreign-write"`, `evidence["path"] == str(p.resolve())` and `paths == [str(p.resolve())]`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_foreign_write_alert_is_sticky_shaped — assert `sentinel.notify.is_sticky(row) is True` for the emitted alert, tying the new evidence to `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` (which already contains `foreign-write` and `alert-log-truncated`, notify.py:40) so the toast is critical and ignores pause.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_truncated_alert_log_raises_alert_log_truncated — `d.emit()` three alerts, record the size, truncate `alerts.jsonl` to zero from outside, `d.handle_write(alerts_path)`; assert one row with `evidence["event"] == "alert-log-truncated"`, `severity == "high"`, and `evidence["size_before"] > evidence["size_after"]` — those two keys are exactly what `investigate._render_self_event` (investigate.py:280-281) already renders.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_rotation_is_not_a_truncation — drive `store.rotate_if_needed(max_bytes=1)` through the daemon's own recording path, then `d.handle_write(alerts_path)`; assert no `alert-log-truncated` row, because rotation legitimately shrinks `alerts.jsonl` to zero and would otherwise fire on every rotation.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_cli_announced_write_raises_no_alert — baseline the ledger, `d.open_control()`, run `action.action_main(["approve", alert_id, "--scope", "forever"])` in-process, `d.poll_control()`, then `d.handle_write(allowlist_path)`; assert no R-SELF row was added and the original alert's status is `approved`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_temp_sibling_of_an_operational_file_raises_no_alert — call `d.handle_write(p)` for `allowlist.json.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp`, `health.json.tmp`, `control.sock` and `.cli-writes`; assert zero alerts — I ran this against HEAD and it produces six spurious high R-SELF rows today, so this test is a regression fix, not just new coverage.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_daemons_own_emit_does_not_trip_the_tripwire — `d.emit(alert)` then `d.handle_write(alerts_path)`; assert `iter_alerts()` holds exactly the one original row and no R-SELF, i.e. the daemon's own append is recorded in the ledger before the inotify event is classified.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_emit_mirrors_each_stored_alert_to_the_journal_once — `_daemon(..., journal=recorder)`; assert one mirror call per row actually appended, that a coalesced duplicate produces no second mirror (mirror sits after `append_alert`, daemon.py:519), and `recorder[0].id == alert.id`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_emit_survives_a_failing_journal_mirror — `journal` raises `OSError`; assert the alert is still in `iter_alerts()` and the injected `notify` callable was still called.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_beyond_the_cap_is_not_paused_and_raises_one_rself_medium — write `pause_until` at now+30d, call `d.handle_write(pause_path)` twice; assert `action.is_paused() is False`, exactly one row with `rule == "R-SELF"`, `severity == "medium"`, `evidence["event"] == "pause-cap-exceeded"` and `evidence["pause_until"]` present, and that the second call adds nothing (the once-only latch).
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_a_valid_pause_inside_the_cap_raises_nothing — CLI-announced `write_pause(timedelta(hours=1))` then `d.handle_write(pause_path)`; assert zero alerts and `is_paused() is True`.
- tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_launches_jsonl_growth_by_the_wrapper_is_not_foreign — `wrap_record.record_launch(...)` from a third process's point of view, then `d.consume_launches()` and `d.handle_write(launches_path)`; assert no `foreign-write` row (this test only makes sense once risk 1 below is decided).
- tests/test_action.py::test_is_paused_rejects_a_pause_beyond_the_default_cap — `write_pause(timedelta(days=30))`; assert `is_paused() is False` while `read_pause_until()` still returns the timestamp, so the file is left intact as evidence for the alert rather than rewritten.
- tests/test_action.py::test_is_paused_honours_the_cap_boundary — monkeypatch `sentinel.action._now` to a frozen datetime as test_action.py:231 already does; assert a pause of exactly `pause_max` is honoured and `pause_max + 1s` is not.
- tests/test_action.py::test_pause_cap_is_configurable — write `[daemon]\npause_max = "1h"` into `XDG_CONFIG_HOME/sentinel/config.toml`; assert a 2h pause gives `is_paused() is False` and a 30m pause gives True, reusing the existing `parse_duration` grammar (action.py:55) so the config and the CLI speak the same units.
- tests/test_action.py::test_pause_cap_falls_back_to_the_default_on_a_junk_config — `pause_max = "banana"`; assert the 24h default applies with no exception, matching how `load_precious_worktrees` (action.py:107) swallows a bad config.
- tests/test_action.py::test_cmd_pause_refuses_a_duration_beyond_the_cap — assert `action_main(["pause","30d"]) == 1`, stderr names the cap, and `pause_until_path()` was not created, so the Owner cannot trip their own tamper alarm by typing a long pause (this one depends on risk 3 below).
- tests/test_config_template.py::test_config_template_documents_the_pause_cap — assert `packaging/config.toml` parses with `data["daemon"]["pause_max"] == "24h"` and that it equals `action.DEFAULT_PAUSE_MAX`; note the template has no `[daemon]` section at all today, so this is a new section, and the existing `load_policy(path) == NotifyPolicy()` assertion in that file must keep passing.
- tests/test_store.py::test_control_socket_and_nonce_are_private — extends the invariant already asserted at test_store.py:60-78; after `led.open_control()` and one nonce fallback write, assert both `control.sock` and `.cli-writes` are mode 0600 under a 0700 state dir.
- BREAKS (rewrite): tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_handle_write_skips_operational_state_files at line 71 writes `alerts.jsonl`, `launches.jsonl`, `allowlist.json` and `watchlist.json` with no ledger record and asserts `iter_alerts() == []`; under W3-07 each is now classified and three or four foreign-write rows appear, so it must become a baseline-then-`unchanged` test and be renamed to something like `test_operational_writes_are_classified_not_ignored`.
- BREAKS (rewrite): tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py::test_pause_until_write_is_operational at line 645 asserts no alert for `pause_until`, `alerts.lock`, `alerts.jsonl.tmp`, `alerts.jsonl.1` and `notify-prefs.json`; `pause_until` and `notify-prefs.json` now go through the ledger while the other three stay ignored outright, so it must split into two tests along the `is_ignored` boundary.
- BREAKS (Chief-owned, Grok forbidden to touch): tests/joint/cases/harness-sticky-alert-ignores-pause.json sets `state.pause_until = "2099-01-01T00:00:00+00:00"` and the harness builds a real `Notifier(load_policy(...), runner=recorder, ...)` at tests/joint/test_adversarial.py:98 with no `paused=` injection, so it calls the real `is_paused()`; once the cap lands, 2099 exceeds any `pause_max`, the pause is void, the case's second ordinary high alert also toasts, and `expect.toasts: 1` becomes 2 with `urgencies` becoming `["critical","normal"]`.
- PRE-EXISTING BUG found while checking the blast radius: `write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:367) creates `<name>.tmp` beside every rewritten state file, but `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` lists only `alerts.jsonl.tmp`, so an IN_CLOSE_WRITE on `allowlist.json.tmp`, `watchlist.json.tmp`, `pause_until.tmp` or `health.json.tmp` falls through to `evaluate_write`, and with `writer_exe=None` `_is_sentinel_writer` returns False and a high R-SELF fires — I reproduced all four plus `control.sock` and `.cli-writes` in a temp XDG against HEAD.
- SEAM ALREADY PRESENT: `Notifier.__init__` takes an unused `on_state_write: Callable[[Path], None]` hook (notify.py:374, invoked at notify.py:454 after the burst-state write), so the daemon should pass `on_state_write=self._ledger.record` where it builds the default Notifier at daemon.py:394 rather than inventing a new mechanism for `notify-burst.json`.
- PACKET CORRECTION: the packet's fallback of `logger -t sentinel` does not carry structured fields, so the Acceptance command `journalctl --user -t sentinel -n 1 --output=json | jq .SENTINEL_RULE` would print null whenever the fallback is used; `logger --journald` (confirmed present, util-linux 2.42.2) reads `NAME=value` lines and does preserve them, but ignores `-t`, so `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=sentinel` has to travel as a field in the payload.
- FACT-CHECK support for Context claim 5: `/run/systemd/journal/socket` on this machine is `srw-rw-rw-` mode 0666 and an unprivileged `AF_UNIX`/`SOCK_DGRAM` `connect()` succeeds, so the native-socket path is viable with no dependency and no root.

**Risks**

- `launches.jsonl` and `health.json` have legitimate third-process writers (`sentinel-wrap` via `wrap_record.record_launch`, and `sentinel-health` via `write_health_report`) that never touch the control socket, so if they are inside the tripwire every agent launch and every health-timer tick raises a high foreign-write on the live machine; decide before implementation whether they are excluded, taught to announce, or watched for shrink only. No existing test catches this because no health or wrap test runs a Daemon.
