- The joint case break is the one item Grok cannot resolve inside the packet: it is Claude's half of Test 3 and AGENTS.md forbids editing it, but a static far-future timestamp will exceed any cap forever, so fixing it needs either a rewritten case or a harness change allowing relative times in `state` — settle this before launch or the packet fails G0 or tempts Grok across an ownership line.
- It is undecided whether `sentinel-action pause 30d` should be clamped, refused with exit 1, or written and alarmed on; writing it means the Owner's own command trips a tamper alert, which is the wrong default, so I wrote the test as `refuses` and flagged it — the test list needs one answer either way.
- Ledger persistence is unspecified: in memory it re-baselines on every daemon restart and tampering during downtime is invisible, while on disk (`state_dir()/ledger.json`) the ledger is itself tamperable and must join the ignore set; neither closes the hole, which is the packet's own Non-goal 1, so recommend in-memory plus a `record_all()` baseline at startup and state the limit plainly in the report.
- The control socket at mode 0600 is reachable by any process running as the same user, so an agent can announce its own write and launder exactly the content it names; the statewatch test `test_an_announcement_cannot_launder_content_it_did_not_name` pins the bound, but this belongs in the report next to Non-goal 1 rather than being presented as prevention.
- Adding `pause-cap-exceeded` to `notify.STICKY_EVENTS` is tempting but out of scope: it would change the contract asserted by tests/test_notify.py::test_sticky_self_tamper_is_critical_without_timeout and the sticky wording in tests/joint/SCHEMA.md, both outside W3-07 — recommend leaving the medium alert non-sticky, which is safe because a capped pause returns `is_paused() is False` so the toast goes out anyway.
- Announcement calls are being added inside `allowlist.approve`, `store.update_alert_status` and `action.write_pause`, which roughly thirty existing tests in tests/test_action.py, tests/test_allowlist.py and tests/test_store.py exercise; if `announce_write` raises on a missing state dir, an absent socket or ECONNREFUSED instead of swallowing it, those all fail at once, so best-effort behaviour needs its own assertion.
- `Daemon.handle_write` is called from `_on_fs_event` with no `writer_pid` or `writer_exe` (daemon.py:703), so foreign-write alerts carry no writer identity at all; `investigate._writer_unknown_limit` already prints that caveat for `foreign-write`, and the alert summary should not imply Sentinel knows who wrote the file.
- Deletion arrives as IN_DELETE and IN_MOVED_FROM rather than IN_CLOSE_WRITE, so if only the write path is wired to the ledger, `rm ~/.local/state/sentinel/alerts.jsonl` — the most direct attack in the packet's Goal — is silently missed; the daemon's `_drain_inotify` routing, not just `check()`, has to cover it.
- Counting: 47 new tests and 2 rewritten against the current 210, so expect roughly 257 passing; any number materially below that means whole groups were skipped, and the report's test count must come from an actual pytest run in Grok's worktree per AGENTS.md.


## Wave 2 — W3-05, W3-06, W6-01a

24 agents reported.

### W3-05 · codebase

W3-05 touches seven existing symbols and asks for two new files. The change surface is real and mostly well-aimed, but three of the eight Context claims do not hold on the current tree/machine, and two of them are load-bearing for the packet's rationale. Claim 3 (PATH order) is refuted: `~/.local/bin` is now FIRST in the login shell, not position 12, because `~/.bashrc`, `~/.bash_profile` and `~/.profile` each end with `. "$HOME/.local/share/../bin/env"`, a uv-style prepender that runs after Omarchy's env-bootstrap. `bash -lc 'command -v claude'` resolves to `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude` — a shim placed in `~/.local/bin` by `scripts/install-wrappers.sh` WOULD be executed today. Claim 6 is refuted in both halves: `Util.execArgv` and `Util.execDetached` in `/usr/share/omarchy/shell/Commons/Util.qml` both run through `bash -lc`, i.e. the LOGIN shell with its login PATH — and `~/.local/bin/{sentinel,sentinel-action,sentinel-scout}` already exist as symlinks into `~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin/`. So `sentinel-action` IS reachable from a toast and a menu row right now. The click-path change is still worth doing, but for a different reason than the packet states: the click runs the CLI with no terminal attached, so its output goes nowhere and stdin is not a tty — the menu is invisible and unanswerable. Claim 2 cannot be verified without violating the packet's own Forbidden section. Baseline is 210 passed in 1.83s.

**Findings**

- NEW FILE COLLISION — /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/vendors.py does not exist. Both W3-05 and W3-03 create it, with disjoint field sets (W3-05: basenames, argv_bypass, config_bypass; W3-03: home, settings, hooks_dirs, mcp, auth, project_dirs, exclude) and both are marked 'parallel-safe' with each other. Whichever lands second must extend, not replace. Worse, W3-03's Non-goals explicitly excludes 'Content hashing and diffing (W6-03)' and 'The bypass-flag half of vendors.py (W3-05)', while W3-05 asks the scout to hash and to store the hash in watchlist.json — the same file whose schema W3-03 rewrites to version 2.
- rules.py — the bypass surface. `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS` is a flat frozenset of five bare tokens. `extract_bypass_flags(cmdline, extra_bypass_flags)` does a per-token membership test with `_normalize_token` stripping `--flag=value` to `--flag`; there is no notion of a value-taking flag or a positional pair. Consequence the packet misses: because `bypassPermissions` is in the set as a BARE token, `grok --permission-mode bypassPermissions` already fires today, while `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` does NOT (normalization strips it to `--permission-mode`, which is unknown). `evaluate_process(cmdline, exe, cwd, *, extra_bypass_flags)` builds the R-BYPASS Alert (severity high) with `evidence={'flag': flags[0], 'flags': flags}` and stores the raw cmdline.
- BASENAME DUPLICATION — `DEFAULT_AGENT_BASENAMES` is defined twice, identically, in src/sentinel/rules.py and src/sentinel/daemon.py: {claude, codex, cursor, cursor-agent, grok}. Neither includes `gemini`, even though README names Gemini and `gemini --yolo` is in the packet's own test list. A vendors table with `basenames` should collapse both; `rules.evaluate_child_shell` and `daemon.Daemon.__init__` (via cfg 'known_basenames') are the two consumers.
- FINGERPRINT COUPLING — src/sentinel/keys.py::alert_flag_set derives the approval fingerprint from `evidence['flags']`, falling back to `extract_bypass_flags(alert.cmdline)`. The packet's change to report value-taking flags as `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` changes that token, which changes every fingerprint for such alerts, which silently invalidates stored allowlist entries. keys.py's own module docstring pins the invariant: 'the CLI (approve) and the daemon (suppress) must derive identical fingerprints for the same alert, or Approve silently does nothing.' This is a direct interaction with W3-01 (allowlist correctness), which the packet declares parallel-safe.
- REDACTION (S4 addendum) — genuinely unimplemented at both named sites. src/sentinel/wrap_record.py::record_launch writes the raw `cmdline` to launches.jsonl with no filtering; rules.evaluate_process copies the raw cmdline into the Alert. The only redaction that exists today is in src/sentinel/summarize.py, and it only guards the opt-in cloud bundle (tests/test_summarize_redaction.py). There is no `evidence.redacted` flag anywhere. Alert.evidence is a free-form dict so the flag needs no model change.
- SCOUT — src/sentinel/scout.py is much thinner than R-BYPASS-CONFIG assumes. `scout(home, seeds)` walks `DEFAULT_SEEDS` (which contains no .grok, .gemini or .codex) and `_entry_for(path)` records only path/kind/size/mtime — no hash field, no version field. The module emits no alerts at all: it imports nothing from sentinel.store or sentinel.notify, and `cli.scout_main` just prints a count. R-BYPASS-CONFIG therefore needs, all new: a sha256 helper (none exists in src/sentinel/fsutil.py, whose whole API is ensure_private_dir / open_private / write_private_atomic), a watchlist schema field, an alert-append path, and a decision about whether the scout notifies. One thing already exists in its favour: models.Alert carries a `hashes: dict[str, str] | None` field that nothing currently populates, and src/sentinel/investigate.py already renders it in `_write_verify` and `_write_facts`.
- NOTIFY — the injection point the packet wants already exists and is even commented for this packet. src/sentinel/notify.py has `_exec_tail(alert_id, exec_prefix=('sentinel-action',))`, `build_argv(alert, policy, *, exec_prefix=...)`, and `Notifier.__init__(..., exec_prefix=...)` with the comment 'Resolved at call time so tests (and W3-05) can swap the module hooks.' The change is the default tuple, in three places. Note `build_summary_argv` uses a DIFFERENT tail — `--exec omarchy-shell shell summon <plugin_id> {}` — which the packet does not mention and which is pinned by tests/test_notify_burst.py; decide deliberately whether the burst-summary toast also needs wrapping (it summons the panel, so probably not).
- ACTION — src/sentinel/action.py::cmd_menu(alert_id) takes one positional arg, prints a header plus `investigate.action_lines(alert)`, and returns 0. Every dispatch target the interactive menu needs already exists with a stable signature: cmd_approve(alert_id, scope), cmd_kill(alert_id, *, session, yes, force_stale), cmd_investigate(alert_id), cmd_summarize(alert_id), cmd_dismiss(alert_id); SCOPES comes from sentinel.allowlist. `_normalize_argv` already accepts both `<id> menu` and `menu <id>`. Two existing tests exercise the non-tty path and must keep passing: tests/test_action.py::test_list_and_status_and_menu and ::test_menu_lists_open_and_logs (both run under pytest, where stdin.isatty() is False, so the cheat-sheet branch is preserved for free).
- MENU JSONC — packaging/omarchy-menu-sentinel.jsonc holds four bare actions (sentinel-action list / status / pause 1h, sentinel-scout --refresh). scripts/install-menu.sh's embedded Python validates only the KEY names via its `expected` tuple ('sentinel', 'sentinel.alerts', 'sentinel.status', 'sentinel.inventory', 'sentinel.pause'), so rewriting the action strings needs no installer change. Menu actions reach `Menu.runAction` -> `Util.execDetached(command)` -> `bash -lc "<string>"`, so a multi-word action string is fine.
- CLICK-PATH PRECEDENT ALREADY IN THE REPO — BarWidget.qml::runInTerminal already builds exactly the argv the packet wants (`['omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation', ...cliArgv(args)]`), and Panel.qml uses the same launcher for install-daemon.sh and for starting the service. Only notify.build_argv and the jsonc lag behind. BarWidget also has a `cliPath` widget setting that falls back to bare 'sentinel-action'.
- INSTALL-WRAPPERS — scripts/install-wrappers.sh: BIN_DIR (from SENTINEL_BIN_DIR, default ~/.local/bin), backup_if_needed (renames to *.sentinel-bak.<ts>, recognizes its own shim by grepping the first 5 lines for 'sentinel-wrap'), find_real (walks PATH, skips BIN_DIR and other shims), env_key, then the per-name loop that writes the shim and chmods it. There is no PATH verification and no failure exit anywhere after the write. Note it has NO --dry-run flag at all, despite the packet's Forbidden section naming one; SENTINEL_BIN_DIR is the only safe harness.
- INSTALL-CLI OVERLAP — scripts/install-daemon.sh ALREADY does what the proposed scripts/install-cli.sh would do: its step '2. CLIs on PATH' runs `ln -sfn "$VENV/bin/$cli" "$BIN_DIR/$cli"` for sentinel, sentinel-action and sentinel-scout, dry-run by default via its do_or_show helper. It is also what actually put the three symlinks on this machine. The genuine gap is backup semantics: `ln -sfn` clobbers whatever is there. Worth an ask on whether a second script is wanted or whether install-daemon.sh's step 2 should be factored out and hardened.
- README ALREADY LIES IN TWO PLACES THAT W3-05 FIXES — README.md's Install section says 'The wrapper installer backs up anything it replaces and refuses to finish if its shim would be shadowed by something earlier on your PATH', and Usage says 'Notifications carry the same actions: click one to open the alert in a floating terminal.' Neither is true of the current tree. Landing W3-05 makes both true; this is the overlap with W3-06 docs-truth.
- FACT-CHECK claim 1, CONFIRMED with corrections — exact flags from `--help` on this machine today. grok: `--always-approve` ('Auto-approve all tool executions'), `--permission-mode <MODE>` [default, acceptEdits, auto, dontAsk, bypassPermissions, plan], `--allow <RULE>` (compat alias --allowedTools), `--sandbox <PROFILE>`. claude: `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` (this one only ENABLES the option and is NOT itself a bypass — a prefix/substring rule would false-positive on it), `--permission-mode <mode>` [acceptEdits, auto, bypassPermissions, manual, dontAsk, plan] (note dontAsk exists on Claude too, and the packet's negative test `claude --permission-mode plan` correctly must not fire). codex: `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, `-s/--sandbox <read-only|workspace-write|danger-full-access>`, `-a/--ask-for-approval <on-request|never>`, `--approve-for-me`; this build has NO `--yolo` and NO `--full-auto`. gemini: `-y/--yolo`, `--approval-mode <default|auto_edit|yolo|plan>`, `--skip-trust`, `--allowed-tools` (deprecated). cursor-agent: NOT INSTALLED, mark UNVERIFIED.
- FACT-CHECK claim 3, REFUTED — `bash -lc 'echo $PATH'` gives `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` FIRST (that path IS ~/.local/bin), then /usr/share/omarchy/bin, then the mise installs dirs, then mise/shims, then /usr/local/*, /usr/bin, then a literal `/home/tav/.local/bin` at position 12. Cause: ~/.bashrc, ~/.bash_profile and ~/.profile each end with `. "$HOME/.local/share/../bin/env"`, whose case statement prepends that spelling. `bash -lc 'command -v claude'` -> /home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude, i.e. the mise launcher in ~/.local/bin, NOT the mise install dir. A Sentinel shim written there is executed. Separately, quickshell's own process env PATH (pid 62861, `quickshell -n -p /usr/share/omarchy/shell`) is /usr/share/omarchy/bin : mise/shims : /usr/local/sbin : /usr/local/bin : /usr/bin : mise/shims : /home/tav/.local/bin : perl dirs — ~/.local/bin at position 7, behind mise shims — but that PATH is not the one that runs clicks.
- FACT-CHECK claim 6, REFUTED both halves — /usr/share/omarchy/shell/Commons/Util.qml: `execArgv(argv)` is `Quickshell.execDetached(['bash','-lc','exec "$@"','bash'].concat(argv))` and `execDetached(command)` is `Quickshell.execDetached(['bash','-lc',command])`. Service.qml calls Util.execArgv on the parsed omarchy-exec-argv hint; Menu.qml::runAction calls Util.execDetached. Both are login shells, so both inherit the login PATH with ~/.local/bin first. And `ls -la ~/.local/bin` shows sentinel, sentinel-action and sentinel-scout as symlinks into /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin/ (dated Sep 5), all resolving and executable. Restate the packet's rationale: the defect is no tty, not no PATH.
- FACT-CHECK claims 4, 5, 7, 8, CONFIRMED — (4) ~/.local/bin/{claude,codex,grok,gemini} are 3-line mise launchers ending `exec mise x "<pkg>" -- "<name>" "$@"`. (5) /usr/bin/omarchy-notification-send builds `omarchy-exec-argv` as a JSON array from --exec (line ~178, `jq -Rsc`), and /usr/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation exists (534 bytes, root-owned) running `omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; omarchy-show-done` via `setsid uwsm-app -- xdg-terminal-exec ... -e bash -c`; it builds `cmd="$*"` and re-splits in a shell, which is safe for `sentinel-action <uuid> menu`. (7) cmd_menu prints and returns 0, no input. (8) the tail is pinned in TWO places, tests/test_notify.py::test_high_is_normal_with_30s_timeout (full-argv equality) and ::test_exec_tail_is_last, plus a third unmentioned pin on the summary tail in tests/test_notify_burst.py.
- FACT-CHECK claim 2, UNVERIFIABLE BY DESIGN — confirming `permission_mode = "always-approve"` under `[ui]` in ~/.grok/config.toml requires reading the body, which this packet's own Forbidden section prohibits. Stat only: the file exists, 659 bytes, mtime 2026-09-06 02:22. Two reasons to doubt the claim as written: 'always-approve' is not one of grok's documented `--permission-mode` values (default/acceptEdits/auto/dontAsk/bypassPermissions/plan), and `[ui]` is where grok's own --help documents `screen_mode`. ~/.codex/config.toml does NOT exist on this machine. ~/.gemini/settings.json (390 B), ~/.claude/settings.json (168 B) and ~/.claude.json (52915 B) do; ~/.claude/settings.local.json and ~/.cursor/ do not.

**Risks**

- The installer's shadow check as specified will misfire. `command -v claude` returns `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude` while `$BIN_DIR/claude` is `/home/tav/.local/bin/claude` — the same file, different strings. A raw string comparison reports a false shadow on every run on this machine. The comparison must canonicalize both sides (readlink -f / realpath) before comparing.
- `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` deliberately rebuilds PATH from the login profile, which is the right thing to measure but breaks the packet's own acceptance command: `PATH=/tmp/fakemise:/tmp/sb:$PATH bash scripts/install-wrappers.sh claude` sets a PATH that the inner login shell then overwrites by sourcing ~/.bash_profile -> ~/.bashrc -> the env prepender. As written, the expected `exit=1` will not reproduce. The check needs an escape hatch (an env var, or a non-login `bash -c` fallback) for the test harness, or the acceptance command needs HOME pointed at a fixture home.
- Reporting value-taking flags as `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` changes `keys.alert_flag_set` output, which changes approval fingerprints and silently voids allowlist entries stored under the old bare-token form. This lands in the same area W3-01 is rewriting. Either keep emitting the bare token alongside the pair form, or coordinate a fingerprint migration with the Chief before changing the reported shape.
- R-BYPASS-CONFIG as specified fights W3-03 over watchlist.json. W3-03 rewrites that file to version 2 with per-entry vendor fields and explicitly lists content hashing as a non-goal deferred to W6-03. Storing a per-file hash there, from a module (scout.py) that today emits no alerts and imports no store, is the largest genuinely-new piece of this packet and the one most likely to conflict on merge. Worth an ask before implementing.
- The alert R-BYPASS-CONFIG would raise says only 'this file changed', never which key or to what — because reading the key requires reading the body. On a file like ~/.grok/config.toml that the user edits for unrelated reasons (screen_mode, model), that is a medium-severity alert with a high false-positive rate and no actionable content. The packet acknowledges this and says to ask the Chief about a narrow keyed read; I'd push for that ask to be filed BEFORE implementing, not after. Note the spec at docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-omarchy-sentinel-design.md leaves room: line 188 forbids opening 'transcript or credential file bodies' specifically, and line 258 explicitly contemplates 'File bodies of settings (hash + path only)'.
- shellcheck is NOT installed on this machine (`command -v shellcheck` is empty), so acceptance step 2 cannot run. AGENTS.md's engineering standard says shell scripts pass shellcheck 'if it is installed', so this is a documented skip rather than a failure — but it must be reported as UNVERIFIED, not claimed as passing, and installing it would need sudo, which is a hard rule violation.
- `tests/test_install_wrappers.sh` will not be collected by pytest — there is no pytest config or testpaths in pyproject.toml and pytest collects test_*.py only. It needs a Python wrapper test that shells out to it. There is also no precedent in the suite for subprocess-invoking an installer, and conftest.py's autouse _isolated_xdg fixture only redirects XDG_STATE_HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME — it does NOT redirect HOME, so any test that runs install-wrappers.sh must set SENTINEL_BIN_DIR (and ideally HOME) explicitly or it will write into the live ~/.local/bin, breaking both the conftest invariant and AGENTS.md security invariant 6.
- A new rule needs an entry in `investigate.RULE_TEXT`; the lookup is `RULE_TEXT.get(alert.rule)` guarded by `if text:`, so a missing R-BYPASS-CONFIG key fails silently — the Investigate view just drops the 'what fired' explanation with no error and no test failure.
- The interactive menu's kill rows will double-confirm: cmd_kill already calls confirm_kill -> kill.confirm_kill, which prompts on a tty, and a precious-worktree session kill requires an interactive tty by design (spec section 11). Dispatching from a numbered prompt inside the floating terminal means the user answers twice. That is arguably correct for a destructive action, but it should be a deliberate decision recorded in the report rather than a surprise.
- packaging/config.toml has no [daemon] section at all, even though daemon.load_config reads extra_bypass_flags and known_basenames from one. If the vendor table becomes the source of truth for basenames and bypass flags, the relationship between the built-in table and those two config keys needs stating, or users get two overlapping mechanisms with no documented precedence.
- Minor, but it affects report accuracy: every line-number citation in the packet's Context is off — DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS is not at rules.py:11-19, build_argv is nowhere near notify.py:52-55, cmd_menu is not at action.py:214-233, and the test pin is not at test_notify.py:45-47. The named symbols are all correct and all exist; only the ranges are stale. Cite symbols in the report, not the packet's line numbers.


### W3-05 · codebase:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- SPEC agent — R8 names the rule-description table `RULE_WHAT` at `src/sentinel/investigate.py:44-52`. It is `RULE_TEXT`, defined at `src/sentinel/investigate.py:43` and read at line 127 via `RULE_TEXT.get(alert.rule)`. R8 also implies four existing rule ids; the `_RENDERERS` dispatch at `src/sentinel/investigate.py:414-421` has six: R-BYPASS, R-HOOK-WRITE, R-SELF, R-CHILD-SHELL, R-ALLOW-EXPIRE, R-NEW-AGENT. A new rule needs a key in both. (CODEBASE agent had `RULE_TEXT` right.)
- SPEC agent — the risk 'if the bare token bypassPermissions stays, then `grok "explain bypassPermissions to me"` false-positives on the prompt text' is false as written. Verified: `extract_bypass_flags(['grok','explain bypassPermissions to me']) == []`, because a quoted prompt is one argv element and matching is exact-token membership. The hazard exists only for an unquoted multi-word prompt where the word lands as its own argv element (`['grok','explain','bypassPermissions','to','me'] -> ['bypassPermissions']`). Keep the recommendation, fix the justification.
- SPEC agent — 'the required tests/test_install_wrappers.sh cannot pass its exit-0 half with `bash -lc` against the real HOME' overstates. Verified both halves: `env -i HOME=/home/tav PATH=<fake>:<sb>:/usr/bin:/bin bash -lc 'command -v claude'` gives `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude` (not hermetic), but `env -i HOME=<tmp> PATH=<fake>:<sb>:/usr/bin:/bin bash -lc 'command -v claude'` gives `<tmp>/fakemise/claude` — the injected PATH survives once HOME has no profile. The test IS writable with `HOME=tmp_path`; an injectable resolver is a convenience, not a prerequisite.
- CODEBASE agent — 'DEFAULT_SEEDS (which contains no .grok, .gemini or .codex)'. `.codex` IS present. `src/sentinel/scout.py:14-22` is `.claude, .codex, .cursor, .config/Cursor, .config/claude, .config/sentinel, .local/state/sentinel`. Only `.grok` and `.gemini` are missing.
- CODEBASE agent — attributes the comment 'Resolved at call time so tests (and W3-05) can swap the module hooks' to the exec_prefix seam. That comment is at `src/sentinel/notify.py:378`, directly above `self._runner = runner`; it documents the runner. The exec_prefix seam is real (`notify.py:219`, `:227`, `:372`, `:383`) but undocumented.
- TESTS agent — seam 5 describes the dispatch as `cmd_kill(id, session=False)`. That raises TypeError: `def cmd_kill(alert_id: str, *, session: bool, yes: bool, force_stale: bool = False)` at `src/sentinel/action.py:393` gives `yes` NO default. The dispatch must be `cmd_kill(id, session=..., yes=False)` — which is also what the SECURITY agent independently requires for invariant 7.
- TESTS agent — seam 6 ('without SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH, tests/test_install_wrappers.py cannot be written reliably') is refuted by the temp-HOME experiment above. Separately, its conditional-break item 4 recommends a `SENTINEL_NO_PROMPT` env var to force non-interactive mode. That directly contradicts the SECURITY agent's correct rule that the interactivity seam must not be env-controlled, since a monitored agent can set env and drive cmd_menu to choice 5 or 6 without a human. Resolve in favour of SECURITY: keyword params only, and have `tests/test_action.py:263` and `:468` pass `isatty=lambda: False` explicitly rather than depending on pytest's stdin or on env.
- SECURITY agent — 'Extend whatever test asserts the daemon's import graph to cover sentinel.vendors.' No such test exists; there are no `sys.modules` assertions anywhere under `tests/`. It would be new work, not an extension. The underlying concern is also already satisfied in the safe direction: `src/sentinel/summarize.py:14` imports from `sentinel.rules`, and `rules.py` imports nothing from `summarize`, so putting redaction in `rules.py` introduces no urllib into the daemon today.
- INTEGRATION agent — 'CLAIM 5 IS PARTIALLY WRONG on the path: omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation lives at /usr/share/omarchy/bin, not /usr/bin.' Both exist: the real 534-byte root-owned file is `/usr/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation`, and `/usr/share/omarchy/bin/...` is a symlink to it (which is what `command -v` returns, since that dir is earlier on PATH). The packet's claim 5 is correct as written.
- INTEGRATION agent — the warning that install-cli.sh could 'relink to a DIFFERENT venv (this worktree's .venv)' is worth guarding but mis-states the precedent: `scripts/install-daemon.sh:17-18` derives `VENV="${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/$ID/venv"` from the manifest id, never from the repo checkout. The existing installer already cannot point at a worktree .venv; install-cli.sh should inherit that derivation rather than invent one.
- SPEC and INTEGRATION agents both — 'position 12 is wrong.' Precisely: `/home/tav/.local/bin` literally IS still at position 12 of the login PATH. The same directory also appears at position 1, spelled `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin`, prepended by `/home/tav/.local/bin/env` (mtime 2026-09-05 20:34). The packet's PATH listing is incomplete, not fabricated; what is refuted is the conclusion 'a shim placed in ~/.local/bin is never executed'.

FOUR THINGS THE PACKET MUST CHANGE BEFORE IT LAUNCHES.
1. Move the S4 redaction paragraph out of `## Non-goals` into `## Changes` (same defect in W3-03's S5 paragraph). It is the packet's only security-finding closure and currently reads as out of scope. Highest-value single edit.
2. Amend Forbidden to 'never store or surface a config body; hashing is permitted' — PROTOCOL §9.2 line 109 already says 'Hashes and paths only', so the packet, not the protocol, is the outlier.
3. Replace every line citation with a symbol name. All named symbols exist; every range is stale (rules.py:12-20 not 11-19; notify.py:219/223 not 52-55; action.py:210-222 not 214-233; test_notify.py:57 and 104, not 45-47), and the LEDGER already records a full Grok run burned on stale line numbers.

FOUR IMPLEMENTATION CONSTRAINTS, now settled by measurement.
- Compare with `[ "$resolved" -ef "$target" ]` or realpath, never string equality. Measured: string MISMATCH, -ef MATCH on a correctly installed shim.
- The installer test is writable with HOME=tmp_path; `bash -lc` honours an injected PATH once the profile is gone. No new env seam is required, and TESTS' SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH should be dropped rather than added.
- cmd_menu's seams are keyword parameters with defaults, mirroring kill.confirm_kill — never an env var. This kills TESTS' SENTINEL_NO_PROMPT suggestion; the two existing menu tests pass isatty=lambda: False explicitly. Choice 5/6 dispatch is `cmd_kill(id, session=..., yes=False)`; `yes` has no default and the double confirm is deliberate (PROTOCOL §9.4).
- Redaction lives in rules.py (or a new redact.py importing only `re`), never in summarize.py, whose module-scope urllib would then reach the daemon.

THREE ASKS THE CHIEF SHOULD ANSWER BEFORE ANY CODE IS WRITTEN, not after.
- Merge order for vendors.py and watchlist.json. W3-03's Non-goals already cede the bypass half of vendors.py to W3-05 and defer content hashing to W6-03, so the cleanest ruling is: W3-03 first, W3-05 declares `Depends on: W3-03`, and W3-05's per-file digest goes at a named key inside W3-03's v2 shape (or into its own state file).
- Whether R-BYPASS-CONFIG ships at all in this form. It can only say 'this file changed', it fires on benign vendor edits, its baseline lives in a file the monitored agent can write, and its digest would ship to api.x.ai through summarize's ALLOWED_KEYS. This overlaps open decision D-005. My recommendation: defer the rule, land the vendor table, the install truth, the click path and S4 redaction now.
- Whether install-cli.sh should exist at all, given install-daemon.sh step 2 already does the job from the plugin-venv path. If it ships, it needs the same PATH-truth check, a root/sudo refusal, and a refusal to relink a symlink that already resolves into the plugin venv.

ONE THING TO TELL ANOTHER PACKET NOW: W3-07 must add `sentinel-scout` to its control.sock announcement, or the first night after both land produces undismissable sticky-critical R-SELF toasts caused by Sentinel's own nightly watchlist write.

### W3-05 · integration

W3-05 is implementable but three of its eight Context claims are stale or refuted against the live machine, and one of them (claim 3, PATH order) is the load-bearing justification for the whole "install truth" change. The Owner's login PATH now puts ~/.local/bin FIRST (as the non-canonical string /home/tav/.local/share/../bin, via uv's /home/tav/.local/bin/env), so the packet's specified check — string-compare `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` against `$BIN_DIR/<name>` — MISMATCHES on the real machine and would make install-wrappers.sh exit 1 with a PATH diagnosis that is factually wrong. Separately, W3-05 creates src/sentinel/vendors.py and rewrites the watchlist/scout contract that W3-03 also creates and rewrites, touches rules.py alongside W3-03 and W3-04, and touches daemon operational-file semantics that W3-07 redefines; two undeclared collisions also exist (TEST-02, currently uncommitted in .worktrees/grok-TEST-02, and W3-06, which owns README.md). The single largest live-machine risk is that the click path W3-05 builds makes "kill session" reachable in two keystrokes from a toast on a machine where a real daemon (pid 18419) has been running for 6h38m.

**Findings**

- Baseline verified: /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel is on feature/omarchy-sentinel at ca0465a, `.venv/bin/pytest -q` gives 210 passed in 1.99s. Only two sibling worktrees exist here (.worktrees/grok-TEST-02, .worktrees/grok-UX-02a); W3-03/W3-04/W3-07 are running elsewhere, so I could not diff their trees and the collisions below are inferred from their packets.
- CLAIM 3 IS REFUTED. `bash -lc 'echo $PATH; command -v claude'` gives PATH starting with `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` (= ~/.local/bin) at position 1, ahead of /usr/share/omarchy/bin and every mise dir, and `command -v claude` resolves to `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude`. This is set by /home/tav/.local/bin/env (uv's shim, mtime 2026-09-05 20:34), which prepends the literal `$HOME/.local/share/../bin`. The packet's premise — 'a shim placed in ~/.local/bin is never executed', 'position 12', 'resolves to ~/.local/share/mise/installs/claude/latest/claude' — no longer holds for a login shell.
- CLAIM 4 IS CONFIRMED and now matters more: /home/tav/.local/bin/claude, codex, grok, gemini are mise launcher scripts (`mise use -g --quiet "claude" || exit 1; exec mise x "claude" -- "claude" "$@"`). Because ~/.local/bin is now first, `scripts/install-wrappers.sh` would BACK UP AND REPLACE the launcher the Owner actually uses. `backup_if_needed` (scripts/install-wrappers.sh line 28) renames it to `*.sentinel-bak.<ts>`, and `find_real` would then resolve the real binary to the mise install dir. That is a live-tooling change, not a no-op, and the packet's Non-goals say the installers are changed, not run — so this must stay Owner-run and dry-run-first.
- CLAIM 6 IS REFUTED. `sentinel`, `sentinel-action`, `sentinel-scout` ALREADY EXIST as symlinks in /home/tav/.local/bin (created 2026-09-05 00:23) pointing into /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin — installed by step 2 of /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/scripts/install-daemon.sh (lines 49-53), whose own header comment already says '~/.local/bin/sentinel{,-action,-scout} (on omarchy-shell's PATH)'. Furthermore /usr/share/omarchy/shell/Commons/Util.qml `execArgv()` runs the toast click as `bash -lc 'exec "$@"' bash <argv>` — a LOGIN shell — so `sentinel-action` is already resolvable on click today. The new scripts/install-cli.sh in W3-05 duplicates install-daemon.sh step 2; the real defect is the missing tty, not resolvability.
- CLAIM 5 IS PARTIALLY WRONG on the path: `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` lives at /usr/share/omarchy/bin, not /usr/bin. Its behavior is confirmed: it sources omarchy-restart-gum, sets `cmd="$*"`, and execs `setsid uwsm-app -- xdg-terminal-exec --app-id=org.omarchy.terminal -e bash -c "omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; if (( $? != 130 )); then omarchy-show-done; fi"`. xdg-terminal-exec, uwsm-app and gum are all present at /usr/bin.
- SECURITY NOTE ON THE CLICK PATH: omarchy-shell hands the --exec argv to bash as positional parameters with no re-tokenizing (Util.qml comment is explicit), but `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` then collapses it with `cmd="$*"` and re-parses it through `bash -c`. So the wrapper REINTRODUCES shell interpretation that omarchy-shell deliberately removed. Today's payload is safe (Alert.id is `str(uuid4())` per src/sentinel/models.py line 53, hex and dashes only), but this must be written into the code as an invariant: nothing agent-controlled (a path, a cmdline, a summary) may ever enter the --exec argv.

Hashing requires opening it. W3-05 even notes the tension ('Reading the key value would require reading the body, which the spec forbids') but does not resolve it for the hash itself. This is a blocking spec contradiction and needs an explicit Chief ruling: open-to-hash-but-never-store is either allowed or it is not.
- COLLISION 3 (hard) — the watchlist.json schema. W3-03 rewrites src/sentinel/scout.py wholesale (classify_kind signature, scout(home, project_roots), 'version': 2, per-entry 'vendor', drops 'other' entries, atomic write) and adds daemon hot-reload keyed on writes to watchlist.json. - COLLISION 4 — src/sentinel/rules.py is touched by three packets at once: W3-05 (DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS at line 12, extract_bypass_flags at line 109, evaluate_process at line 124 for S4 redaction), W3-04 (per-kind severity in evaluate_child_shell, currently hard-coded severity='high' at line 384, and the curl case at line 328), and W3-03 (_path_under at line 149, pre-resolved roots). These are different functions in the same file — textually mergeable, semantically independent, but any of them touching the module header (imports, the frozenset block) will conflict.

W3-05 sits downstream of both: if W3-05 has the scout emit R-BYPASS-CONFIG alerts, the scout process appends to alerts.jsonl via src/sentinel/store.py `append_alert`, and W3-07 classifies any write to an operational file that the daemon did not make as 'foreign' → R-SELF high. W3-07's design only teaches the daemon and `sentinel-action` to announce their writes over control.sock; `sentinel-scout` is not covered, and it already writes watchlist.json on a nightly enabled timer.
- COLLISION 6 (undeclared by the orchestrator) — TEST-02 is in flight and uncommitted at /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/.worktrees/grok-TEST-02/tests/acceptance/test_blackbox_grok.py (715 lines, plus an unwritten report). - LIVE DAEMON, EXACTLY: pid 18419, `/home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin/python3 .../bin/sentinel daemon`, started Sat Sep 5 20:03:19 2026, elapsed 6h38m, cwd /home/tav, interpreter /usr/bin/python3.14. **Risks**

- THE INSTALL-TRUTH CHECK AS SPECIFIED IS BROKEN ON THIS MACHINE. The packet says compare `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` to the shim path. Reproduced: resolved=/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude, BIN_DIR=/home/tav/.local/bin → naive string compare MISMATCH, but `readlink -f` on both sides MATCHES. As written, install-wrappers.sh would exit 1 on the Owner's own machine and print a PATH-reordering fix for a PATH that is already correct. FIX: canonicalize both sides with `readlink -f` (or `cd "$(dirname …)" && pwd -P`) before comparing, and add a regression test using a non-canonical BIN_DIR such as $HOME/.local/share/../bin.
- `bash -lc` IS THE WRONG ORACLE ANYWAY. quickshell's own environment (pid 62861) has PATH=/usr/share/omarchy/bin:…/mise/shims:…:/usr/bin:/home/tav/.local/bin:… — mise shims at position 2, ~/.local/bin at position 7. A login shell puts ~/.local/bin first; a GUI-launched process does not. So an agent started from a terminal would hit a Sentinel shim while one started from the shell/menu would hit the mise shim. A check that only consults `bash -lc` will report 'installed correctly' while the shim is still bypassed for GUI launches. The installer should report BOTH resolutions, or the packet should state plainly which launch path it covers.
- TWO KEYSTROKES TO A REAL KILL. The interactive menu puts '4 approve forever', '5 kill child' and '6 kill session' on adjacent digits, dispatched from a toast click. Inside the floating terminal stdin IS a tty, so src/sentinel/kill.py `confirm_kill` (line 71) takes its interactive branch and a non-precious session kill needs only a y/n; only a PRECIOUS worktree demands the typed phrase 'kill session' (line 95). Before W3-05 this path was unreachable from a toast. On a machine that has been running agents for hours this is the biggest live regression vector. RECOMMEND: kill rows must not be single digits — require a typed word — and 'approve forever' (which writes a permanent allowlist entry; the Owner has 95 of them) must not sit one key away from a kill.
- A HANGING TEST SUITE. If the interactive branch of cmd_menu (src/sentinel/action.py line 210) keys on `sys.stdin.isatty()` without the injectable seam the packet asks for, `pytest -s` or any CI without captured stdin will block forever on `input()`. tests/joint/test_adversarial.py `_run_cli` (line 104) already drives `action_main` directly under redirect_stdout, and TEST-02's acceptance suite drives the real CLI in-process too — both would be exposed. The isatty and prompt callables must be parameters with defaults, exactly like kill.confirm_kill already does (isatty/prompt/warn at lines 76-78); copy that shape.
- FALSE R-SELF STORM FROM W3-05 x W3-07. W3-07 removes the OPERATIONAL_NAMES ignore and treats any write the daemon did not make as foreign → R-SELF high, which src/sentinel/notify.py `is_sticky` sends as CRITICAL WITH NO TIMEOUT and IGNORING PAUSE. W3-05 makes `sentinel-scout` an alert emitter (it appends to alerts.jsonl) and it already rewrites watchlist.json nightly under an enabled timer. Unless sentinel-scout is added to W3-07's control.sock announcement, the first night after both land produces undismissable critical toasts caused by Sentinel itself. Flag this to the W3-07 agent now, not at merge.
- THE R-BYPASS-CONFIG ALERT IS NEAR-USELESS AS SPECIFIED, AND MAY BE WORSE THAN NOTHING. It can only say 'this file changed', never which key changed. ~/.grok/config.toml, ~/.codex/config.toml and ~/.gemini/settings.json are edited by their own CLIs for ordinary reasons (theme, model, last-used project), so the medium alert will fire on benign edits and train the Owner to dismiss it — the exact desensitization that produced the 127-alert R-HOOK-WRITE storm already recorded in docs/collab/LEDGER.md. The packet itself says to ask the Chief about a narrow keyed read; that ask should be raised BEFORE implementation, and it overlaps open decision D-005 in the ledger.
- SHELL RE-PARSING IN THE PRESENTATION WRAPPER. omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation does `cmd="$*"` then `bash -c "omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; …"`. Safe for a uuid4 alert id, unsafe forever after. If anyone later adds a path or a basename to the exec argv it becomes command injection from an agent-controlled string, and omarchy-shell's careful no-re-tokenizing guarantee (Util.qml execArgv) will not save it. Needs a code comment at the _exec_tail definition and a test asserting the argv contains only the fixed program names plus a hex/dash id.
- SILENT CLICK FAILURE. The wrapper chain is setsid → uwsm-app → xdg-terminal-exec → bash. If any link fails in the shell's exec environment the click does nothing and there is no error surface at all — worse than today, where sentinel-action at least runs (headless, output discarded). This cannot be proven by pytest; it needs an Owner-run live smoke of one synthetic alert from an isolated XDG dir, the same way UX-01 was proven on 2026-09-05 15:18.
- WRAPPING EVERY MENU ROW DEGRADES TWO OF THEM. `sentinel-action pause 1h` and `sentinel-scout --refresh` need no input, yet the packet's tests/test_menu_jsonc.py would assert that EVERY action starts with the wrapper — so both now open a floating terminal and wait on omarchy-show-done's 'Press any key to close'. Only the rows that need a tty (list, status, and the per-alert menu) should be wrapped; the test should encode that distinction rather than a blanket rule.
- install-cli.sh DUPLICATES install-daemon.sh AND CAN BREAK THE LIVE CLI. The three symlinks already exist and point at /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin. A new installer with its own 'backup semantics like the wrapper installer' could rename a working symlink to *.sentinel-bak.<ts> and relink to a DIFFERENT venv (this worktree's .venv), silently switching the Owner's live sentinel-action onto unmerged code. Either fold the CLI step into the existing install-daemon.sh or make install-cli.sh refuse to touch a symlink that already resolves into the plugin venv.
- PACKET LINE NUMBERS HAVE DRIFTED, which the ledger's own 'Lessons' row warns about. DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS is at src/sentinel/rules.py:12-20 not 11-19; cmd_menu is at src/sentinel/action.py:210-222 not 214-233; build_argv is at src/sentinel/notify.py:223 not 52-55; tests/test_notify.py pins the tail at lines 55-57 and 104, not 45-47. An implementer that trusts these will either fail to find the code or, worse, edit the wrong region.
- UNVERIFIED BY DESIGN: I did not run `grok --help`, `claude --help`, `codex --help` or `gemini --help`, and I did not read ~/.grok/config.toml — the packet's own Forbidden section bars reading vendor config bodies, which is in direct tension with its Context claim 2 asserting that file's contents. The vendor table's argv_bypass tokens therefore remain unverified from my side and the implementing agent must produce the --help output as the packet's Fact-check section requires.

**Settled by the squad**

- BASELINE, re-run by me at ca0465a: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` → 210 passed in 1.91s; `--collect-only` → 210 tests collected. Any report claiming a different number must have re-run it.
- CLAIM 3 IS REFUTED — all four agents converge and I reproduced it. `bash -lc 'echo $PATH'` puts `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` (which IS ~/.local/bin) at position 1, ahead of /usr/share/omarchy/bin and every mise entry; `command -v claude` → `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude`. Cause confirmed by reading /home/tav/.local/bin/env (mtime 2026-09-05 20:34), a uv-style prepender whose case statement injects that exact spelling. The packet's premise 'a shim placed in ~/.local/bin is never executed' is false today.
- THE STRING-COMPARISON BUG IS REAL — reproduced exactly. resolved=`/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude`, target=`/home/tav/.local/bin/claude`; `[ "$r" = "$t" ]` → MISMATCH, `[ "$r" -ef "$t" ]` → MATCH, `realpath` on both → identical. R14 as literally specified makes install-wrappers.sh exit 1 on a correctly installed shim on the Owner's own machine. Canonicalize (`-ef` or `realpath`) before comparing, and add a regression case using a non-canonical BIN_DIR.
- `bash -lc` CANNOT BE MADE HERMETIC — I ran the spec agent's exact experiment. `env -i HOME=/home/tav PATH=/tmp/sb:/usr/bin:/bin bash -lc 'command -v claude'` still returns `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude` with PATHHEAD=`/home/tav/.local/share/../bin`: the login profile rebuilds PATH and discards the injected one. So acceptance command 3 cannot produce exit=1 for the right reason, and tests/test_install_wrappers cannot write its exit-0 half. The installer needs an injectable probe (the tests agent's `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` is the cleanest shape) or a fixture HOME.
- VENDOR FLAGS — I re-ran the --help commands and confirm the spec and codebase agents' capture, which agree with each other. grok: `--always-approve`, `--permission-mode <default|acceptEdits|auto|dontAsk|bypassPermissions|plan>`, `--sandbox <PROFILE>`, `--allow <RULE>`. codex: `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, `--approve-for-me`, `-s/--sandbox`, `-a/--ask-for-approval`; NO --yolo, NO --full-auto. gemini: `-y/--yolo`, `--approval-mode <default|auto_edit|yolo|plan>`, `--skip-trust`. claude: `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` (an ENABLER, not itself a bypass), `--permission-mode <mode>`, `--permission-prompts`. Both `cursor` and `cursor-agent` are ABSENT — note DEFAULT_AGENT_BASENAMES contains both, so a table honouring it carries two UNVERIFIED entries.
- CURRENT DETECTION BEHAVIOUR, measured not inferred: `--always-approve` → [], `--permission-mode dontAsk` → [], `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` → [], `--yolo` → ['--yolo'], `--permission-mode plan` → [] (correctly), `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` → ['bypassPermissions'] (fires TODAY via the bare token), `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` → [] (a live false negative), `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` → [] (correctly). The codebase agent's asymmetry finding and the tests agent's joined-form test are both justified.
- CLAIM 6 IS REFUTED — confirmed by the codebase and integration agents and by me. `ls -la ~/.local/bin` shows sentinel, sentinel-action and sentinel-scout as symlinks into /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin dated Sep 5 00:23, placed by install-daemon.sh step 2 (lines 49-53). Util.qml `execArgv` is `['bash','-lc','exec "$@"','bash'].concat(argv)` and `execDetached` is `['bash','-lc',command]` — both LOGIN shells, both inheriting the PATH where ~/.local/bin is first. sentinel-action is already reachable from a toast. The defect W3-05 actually fixes is the missing tty, not resolvability, and the packet's rationale must be rewritten to say so.
- THE SHELL RE-PARSE IS REAL AND MUST BE PINNED IN CODE. `/usr/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` does `cmd="$*"` then `bash -c "omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; ..."`. omarchy-shell deliberately avoids re-tokenizing (Util.qml's own comment says so) and this wrapper reintroduces it. Safe today only because Alert.id is `str(uuid4())` in `Alert.new`. The security agent's fix is the right one: validate the id in `_exec_tail`/`build_argv` and reject whitespace or `; | & $ ( ) < >` in any tail element — and, critically, DEGRADE the tail rather than return None, because build_argv is shared with the sticky R-SELF path at notify.py:422 and a guard that suppresses the toast would silence a tamper alert.
- THE `hashes` CLOUD LEAK IS A GENUINELY NEW FINDING, and only the security agent caught it. Verified: `summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS` (src/sentinel/summarize.py:16-28) includes `"hashes"`. Once R-BYPASS-CONFIG puts a digest of ~/.grok/config.toml into `Alert.hashes`, a Summarize click ships that digest to api.x.ai. For a file whose variable content is essentially one line, a digest is close to a content oracle. This must be resolved before implementation, not after.
- SEAMS AND PINS. The exec_prefix seam already exists at notify.py:219, 227 and 383 with the in-code comment naming W3-05 — the change is the default tuple, not a restructure. The per-alert tail is pinned in exactly two places, tests/test_notify.py:57 (full-argv equality) and :104 (`calls[0][-4:]`, which must become `[-5:]`); tests/test_notify_burst.py pins the SUMMARY tail via build_summary_argv (notify.py:243-270), which this packet does not touch and must not 'tidy'. install-menu.sh hard-codes the five menu keys at line 104. DEFAULT_AGENT_BASENAMES is duplicated identically in rules.py:43 and daemon.py:34, and neither includes gemini.
- THE HANG RISK IS REAL. tests/joint/test_adversarial.py drives `action_main` in-process under `redirect_stdout` (lines 104-107). If cmd_menu's interactive branch keys on `sys.stdin.isatty()` with no injectable seam, `pytest -s` and that suite block on `input()` forever — and tests/joint/ is the Chief's, so nobody on this packet may fix it. The seam must be keyword parameters with defaults, mirroring `kill.confirm_kill(..., isatty=, prompt=)`, never an env var (the security agent is right that an env var hands a monitored agent a non-interactive route to option 5).
- NO SILENT AUTO-KILL. cmd_kill must be called with `yes=False` from the menu so `kill.confirm_kill` still gates it. The double-confirm the spec and codebase agents flagged is correct behaviour, not a bug — but it is a deliberate decision that belongs in the report. The integration agent's stronger point stands too: '4 approve forever' sitting one digit from '5 kill child' on a machine with 95 stored approvals is a UX hazard worth raising with the Chief.
- shellcheck IS NOT INSTALLED (`command -v shellcheck` → empty; installing it needs sudo, which is forbidden). Acceptance line 2 is a documented SKIP under AGENTS.md's 'if it is installed' wording. It must be reported UNVERIFIED and must not be counted as a pass. The tests agent's suggestion — a pytest test that skips when `shutil.which('shellcheck')` is None — is the right permanent answer.
- LIVE-MACHINE SAFETY. Daemon pid 18419 is running from /home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv, whose editable .pth reads `/home/tav/.config/omarchy/plugins/tav.sentinel/src` — NOT this worktree. I read the .pth to confirm. Merging W3-05 changes nothing live, and that .pth is exactly why 'no writes under ~/.config' is a real safety rule rather than a formality. Both installers may only ever be exercised with SENTINEL_BIN_DIR pointed at a temp dir.
- README.md ALREADY MAKES BOTH CLAIMS FALSELY — verified: line 34 'refuses to finish if its shim would be shadowed by something earlier on your PATH' and line 43 'click one to open the alert in a floating terminal'. Landing W3-05 makes both true. That is the overlap with W3-06, which is in progress and owns README.md.
- THE FOUR CROSS-PACKET BLOCKERS, on which all five of us agree and which need a Chief ruling BEFORE Grok launches: (1) R10 requires hashing a vendor config while ## Forbidden bans 'reading vendor config bodies' and W3-03 bans opening scouted files — the packet must say 'hashing is permitted; never store or surface the body'. (2) src/sentinel/vendors.py and the watchlist.json schema are created and rewritten by both W3-03 and W3-05, which are declared parallel-safe and are not — serialize W3-03 first or make W3-05 declare `Depends on: W3-03`. (3) Reporting `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` changes `keys.alert_flag_set` → `allowlist.fingerprint`, silently voiding stored approvals; keys.py's own docstring names this as the invariant. (4) The S4 redaction requirement is pasted under `## Non-goals` where a literal reader takes the packet's only security-finding closure as out of scope — move it to `## Changes`. W3-03 has the identical defect with its S5 paragraph.

**Final position.** W3-05 must not launch as written. The four analysts converge on the same picture and I confirmed it against the tree and the machine rather than trusting any of them; the disagreements between them are small and I have named them above.

WHAT THE FIVE OF US SHOULD TREAT AS SETTLED

Machine facts, each re-measured by me at ca0465a: baseline is 210 passed / 210 collected in ~1.9s. Claim 3 is REFUTED — ~/.local/bin is PATH position 1 for a login shell (as the non-canonical spelling /home/tav/.local/share/../bin, injected by ~/.local/bin/env), so a shim written there IS executed. Claim 6 is REFUTED — sentinel, sentinel-action and sentinel-scout have been symlinked into ~/.local/bin since 2026-09-05 00:23 by install-daemon.sh step 2, and omarchy-shell reaches them through bash -lc in both Util.qml paths. Claim 5 is CONFIRMED and the packet's /usr/bin path is correct (the /usr/share/omarchy/bin entry is a symlink to it — the integration agent's "correction" was the error). Claim 1 is CONFIRMED by re-running the four --help commands; cursor and cursor-agent are both absent and stay UNVERIFIED. Claim 2 is UNVERIFIABLE BY DESIGN. ~/.codex/config.toml does not exist on this machine; ~/.grok/config.toml does and its mtime moved from 02:22 to 02:54 between the analysts' runs and mine — which is itself the best evidence for the R-BYPASS-CONFIG false-positive argument.

Two design defects are settled and reproducible. The mandated string comparison in R14 reports a false shadow on the Owner's own machine (resolved and target are the same file, different strings; -ef and realpath both match) — it must canonicalize. And `bash -lc` cannot be made hermetic: even under `env -i` with an injected PATH, the login profile rebuilds PATH and returns the real-home path, so acceptance command 3 cannot fail for the right reason and the required exit-0 test cannot be written. The installer needs an injectable probe.

Cite symbols, not the packet's line numbers. Every range in the Context section has drifted: DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS is rules.py:12-20, extract_bypass_flags rules.py:109, evaluate_process rules.py:124, _exec_tail notify.py:219, build_argv notify.py:223, cmd_menu action.py:210, cmd_kill action.py:393, the notify pins test_notify.py:57 and :104. The LEDGER already records that stale line numbers burned a Grok run on UX-01.

THE SIX EDITS THE CHIEF OWES THE PACKET BEFORE IT LAUNCHES

Rewrite Context claims 3 and 6 to what is actually true (the click-path defect is no tty, not no PATH; the install-truth check is worth building as a guard, not as a fix for a present breakage). Move the S4 redaction paragraph out of ## Non-goals into ## Changes, and apply the same fix to W3-03's S5 paragraph. Amend ## Forbidden to "never store or surface a config body; hashing for change detection is permitted", and pre-mark claim 2 UNVERIFIED-BY-DESIGN. Declare `Depends on: W3-03` or serialize the two, since they collide on vendors.py and on watchlist.json. Rule on the fingerprint break from the canonical `=` form. And decide whether install-cli.sh should exist at all, given install-daemon.sh step 2 already does the job and the only real gap is that `ln -sfn` clobbers instead of backing up.

WHAT I WOULD ADD THAT NO ONE ELSE PUT FIRST

Two things deserve to be raised as asks before implementation rather than discovered at review. First, the `hashes` field is already in summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS, so R-BYPASS-CONFIG's digest would ride to api.x.ai on a Summarize click — resolve that before the rule exists, not after. Second, R-BYPASS-CONFIG as specified can only ever say "this file changed", on files their own CLIs rewrite for unrelated reasons, and its baseline lives in a file the monitored agent can write. That is a medium-severity alert with a high false-positive rate, no actionable content, and no tamper resistance until W3-07 lands. The packet already tells the implementer to ask about a narrow keyed read; that ask should be filed first, and it overlaps open decision D-005.

Read-only throughout. No files were edited; the only writes anywhere were the vendors' own --help invocations, which touch nothing.

### W3-05 · security

Security analysis of packet W3-05 (`docs/collab/packets/W3-05-vendor-bypass-install-truth-click-path.md`), read against `docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md` §S4/S11 and PROTOCOL §9. Read-only; no files edited. Verdict: the packet is implementable without breaking any invariant, but three of its four changes sit directly on top of one. (1) The click-path change is a real structural downgrade of "argv arrays only": I read `/usr/share/omarchy/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` on this machine and it does `cmd="$*"` then `bash -c "omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; ..."` — everything after argv[0] becomes shell source text. Sentinel is safe today only because `Alert.new` sets `id=str(uuid4())` (`src/sentinel/models.py:53`); the invariant must be re-established by validating the id in `notify.build_argv`, not left resting on the id's shape. (2) The new `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` rule opens files inside directories the monitored agent controls, one symlink away from `~/.claude/.credentials.json` and `~/.ssh`, so hashing needs `O_NOFOLLOW` + a regular-file check + an auth-kind refusal. (3) The S4 redaction requirement — the only reason this packet closes a security finding — is pasted under `## Non-goals` at line 44, above the actual non-goal bullets, where a literal reader can take it as out of scope. That is the single highest-value fix before launch. Two packet defects also need a Chief edit: the Fact-check section requires verifying claim 2 (the contents of `~/.grok/config.toml`) while the packet's own Forbidden section says "Reading vendor config bodies", and `install-cli.sh` is exempted from the PATH-truth check that `install-wrappers.sh` is required to grow.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT 1 — Runs as the user, never root. How W3-05 could break it: both installers derive their target from `BIN_DIR="${SENTINEL_BIN_DIR:-$HOME/.local/bin}"` (`scripts/install-wrappers.sh:15`) and neither has a root guard today; under `sudo` the new `scripts/install-cli.sh` would drop root-owned symlinks to the venv into root's `~/.local/bin`, and the packet's new truth check `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` would resolve root's PATH and source root's profile. `bash -l` also executes the user's rc, which a monitored agent can edit — so this installer now runs agent-writable code, and doing that with elevated privileges is escalation. Separately, the interactive `cmd_menu` adds a kill entry point; if it reaches `os.kill` by any route other than `kill.plan_kill` it loses the uid and start-time checks at `src/sentinel/kill.py:131-166`. What the code must do: add `[[ $EUID -eq 0 ]]` and `[[ -n ${SUDO_USER:-} ]]` refusals at the top of both `scripts/install-wrappers.sh` and `scripts/install-cli.sh`; treat `bash -lc` output as a string to compare, never to `eval`; have `install-cli.sh` verify each venv source is a regular file owned by the invoking uid and not group/other-writable before symlinking (S11 makes the same demand of `BarWidget.qml` `cliArgv`); and route menu options 5/6 through the existing `action.cmd_kill` → `kill.plan_kill` path unchanged.
- INVARIANT 2 — Metadata only, never file bodies or credentials. Three distinct ways this packet can break it. (a) `R-BYPASS-CONFIG`: the vendor table's `config_bypass` field is specified as "file, TOML/JSON key path, values that mean bypass", which invites a `tomllib.load` of `~/.grok/config.toml` to read `permission_mode`. No code may do that — the packet already says so and tells the implementer to ask the Chief rather than act unilaterally. The table may declare the key path as static documentation; the alert summary must be a table-derived sentence ("~/.grok/config.toml changed; this file can set permission_mode") with the limitation stated, never an observed value. Hashing is the one permitted read: the bytes must reach nothing but the digest — no content in `evidence`, and `Alert.hashes` (`src/sentinel/models.py:29`) gets `{"sha256": <hex>}` and nothing else. (b) S4 redaction: `record_launch` writes the full argv at `src/sentinel/wrap_record.py:35` and `51-52`, and `evaluate_process` copies it into the alert at `src/sentinel/rules.py:143`. Both write paths need redaction — the daemon's /proc sampler never goes through `record_launch` — and it must replace values, not drop tokens: `["--api-key","sk-test"]` → `["--api-key","<redacted>"]`, `--api-key=sk-test` → `--api-key=<redacted>`, bare bearer/key-shaped tokens → `<redacted>`, with `evidence["redacted"] = true` set on the alert and on the `launches.jsonl` row (otherwise a row is ambiguous between redacted and absent). Redaction must fire before `json.dumps` in `record_launch`, so no unredacted copy ever touches disk. (c) The report: `docs/collab/reports/W3-05-report.md` is committed history, so no vendor config content and no `--help` output containing a machine-specific value may be pasted into it.
- INVARIANT 2, ordering traps that will silently break Approve. `keys.alert_flag_set` (`src/sentinel/keys.py:29-38`) and `summarize._flag_list` (`src/sentinel/summarize.py:33-40`) both fall back to `extract_bypass_flags(alert.cmdline)` when evidence carries no flags. If redaction lands on `cmdline` and any path re-derives flags from the redacted copy, the fingerprint the CLI computes stops matching the one the daemon suppresses on, and Approve becomes a no-op with no error — exactly the failure `keys.py`'s module docstring names as its invariant. Two requirements: `evaluate_process` must always populate `evidence["flags"]` (it does today, `rules.py:145`) so the fallback is never taken for a redacted alert; and the redaction regex must match only on the flag *name* after stripping dashes and any `=value`, so that a value-taking bypass pair like `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` — which W3-05 is specifically adding — is never eaten by a naive "redact the token after a flag" rule. Add a test that a redacted `--api-key sk-test` argv still yields the same fingerprint as the unredacted one, alongside the packet's existing `tests/test_vendors.py` cases.
- INVARIANT 3 — auth-kind files are never opened. `scout.classify_kind` (`src/sentinel/scout.py:27-40`) already labels `auth` on `credentials`/`auth` names, and W3-03's Forbidden section calls out `~/.claude.json` as holding an OAuth account block. W3-05 introduces the first code that deliberately opens a vendor file. The sharp edge: the hash target lives in a directory the monitored agent can write, so an agent can replace `~/.grok/config.toml` with a symlink to `~/.claude/.credentials.json` or `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` and make Sentinel open it for them. What the code must do: `config_bypass` entries are exact relative paths, never globs and never directories; every candidate goes through `classify_kind` (post-W3-03 signature `classify_kind(path, vendor)`) and is refused if the kind is `auth` or the basename appears in that vendor's `auth` list; the open is `os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NOFOLLOW)` with `stat.S_ISREG` checked on the resulting fd and a size cap (1 MiB) so a symlink to `/dev/zero` or a padded file cannot hang the scout; and the resolved path must stay inside the vendor's `home`. Test it by putting `.credentials.json` into a fixture vendor's `config_bypass` and asserting the hasher skips it and emits no alert, plus a symlink-escape case.
- INVARIANT 4 — State files 0600 inside 0700 dirs. `src/sentinel/fsutil.py` is the only sanctioned writer (`ensure_private_dir` line 19, `open_private` line 30, `write_private_atomic` line 44), and `scout.write_watchlist` (`src/sentinel/scout.py:84-87`) already uses it. Ways W3-05 could regress: any new module (`vendors.py`, or a hash cache separate from `watchlist.json`) reaching for `Path.write_text` or bare `open(..., "w")` under umask 022, which reintroduces S3 for a file that now records which agents have bypass configs; `scripts/install-cli.sh` writing anything under `~/.local/state/sentinel` or `~/.config/sentinel`, which belong to `fsutil`; and the interactive `cmd_menu` persisting a history or transcript of the user's choices. What the code must do: keep every new state write on `write_private_atomic`/`open_private` (grep the diff for `write_text(`/`open(` in new files); have `install-cli.sh` touch only `$BIN_DIR` and refuse outright if `$BIN_DIR` is group- or world-writable — a writable `~/.local/bin` lets an agent swap the `sentinel-action` symlink and own the very click path this packet is building; and store nothing from the interactive menu.
- INVARIANT 5 — argv arrays only, never shell strings. This is the packet's headline risk and it is a genuine downgrade, not a theoretical one. Today the chain is clean: `notify.build_argv` (`src/sentinel/notify.py:219-235`) returns a list, `omarchy-notification-send:178` serializes it with `jq -Rsc` into the `omarchy-exec-argv` hint, and `NotificationLogic.js:160-177` `parseExecArgv` validates it structurally and hands back a real argv array — no shell anywhere. W3-05 inserts `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` as argv[0]. That script, read on this machine, does `cmd="$*"` then `presentation_script="omarchy-show-logo; $cmd; ..."` then `exec setsid uwsm-app -- xdg-terminal-exec ... -e bash -c "$presentation_script"`. Every element after argv[0] is joined on IFS and interpolated into shell source. The only thing standing between an alert field and code execution in the user's session is that `Alert.new` sets `id=str(uuid4())` (`src/sentinel/models.py:53`). What the code must do: re-establish the invariant explicitly at the boundary in `notify._exec_tail`/`build_argv` — require `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-fA-F-]{36}", alert.id)` and reject any exec-tail element containing whitespace or any of `; | & $ ( ) < > ` newline`. On failure, degrade the tail to the alert-free `--exec omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation sentinel-action list`; do not suppress the toast (see invariant 8). Add a `tests/test_notify.py` case with `alert.id = "x; touch /tmp/pwn"` asserting no emitted argv contains `;`.
- INVARIANT 5, the other two surfaces. `packaging/omarchy-menu-sentinel.jsonc` action strings are shell by platform design — `Menu.qml:137-141` `runAction` does `Util.execDetached(String(action))` with no array anywhere — so those rows must stay static literals forever. `tests/test_menu_jsonc.py` should assert more than the packet's "starts with `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation`": also that no action contains a shell metacharacter or a `{}`/`$` template placeholder, which is what makes "alert data can never reach this file" a checked property rather than a convention. In `cmd_menu`, the numbered dispatch must map the digit through a dict of Python callables to the existing `cmd_approve`/`cmd_kill`/`cmd_investigate`/`cmd_summarize`/`cmd_dismiss` functions (`src/sentinel/action.py:154-233`) and reject anything not in the dict — never `subprocess.run(..., shell=True)` and never a command string built from user input. `install-cli.sh` should quote with `printf '%q'` the way `install-wrappers.sh:105-107` already does, and must not `eval` the `bash -lc` result. The `$PAGER` path is already safe (`investigate._pager_argv` at `src/sentinel/investigate.py:532-535` uses `shlex.split` into an argv list); leave it alone.
- INVARIANT 6 — No network from the daemon. W3-05 adds no HTTP, but it can break this through the import graph: `src/sentinel/summarize.py:6-7` imports `urllib.error`/`urllib.request` at module scope, and `vendors.py` will be imported by `rules.py` and the daemon. If the redaction helper is placed in `summarize.py` and imported from `vendors.py` or `rules.py`, the daemon inherits `urllib` — the dependency must run the other way. Put redaction in `rules.py` or a new `redact.py` whose only import is `re`, and let `summarize.py` import from it. Second risk: `vendors.py` must be static data. Do not have it shell out to `grok --help`/`claude --help` at runtime to discover flags — the packet's Fact-check runs those commands once, by hand, to author the table, and that is where it ends. Third: the new `bash -lc` check invokes a login shell, which on this machine sources mise and can make network calls from the user's rc. That is an installer, not the daemon, so it does not violate the invariant, but keep it to `command -v` and add no `--version` probes of vendor CLIs. Extend whatever test asserts the daemon's import graph to cover `sentinel.vendors`.
- INVARIANT 7 — No silent auto-kill. The interactive `cmd_menu` is the entire risk: it puts `5 kill child` and `6 kill session` one keystroke away from a toast click. `kill.confirm_kill` (`src/sentinel/kill.py:74-104`) is the gate — `yes=True` returns True immediately, `yes=False` requires a tty and asks `Kill target PIDs? [y/N]`, and a session kill in a precious worktree demands the typed phrase `kill session` regardless. What the code must do: call `cmd_kill(alert_id, session=..., yes=False)`. The temptation to pass `yes=True` "because the user already picked 5" is exactly the invariant's failure mode — it collapses toast-click plus one digit into a kill with no confirm. The prompt must default to no-action on empty input, EOF, or any unrecognized token, with `0 nothing` as the fallback rather than any destructive entry. Non-tty must keep the existing cheat-sheet behavior and dispatch nothing; add a test asserting a non-tty `menu` never reaches `cmd_kill`. Finally, the `isatty`/prompt injection seam the packet asks for in `tests/test_action.py::test_menu_interactive_dispatch` must be keyword parameters with defaults, mirroring `kill.confirm_kill(..., isatty=None, prompt=None)` — never an environment variable, or a monitored agent can set it and drive the menu to option 5 without a human.
- INVARIANT 8 — Sticky self-tamper alerts bypass pause and prefs. The mechanism: `notify.is_sticky` (`src/sentinel/notify.py:78-80`) is `rule == "R-SELF"` and `evidence["event"]` in `STICKY_EVENTS` (`notify.py:40`, deliberately code and not config), and `Notifier.send` (`notify.py:420-424`) checks it as its first statement, before `self._is_paused()` and before `self.effective()` reads prefs. Three ways W3-05 could break it. First, `build_argv` is shared: the sticky branch calls `build_argv(alert, STICKY, exec_prefix=self._exec_prefix)` at line 422, so the new exec prefix must apply to sticky toasts too, and the id-validation guard from invariant 5 must degrade the tail rather than return early — a guard written as "return None on a bad id" would silence a tamper alert, which is the worst possible failure here. Second, `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` must not be added to `STICKY_EVENTS`: it is severity medium, it fires on ordinary vendor config edits, and making it critical-and-never-expiring would train the Owner to dismiss the class that actually matters. If the diff touches that frozenset, it is out of scope — file an ask. `action.cmd_notify` prints that set at `src/sentinel/action.py:330` and stays accurate only if it is unchanged. Third, the packet puts `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` in the scout, a separate process: it must emit through `store.append_alert` and `notify.send_alert` so one policy applies. A scout that shells out to `omarchy notification send` directly would give a medium alert the pause- and prefs-bypassing behavior reserved for tamper alerts, inverting the invariant.

**Risks**

- PACKET DEFECT, fix before launch: the S4 redaction requirement is pasted under `## Non-goals` at line 44 of `docs/collab/packets/W3-05-vendor-bypass-install-truth-click-path.md`, above the two real non-goal bullets at lines 47-48. Grok is instructed by `AGENTS.md` to execute the packet as written; a literal reader can take the packet's only security-finding closure as explicitly out of scope. Move it into `## Changes` and move the `--api-key sk-test` case into `## Tests`. The identical malformation exists in `W3-03-scout-v2-precise-watchlist.md` (the S5 paragraph under its Non-goals), so the fix is worth applying to both before either launches.
- PACKET SELF-CONTRADICTION: `## Fact-check` requires verifying claims 1 through 8, and claim 2 asserts that `~/.grok/config.toml` contains `permission_mode = "always-approve"` under `[ui]` — which can only be verified by reading the file body, while `## Forbidden` says "Reading vendor config bodies" and PROTOCOL §9.2 forbids it outright. Resolution the Chief should write into the packet: claim 2 is verified by `stat` only (file exists, is a regular file), the key/value half is marked UNVERIFIED-BY-DESIGN, and no vendor config content is pasted into the committed report.
- NEW CLOUD EXPOSURE the packet does not mention: `summarize.ALLOWED_KEYS` includes `hashes` (`src/sentinel/summarize.py:16-28`) and `build_redacted_bundle` passes `dict(alert.hashes)` through at line 68. Once `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` starts putting a digest of the user's private vendor config into `Alert.hashes`, a Summarize click ships that fingerprint to `https://api.x.ai/v1/chat/completions`. For a file whose only variable content is one `permission_mode` line, a digest is close to a content oracle. Either exclude config-hash alerts' `hashes` from the bundle or label them; `tests/test_summarize_redaction.py` should pin whichever is chosen.
- BASELINE-POISONING, inherent to the design and worth stating in the alert text and README: the packet stores the comparison hash in `watchlist.json`, which lives in the state dir the monitored agent can write, and which the daemon ignores today via `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:42-50`). An agent can edit its own vendor config and then rewrite the stored baseline, and R-BYPASS-CONFIG never fires. This is S1's shape; it is only closed once W3-07's `StateLedger` covers `watchlist.json`. R-BYPASS-CONFIG is tamper evidence at best, and the alert summary and README should say so alongside the "we cannot read the value" limitation the packet already requires. Also define first-run semantics: no stored hash must mean no alert, or every first scout alerts on every vendor.
- ASYMMETRY between the two installers: the packet mandates the `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` truth check only for `scripts/install-wrappers.sh`, while `scripts/install-cli.sh` is the one placing `sentinel-action` at PATH position 12, behind six mise entries per claim 3. Nothing named `sentinel` is in mise today, so it resolves — but the shadow is silent if that ever changes, and the shadowed binary here is the click path itself. Give `install-cli.sh` the same check and the same non-zero exit with the same two printed fixes.
- PRE-EXISTING, out of scope, worth an ASK rather than scope creep: `wrappers/sentinel-wrap:84-85` passes the agent's full argv as command-line arguments to `python -m sentinel.wrap_record`, so any secret in that argv is visible in `/proc/<recorder-pid>/cmdline` to every process on the machine for the recorder's lifetime. S4 redaction fixes what lands in `launches.jsonl`; it does not fix this. The fix is passing argv on stdin, and it belongs in its own packet.
- TEST-SEAM RISK on the new interactive menu: the packet asks for injected `isatty=True` and a prompt returning `9`. If that seam is exposed as an environment variable or a CLI flag rather than keyword parameters, a monitored agent gains a non-interactive way to drive `cmd_menu` into option 5 or 6. Mirror the existing shape in `kill.confirm_kill(..., isatty: bool | None = None, prompt: Callable | None = None)` (`src/sentinel/kill.py:74-79`) and have tests pass them explicitly.
