BLOCKERS, in the order they must be fixed:
(1) Move the S4 redaction paragraph out of `## Non-goals` into `## Changes`. It is the packet's only security-finding closure and it currently reads as out of scope.
(2) Resolve the hash-versus-Forbidden contradiction explicitly: hashing the specific files named in `config_bypass` is permitted, the bytes reach nothing but the digest, and no body ever enters evidence, the alert, or the committed report. Amend the Forbidden line to say so and pre-mark Context claim 2 as stat-only. Without this, an auditor reading only the Forbidden line fails the packet.
(3) Rewrite the Context. Claims 3 and 6 are refuted on the live machine; the install-truth change survives on different grounds (a shim in a non-canonical PATH spelling, and GUI-launched processes seeing mise shims at position 2), and the click-path change survives because there is no tty, not because there is no PATH.
(4) Rule on the vendors.py / watchlist.json collision with W3-03 and on install-cli.sh versus install-daemon.sh step 2 before either agent starts.

DESIGN POINTS I want carried into the packet as written requirements, all verified in the tree today:
- Canonicalize with the `cd ... && pwd -P` idiom already in find_real (install-wrappers.sh:56), not a new realpath dependency.
- Gate the installer's exit 1 on the INHERITED PATH and print the `bash -lc` resolution as advisory. Reject the `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` seam: an agent-settable env var that redefines PATH truth defeats the check it gates, and a temp HOME does not make `bash -lc` hermetic anyway because /etc/profile rebuilds PATH.
- Validate the alert id in notify._exec_tail and DEGRADE the tail to the alert-free form on failure. Never return early — Notifier.send:421 routes sticky R-SELF tamper alerts through the same build_argv, and a suppressing guard would silence exactly the class that must never be silenced.
- Put redact_cmdline in rules.py. summarize.py already imports from rules.py, so this is both the test seam the tests agent asked for and the only placement that keeps urllib out of the daemon's import graph.
- Keep the bare tokens in DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS and add the pair form alongside. The false-positive I and the spec agent both worried about does not exist (exact-element matching), and removing them breaks tests/test_rules.py:23-27.
- Leave secret_flags empty. No installed vendor exposes a secret-bearing argv flag; the generic regex is the whole of S4's exercisable surface.
- Add R-BYPASS-CONFIG to investigate.RULE_TEXT, or the Investigate view drops its explanation silently with no test failure.
- Do NOT add R-BYPASS-CONFIG to STICKY_EVENTS, and do not let the scout shell out to omarchy directly — it must emit through store.append_alert and the Notifier so one pause-and-prefs policy applies.

REMAINING OPEN QUESTION I would raise before implementation, not after: R-BYPASS-CONFIG as specified can only say 'this file changed', its baseline lives in a file the monitored agent can write, and its digest flows into summarize's cloud bundle via ALLOWED_KEYS (summarize.py:25,68). On a file whose only variable content is one permission_mode line, a digest is close to a content oracle. The narrow keyed read the packet defers to the Chief should be asked now, and whichever way it goes, the hashes key for this rule needs an explicit decision about the Summarize bundle.

### W3-05 · spec

Read W3-05 in full plus every file and symbol it cites, and verified the machine-level claims that drive the design. The packet demands 35 distinct testable behaviours across seven areas: a `src/sentinel/vendors.py` bypass table (argv + config), a config-file-hash rule `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` evaluated by the scout, an install-truth check in `scripts/install-wrappers.sh`, a new `scripts/install-cli.sh`, a floating-terminal click path in `notify.build_argv` and the menu jsonc, an interactive `cmd_menu`, and S4 argv redaction. I confirmed claims 1, 4 and 5 by running the vendor `--help` commands and reading the Omarchy scripts, and captured the exact bypass flags as the source of truth for the vendor table. Three findings block a clean run: claim 3 is now REFUTED (`~/.local/bin` is FIRST on PATH today, not position 12), the mandated `bash -lc 'command -v'` check returns a path string that cannot be compared literally to the shim path and cannot pass the required exit-0 test in a hermetic subprocess, and the packet's Forbidden rule contradicts both its own Fact-check (claim 2) and its hashing requirement. `.venv/bin/pytest -q` is green at 210 today; `shellcheck` is not installed, so acceptance line 2 cannot run here. No files were edited.

**Findings**

- **R1 — vendors.py exists and is complete.** `src/sentinel/vendors.py` defines one entry per vendor for Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, Cursor and Grok Build, and every entry has a non-empty `basenames` field (`tests/test_vendors.py`).
- **R2 — each vendor entry carries `argv_bypass`.** Each entry declares `argv_bypass` holding both exact bare tokens (e.g. `--dangerously-skip-permissions`) and `--flag value` pairs expressed as `(flag, {values})`, e.g. `("--permission-mode", {"bypassPermissions", "dontAsk"})`.
- **R3 — each vendor entry carries `config_bypass`.** Each entry declares `config_bypass` as (file path relative to `$HOME`, TOML/JSON key path, set of values that mean bypass), covering at minimum `~/.grok/config.toml` `[ui] permission_mode`, `~/.codex/config.toml` `approval_policy` and `sandbox_mode`, and Gemini settings.
- **R4 — `extract_bypass_flags` consumes the vendor table.** `sentinel.rules.extract_bypass_flags` (currently `src/sentinel/rules.py:109-121`, backed by `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS` at `src/sentinel/rules.py:12-20`) draws its known-flag set from every vendor's `argv_bypass` plus any caller-supplied `extra_bypass_flags`, not from a hand-maintained frozenset alone.
- **R5 — value-taking flags are reported in canonical `=` form.** A detected `--flag value` pair is reported as a single string `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions`, not as `--permission-mode` or as the bare value.
- **R6 — the four positive detection cases.** `extract_bypass_flags(["grok","--always-approve"])`, `["grok","--permission-mode","dontAsk"]`, `["codex","--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox"]` and `["gemini","--yolo"]` each return a non-empty list (`tests/test_vendors.py`).
- **R7 — the one negative case.** `extract_bypass_flags(["claude","--permission-mode","plan"])` returns an empty list.
- **R8 — `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` is a new rule with severity `medium`.** It joins the existing rule ids (`R-BYPASS`, `R-HOOK-WRITE`, `R-SELF`, `R-CHILD-SHELL`) and needs a description entry in `RULE_WHAT` (`src/sentinel/investigate.py:44-52`) and a renderer in the dispatch table at `src/sentinel/investigate.py:417-422`.
- **R9 — `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` is evaluated by the scout, never by the daemon.** The rule fires from the `sentinel-scout` path (`src/sentinel/scout.py`, `sentinel.cli.scout_main` at `src/sentinel/cli.py:43-64`) and is not added to `PROCESS_RULES` or any daemon evaluation (`src/sentinel/daemon.py:61`).
- **R10 — the rule compares hashes, not contents.** When a vendor `config_bypass` file exists the scout hashes it and compares the digest to the last digest recorded in `watchlist.json`; a change emits exactly one alert.
- **R11 — the alert never carries file content.** The `R-BYPASS-CONFIG` alert states only that the file changed (path + digests), and its `summary` explicitly states that Sentinel cannot say which key changed because reading the body is forbidden.
- **R12 — the same limitation is written into the README.** The README gains a sentence saying config-file bypass detection reports change, not the offending key or value.
- **R13 — a narrow keyed read requires the Chief's approval.** Reading only the bypass key out of a vendor config must not be implemented unilaterally; it needs an ask to the Chief first.
- **R14 — the wrapper installer verifies its own shim.** After writing each shim, `scripts/install-wrappers.sh` (loop at `scripts/install-wrappers.sh:81-111`, `BIN_DIR` at line 15) runs `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'` and compares the result to the shim path it just wrote.
- **R15 — a shadowed shim is a hard failure.** On mismatch the installer prints the resolved path, explains the PATH-position problem, prints the two fixes (put `$BIN_DIR` ahead of the mise entries in the shell rc, or set `SENTINEL_BIN_DIR` to a directory that already precedes mise — with `~/.local/share/mise/shims` named as NOT acceptable), and exits 1.
- **R16 — no silent shadowed shim ever remains.** There is no code path in which the installer prints `installed ...` and exits 0 while the shim is not what `command -v` resolves.
- **R17 — `scripts/install-cli.sh` exists and symlinks the three CLIs.** It links `sentinel`, `sentinel-action` and `sentinel-scout` from the project venv (entry points declared in `pyproject.toml` `[project.scripts]`) into `~/.local/bin`.
- **R18 — `install-cli.sh` has the same backup semantics as the wrapper installer.** An existing non-Sentinel file at a target path is renamed to `*.sentinel-bak.<UTC timestamp>` before the symlink is created (mirroring `backup_if_needed` at `scripts/install-wrappers.sh:28-44`).
- **R19 — `install-cli.sh` is dry-run by default.** Running it with no flags writes nothing and prints what it would do; writing requires an explicit `--apply` (the `--dry-run`/`--apply` pattern already used by `scripts/install-menu.sh:11,25-27`).
- **R20 — `install-cli.sh` honours `SENTINEL_BIN_DIR`.** The target directory is overridable by the same env var the wrapper installer uses, so tests and dry-runs never touch the real `~/.local/bin`.
- **R21 — toast clicks open a terminal.** `sentinel.notify.build_argv` / `_exec_tail` (`src/sentinel/notify.py:219-235`) emit the tail `--exec omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation sentinel-action <id> menu`; the cleanest implementation changes the `exec_prefix` default at lines 219, 227 and 372 to `("omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation", "sentinel-action")`.
- **R22 — the burst summary toast is unchanged.** `build_summary_argv` keeps its `--exec omarchy-shell shell summon <plugin_id> {}` tail (`src/sentinel/notify.py:258-269`), because it opens the panel rather than a CLI.
- **R23 — every menu row opens a terminal.** All four action strings in `packaging/omarchy-menu-sentinel.jsonc` become `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation sentinel-action list`, `... sentinel-action status`, `... sentinel-action pause 1h`, and `... sentinel-scout --refresh`.
- **R24 — a test pins the jsonc.** New `tests/test_menu_jsonc.py` asserts that every `action` value in `packaging/omarchy-menu-sentinel.jsonc` starts with `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation`.
- **R25 — the notify pin is updated in both places.** `tests/test_notify.py` currently pins the old tail at line 57 (inside the full-argv equality assertion) AND at line 104 (`assert calls[0][-4:] == ["--exec","sentinel-action", a.id, "menu"]`); the slice at line 104 must become `[-5:]` with the launcher inserted, or both assertions fail.

**Risks**

- **CONTRADICTION — claim 3 is stale; `~/.local/bin` is FIRST on PATH today, not position 12.** `bash -lc 'echo $PATH'` puts `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` (which IS `~/.local/bin`) at position 1, ahead of `/usr/share/omarchy/bin` and every mise dir, because `~/.local/bin/env` (mtime 2026-09-05 20:34) prepends it. The packet's premise "a shim placed in `~/.local/bin` ... is never executed" and the LEDGER's known limit "Wrapper shims in `~/.local/bin` are shadowed by mise on this machine" are both currently REFUTED for the login shell. The install-truth check is still worth building, but the packet's Context must be corrected or Grok will file a blocking ask.
- **CONCRETE BUG THE SPEC MUST ADDRESS — string comparison will produce a false mismatch.** `bash -lc 'command -v claude'` returns `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude` while `install-wrappers.sh` writes `$BIN_DIR/claude` = `/home/tav/.local/bin/claude`. These are the same file but different strings. R14's comparison must normalize (`realpath -m`, or `[ "$resolved" -ef "$target" ]`), or the installer exits 1 on a correctly-installed shim every time.
- **CONTRADICTION — the required `tests/test_install_wrappers.sh` cannot pass its exit-0 half with `bash -lc` against the real HOME.** Verified: `env -i HOME=/home/tav PATH=/tmp/sb:/usr/bin:/bin bash -lc 'command -v claude'` returns `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin/claude`, because the login profile re-prepends `$HOME/.local/bin` and mise shims regardless of the injected PATH. A hermetic pytest subprocess gives exactly this clean environment. The test needs either a temp `HOME` with a fake profile, or the installer needs an injectable resolver (e.g. `SENTINEL_RESOLVE_CMD`) so the test can substitute a non-login shell. The packet mandates `bash -lc` without saying how the test escapes it.
- **CONTRADICTION — Fact-check demands claim 2 be verified, Forbidden bans the only way to verify it.** Claim 2 asserts `~/.grok/config.toml` contains `permission_mode = "always-approve"` under `[ui]`; Forbidden says "Reading vendor config bodies." Grok cannot mark claim 2 CONFIRMED without violating the packet. It must be pre-marked UNVERIFIED-BY-DESIGN, or the packet must carve out a one-time read.
- **CONTRADICTION — R10 (hash the config file) requires reading the body the Forbidden section bans.** Hashing streams the whole file through `hashlib`. This is compatible with PROTOCOL §9.2 ("Hashes and paths only") but not with the packet's literal wording. The packet should say "never store or surface the body; hashing is permitted".
- **CONTRADICTION with W3-03 — content hashing is explicitly deferred.** W3-03's Non-goals say "Content hashing and diffing (W6-03)"; W3-05 R10 requires hashing vendor configs into `watchlist.json` now. One of the two packets must move.
- **"Parallel-safe with W3-03" is false.** Both packets create/extend `src/sentinel/vendors.py`, both change `src/sentinel/scout.py`, and both change the `watchlist.json` schema (W3-03 adds `"version": 2` and per-entry `"vendor"`; W3-05 adds a per-entry hash). Merging them independently will conflict. Recommend serializing W3-03 before W3-05, or having W3-05 declare `Depends on: W3-03`.
- **AMBIGUITY — the scout has no alert-emitting path today.** `src/sentinel/scout.py` only builds a metadata dict and `write_watchlist` writes it; `sentinel.cli.scout_main` prints a count. R8/R9 need the packet to say who appends the alert (`sentinel.store.append_alert`) and whether the scout also notifies (`sentinel.notify.Notifier`) — note PROTOCOL §9.3 forbids the *daemon* from network calls, and `sentinel-scout` is a separate oneshot, so notifying is permissible but unspecified.
- **AMBIGUITY — where the previous hash lives.** "compare with the last hash in `watchlist.json`" collides with `write_watchlist` (`src/sentinel/scout.py:84-86`) which fully overwrites the file. The order (read old digest → hash → compare → emit → write new) must be specified, and the very first run (no prior digest) must be defined as "record, do not alert".
- **REGRESSION RISK — R5's canonical `=` form changes approval fingerprints.** `sentinel.keys.alert_flag_set` (`src/sentinel/keys.py:29-39`) feeds `fingerprint(...)` in `cmd_approve` (`src/sentinel/action.py:154-169`). Changing a reported flag from today's bare `bypassPermissions` (which `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS` already matches as a standalone token) to `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions` silently invalidates every existing allowlist entry for that flag. The packet says nothing about migration, and W3-01 (allowlist correctness) is running in parallel on the same code.
- **AMBIGUITY — does `argv_bypass` replace or supplement `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS`?** If the bare token `bypassPermissions` stays in the known set alongside the new `("--permission-mode", {...})` pair, then `grok "explain bypassPermissions to me"` false-positives on the prompt text. The packet does not say whether bare-value entries are removed once the pair form exists. Recommend: pair form only for value-taking flags, and drop bare `bypassPermissions`. The unattributed legacy entries `trust-all` / `--trust-all` also appear in no installed vendor's `--help` and need a home or a deletion.
- **SPEC FORMATTING BUG — the S4 requirement is filed under `## Non-goals`.** The redaction paragraph (R31-R34) sits inside the Non-goals section, above its bullets, where it reads as out of scope. It is a requirement. (W3-03 has the identical defect with its S5 paragraph.) Move it to `## Changes` or give it its own `## Security review` heading.
- **AMBIGUITY — S4's "secret-bearing flags per vendor" has no examples and no observed instances.** None of `claude`, `codex`, `gemini` or `grok` expose an `--api-key`-style argv flag in `--help` on this machine (claude uses `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`apiKeyHelper`). The per-vendor list would be empty; only the generic regex redaction is exercisable. Either drop the per-vendor list or name a concrete flag.
- **AMBIGUITY — double confirmation on menu choices 5 and 6.** `cmd_kill` already routes through `confirm_kill` (`src/sentinel/action.py:148-152`), which demands a tty. Choosing `5 kill child` from the interactive menu will prompt twice unless the packet says to pass `yes=True`, and PROTOCOL §9.4 ("No silent auto-kill") argues for keeping the second confirm. Needs an explicit decision.
- **AMBIGUITY — `cmd_menu`'s injection signature is unspecified.** R30's test requires injecting `isatty` and a prompt, but `cmd_menu(alert_id: str) -> int` takes neither, and `action_main` calls it positionally. The packet does not name the new keyword parameters or their defaults.
- **ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 2 IS UNRUNNABLE HERE — `shellcheck` is not installed** (`command -v shellcheck` → absent). AGENTS.md only requires shellcheck "if it is installed", so this line will be skipped rather than passed; the Chief's review must not treat a skip as a pass.
- **ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 3 IS NOT COPY-PASTE RUNNABLE.** The `/tmp/fakemise/claude` stub it depends on is described only in a trailing comment. Also note `/tmp/sb` and `/tmp/fakemise` are outside the scratchpad; and with the current PATH the login shell resolves `claude` to `~/.local/bin/claude`, so the command yields `exit=1` for the *wrong* reason (real-home shadowing, not the fake mise dir) — it cannot distinguish a working check from a broken one.
- **INCOMPLETE PIN LIST — the packet names one `tests/test_notify.py` pin, there are two.** Line 57 (full-argv equality) and line 104 (`calls[0][-4:]`). The `[-4:]` slice silently starts matching the wrong window once a fifth element is inserted; both must change.
- **STALE LINE CITATIONS throughout the Context section.** `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS` is `src/sentinel/rules.py:12-20` not 11-19; `build_argv` is `src/sentinel/notify.py:223-235` (with `_exec_tail` at 219-220) — the cited 52-55 is the `BurstPolicy` dataclass; `cmd_menu` is `src/sentinel/action.py:210-222` not 214-233; the `tests/test_notify.py` pin is at 57/104 not 45-47. The LEDGER already records that stale line numbers burned a full Grok run on UX-01, so these should be corrected or replaced with symbol names before launch.
- **UNVERIFIED — claim 6 (`omarchy-shell`'s PATH contains no Sentinel venv).** No `omarchy-shell` process is running right now, so `/proc/<pid>/environ` could not be read. The claim is plausible and drives R17-R20, but it is currently unproven; the fix (symlinks into `~/.local/bin`) also becomes questionable if `omarchy-shell` inherits the same PATH the login shell now has.
- **SHELL-SAFETY NOTE for R21/R23.** `omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` does `cmd="$*"` and re-evaluates through `bash -c`, so the `--exec` argv is re-split by the shell inside the floating terminal. `sentinel-action pause 1h` and `sentinel-scout --refresh` survive; anything carrying a path with spaces or a quote would not. Worth stating as an invariant so no future menu row breaks it.


### W3-05 · spec:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- SPEC — `RULE_WHAT` does not exist. The rule-description dict is `RULE_TEXT` at /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/investigate.py:43-55. Spec's R8 cites `RULE_WHAT (investigate.py:44-52)`; an implementer following it will not find the symbol.
- SPEC — R8 says R-BYPASS-CONFIG 'joins the existing rule ids (R-BYPASS, R-HOOK-WRITE, R-SELF, R-CHILD-SHELL)'. There are SIX, not four: `RULE_TEXT` also carries `R-ALLOW-EXPIRE` and `R-NEW-AGENT`, and `_RENDERERS` (investigate.py:414-423) has entries for both.
- SPEC — R8 implies a renderer entry is required. It is not. investigate.py:479 is `renderer = _RENDERERS.get(alert.rule, _render_generic)`, so a missing renderer degrades to the generic one with no crash. Only the missing `RULE_TEXT` key fails silently, via the `if text:` guard at investigate.py:128. CODEBASE stated this half correctly (RULE_TEXT silent) but did not note the renderer fallback.
- SPEC — the false-positive argument against keeping the bare `bypassPermissions` token is wrong. I ran it: `extract_bypass_flags(['grok','explain bypassPermissions to me'])` returns `[]`. The membership test in rules.py:118-120 compares whole argv elements, not substrings, so a prompt only fires if it is *exactly* the token. The stated reason for dropping the bare entry evaporates — and tests/test_rules.py:20 requires keeping it (TESTS is right on that constraint).
- CODEBASE — 'the expected exit=1 will not reproduce' for acceptance command 3 is wrong. Verified: `PATH=$FAKEMISE:$SB:$PATH bash -lc 'command -v claude'` resolves to `$FAKEMISE/claude`. The reason is `~/.local/bin/env`'s case statement — it finds its own spelling already present in the inherited PATH and therefore does NOT re-prepend, so the injected head survives the login shell. The acceptance command does exercise the intended failure.
- TESTS — 'A test cannot make that hermetic' is refuted, and the mandated `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` seam (seam 6) is unnecessary. Verified three cases: (A) `env -i HOME=<tmp_path> PATH=<fakemise>:<sb>:/usr/bin:/bin bash -lc 'command -v claude'` -> `<fakemise>/claude`, injected PATH intact, because a fixture HOME has no rc files to source; (B) real HOME with a PATH lacking the uv spelling -> the env script prepends and shadows the injection; (C) real HOME with a PATH containing it -> injection survives. TESTS' own fixture already sets `HOME=tmp_path`, i.e. case A, so `bash -lc` IS hermetic there. Adding an env-var override is also the exact non-interactive drive-the-installer seam SECURITY warns against.
- SPEC — the same blocker is overstated in the other direction: 'the test needs a temp HOME with a fake profile'. No fake profile is needed; an empty tmp_path HOME is sufficient and is what makes case A hermetic.
- INTEGRATION — 'CLAIM 5 IS PARTIALLY WRONG on the path: omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation lives at /usr/share/omarchy/bin, not /usr/bin' is backwards. The real 534-byte root-owned file is `/usr/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation`; `/usr/share/omarchy/bin/omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation` is a symlink to it. Claim 5's path was correct as written.
- CODEBASE — `DEFAULT_SEEDS` 'contains no .grok, .gemini or .codex' is wrong about `.codex`, which is present at src/sentinel/scout.py:16. `.grok` and `.gemini` are genuinely absent, which is the part that matters for R-BYPASS-CONFIG.
- ALL FIVE — the packet's 'position 12' was not fabricated, and the refutation should be stated precisely rather than as a flat error. Both spellings are on the login PATH today: `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` at position 1 (prepended by uv's `~/.local/bin/env`, mtime 2026-09-05 20:34) and the canonical `/home/tav/.local/bin` at position 14, behind mise shims at position 10. The packet was right that the canonical entry is buried; it was wrong that a shim there is never executed, because the non-canonical alias resolves first. The correction to the packet Context is one sentence, not a rewrite.
- TESTS — seam 5 proposes `cmd_menu(..., isatty: Callable[[], bool] = sys.stdin.isatty)`. That does not mirror the existing house shape: `kill.confirm_kill` (src/sentinel/kill.py:71-79) takes `isatty: bool | None = None` and `prompt: Callable[[str], str] | None = None`, resolving with `sys.stdin.isatty() if isatty is None else isatty` at line 81. SECURITY named the right shape. Use `bool | None`, not a callable, or the codebase grows two conventions for the same seam.

- The two Forbidden-section contradictions are real and both need a Chief ruling before implementation: (a) Fact-check claim 2 requires reading ~/.grok/config.toml's body, which Forbidden bans — resolve as stat-only plus UNVERIFIED-BY-DESIGN; (b) R10 requires hashing a vendor config, which requires opening it — resolve as 'hashing permitted, body never stored or surfaced'. SPEC, CODEBASE, TESTS and SECURITY all independently raised these.
- SECURITY's symlink-escape hardening for the hasher is correct and unaddressed by the other three: the hash target sits in a directory the monitored agent can write, so it needs `O_NOFOLLOW`, an `S_ISREG` check on the fd, a size cap, an `auth`-kind refusal, and a containment check against the vendor home.
- No files were edited. This was a read-only verification pass.

**Final position.** SETTLED FACTS the five of us should stop re-litigating (each verified in the tree or on the machine this session, not inherited from another report):

1. Baseline is 210 passed in 1.87s at ca0465a. shellcheck ABSENT (acceptance line 2 is an honest skip, never a pass). cursor-agent ABSENT (Cursor row is UNVERIFIED).
2. Packet Context claim 3 needs a one-sentence correction, not a rewrite: `~/.local/bin` is on the login PATH twice — as `/home/tav/.local/share/../bin` at position 1 (uv's `~/.local/bin/env` prepender) and canonically at position 14, behind mise shims at 10. A shim written there IS executed today. The install-truth check is still worth building; the packet's rationale is not.
3. The install-truth check must canonicalize. Measured: raw string compare MISMATCHES on a correctly-installed shim; `-ef` and `realpath -m` both match. Without canonicalization the installer fails on the Owner's own machine and prints a fix for a PATH that is already correct.
4. `bash -lc` IS testable and needs NO new seam. With `HOME=<tmp_path>` there are no rc files to source and the injected PATH survives intact; with the real HOME and an inherited `$PATH` the uv prepender skips itself, so the packet's acceptance command also reproduces correctly. Drop the proposed `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` override — it is unnecessary and it is exactly the non-interactive handle SECURITY warns about.
5. The seams are named: `RULE_TEXT` (not `RULE_WHAT`); `_RENDERERS.get(..., _render_generic)` means a renderer is optional but a `RULE_TEXT` key is mandatory or the explanation vanishes silently; `cmd_menu` injection mirrors `kill.confirm_kill`'s `isatty: bool | None = None, prompt: Callable | None = None`, not a callable-returning-bool.
6. Bare `bypassPermissions` STAYS in `DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS`. The prompt-text false-positive argument does not hold — matching is on whole argv elements — and tests/test_rules.py:20 depends on it. Add the pair form alongside; do not remove.
7. Two notify pins (test_notify.py:57 and :104), burst pin untouched. `exec_prefix` default changes at notify.py:219/227/372 and nothing else.
8. The launcher's `cmd="$*"` -> `bash -c` re-parse is confirmed by reading the file. Validate the alert id at the `notify` boundary and DEGRADE the tail on failure — never return early, or a sticky R-SELF tamper alert gets silenced by a guard meant to protect it.
9. Wrapping every menu row is wrong. `omarchy-show-done` blocks on a keypress when a tty exists, so `pause 1h` and `scout --refresh` would each open a window and hang. The jsonc test must encode which rows need a tty.

THREE THINGS THAT SHOULD BLOCK THE LAUNCH, in priority order:

A. The S4 redaction requirement is filed under `## Non-goals` in the packet. It is the only security-finding closure in W3-05 and a literal reader takes it as out of scope. Moving that paragraph is the single highest-value edit before Grok starts, and W3-03 has the identical defect. This is a five-minute Chief edit with an outsized failure mode.

B. Two Forbidden-section contradictions need an explicit ruling, not an implementer's judgment call: claim 2 is verifiable by stat only and should be pre-marked UNVERIFIED-BY-DESIGN; hashing must be carved out as permitted with the body never stored or surfaced. Both were found independently by four of us, which is itself the signal.

C. R-BYPASS-CONFIG is the largest new surface, collides with W3-03 on three axes (vendors.py, scout.py, watchlist.json schema), and produces a medium alert that can only say "this file changed" on files their own CLIs rewrite for benign reasons. The LEDGER already records a 127-alert R-HOOK-WRITE desensitization storm. My recommendation: split R8-R13 out of W3-05 and serialize it behind W3-03, landing the vendor table, install truth, click path and S4 redaction now. That makes the packet parallel-safe in fact rather than by assertion, and it removes the one piece whose Forbidden-section carve-out is still unwritten.

ONE THING NONE OF US SHOULD LET THROUGH: `install-cli.sh` as specified can rename the Owner's working `~/.local/bin/sentinel-action` symlink to a backup and relink it at this worktree's `.venv`, silently moving the live CLI onto unmerged code. Either fold the step into the existing install-daemon.sh or make the script refuse to touch a symlink that already resolves into the plugin venv. This is the only finding here that can damage a working machine rather than just fail a test.

### W3-05 · tests

Test design for W3-05, read-only stage. 33 new tests across 6 files (2 new modules, 4 extended), plus 4 existing tests that must change and 3 more that become conditional. Baseline is 210 collected tests in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel; target after this packet is ~243. The design depends on five seams the implementer must expose (vendor table accessors, a redaction helper, a scout config-hash entry point, an injectable cmd_menu, and a PATH-probe override in install-wrappers.sh); each is named exactly below. Two blockers need a Chief decision before the implementer starts: hashing vendor config files vs. the packet's own "Forbidden: reading vendor config bodies", and scripts/install-cli.sh duplicating step 2 of scripts/install-daemon.sh.

**Findings**

- SEAMS THE TESTS REQUIRE (state these to the implementer before code is written; without them several tests below cannot be written at all).
1. /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/vendors.py must export: a frozen `Vendor` dataclass with fields `name`, `basenames: tuple[str,...]`, `argv_bypass: tuple[...]`, `config_bypass: tuple[ConfigBypass,...]`, `secret_flags: tuple[str,...]`, `verified: bool`; a `VENDORS: tuple[Vendor, ...]`; and three pure accessors `by_basename(name) -> Vendor | None`, `argv_bypass_tokens() -> frozenset[str]`, `argv_bypass_pairs() -> dict[str, frozenset[str]]`. `ConfigBypass` carries `relpath: str`, `fmt: Literal['toml','json']`, `key_path: tuple[str,...]`, `values: frozenset[str]`.
2. `sentinel.rules.extract_bypass_flags(cmdline, extra_bypass_flags=None)` keeps its exact current signature (src/sentinel/rules.py:109) — it is called from keys.py:38, summarize.py:40, wrap_record.py:36, daemon.py:559/636 and four test files — and internally unions DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS with `vendors.argv_bypass_tokens()`.
3. `sentinel.rules.redact_cmdline(cmdline: Sequence[str]) -> tuple[list[str], bool]` as a standalone pure function, so redaction is testable without building an Alert or writing launches.jsonl.
4. `sentinel.scout.evaluate_config_bypass(home: Path, previous: Mapping[str, str] | None) -> tuple[list[Alert], dict[str, str]]`, pure, no I/O beyond hashing the vendor config files under `home`.
5. `sentinel.action.cmd_menu(alert_id, *, isatty: Callable[[], bool] = sys.stdin.isatty, prompt: Callable[[str], str] = input)`, dispatching through module globals (`cmd_approve`, `cmd_kill`, ...) so monkeypatch.setattr('sentinel.action.cmd_approve', ...) intercepts.
6. `scripts/install-wrappers.sh` must honour `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH`: when set, the login-shell probe is `PATH="$SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH" command -v <name>` instead of `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'`. See the risk note on `bash -lc` for why the packet's design is otherwise untestable.
7. `sentinel.notify` must export the launcher name as a constant, e.g. `TERMINAL_LAUNCHER = 'omarchy-launch-floating-terminal-with-presentation'` and `DEFAULT_EXEC_PREFIX = (TERMINAL_LAUNCHER, 'sentinel-action')`, so tests assert against the constant rather than re-typing a 47-character string.

- test_every_vendor_has_basenames: `assert VENDORS` and for each `v`: `assert v.basenames`, `assert all(v.basenames)`, `assert v.name`.
- test_basenames_are_unique_across_vendors: flatten all basenames, `assert len(all_names) == len(set(all_names))` — a basename owned by two vendors makes `by_basename` ambiguous.
- test_basenames_cover_default_agent_basenames: `assert DEFAULT_AGENT_BASENAMES <= frozenset(all basenames)` (rules.py:43-51 is claude, codex, cursor, cursor-agent, grok); this is the link that keeps R-CHILD-SHELL and the vendor table talking about the same processes.
- test_argv_bypass_pairs_have_nonempty_value_sets: for each flag in `argv_bypass_pairs()`, `assert flag.startswith('--')` and `assert values`, and `assert 'plan' not in values and 'default' not in values and 'acceptEdits' not in values` — the three Grok/Claude permission modes that are NOT bypasses.
- test_grok_always_approve_detected: `assert extract_bypass_flags(['grok','--always-approve']) == ['--always-approve']`.
- test_permission_mode_pair_detected_split_and_joined: `assert extract_bypass_flags(['grok','--permission-mode','dontAsk']) == ['--permission-mode=dontAsk']` AND `assert extract_bypass_flags(['grok','--permission-mode=dontAsk']) == ['--permission-mode=dontAsk']`. The joined form is currently a real miss: `_normalize_token` (rules.py:102-106) reduces it to `--permission-mode`, which is in no set, so today it returns []. Both spellings must normalise to the same reported string or the same launch gets two different approval fingerprints via keys.alert_flag_set (keys.py:29-38).
- test_codex_and_gemini_bypasses_detected: `assert extract_bypass_flags(['codex','--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox'])` is truthy and `assert extract_bypass_flags(['gemini','--yolo']) == ['--yolo']`.
- test_permission_mode_plan_is_not_a_bypass: `assert extract_bypass_flags(['claude','--permission-mode','plan']) == []` and likewise for `'default'`, `'acceptEdits'`, `'auto'` — the false-positive guard.

- test_unknown_argument_exits_two: `assert subprocess.run([... , '--nope']).returncode == 2`, matching install-daemon.sh:27 and install-menu.sh:38.
- EXISTING TESTS THAT BREAK — certain (2). Both are in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_notify.py and both break for the same reason: build_argv's `--exec` tail grows from 4 elements to 5 once the terminal launcher is prepended.
1. tests/test_notify.py:50 test_high_is_normal_with_30s_timeout — asserts the whole argv list literally, ending `'--exec', 'sentinel-action', a.id, 'menu'` at lines 55-57. Fix: insert the launcher between '--exec' and 'sentinel-action'.
2. tests/test_notify.py:100 test_exec_tail_is_last — `assert calls[0][-4:] == ['--exec','sentinel-action',a.id,'menu']`. The last four become `[TERMINAL_LAUNCHER,'sentinel-action',a.id,'menu']`, so the '--exec' comparison fails. Fix: slice `[-5:]`.
These are the only two places in the suite that pin the per-alert exec tail (verified by grepping '--exec' and 'sentinel-action' across tests/). tests/joint/test_adversarial.py does not reference build_argv, the exec tail, or extract_bypass_flags at all — its only nearby line is 75, `cmdline=stub.get('cmdline', ['agent','--yolo'])`, which stays green as long as bare '--yolo' remains detected. Good news, since that file is the Chief's and must not be edited.
- EXISTING TESTS THAT BREAK — conditional on implementation choices (5). Each is a decision the implementer can get wrong; name them in the packet handoff so they are treated as constraints rather than as failures to "fix" by editing the test.
1. tests/test_rules.py:20 test_rbypass_detects_other_flags iterates `('bypassPermissions','--yolo','trust-all','--trust-all')` as BARE argv tokens and asserts `alert.evidence['flag'] == flag`. It breaks the moment `bypassPermissions` is moved out of DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS (rules.py:12-20) and into the vendor table only as the value half of `('--permission-mode', {...})`. Constraint: the bare tokens in DEFAULT_BYPASS_FLAGS stay, and the pair form is added alongside them.
2. tests/test_keys.py:53 test_alert_flag_set_prefers_evidence_then_cmdline falls through to `extract_bypass_flags(['claude','--dangerously-skip-permissions'])` and expects `frozenset({'--dangerously-skip-permissions'})`. Any change that makes extraction return a normalised or prefixed string breaks approval fingerprints repo-wide (keys.py:38 feeds allowlist.fingerprint, which both action.cmd_approve and daemon._is_alert_allowed call — a mismatch means Approve silently stops working, exactly the invariant keys.py's docstring names).
3. tests/test_investigate.py:511 test_actions_section_matches_menu asserts the investigate detail's Actions section equals `action_lines(alert)` (investigate.py:426-439). It breaks if the implementer rewrites action_lines into the numbered interactive list. Constraint: action_lines stays the non-tty cheat sheet; the numbered prompt is new code in cmd_menu, not a rewrite of action_lines.
4. tests/test_action.py:263 test_list_and_status_and_menu and tests/test_action.py:468 test_menu_lists_open_and_logs both drive `action_main([alert.id,'menu'])` and assert on cheat-sheet output. They stay green only because pytest replaces sys.stdin with a non-tty object, so the new default `isatty=sys.stdin.isatty` returns False. That is true under `.venv/bin/pytest -q` but is an environment dependency, not a guarantee. Recommend cmd_menu also treats a set `SENTINEL_NO_PROMPT` env var as non-interactive, and that these two tests set it explicitly — a one-line monkeypatch.setenv in each, no assertion changes.
5. tests/test_config_template.py:8 asserts `load_policy(packaging/config.toml) == NotifyPolicy()`. It breaks if any new key lands under `[notify]`. Constraint: vendor and scout settings go in their own section (`[vendors]` / `[scout]`), not `[notify]`.
Also worth noting as non-breakage: tests/test_wrap_record.py:34 asserts `record['cmdline'] == ['claude','--dangerously-skip-permissions','do stuff']`. It survives redaction only because that argv contains no secret-shaped token — provided the redaction pattern matches flag NAMES and key-shaped values, not the substring 'auth' or 'token' wherever it appears. tests/test_summarize_redaction.py is unaffected: build_redacted_bundle already excludes cmdline (summarize.py:16-28).
- COVERAGE THE PACKET'S OWN TEST LIST OMITS, worth adding (already folded into the files above, listed here so the gap is visible): (a) the joined `--flag=value` spelling, which is a live false negative today and a fingerprint-splitting hazard; (b) the pair parser not swallowing a following benign token; (c) proof that the R-BYPASS-CONFIG alert carries no file content, which is the security invariant the whole rule turns on; (d) proof that a menu-initiated kill does not pass `yes=True`; (e) EOF/Ctrl-C on the prompt leaving the alert untouched; (f) the failing installer still reporting every shadowed name, not just the first; (g) install-cli.sh idempotency and dry-run-by-default, since it writes into ~/.local/bin and the Forbidden section bans running it for real.

**Risks**

- BLOCKER, needs a Chief decision before implementation: R-BYPASS-CONFIG requires hashing vendor config files, and hashing means opening and reading the whole body into memory. The same packet's Forbidden section says "Reading vendor config bodies", and W3-03's Forbidden section says of ~/.claude.json "you may stat it, never open it". The packet's Changes section resolves this in favour of hashing, but the two Forbidden lines are not amended. Recommendation: hash only the files listed in `config_bypass` (never ~/.claude.json, which W3-03 explicitly protects and which holds an OAuth block), state the exemption in the packet or an ASK, and let tests/test_bypass_config.py::test_alert_never_carries_file_content be the enforcement. An auditor reading only the Forbidden line will otherwise fail the packet.
- BLOCKER, needs a Chief decision: scripts/install-cli.sh duplicates step 2 of scripts/install-daemon.sh, which already does `ln -sfn "$VENV/bin/$cli" "$BIN_DIR/$cli"` for the same three CLIs (install-daemon.sh:49-53, same SENTINEL_BIN_DIR default at line 19) and which README.md:22 documents as the thing that puts them on PATH. Two installers writing the same three symlinks with different backup semantics is a real divergence risk. Options: (a) install-daemon.sh delegates step 2 to install-cli.sh, (b) install-cli.sh is a standalone repair tool and a test asserts both produce identical link targets. Either way, tests/test_install_cli.py should include a cross-check against install-daemon.sh's target so the two cannot drift.
- The packet's install-truth design uses `bash -lc 'command -v <name>'`, which sources /etc/profile and the user's own ~/.bash_profile and ~/.bashrc. A test cannot make that hermetic: the login shell will rebuild PATH from the real machine and may discard the fake mise directory entirely, so the test would pass or fail depending on whose machine it runs on, and the acceptance line `SENTINEL_BIN_DIR=/tmp/sb PATH=/tmp/fakemise:/tmp/sb:$PATH bash scripts/install-wrappers.sh claude` may not even reproduce the failure the packet expects. Recommended design, which satisfies both: the installer checks the INHERITED PATH (deterministic, and exactly what the acceptance line exercises) AND separately reports what `bash -lc` resolves, exiting 1 if either disagrees with the shim path; plus a `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` override that replaces the `bash -lc` probe for tests. Without that override, tests/test_install_wrappers.py cannot be written reliably.
- shellcheck is NOT installed on this machine (`command -v shellcheck` returns nothing). Acceptance step 2, `shellcheck scripts/install-wrappers.sh scripts/install-cli.sh`, cannot be run here. AGENTS.md says shell scripts pass shellcheck "if it is installed", so this is not a packet failure, but the report must mark it UNVERIFIED rather than claim it green. Consider a pytest test that skips when shutil.which('shellcheck') is None and otherwise runs it — that way the check runs wherever the tool exists and is honestly skipped where it does not.
- Merge collision with W3-03, which is parallel-safe by the packet header but shares two files. Both packets create src/sentinel/vendors.py, and W3-03 additionally rewrites tests/test_scout.py and tests/fixtures/fake_home. Mitigations baked into this design: W3-05's config-bypass tests live in a NEW tests/test_bypass_config.py with tmp_path-built homes rather than the shared fixture tree; W3-05 adds exactly one new test to tests/test_scout.py and edits none; and vendors.py must be authored additively — W3-05 adds `argv_bypass`, `config_bypass` and `secret_flags` fields to whatever Vendor shape W3-03 defines, and never reshapes `home`, `settings`, `hooks_dirs`, `mcp`, `auth`, `project_dirs`, `exclude`. Whichever packet lands second must extend, not replace.
- The vendor table is only as good as the fact-check. The packet asks for `grok --help`, `claude --help`, `codex --help`, `gemini --help`, `cursor-agent --help` output pasted into the report, and the tests I designed assert on specific flag strings — `--always-approve`, `--permission-mode dontAsk`, `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, `--yolo`. If the fact-check refutes any of those spellings, the corresponding test in tests/test_vendors.py must be corrected to the real flag, not deleted. test_unverified_vendors_are_marked exists to keep an unrunnable vendor visibly UNVERIFIED rather than quietly guessed at.
- Changing what extract_bypass_flags returns changes approval fingerprints. keys.alert_flag_set (src/sentinel/keys.py:29-38) feeds allowlist.fingerprint, and both action.cmd_approve and daemon._is_alert_allowed must derive the identical value or Approve silently does nothing — the invariant keys.py's own docstring names. A `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` launch that previously fingerprinted on the bare token `bypassPermissions` will now fingerprint on `--permission-mode=bypassPermissions`, so any approval a user already stored for it stops matching. That is acceptable (approvals live in the user's state dir and this is pre-release), but it should be stated in the report and in README rather than discovered by the Owner. There is no migration test in this list because there is no migration; if the Chief wants one, it belongs in tests/test_allowlist.py.
- Test-count discipline: the baseline is 210 collected (`.venv/bin/pytest -q --collect-only` in this worktree, confirmed). This design adds 33 new tests and modifies 2, landing at ~243. AGENTS.md forbids claiming a count not obtained by running pytest in this worktree, so the report must quote the real number after implementation, not this estimate.
- Scope guard: tests/joint/ is the Chief's and none of this design touches it. tests/joint/test_adversarial.py stays green under every change above, verified by grep — it references neither the exec tail, nor cmd_menu, nor extract_bypass_flags, and its only bypass-adjacent line is a default `['agent','--yolo']` cmdline at line 75.

**Final position.** TREAT AS SETTLED (verified in the tree/machine today by me, on top of the others):

FACTS. Claim 3 REFUTED — ~/.local/bin is PATH position 1 (spelled /home/tav/.local/share/../bin) via ~/.local/bin/env; the literal spelling is position 14, not 12. Claim 6 REFUTED both halves — the three CLI symlinks already exist and Util.qml runs clicks through `bash -lc` (a login shell), so the defect is the missing tty. Claims 1, 4, 5, 7, 8 CONFIRMED; the launcher's real file IS /usr/bin/… with /usr/share/omarchy/bin/… a symlink to it. Claim 2 is UNVERIFIABLE-BY-DESIGN — stat only (~/.grok/config.toml, 659 B, mtime 2026-09-06 02:22); ~/.codex/config.toml does NOT exist on this machine. Baseline 210 passed; shellcheck absent; cursor-agent absent.

THE ONE BLOCKER I RETIRED. The `bash -lc` hermeticity objection is dead. With `HOME=tmp_path` the injected PATH wins in both orderings — I ran it. tests/test_install_wrappers.py needs `HOME=tmp_path` and `SENTINEL_BIN_DIR`, nothing more. Drop the proposed `SENTINEL_LOGIN_PATH` seam: an env var that lets the caller substitute the PATH being checked is a way to fake a passing install check, and it is now unnecessary. What DOES stay is the canonicalization fix (`readlink -f` or `-ef`) — I confirmed both spellings resolve to the same file, and without it the installer exits 1 on a correct install on the Owner's own machine.

THE ONE THING NONE OF US SHOULD SOFTEN. I reproduced the target defect: with a fake mise dir ahead of BIN_DIR, install-wrappers.sh printed `installed … → sentinel-wrap claude` and exited 0 while `command -v claude` resolved elsewhere. R14-R16 are earning their place.

STILL BLOCKING, needs a Chief ruling before Grok starts (three, not five): (1) the S4 paragraph must move out of `## Non-goals` — it is the packet's only security-finding closure and a literal reader drops it; (2) hashing vs. the Forbidden line — PROTOCOL §9.2 says "Hashes and paths only", so the protocol permits it and only the packet's own wording needs amending to "never store or surface the body; hashing the files named in config_bypass is permitted"; (3) install-cli.sh vs. install-daemon.sh step 2 — one of them owns those three symlinks.

WHAT I'D DROP FROM OUR COLLECTIVE LIST. The vendors.py "hard collision" — both packets declare it and split the fields disjointly with an extend-don't-replace contract. W3-07's "undeclared" collision — it declares parallel-safety with W3-05 explicitly. What survives is the watchlist.json schema overlap (W3-03 v2 vs W3-05's hash key), which is genuinely unspecified, and the scout-as-alert-emitter × W3-07 foreign-write risk, which is a real design interaction worth flagging to the W3-07 agent now.

ONE HARD RULE THE REPORTS UNDERSTATE. AGENTS.md invariant 6 is stricter than PROTOCOL §9.6: "Never run `systemctl`, `omarchy`, `sudo`, or edit anything under `~/.config`, `~/.local/bin`, or `~/.local/state`." Neither installer may be run against a real target at all during this packet — temp SENTINEL_BIN_DIR only, which is how I ran mine. And the S4 per-vendor secret-flag list is empty for every verified vendor (zero `--api-key`-shaped flags across all four `--help`s), so the generic regex redaction plus `evidence.redacted` is the whole deliverable; ask the Chief to drop the per-vendor list rather than have Grok invent entries for it.

### W3-06 · codebase

W3-06 is a docs-only packet over six existing files plus one new one, and nothing in the test suite reads any of them, so the "210 green, .md only" acceptance is safe. I verified the packet's code-side assumptions against the tree: `sentinel-action notify` and `sentinel-action <id> open [--logs]` both exist, the `[notify]` config block is real and already fully documented in packaging/config.toml, and four of the five security findings the packet wants marked fixed genuinely are. The exception is S5: UX-00 fixed only the alert/re-inventory half, the unbounded watch growth is still in daemon.py, so marking it plainly "fixed by UX-00" would put a false claim into the security review. The packet also asks for a TEST-03 ledger row for a packet that does not exist in the tree, and its acceptance command `git diff --stat` will not show the new STATUS.md.

**Findings**

- Change surface is exactly seven files, all documentation: /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/docs/collab/LEDGER.md, docs/collab/ROADMAP.md, README.md, docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, AGENTS.md, docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md, and a new docs/collab/STATUS.md. No test reads any of them (checked by grep over tests/*.py and tests/joint/*.py); the only test that reads a non-test file is tests/test_config_template.py, which parses packaging/config.toml. So the packet's 'pytest unchanged' acceptance is structurally guaranteed. Baseline is .venv/bin/pytest -q => 210 passed in 1.87s, run just now.
- LEDGER.md stale facts, concretely. Header table: 'HEAD at last update 48b2eeb' (actual HEAD is ca0465a, six commits later); 'Tests 151 passed' (actual 210); 'Last reviewed range ..4ebfb5d' and 'Unreviewed commits e0f2c86, 30533f6, c015834' are contradicted by docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, which reviewed the whole branch diff at fb12040 -- a descendant of all three (verified with git merge-base --is-ancestor). Packet table: the W3-06 row says 'in progress'; the UX-02a row still says 'packet written; next Grok run after UX-01' although UX-02a landed as 964c461 + 743dee3 and merged at 0e1fe2e with a report at docs/collab/reports/UX-02a-report.md; there is no row at all for UX-00 (50992ba), TEST-02, the joint adversarial harness (dd6678a), D-008's implementation (d4173cd), or the SkillSpector self-scan (fa50aab). 'Canonical checkout ~/Work/sentinel at 1161091' is still true (git worktree list confirms).
- LEDGER decisions and Grok-log surface. The '## Open decisions for Owner' table lists D-001 through D-007 only; D-003 already carries an inline 'Resolved as Apache-2.0' note, and D-008 is absent from the table despite docs/collab/decisions/D-008-risk-visual.md existing and the decision being recorded only in the UX-04 packet row. For the closing Grok row the hard numbers are in the untracked run logs: docs/collab/runs/TEST-02-20260906T062614Z.json (stopReason cancelled, 2 turns, $0.0116), ...062814Z.json (cancelled, 10 turns, $0.0864), and cont1 through cont5 (each cancelled on turn 1, ~$0.0365 total) -- all on sessionId fcada16b, and grok/TEST-02 sits at 6cd95b6, an ancestor of feature, i.e. zero commits produced. Those TEST-02 runs are missing from the existing usage log, which stops at the UX-02a resumes.
- ROADMAP.md. The '## Where we are (2026-09-04)' block is false on all three counts: it claims 20 commits (actual 61 on feature/omarchy-sentinel, 60 since main), 116 tests green (actual 210), and 'nothing installed live' (the ledger's live-install row and live-evidence log say the plugin and daemon are installed and running). The '## Wave 3' table lists only W3-01 through W3-07 with a 'Parallel-safe' column and no state column; UX-00, UX-01, UX-02a, UX-02b, UX-03, UX-04, TEST-02 and the joint harness appear nowhere in it. Wave 6's W6-01 bullet already names packets W6-01a to W6-01e, and docs/collab/packets/W6-01a-scanner-core.md exists, so wave 6 is partly ahead of wave 3's own table.
- README.md, what is actually missing. The Usage section's command-line bullet enumerates list / approve --scope / kill [--session] / investigate / dismiss / pause 1h / sentinel-scout --refresh and omits both `notify` and `open`. `notify` does already appear twice elsewhere (the severity-chips bullet and the Configure section's 'sentinel-action notify --severity low=on'), so only `open` is wholly undocumented. The Configure section lists sampler interval, extra bypass flags, known agent names and precious_worktrees for ~/.config/sentinel/config.toml but never mentions the [notify] block. The Security section names SkillSpector generically and links nothing. Everything else I spot-checked in Usage is accurate: Panel.qml's onTextKey handler implements exactly j k a A x i d o 1 2 3 p r, and BarWidget.qml's MouseArea maps MiddleButton to pauseOneHour() and RightButton to runInTerminal(["list"]).
- The CLI surface the README must describe, verified in src/sentinel/action.py. action_main registers subparsers 'notify' (options --severity SEV=on|off repeatable, --reset, --json) and 'open' (positional alert_id, flag --logs), dispatching to cmd_notify and cmd_open; both names are in the COMMANDS frozenset, so _normalize_argv accepts the `sentinel-action <id> open` word order the packet writes. Note that open's alert_id is a required positional even with --logs, so `sentinel-action open --logs` alone is an argparse error -- the packet's own phrasing `sentinel-action <id> open [--logs]` is the correct form to put in the README. open_target picks select-file / parent-dir / cwd / state_dir, open_argv builds ['uwsm-app','--','nautilus',...], and cmd_open prints the path and returns 1 when nautilus is absent.
- The [notify] block the README must document, verified in src/sentinel/notify.py. load_policy() reads the 'notify' table of config.toml and honours toast_high / toast_medium / toast_low, high_timeout / medium_timeout / low_timeout (seconds, clamped to 1..30 by _clamp_timeout against MAX_TIMEOUT_MS), burst_threshold / burst_window / burst_quiet, and plugin_id; defaults live in NotifyPolicy, SeverityPolicy and BurstPolicy (high normal/30s/on, medium normal/15s/on, low low/5s/off, burst 5 per 300 s with 600 s quiet, plugin_id tav.sentinel). Runtime chips write ~/.local/state/sentinel/notify-prefs.json via prefs_path/load_prefs/write_prefs and override toast_* only. packaging/config.toml already carries a commented, accurate copy of this whole block and tests/test_config_template.py asserts it matches the code defaults, so the README should mirror that file rather than restate keys from scratch. is_sticky() plus STICKY_EVENTS = {foreign-write, alert-log-truncated} back the README's existing 'Tamper alerts always show' claim.
- Security-review Status column, evidence per finding. S3 (world-readable state), S6 (recycled PID), S7 (notify blocks the daemon) and S8 (non-atomic, unrotated alert log) are all fixed by the single commit 72031f6 (W3-02), confirmed by git log -S on each mechanism: S3 by the new src/sentinel/fsutil.py (DIR_MODE 0o700, FILE_MODE 0o600, ensure_private_dir / open_private / write_private_atomic) now used by store.py and wrap_record.py; S6 by kill.plan_kill's expected_starttime/get_starttime parameters plus procinfo.read_starttime, with action.expected_starttimes and alert_is_stale gating cmd_kill behind --force-stale; S7 by subprocess.run(..., timeout=5) in notify.py's send path; S8 by store._locked (fcntl.flock on alerts.lock), rotate_if_needed with ROTATED_FILENAME/MAX_ROWS/MAX_BYTES, and write_private_atomic. Every one of those commits is a descendant of fb12040, the commit the review was written against, so the review's header and the new column agree.
- Still-open findings, so the Status column does not overclaim. S1 (self-state tampering) waits on W3-07, which is only a written packet. S2 waits on W6-04. S4 (secrets in wrapper argv) waits on W3-05. S9 on W5-02, S10 on W5-06, S12 on W6-01a (packet written, no code). S11 is partly addressed but not closed: manifest.json now ships a barWidget.schema cliPath setting, yet BarWidget.qml's cliArgv() still falls back to the bare name 'sentinel-action' resolved from PATH and performs no owner-or-regular-file check, which is exactly what the finding asked for.
- AGENTS.md / PROTOCOL.md as they stand. AGENTS.md is titled 'Grok Build rules for this repository (Lead Developer)' and is a live input: scripts/collab/grok-run.sh line 59 instructs Grok to read AGENTS.md and PROTOCOL.md, and scripts/collab/grok-review.sh line 23 points at 'the six security invariants in AGENTS.md'. PROTOCOL.md section 1 is a three-row roles table (Owner / Chief of Design / Lead Developer), section 2 maps AGENTS.md to 'Grok's standing rules', section 3 step 2 hard-codes scripts/collab/grok-run.sh and the grok/<ID> branch-and-worktree flow, section 10 pins the branch model to grok/<ID>, and section 11 is written around --max-turns. The contract the packet says to keep intact is sections 4 (packet format), 5 (report format), 6 (asks) and 2's channel table. CLAUDE.md also still opens with 'a two-agent workflow with Grok Build (Lead Developer)' -- it is not in the packet's Changes list, so it would be left as the only file still asserting the old flow.
- For the new docs/collab/STATUS.md: docs/superpowers/plans/STATUS-AND-NEXT.md already exists and already carries a 'Superseded 2026-09-04' banner redirecting to LEDGER / PROTOCOL / ROADMAP. A fourth status surface needs to say where it sits relative to those three, and that old banner will not mention it. Current true facts for such a page: 210 tests; HEAD ca0465a on feature/omarchy-sentinel; landed this wave = W3-01 (cfd221c), W3-02 (72031f6), UX-00 (50992ba), UX-01 (af74d16), UX-02a (0e1fe2e), UX-03 (48b2eeb), D-008 risk bar (d4173cd), SkillSpector self-scan and baseline (fa50aab), joint adversarial harness (dd6678a); still only packets = W3-03, W3-04, W3-05, W3-07, UX-02b, TEST-02, W6-01a.

**Risks**

- S5 is the packet's one factually wrong instruction. It says to mark 'S5 as fixed by UX-00'. UX-00 (50992ba) fixed only half the finding: Daemon gained the _auto_wds set and the IN_DELETE_SELF/IN_MOVE_SELF branch of the inotify drain loop now discards an auto-added watch and `continue`s instead of calling handle_watch_lost, so a vanishing lock directory no longer emits a medium R-SELF or triggers a full re-inventory. The thrash vector itself is untouched: the IN_CREATE|IN_ISDIR branch immediately above still calls self._inotify.add_watch(path) for every new directory under a watched directory, uncapped, which is the first sentence of the finding. Correct wording is 'mitigated by UX-00 (50992ba); unbounded watch growth still open, W3-03'. Writing plain 'fixed' would violate the packet's own 'do not restate a finding as fixed without checking'.
- The packet asks the ledger's packet table to cover 'TEST-03', but there is no docs/collab/packets/TEST-03-*.md and no report for it. The only artifact is tests/joint/ (test_adversarial.py, SCHEMA.md, three case JSONs) landed by dd6678a, whose commit subject calls it 'Test 3, Claude's half'. TEST-02 has a packet (docs/collab/packets/TEST-02-blackbox-acceptance.md) but produced no commits and no report. Writing a TEST-03 row with a state would be inventing a status for work not in the tree, which the packet's Forbidden section prohibits. Also note the hard rule that tests/joint/ belongs to the Chief, so its state can only be reported, not adjusted.
- The acceptance command `git diff --stat` will not list docs/collab/STATUS.md, because a newly created file is untracked; the check passes vacuously on the one file the packet's whole point is to add. Use `git status --porcelain` or `git add -N` before `git diff --stat HEAD`. Separately, the 'unchanged, still green' baseline the ledger records is 151, while the real number is 210 -- comparing against the ledger rather than a fresh run would read as a regression.
- The ledger's live-install line ('sentinel.service running since 2026-09-05 00:33', inventory timer enabled) and the live-evidence log's 2026-09-06 01:20 UX-02a deployment cannot be re-verified from this worktree: the hard rules forbid systemctl and omarchy. Any 'what is live on the Owner's machine' section in STATUS.md is Owner testimony, so it must be dated and attributed, or raised as a question, never asserted as checked fact.
- AGENTS.md is not inert prose. scripts/collab/grok-run.sh:59 and scripts/collab/grok-review.sh:23 both reference it by name and the latter references 'the six security invariants in AGENTS.md' specifically. The packet forbids editing anything outside documentation, so those scripts will keep pointing at whatever AGENTS.md becomes; the numbered invariant list has to survive the rewrite intact. AGENTS.md's 'What you never do' also forbids editing LEDGER.md, PROTOCOL.md and packets -- a rule written for Grok that, if carried over verbatim into a Claude-squad document, forbids exactly the work W3-06 performs.
- The security review's findings table cites stale line numbers (daemon.py:42-50, daemon.py:646-655, store.py:19, store.py:52, kill.py:131, notify.py:17, rules.py:175, wrap_record.py:37, summarize.py:93-97, install-track-a.sh:45); several no longer point at the cited code after 72031f6 and 50992ba. The packet forbids deleting historical rows and the ledger's own lessons paragraph says never to cite line numbers, so the Status column should carry the corrective and the original citations should stay put with the review's as-of commit (fb12040) stated in the header.
- README Security must point only at tracked files. docs/collab/.gitignore ignores reviews/*.json, so docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json and skillspector-self-baselined.json exist on disk but are untracked and would be dead links in a public repo. The tracked artifacts are docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md, docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md and .skillspector-baseline.yaml (confirmed via git ls-files).
- Version skew will trip any status or README claim that names a release: manifest.json says version 0.2.0, pyproject.toml says 0.1.0, and README's status banner says 'until v0.1 is tagged'. PUBLISHING.md requires bumping the manifest version per release. This is an Owner decision, not something to normalize silently inside a docs-truth packet.
- Packet Context claim 3 slightly overstates its source: PUBLISHING.md's Publish step 1 requires only 'README with Install, Usage, Configure, Remove sections, a LICENSE file'. Development and Security are our own additions, not marketplace requirements. Harmless, but the packet presents the checklist as demanding all six.
- The W3-06 packet itself does not follow PROTOCOL.md section 4's packet format: it has no ## Fact-check, ## Non-goals or ## Report section. Since PROTOCOL section 4 is one of the things this packet is meant to keep intact, and PROTOCOL declares itself the winner in any packet-versus-protocol disagreement, the report location for this packet is undefined -- worth settling before the write stage rather than inventing docs/collab/reports/W3-06-report.md by analogy.


### W3-06 · codebase:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- TESTS agent, T1 is a false negative and its "Fails today" claim is wrong. I ran the exact predicate the design specifies (word-boundary membership over the `## Usage` section of README.md): `notify` -> True and `open` -> True, today, at ca0465a. `notify` appears via the severity-chips bullet ("shared with the daemon through `sentinel-action notify`") and `open` appears via the keys bullet ("`o` open folder"). T1 as written would be green before and after the packet and would prove nothing. - TESTS agent, T5 names an untracked file. It asserts README's Security section must cite `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self-baselined.json` and that it exists. It exists on disk but is gitignored: `docs/collab/.gitignore` contains `reviews/*.json`, and `git ls-files docs/collab/reviews` returns only SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md and W3-01-grok-review.md. Citing it puts a dead link in a public checkout and would make the tests agent's own T6 ("every repo path cited in docs exists") fail on a fresh clone. - SPEC agent, R5's evidence is not in the tree. Do not write a doc row whose citation a reader cannot resolve.
- SPEC agent, R16 restates the packet's one factual error as a requirement. R16's testable is "the S5 row names 50992ba" as fixed by UX-00, and only the risks section walks it back. Verified at src/sentinel/daemon.py:720-724: the `IN_CREATE & IN_ISDIR` branch still calls `self._inotify.add_watch(path)` for every new directory, uncapped, which is the first sentence of S5; and `handle_watch_lost` (daemon.py:591-608) still calls `refresh_watchlist` plus a medium R-SELF for a lost root, which is the second. UX-00 (50992ba) fixed only the `_auto_wds` discard path (daemon.py:729-736). - INTEGRATION agent, two citation errors. (a) `IMPLEMENTED = {"ux-01", "ux-02a"}` is at tests/joint/test_adversarial.py:27, not :29 (the tests agent has :27 right). (b) Its S3 evidence is a live-machine observation ("~/.local/state/sentinel is drwx------ ... ~/.config/sentinel/config.toml is still -rw-r--r--") that cannot be reproduced from this worktree and should not be cited as tree evidence in a doc row. The in-tree proof of the same conclusion is scripts/install-daemon.sh:69-71 (`mkdir -p` then plain `cp packaging/config.toml`, so the file lands at the caller's umask) plus src/sentinel/fsutil.py:19-27, where `ensure_private_dir` chmods the directory only and never an existing file, and daemon.py:451 `ensure_private_dir(config_dir())` which therefore leaves that config.toml alone. Cite those.
- SECURITY agent, the "hashes" overclaim is real but must be scoped to one sentence. Confirmed: `Alert.hashes` is declared at models.py:29 and only ever passed through (daemon.py:320), serialized (summarize.py:25,68) or read (investigate.py:236,249,519) - grep finds no producer anywhere in src/, so nothing computes a content hash. But the phrase appears in three places with two different meanings. README.md:5 ("It reads metadata only: paths, hashes, process names and command lines") is a description of behavior and is false today - correct it. PROTOCOL.md section 9 item 2 ("Hashes and paths only") and AGENTS.md invariant 2 ("Paths and hashes only") are ceilings on what a packet may read, not claims that hashing happens; the spec agent's R19 requires section 9 be left unchanged, so leave both verbatim. Run `git status --porcelain` alongside it and record both in the report rather than silently reinterpreting the verbatim command.
- Nothing at runtime reads any target doc except through those two collab scripts. No test in tests/*.py or tests/joint/*.py reads a repo .md file, and the live daemon runs from ~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv, out of this worktree's reach. The pytest-unchanged half of acceptance is structurally guaranteed.

**Final position.** "Treat as settled, all five of us: (1) baseline is ca0465a / clean / 210 passed, and the 210 includes tests/joint - any count written into a doc carries the command and the sha. (2) S3/S6/S7/S8 are fixed by 72031f6, cited as that commit, with S3 carrying the installer-config caveat. (3) S5 is 'partly fixed by UX-00 (50992ba); unbounded auto-watch add and rescan-on-root-loss remain, W3-03' - the packet is wrong here and the report records the deviation. (4) S1, S2, S4, S9, S10, S11, S12 stay OPEN; no row is marked fixed without a sha. (5) No TEST-03 row anywhere - it is an ask, not a status. (6) TEST-02 is 'work uncommitted in .worktrees/grok-TEST-02, zero commits' or it does not get a state word at all. (7) README's Security section cites only the three tracked artefacts: docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md, .skillspector-baseline.yaml - never a reviews/*.json or a runs/*.json, all of which are gitignored. (8) README's Configure points at packaging/config.toml as the authoritative [notify] list rather than restating defaults that tests/test_config_template.py already pins. (9) README.md:5's 'hashes' sentence is corrected; PROTOCOL section 9 and AGENTS.md invariant 2 are left verbatim. (10) Historical rows are corrected in place with a dated note; the security review's 'at fb12040 vs main' header and its original line-number citations are a dated snapshot and stay. (11) Every edit takes an explicit absolute path - no recursive sed, or the two nested worktrees lose the only copy of the TEST-02 work. (12) STATUS.md's 'live on the Owner's machine' section is written as dated Owner testimony citing the ledger's live-install and live-evidence rows; no systemctl, no omarchy, no reading ~/.local/state.\n\nFour things need a ruling before anyone writes, and I agree with the integration agent that they are sequencing questions, not wording ones: (a) TEST-03 - ask or drop; (b) whether CLAUDE.md and README's Development sentence, both still asserting the two-agent Grok flow and neither in the Changes list, are in scope, since leaving them unmet is the packet's Goal unmet; (c) what AGENTS.md becomes, given grok-run.sh and grok-review.sh will keep pointing at it and the six invariants are load-bearing for in-flight squads; (d) whether to split - README, PROTOCOL, AGENTS and the review's Status column now, holding the LEDGER wave-3 rows, the ROADMAP wave-3 table and STATUS.md until W3-03/04/07 land. I lean to the split: STATUS.md is born stale the moment any of those three merges, and W3-04's Context claim 1 already asserts 'the STATUS doc repeats it' about a file that does not exist yet.\n\nOn the tests agent's Lane B: it is a good idea failing the packet's own acceptance gate, and two of its twelve tests are wrong as designed (T1 green today, T5 citing an untracked file). Take Lane A now - the eight hand-run fact-check commands recorded in the report - and file Lane B as a follow-up packet with T1 rewritten against the CLI bullet, T5 against the three tracked paths, and STATUS.md's count written only after the new tests land and pytest is rerun."

### W3-06 · integration

W3-06 is documentation-only and cannot reach the live daemon (pid 18419 runs from ~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv, not this worktree), but it is not risk-free. Its real hazards are three: it rewrites the exact ledger and roadmap rows that W3-03, W3-04 and W3-07 must each rewrite on merge, guaranteeing conflicts; it edits AGENTS.md, which scripts/collab/grok-run.sh:59 and grok-review.sh:23 inject as standing rules and which carries the six security invariants the in-flight squads are running under right now; and, because the auto-mode instruction favours sed, a repo-wide substitution would silently corrupt two live nested worktrees (.worktrees/grok-TEST-02, .worktrees/grok-UX-02a) whose docs/collab/LEDGER.md is byte-identical to ours and which hold untracked, unrecoverable work. Two of the packet's own instructions also conflict with its Forbidden section: marking S5 fixed by UX-00 is an overclaim (UX-00 fixed only the transient-directory half; the every-new-directory auto-watch at daemon.py:720-723 and the missing refresh rate limit are still W3-03's job), and TEST-03 exists nowhere in the tree except this packet. Recommend splitting the packet: do README, PROTOCOL, AGENTS and the security-review Status column now, hold the ledger wave-3 rows, roadmap table and the new STATUS.md until W3-03/04/07 land.

**Risks**

- REPO-WIDE `sed -i` WOULD CORRUPT LIVE IN-FLIGHT WORKTREES. This is the sharpest mechanical hazard, and it is amplified by the active instruction to prefer sed and heredocs over the Edit tool. Two other worktrees are nested INSIDE this one and hidden from git by .gitignore:7 (`.worktrees/`): .worktrees/grok-TEST-02 and .worktrees/grok-UX-02a, each carrying a full docs/collab/ copy. .worktrees/grok-TEST-02/docs/collab/LEDGER.md is byte-identical to ours (both md5 87d955dc052c8f184484f127fbd7c3b7), so any recursive substitution or `find -exec sed` matching a ledger string rewrites it silently, and `git status` here will never show it. grok-TEST-02 also holds untracked, unrecoverable work: docs/collab/reports/TEST-02-report.md and tests/acceptance/. Mitigation: pass every edit an explicit absolute path list; never use -r, --include, or find -exec for this packet.
- THE PACKET ASKS FOR A ROW THAT WOULD VIOLATE ITS OWN FORBIDDEN CLAUSE. W3-06's Context claim 2 and Changes reference "the TEST-02/TEST-03 split", but the string TEST-03 appears nowhere in the tree except the W3-06 packet itself (grep over all .md outside .worktrees). There is no docs/collab/packets/TEST-03-*.md, no branch, no report. Writing a TEST-03 ledger or roadmap row is exactly the "inventing a status for work not in the tree" the packet forbids. Per PROTOCOL.md section 12 that is not Done. The strongest true statement is "in progress, work uncommitted in .worktrees/grok-TEST-02".
- STATUS.md IS BORN STALE AND IS ALREADY LOAD-BEARING FOR ANOTHER PACKET. W3-04's Context claim 1 asserts "the STATUS doc repeats it" about `evaluate_child_shell` being unwired - but docs/collab/STATUS.md does not exist yet, so the W3-04 agent's fact-check of that claim must come back REFUTED or UNVERIFIED today. Worse, whatever STATUS.md says about the child-shell rule, the watch-root count, or the test total becomes false the moment W3-03, W3-04 or W3-07 merges. The packet itself says STATUS.md is "regenerated by hand at the end of a wave", which argues for writing it after those merges, not now.

Each in-flight packet adds config keys the README does not yet document: W3-03 adds `[scout] project_roots`, W3-04 adds `[daemon] child_shell` and `[rules] net_helper_ignore`, W3-07 adds `pause_max`. - EDITING README's REMOVE BLOCK IS THE ONE WAY THIS PACKET CAN DESTROY LIVE DATA. README.md:55-58 are commands the Owner pastes on a machine that currently holds 95 hand-approved allowlist entries (22 KB allowlist.json) and 72 KB of alerts.jsonl under ~/.local/state/sentinel, plus the installed venv at ~/.local/share/tav.sentinel.
