- BLURRING THE "INSTALLER DOES NOT START THE DAEMON" LINE COULD PUSH THE OWNER TO A systemctl HE DID NOT INTEND. README.md:22 currently states the installer deliberately does not start the service, and the ledger records that the Owner started it by hand on 2026-09-05 00:33. Any rewording that implies the installer or the panel's Install daemon button starts it invites a restart of a service that has been running for a day.
- EDITING AN IN-FLIGHT PACKET FILE WOULD DESYNC A RUNNING AGENT. Packets are documentation, so "only .md" does not protect them. docs/collab/packets/W3-03-scout-v2-precise-watchlist.md has a genuine defect - the paragraph beginning "Added by security review 2026-09-05 (S5)" sits under `## Non-goals` when its content (auto-watch only under hooks roots, drop auto-added descriptors silently, rate-limit refresh_watchlist to 60 s, the 20-cycle lock-dir regression test) is clearly `## Changes`. Fixing it now would change the spec under a running implementer. Report it as an ask; leave the file alone. Same for W3-04 and W3-07.
- THE W3-02 LEDGER ROW HAS A VERDICT THAT CAN NEVER ARRIVE. LEDGER.md:20 says "pending Grok review (inline)", but W3-06's own Context claim 5 is that the Owner ended the Grok collaboration on 2026-09-06. The packet forbids deleting historical rows, so this must be corrected in place with a dated note (for example: reviewed by Chief; Grok review never ran, collaboration ended 2026-09-06) rather than quietly dropped or left dangling.
- REWRITING AGENTS.md LEAVES scripts/collab/ LYING, AND THIS PACKET CANNOT FIX IT. grok-run.sh:59 and grok-review.sh:23 both instruct a Grok developer to read AGENTS.md; if AGENTS.md becomes a Claude-squad document those scripts are incoherent. They are shell, not .md, so touching them breaks the acceptance criterion. The correct move is an ask for a follow-up packet covering scripts/collab/, noted in the W3-06 report as Not done.
- ROLE-RULE TENSION COULD SELF-BLOCK A SUBAGENT. AGENTS.md:35 forbids editing LEDGER.md, PROTOCOL.md or any packet, while W3-06 assigns exactly those files. That is coherent only because W3-06 is Chief-owned (LEDGER.md:24 marks it "in progress" against the Chief). Any subagent that loads AGENTS.md as standing rules may refuse the work or, worse, edit AGENTS.md to unblock itself. State the Chief-owned exception explicitly when delegating.
- SEQUENCING: landing W3-06 now buys a stale page and three merge conflicts. The lower-risk shape is to split it - do README, PROTOCOL.md, AGENTS.md and the SECURITY-REVIEW Status column now (each collides with at most one in-flight packet), and hold the LEDGER wave-3 rows, the ROADMAP wave-3 table and STATUS.md until W3-03, W3-04 and W3-07 merge. If it must land whole, keep every edit to a tight line-scoped hunk so the later conflicts resolve cheaply, and stamp STATUS.md and the ledger with the commit and test count they were true at (ca0465a, 210 tests) so staleness is visible rather than silent.


### W3-06 · security

W3-06 is docs-only, so it cannot break a runtime invariant by executing anything — its security failure mode is false assurance: a README line, a STATUS.md row, or a "fixed" cell in the review table that promises a control the tree does not implement, on which the Owner then relies on a live machine. The sharpest instance is invariant 8: notify.is_sticky (src/sentinel/notify.py:78-80) and Notifier.send (notify.py:420-424) do bypass pause and prefs for STICKY_EVENTS = {"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"} (notify.py:38-40), and SentinelModel.js:23,58 mirrors it — but nothing in the tree ever emits those events. The daemon's only R-SELF emissions are IN_Q_OVERFLOW (daemon.py:568-574), IN_DELETE_SELF/IN_MOVE_SELF (daemon.py:589-596) and the self-path branch of rules.evaluate_write (rules.py:205-217), and that last one is unreachable for the files that matter because daemon.OPERATIONAL_NAMES (daemon.py:45-59) deliberately ignores alerts.jsonl, allowlist.json, allowlist-session.json, pause_until, watchlist.json, notify-prefs.json and notify-burst.json. S1 is still open and W3-07 is unstarted, so README's opening claim "or something touched Sentinel itself" and the panel's "Tamper alerts always show" describe a sticky path with no producer. The second failure mode is exfiltration by documentation: alerts.jsonl is 0600 and carries full agent command lines (S4 unfixed), while docs/ is a 0644 tracked file — a pasted live row moves metadata across that boundary. The third is method: fact-checking "the timer is enabled" by running systemctl/omarchy on the Owner's machine would break the packet's own Forbidden list and AGENTS.md invariant 6.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT 1 — runs as the user, never root. How W3-06 breaks it: a rewritten Install/Remove section or a STATUS.md 'how it is deployed' row that reaches for sudo or `systemctl --system` to make install read cleanly, or that documents `--force-stale` as a convenience. Ground truth to write against: scripts/install-daemon.sh:65-66,81 use `systemctl --user` only; kill.plan_kill (src/sentinel/kill.py:107-166) compares os.getuid() against pid_uid() and re-checks expected_starttime; action.cmd_kill (src/sentinel/action.py:~400-412) refuses a PID-bearing alert older than 10 minutes with no recorded start time unless --force-stale. Docs must keep every command `--user`, never introduce sudo, describe the units as user units under ~/.config/systemd/user, and if --force-stale appears at all, say it disables the stale-PID guard and does not disable the uid or start-time checks.
- INVARIANT 2 — metadata only, never file bodies or credentials. How W3-06 breaks it: the new STATUS.md ('what is live on the Owner's machine') and the ledger's live-evidence rows are exactly where a rendered `sentinel-action <id> investigate` or a raw alerts.jsonl line gets pasted. Alert rows carry `cmdline` (rules.evaluate_process, src/sentinel/rules.py:132-146; wrap_record.record_launch, src/sentinel/wrap_record.py:37-40) and S4 (secrets passed as agent flags) is still open, assigned to W3-05. alerts.jsonl is 0600; docs/ is committed 0644. Docs must cite counts, rule ids, alert ids and a line number in alerts.jsonl — the way the 2026-09-06 01:20 ledger row already does — and never a pasted row, argv array, or config value. Any illustrative example comes from tests/fixtures/, not live state. Separately, README's line 'It reads metadata only: paths, hashes, process names and command lines' must be corrected: the only sha256 in src/sentinel is allowlist.fingerprint over public alert fields (src/sentinel/allowlist.py:30); nothing computes a content hash of a watched file, investigate._write_verify (src/sentinel/investigate.py:236-240) only prints a `sha256sum` line for the user to run, and content hashing is W6-03. Left as-is, the README claims Sentinel opens watched files.
- INVARIANT 3 — auth-kind files are never opened. How W3-06 breaks it: describing Investigate as showing 'what changed in the file', documenting a diff/content option in Configure, or quietly treating D-005 ('May Sentinel parse agent settings bodies?', open in LEDGER.md) as decided. Ground truth: scout.classify_kind (src/sentinel/scout.py:26-40) derives 'auth' from the filename alone ('auth' or 'credentials' in the lowered name); scout._entry_for (scout.py:43-50) records path, kind, size and mtime from a single stat(); daemon watches auth files via inotify on the parent dir (WATCH_KINDS, daemon.py:44; daemon.py:197) and hands rules.evaluate_write a Path it only resolve()s. Docs must state that auth files are classified by name, watched through their parent directory, recorded as path/size/mtime, and never opened; that the only content-reading step is the `sha256sum` command the user may choose to run from Investigate's 'Verify it yourself' section; and that D-005 remains open.
- INVARIANT 4 — state files 0600 inside 0700 dirs. How W3-06 breaks it: marking S3 'fixed by W3-02' and then generalising it in the README Security section to 'everything Sentinel writes is private'. What is actually true: sentinel.fsutil DIR_MODE 0o700 / FILE_MODE 0o600 (src/sentinel/fsutil.py:15-16), applied by store._locked/append_alert/update_alert_status, allowlist.py:58, health.py:156, notify.write_prefs and BurstTracker.save, wrap_record.record_launch, and daemon.py:450-461 — landed in 72031f6. What is not true: scripts/install-daemon.sh:69-71 creates ~/.config/sentinel with plain `mkdir -p` and copies config.toml with `cp` under umask 022, so the file lands 0644; fsutil.ensure_private_dir (fsutil.py:19-27) chmods the directory only, never existing files, so the daemon's tightening at daemon.py:451 leaves that config.toml at 0644. The Summarize key is only checked, never created or tightened: summarize.resolve_api_key (src/sentinel/summarize.py:78-88) returns None when `mode & 0o077`. Write exactly that, scoped to ~/.local/state/sentinel plus the daemon's start-time chmod of ~/.config/sentinel, with the config.toml and api_key caveats.
- INVARIANT 5 — argv arrays only, never shell strings. How W3-06 breaks it: a README Usage or STATUS.md repro one-liner that interpolates alert data into a shell command (`sentinel-action $(sentinel-action list | ...) kill`), or a hand-retyped 'Verify it yourself' line with the quoting dropped. Ground truth to preserve: notify.run (src/sentinel/notify.py:186-192, argv + timeout=5, no shell), build_argv/build_summary_argv return lists (notify.py:223-270), action.open_argv and launch_detached (action.py:358-372), BarWidget.cliArgv/runInTerminal/openLogs (BarWidget.qml:35-66) and Panel.qml:126,131 all pass arrays to Quickshell.execDetached, and investigate quotes display-only commands through shlex.quote (investigate.py:94). Every command in the docs must be a literal argv the user types with `<id>` placeholders, and any reproduced Investigate command must be copied verbatim with its quoting intact.
- INVARIANT 6 — no network from the daemon. How W3-06 breaks it: a Configure section that documents SENTINEL_SUMMARIZE_URL and SENTINEL_API_KEY as supported settings, which advertises the unfixed S10 endpoint redirect as a feature and pushes a key into the environment; or a STATUS.md row implying Summarize is wired up. Ground truth: daemon.py's imports (lines 1-28) contain no urllib, socket, http client or cloud SDK, and the daemon never imports sentinel.summarize or sentinel.kill; the single network call in the tree is summarize._default_http_post (src/sentinel/summarize.py:93-126), a separate oneshot whose bundle is key-filtered by ALLOWED_KEYS (summarize.py:~60-71) and whose URL still comes from the environment (S10, open, W5-06). Configure should document only what exists: [kill].precious_worktrees and the [notify] block from packaging/config.toml, plus the [daemon] keys load_config actually reads — sampler_interval, coalesce_window, extra_bypass_flags, known_basenames (daemon.py:244-251). Note that README currently lists 'sampler interval, extra bypass flags, known agent names' while packaging/config.toml ships neither those keys nor a [daemon] section.
- INVARIANT 7 — no silent auto-kill. How W3-06 breaks it: the README click-path rewrite claiming a notification action kills a process. notify.build_argv appends `--exec sentinel-action <id> menu` (notify.py:219-234), and the ledger's own 'known limits' says toast click and menu actions run headless until W3-05. Ground truth: only sentinel-action imports sentinel.kill (action.py:18) — the daemon never does — and every kill goes through kill.confirm_kill (kill.py:71-105), which for a precious worktree demands the typed phrase 'kill session' on an interactive tty. Docs must say the notification action opens the alert menu in a terminal that confirms, keep the W3-05 headless limit in the ledger until W3-05 lands, and document `-y/--yes` only as a scripting flag that skips the confirm while leaving the uid and start-time checks in place.
- INVARIANT 8 — sticky self-tamper alerts bypass pause and prefs. The mechanism is real and must not be weakened by a doc rewrite: notify.is_sticky (notify.py:78-80) is checked before _is_paused() and before the per-severity enabled check in Notifier.send (notify.py:420-424), STICKY_EVENTS is code rather than config by deliberate comment (notify.py:38-40), SentinelModel.js:23,58 mirrors the same set so the panel always shows them, and action.cmd_notify prints the sticky line (action.py:330). The problem W3-06 must not paper over: nothing produces those events. grep for 'foreign-write' and 'alert-log-truncated' hits only consumers (notify.py, investigate.py:58,285, SentinelModel.js, tests/) — no daemon emitter. R-SELF today fires only for IN_Q_OVERFLOW (daemon.py:568-574), a deleted or moved watched root (daemon.py:589-596), and a write to a self path by a non-Sentinel writer (rules.py:205-217) that daemon.OPERATIONAL_NAMES (daemon.py:45-59) blocks for alerts.jsonl, allowlist.json, allowlist-session.json, pause_until, watchlist.json, notify-prefs.json and notify-burst.json. README and STATUS.md must name what R-SELF covers today and state plainly that the state files are ignored on purpose so self-tamper is not yet detected (S1 open, W3-07). The review's Status column must show S1 OPEN.

**Risks**

- 'S5 as fixed by UX-00' is wrong as stated and must not go in the Status column that way. UX-00 (50992ba) fixed one half: an auto-added watch on a transient directory no longer alerts or re-inventories (daemon.py:715-740, the _auto_wds set introduced at daemon.py:414). The other half stands — every new directory under a watched dir still gets an unbounded watch (daemon.py:720-724, no cap, no accounting against max_user_watches) and losing a real root still runs refresh_watchlist plus a medium alert (handle_watch_lost, daemon.py:589-611). Honest cell: 'partly fixed by UX-00 (50992ba); unbounded auto-watch add and rescan-on-root-loss remain, W3-03'.
- The S3/S6/S7/S8 'fixed by W3-02' cells need the commit checked before they are written: all four land in 72031f6 (fsutil.py and kill.py have no earlier commit; notify.py's 5 s timeout came with 72031f6, not af74d16). The ledger row for W3-02 cites 72031f6 while the report commit is 961e71e — cite the code commit, not the docs commit. And S3's fix does not extend to ~/.config/sentinel/config.toml (see invariant 4).
- S1 and S2 must stay OPEN. S2 (rules._is_sentinel_writer, rules.py:170-188, trusting any writer whose basename starts with 'sentinel' or any python process with 'sentinel' in argv) is dormant only because inotify supplies no writer PID. Docs must not describe 'Sentinel skips its own writes' as a security control; it is a name check that becomes exploitable the moment W6-04 lands writer attribution.
- Nothing caps a pause. action.parse_duration (action.py:44,54-67) accepts any Ns/Nm/Nh/Nd, write_pause writes it verbatim, and is_paused (action.py:95-102) then gates every non-sticky toast — `sentinel-action pause 3650d` silently mutes the product forever. The pause cap is item 3 of the S1 plan and is unbuilt. README's 'Middle click pauses notifications for an hour' is true but must not be surrounded by language implying pauses are bounded or self-expiring.
- STATUS.md is the highest-risk new file in this packet: 'what is live on the Owner's machine' invites both a paste out of a 0600 alerts.jsonl into a 0644 tracked file and a live-machine query to verify it. Neither is allowed. Verify deployment claims by reading scripts/install-daemon.sh and the ledger's dated evidence rows, or by asking the Owner — never by running systemctl, omarchy, or reading ~/.local/state/sentinel; that is the packet's own Forbidden list and AGENTS.md invariant 6.

Write '210 passed (.venv/bin/pytest -q at <sha>, includes tests/joint)' rather than a bare number.
- Do not describe the installer as path-safe. S9 is still open: scripts/install-daemon.sh:60 rewrites units with `sed "s|%h/Work/sentinel/.venv|$VENV|g"`, so a venv path containing a pipe corrupts the unit silently (W5-02). Likewise S11 stands: BarWidget.cliArgv (BarWidget.qml:35-39) resolves the bare name 'sentinel-action' from PATH when the widget's cliPath setting is empty.

- CONTRADICTION - TEST-03 does not exist in the tree. The packet (line 22) requires a ledger row for TEST-03, but there is no `docs/collab/packets/TEST-03-*.md`, no report, and no ledger row; `grep -rn 'TEST-03' docs/` returns only the W3-06 packet itself. Note also that SCHEMA.md:6 names a report at `docs/collab/reports/UX-tests-report.md` which does not exist.
- CONTRADICTION - TEST-02 has no state word that fits. All seven `docs/collab/runs/TEST-02-*.json` runs ended `stopReason: cancelled`, yet the work exists: `.worktrees/grok-TEST-02` (branch `grok/TEST-02`, HEAD `6cd95b6`) has untracked `tests/acceptance/test_blackbox_grok.py` (715 lines), `tests/acceptance/__init__.py` and `docs/collab/reports/TEST-02-report.md` (35 lines), with zero commits on the branch beyond the feature branch. PROTOCOL's state vocabulary (written, running, reported, returned, accepted, escalated) has no word for "delivered uncommitted in a worktree". W3-06 is docs-only, so it cannot commit that work. Needs a ruling on the state word and on whether a separate packet lands the tree.
- CONTRADICTION - S5's attribution. The packet (line 29) says mark S5 fixed by UX-00, but the review's own Fix-in column for S5 says W3-03, and W3-03 is still `written` in the ledger. UX-00 (50992ba, daemon.py:732-748) stops a lost transient-directory watch from raising R-SELF or triggering an inventory - which does kill the observed Claude Code lock-dir thrash - but S5's text also covers unbounded watch growth ("every new directory under a watched directory gets a watch"), which UX-00 does not address. Recommend Status = "mitigated by UX-00 (50992ba); watch-growth half still open, W3-03" rather than a flat "fixed", or get an explicit ruling.
- The `git diff --stat` acceptance command cannot prove what it claims. `docs/collab/STATUS.md` is a new file: while untracked it does not appear in `git diff --stat` at all, and once `git add`ed it disappears from unstaged diff too. As written the command would print nothing and still "pass" on a tree containing a stray .py edit that was staged. Suggest running `git status --porcelain` as well, or `git add -A && git diff --cached --stat`, and confirming with the Chief before deviating from the verbatim command.
- Scope gap - `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root carries the same now-false claim the packet fixes elsewhere: "You are the Chief of Design in a two-agent workflow with Grok Build (Lead Developer) under a human Owner", plus "Grok's worktrees live at `.worktrees/grok-<ID>`". It is documentation, so Forbidden permits editing it, but the Changes list omits it. Leaving it makes the Goal ("every claim in the repository's documentation is true") unmet. Needs a ruling.
- Scope gap - `README.md` §Development says "How this project is built, by a design agent and a developer agent under one human owner, is described in `docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md`". README is in the Changes list but only for Usage / Configure / Security, so this stale sentence is not covered by any bullet. Same question as CLAUDE.md.
- Rewriting AGENTS.md leaves live tooling describing a dead workflow. `scripts/collab/grok-run.sh`, `scripts/collab/grok-review.sh` and `.grok/roles/` remain in the tree and are not documentation, so this packet cannot touch them; PROTOCOL §3 step 2 currently points at grok-run.sh by name. Decide whether PROTOCOL should describe those scripts as retired tooling (a docs-only change) or whether a follow-up packet removes them.
- Ambiguity - what AGENTS.md becomes. The file's entire premise is "rules Grok appends to its system prompt". `AGENTS.md` is also a cross-vendor convention many coding agents read automatically. The packet says only "describe the current workflow" without saying whether AGENTS.md survives as generic contributor-agent rules, is folded into CLAUDE.md, or becomes a pointer to PROTOCOL.md. The engineering standard and six security invariants it carries are load-bearing and must survive wherever they land.
- The packet violates PROTOCOL §4's own packet format: it has no `## Fact-check`, no `## Non-goals`, and no `## Report` section. PROTOCOL §"Status of this document" says that when a packet and PROTOCOL disagree, PROTOCOL wins and the packet is wrong. Practical consequence: the report path is unspecified. Convention gives `docs/collab/reports/W3-06-report.md`; confirm before writing elsewhere.
- STATUS.md's "what is live on the Owner's machine" cannot be verified from the repository. The only in-tree sources are the LEDGER Live install row (daemon running since 2026-09-05 00:33) and the live-evidence row for 2026-09-06 01:20 (`omarchy plugin update tav.sentinel --yes`). Standing rules forbid touching the live machine and Forbidden forbids inventing status, so that section must be written as "last confirmed by the Owner on <date>" rather than as present fact, or the Owner must be asked.
- Do not "correct" the security review's own header. `SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md:1` says "at fb12040 vs main" and the method paragraph says "86-file branch diff"; the tree is now ca0465a with 117 files changed vs main. Those are a dated snapshot of when the review ran, not stale claims - rewriting them would falsify the record. Only the Fix in / Status columns are in scope.
- Two version numbers disagree and neither file is .md: `manifest.json:5` is `"version": "0.2.0"` while `pyproject.toml:4` is `version = "0.1.0"`. This makes README's "Status: pre-release ... Do not install ... until v0.1 is tagged" read oddly. The version files are out of scope for a docs-only packet, so either soften the README sentence or file an ask; do not edit either file.

So README:41 "Tamper alerts always show" describes a path with no producer. S1 must show OPEN.
- README:5 "It reads metadata only: paths, hashes, process names and command lines" needs the word "hashes" corrected or dropped. Alert.hashes (models.py:29) is declared and plumbed but never populated by any producer; the only sha256 in src/sentinel is allowlist.fingerprint (allowlist.py:30) over public alert fields, and investigate.py:236-240 merely prints a `sha256sum` line for the user to run. Content hashing is W6-03.
- Nothing caps a pause: parse_duration (action.py:54-67) accepts any Ns/Nm/Nh/Nd and is_paused just compares timestamps, so `sentinel-action pause 3650d` mutes every non-sticky toast indefinitely. Item 3 of the S1 plan (`pause_max`) is unbuilt. Don't surround README:39 with language implying pauses are bounded.
- S11 stands: BarWidget.qml cliArgv falls back to the bare name "sentinel-action" resolved from PATH when the widget's cliPath setting is empty, with no owner-or-regular-file check. S9 stands: install-daemon.sh builds units with a sed `|` delimiter over user paths.
- The nested-worktree hazard is real and is the sharpest mechanical risk. .worktrees/ is gitignored, and md5sum confirms .worktrees/grok-TEST-02/docs/collab/LEDGER.md is byte-identical to ours (87d955dc052c8f184484f127fbd7c3b7); that worktree holds unrecoverable untracked work. Any recursive sed, `--include`, or `find -exec` would corrupt it and `git status` here would never show it. Explicit absolute path per edit, always.
- Do not touch the security review's header. "at fb12040 vs main" and "86-file branch diff" are a dated snapshot of when the review ran, not stale claims; rewriting them falsifies the record. Only the Fix in / Status columns are in scope. Same principle protects the LEDGER's Grok usage log rows and the Lessons paragraph.
- STATUS.md's "what is live on the Owner's machine" is Owner testimony only — the sources are LEDGER:12 and the live-evidence rows at :66-68 — and no systemctl or omarchy may be run to check. Write it as "last confirmed by the Owner on <date>". Its test count must carry its command and scope: "210 passed (`.venv/bin/pytest -q` at ca0465a; pyproject sets no testpaths, so this includes the Chief-owned tests/joint suite)". Also note docs/superpowers/plans/STATUS-AND-NEXT.md already exists carrying a "Superseded 2026-09-04" banner that redirects to LEDGER/PROTOCOL/ROADMAP and will not mention a fourth surface.
- tests/joint/ is off limits and holds two truths this packet can only report: test_adversarial.py:27 `IMPLEMENTED = {"ux-01", "ux-02a"}` will silently disagree with a ledger that says W3-01/W3-02 landed, and SCHEMA.md:6-7 points at docs/collab/reports/UX-tests-report.md, which does not exist. Both are asks.
- Version skew is out of scope: pyproject.toml:3 = 0.1.0, manifest.json:5 = 0.2.0, and README:7 says "until v0.1 is tagged". Neither file is .md. Soften the README sentence or file an ask; do not normalize the versions inside a docs-truth packet.
- The tests agent's Lane B (tests/test_docs_truth.py) fails the packet's own "only .md files" gate. Take Lane A — its eight fact-check commands map cleanly onto the report's required evidence table — or get the acceptance line amended in writing. Do not add the file and reinterpret the gate. If Lane B is ever taken, STATUS.md's count must be written after the new tests land, or the page lies on its first day.

**Final position.** Settled and safe to build on: the 210/ca0465a/60-commit/117-file baseline; S3, S6, S7, S8 all fixed by 72031f6 and by nothing else; S1, S2, S4, S9, S10, S11, S12 open; S3 scoped to state Sentinel writes, not the installer-written config.toml; the README's three concrete gaps (open, [notify], no Security paths); the LEDGER's five stale facts; the ROADMAP's three false numbers; and the six AGENTS.md invariants surviving verbatim because two shell scripts cite them by name.

Three items are blocking and belong to the Chief, not to the writer: (1) TEST-03 exists in no file but the packet itself — a row would break the packet's own Forbidden clause; (2) S5 must read "partly fixed by UX-00 50992ba; unbounded auto-watch add and rescan-on-root-loss remain, W3-03" — the packet's flat "fixed" is factually wrong and daemon.py:721-723 proves it; (3) TEST-02 has no legal state word for "delivered uncommitted in a worktree". A fourth, softer: the packet lacks the ## Report section PROTOCOL §4 requires, so the report path needs confirming.

Two corrections change what the write stage should do. The run JSONs and the SkillSpector self-scan JSONs are gitignored, so no ledger row or README line may cite them as in-tree evidence, and the tests agent's path-existence tests must query `git ls-files` rather than the filesystem or they will pass here and fail on a clean clone. And the integration agent's sequencing argument rests on W3-03/04/07 squads that the tree shows no trace of — the merge-conflict risk on the two tables is real, but it is a risk about future merges, not about live agents.

On sequencing I part company with the integration agent's split: I would land W3-06 whole, in tight line-scoped hunks, with every number stamped "as of ca0465a, 210 tests". Holding STATUS.md for an event nobody in this worktree can observe trades a page that dates its own staleness for a page that does not exist, and the ledger rows most in need of correction (UX-02a, W3-02's unreachable verdict) collide with nothing.

Mechanically: explicit absolute paths on every edit, never a recursive sed — .worktrees/grok-TEST-02/docs/collab/LEDGER.md is byte-identical to ours, gitignored, and holds unrecoverable untracked work. Run `git status --porcelain` beside the packet's verbatim `git diff --stat`, since the latter cannot see the new STATUS.md at all.

### W3-06 · tests

Test design for W3-06 (docs truth), read-only stage. Baseline confirmed: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` = 210 passed, 210 collected, in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel. The packet declares "No unit tests" and its acceptance gate is `git diff --stat # only .md files`, so the design comes in two lanes. Lane A (fits the packet as written): 8 fact-check commands run by hand and recorded in the report. Lane B (recommended, needs a one-line packet amendment to allow one .py file): a single new file /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_docs_truth.py holding 12 tests that keep the docs honest after this packet, modelled on the existing precedent /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/tests/test_config_template.py (ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1], reads a repo file, compares it to code). No existing test breaks either way; the suite touches no .md file. Every "fixed" claim the packet wants to write into the security review was checked against code here and four of five hold; S5's attribution is contested.

**Findings**

The autouse `_isolated_xdg` fixture in tests/conftest.py:12 already applies and is harmless here. No tmp_path, no monkeypatch needed except in T13.

- EXISTING TESTS THAT BREAK: none, in either lane. I ran `.venv/bin/pytest -q` on the current tree: 210 passed in 1.80s, and no test in tests/*.py reads a .md file (`grep -rn 'README\|\.md' tests/*.py` returns only string literals in tests/test_investigate.py:267/292/453/455/459, which are fixture paths like /tmp/skillspector-report.md, not repo docs). Two near-misses worth naming: (1) tests/test_config_template.py::test_config_template_parses_and_matches_code_defaults asserts `load_policy(packaging/config.toml) == NotifyPolicy()` -- it breaks the moment anyone 'documents the [notify] block' by editing packaging/config.toml rather than README.md, and packaging/config.toml is not a .md file so the packet already forbids it; (2) tests/test_systemd_units.py and tests/test_kill.py:200 test_config_template_precious_worktrees_empty read packaging/ files for the same reason. Lane B changes the suite total from 210 to 210+N, so STATUS.md's count must be written AFTER the new tests land and pytest is rerun -- writing '210' first and adding tests second leaves the new page lying on its first day.

**Risks**

- Acceptance-gate conflict, needs a Chief decision before anyone writes code: the packet says 'Tests: No unit tests' and its acceptance is `git diff --stat  # only .md files`. Lane B adds tests/test_docs_truth.py, a .py file, and fails that gate as written. Either amend the acceptance line to 'only .md files plus tests/test_docs_truth.py' or take Lane A. Do not add the file and quietly reinterpret the gate.
- TEST-03 has no packet file. The packet's Changes ask the LEDGER and ROADMAP to reflect 'TEST-02, TEST-03', but `ls docs/collab/packets/` shows TEST-02-blackbox-acceptance.md and no TEST-03. Writing a TEST-03 row is 'inventing a status for work not in the tree', which the packet's Forbidden section bans. T7 and T9 would fail on a TEST-03 row with no file. Resolve first: write the TEST-03 packet, or drop it from the docs.
- S5 attribution is contested and the packet asserts one side. docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md:15 routes S5 to W3-03 and the Actions section repeats 'S5 to W3-03'; the packet says to mark S5 fixed by UX-00. UX-00 (50992ba) does stop transient directories raising R-SELF and triggering inventory, but the W3-03 packet (scout v2, precise watchlist) is still 'written' and the S5 finding also covers 'every new directory under a watched directory gets a watch'. Safest wording, and what T10/T11 should encode: S5 'partly fixed by UX-00 `50992ba`; watch-count half remains in W3-03'.
- T14's Grok-keyword assertion is brittle and can collide with a hard rule. The packet forbids deleting historical ledger rows, and the LEDGER's Grok usage log plus the 'Lessons' paragraph must survive verbatim; scoping the test to AGENTS.md and PROTOCOL.md only is what keeps those two rules from fighting. Even so, a blacklist test will fire on a legitimate future sentence. Consider shipping only the positive half (the packet/report/ask/ledger contract still documented).
- Self-referential test count. Any assertion that STATUS.md's number equals the live count either needs a nested pytest subprocess (slow, recursive, fragile) or a `pytest_collection_modifyitems` hook in tests/conftest.py, which couples every run to a doc file and would surprise the next person to add a test. T13 as designed only checks LEDGER and STATUS agree with each other and that the STATUS line names its command and date; the number's freshness stays a human review duty. Accept that gap deliberately rather than paper over it.
- tests/joint/ is the Chief's and off limits, and it holds a truth claim this packet cannot reach: tests/joint/test_adversarial.py:27 has `IMPLEMENTED = {"ux-01", "ux-02a"}`. If the LEDGER starts saying W3-01/W3-02 are landed, that gate set silently disagrees and no test notices. Report it as an ask rather than editing it here.
- Doc-shape tests are coupled to markdown formatting. T7, T8, T9 and T10 all parse pipe tables by splitting on '|'; a reflow, a stray escaped pipe (the review's S9 row literally contains `\|` in its text at docs/collab/reviews/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md:19), or a switch to a bullet list breaks them with a confusing failure. Write the parser to skip escaped pipes and give each assertion a message naming the file and the row, or these become the tests everyone deletes.
- Scope creep guard: T3 and T1 pull README into lockstep with code, so a later packet that adds a subcommand or a [notify] key now fails a docs test in a code-only change. That is the intent, but it means W3-03/W3-04/W3-05/W3-07 authors must budget a README line. Flag it to the Chief before landing, otherwise the first person to hit it will assume the test is broken.


### W3-06 · tests:cross

**Corrections to other agents**

- CODEBASE analyst is wrong that "there is no row at all for UX-00". docs/collab/LEDGER.md line 33 does carry a UX-00 row: `| UX-00 | Hotfix: transient directory churn silent; directory events not writes | accepted, live | feature | | zero alerts from 5 lock-dir cycles against the running daemon |`. The real defect is narrower: the row cites no commit (50992ba) and no report. Rows that are genuinely absent are TEST-02, W6-01a, the joint harness (dd6678a) and the SkillSpector self-scan (fa50aab). D-008 is absent from the decisions table but does appear in the UX-04 row's Verdict cell. Spec analyst's R1/R7 wording is the accurate one.
- TESTS analyst (me, in the prior pass) is wrong in T5: asserting that README §Security cites `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self-baselined.json` would put a dead link in the repo. `docs/collab/.gitignore` contains `reviews/*.json`, and `git ls-files docs/collab/reviews/` returns only SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md and W3-01-grok-review.md. The two JSONs exist on disk but are untracked. The spec analyst's R13 triple is correct: SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-09-05.md, SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md, and .skillspector-baseline.yaml (tracked, confirmed by git ls-files). The codebase analyst caught this and was right.
- TESTS analyst T6 has the same bug one level up: `(ROOT/hit).exists()` certifies untracked and gitignored paths as valid citations. It must check membership in `git ls-files` output, not the filesystem, or it will pass locally on exactly the links that break in a clone. This also applies to any LEDGER row citing `docs/collab/runs/TEST-02-*.json`, which are ignored by `runs/*.json` — only the `.continue.md` and `.session` files under runs/ are tracked.
- TESTS analyst T7 is wrong that UX-02b has no LEDGER row. LEDGER.md line 36 reads `| UX-02b | Evidence v2 capture | planned, after W3-02/03/04 | | | |`. The packet files with no ledger row are only TEST-02 and W6-01a. The NO_PACKET_FILE escape set (UX-00, UX-03, UX-04, W5-03) is correct as stated.
- TESTS analyst T8's list of illegal state words is incomplete. Beyond 'done' (UX-03), 'published' (UX-04), 'scaffold' (W5-03), 'packet' (UX-02a) and 'planned' (UX-02b), W3-06's own row reads 'in progress', which the legend `States: written, running, reported, returned, accepted, escalated` also does not allow. Six rows violate the legend, not five.
- SPEC analyst R5 is wrong that the closing Grok row's cause needs new attestation. The explanation is already in the tree, verbatim, in LEDGER.md's Lessons paragraph at the end of §"Grok usage log": "The headless client (`grok 1.0.13`, `--output-format json`) treats a text-only assistant turn as the end of the prompt and reports `stopReason: cancelled`". The closing row should cite that existing sentence rather than re-derive it, which also removes the only place where 'grok 1.0.13' would otherwise be an unverifiable new claim.
- SPEC analyst R5's evidence base needs restating: the seven `docs/collab/runs/TEST-02-*.json` files are gitignored and untracked, so a ledger row cannot cite them as in-tree evidence. Their content must be transcribed into the row instead.
