### W6-01a · codebase

Change surface for W6-01a is almost entirely greenfield: `/home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/src/sentinel/scan.py`, `tests/test_scan.py`, `scripts/install-scanner.sh`, plus one function (`scan_main`) in `src/sentinel/cli.py` and one line in `pyproject.toml [project.scripts]`. Everything the packet leans on for state and privacy already exists and needs no change: `sentinel.paths.state_dir`, `sentinel.fsutil.ensure_private_dir/open_private/write_private_atomic` (0700 dirs, 0600 files, atomic rewrites), the autouse XDG-isolation fixture in `tests/conftest.py`, and the argv-array-plus-timeout subprocess convention used by `health._run`, `notify.run` and `investigate`. The suite is green at 210 (`.venv/bin/pytest -q`, Python 3.14.7 in .venv). The packet's Context claims 1, 4 and 5 check out; claim 2 is accurate but incomplete; claims about `top` construction and the systemd sandbox are wrong as written, and the ScanResult field names collide with two already-shipped consumers.

**Findings**

- NEW: `src/sentinel/scan.py` does not exist. Nothing in `src/sentinel/` currently references SkillSpector or scanning; the only in-tree mentions are `investigate._render_new_agent` (a renderer for an alert nobody emits yet) and the QML/JS panel. `grep -rn 'ScanResult|tree_hash|scan_main' src/` returns nothing.
- TOUCHED: `src/sentinel/cli.py` — currently 74 lines with `main` (only subcommand: `daemon`/`run`), `scout_main` (`--refresh`, `--home`), and `action_main` (a thin lazy-import shim to `sentinel.action.action_main`). `scan_main` follows the same shape: argparse, lazy import, return an int exit code. Note both existing mains import their implementation module inside the function body, keeping `cli` import-light.
- TOUCHED: `pyproject.toml` `[project.scripts]` currently declares `sentinel`, `sentinel-scout`, `sentinel-action` only. Adding `sentinel-scan = "sentinel.cli:scan_main"` will NOT create `.venv/bin/sentinel-scan` without a reinstall (the venv has only the three existing shims and a `src/omarchy_sentinel.egg-info/entry_points.txt` listing them). Tests must call `sentinel.cli.scan_main([...])` directly, which is exactly the existing convention in `tests/test_action.py` (`from sentinel.cli import action_main`).
- ALREADY EXISTS, reuse it: `sentinel.paths.state_dir()` (XDG_STATE_HOME-aware) and `sentinel.fsutil.ensure_private_dir` / `open_private` / `write_private_atomic` with `DIR_MODE=0o700`, `FILE_MODE=0o600`. The packet's `state_dir()/scans/<tree_hash>.json` at 0600 is `write_private_atomic` verbatim — no new file-mode code is needed, and hand-rolling `open()` would break the S3 invariant the module documents.
- ALREADY EXISTS: `tests/conftest.py` has an autouse `_isolated_xdg` fixture setting `XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` per test, so `tests/test_scan.py` gets state isolation for free. `tests/fixtures/` currently holds only `fake_home/` (`.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/hooks`, `.codex/auth.json`); the recorded-report fixture is a new subtree. `tests/bin/` (the stub scanner the integration plan calls for) does not exist.
- SUBPROCESS PRECEDENT to match: `health._run(cmd, timeout=5.0)` and `health._systemctl_is_active` wrap `subprocess.run(argv, check=False, capture_output=True, timeout=...)` inside `except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired)`; `notify` and `investigate` do the same; `health.send_notification` uses `shutil.which` to resolve a binary before calling it. `grep -rn 'shell=True' src/` is already empty tree-wide, so the acceptance grep is a regression guard rather than a new property.
- HASHING: there is no file-content hashing helper anywhere. `allowlist.fingerprint` is the only `hashlib` user in `src/sentinel/` and it hashes a fixed `rule\0basename\0flags\0cwd_prefix` string, not file bytes. `scout._entry_for` collects `path`/`kind`/`size`/`mtime` only. So `tree_hash(root)` is genuinely new, and there is no existing size-cap constant to borrow — the packet's "skipping anything over the size cap" needs a new named constant in `scan.py`.
- DOWNSTREAM CONSUMER 1: `investigate._render_new_agent` (registered in `investigate._RENDERERS` under `"R-NEW-AGENT"`) already reads `alert.evidence['scan']` with keys `tool`, `version`, `risk_score`, `verdict`, `counts`, `report`, and `top[]` items shaped `{title, file, line}`. It runs the dict through `investigate.redact_mapping`, whose `_SENSITIVE_KEY_RE`/`_SENSITIVE_SUBSTR_RE` drop keys named `body`/`file_body`/`token`/`secret`/etc. — they do NOT drop `code_snippet` or `finding`, so redaction is not a safety net for this packet; `parse_report` must never construct those keys in the first place. Pinned by `tests/test_investigate.py::test_rnew_agent_scan_shows_score_verdict_report_no_bodies`.
- DOWNSTREAM CONSUMER 2: `SentinelModel.js` `riskValue()` reads `a.evidence.scan.risk_score` as a 0..100 number and `riskVerdict()` reads `a.evidence.scan.verdict` as a display string; `Panel.qml` (the D-008 risk bar) draws bands 0-20 SAFE / 21-50 CAUTION / 51+ DO NOT INSTALL and prints the verdict string verbatim. These are shipped and asserted, so whatever `ScanResult` calls its fields, the alert-building step must emit `risk_score`/`verdict`/`report`.
- CONFIG: there is no `[scan]` support today. `daemon.load_config` reads only `sampler_interval`, `coalesce_window`, `extra_bypass_flags`, `known_basenames` from a `[daemon]` section, and `packaging/config.toml` ships only `[kill]` and `[notify]` (`tests/test_config_template.py` asserts exactly that). The integration plan's `[scan] path/timeout_sec/memory_max/osv_lookup` block exists only in the plan, so the packet has no stated way to locate the scanner binary or to point tests at a stub.
- SCRIPT PRECEDENT: `scripts/install-daemon.sh`, `install-menu.sh`, `install-track-a.sh` all default to dry-run and take `--apply` (or `--dry-run`), derive the plugin id from `manifest.json` via `jq` (`ID` -> `tav.sentinel`, confirmed in `manifest.json`), and put the venv at `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/$ID/venv`. `install-daemon.sh` symlinks exactly `sentinel sentinel-action sentinel-scout` into `~/.local/bin`, so a new `sentinel-scan` console script will not be installed by any existing script.
- VERIFIED PACKET CLAIMS: claim 1 (tag v2.9.6, git install, `--no-llm` default) matches `docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md`; claim 4 is true — `.skillspector-baseline.yaml` is at the repo root (4942 bytes); claim 5 matches AGENTS.md/PROTOCOL §9 invariant 3; `Depends on: W3-02 (merged)` matches LEDGER row W3-02 `accepted (Chief implemented) 72031f6`. Two recorded real reports are already committed and make ideal parser fixtures: `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json` (score 100, CRITICAL, DO_NOT_INSTALL, 57 issues, ~92 KB) and `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self-baselined.json` (score 0, LOW, SAFE, 0 issues, suppressed_count 57).

**Risks**

- FALSE, and it breaks the packet's own privacy rule: the packet says `top` entries are "built from `finding` plus `location`" while never carrying a file body. In the real reports, `finding` IS verbatim source text — e.g. `"finding": "        sp = getattr(eff, name)"` and a 7-line `subprocess.run(...)` block for `src/sentinel/health.py` start_line 70 end_line 76. Using `finding` as `top[].title` copies file bodies into alerts and state, violating PROTOCOL §9 invariant 2. The safe field is `pattern` (e.g. "Dynamic attribute access via getattr()"), which is also what the integration plan's example title looks like ("curl to external host in hook"). The packet must be corrected before implementation, or `parse_report` will ship the exact leak the packet forbids.
- INCOMPLETE claim 2: issues also carry `id`, `finding_id`, `pattern`, `intent`, `tags` (confirmed against `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json`). More importantly there is no top-level `version` or `tool` key at all — the version lives at `metadata.skillspector_version` ("2.9.6") and `tool` has to be a constant. `skill.name` is `"unknown"` for a directory target, so it cannot name the scanned root. `ScanResult.version` and `.tool` need sources the packet does not state.
- WRONG SANDBOX MECHANISM: `systemd-run --user --scope` cannot carry `PrivateNetwork=yes` or a read-only bind. `man systemd.scope` (systemd 261 on this machine) says scope units accept only `[Unit]`, `[Scope]` (OOMPolicy, RuntimeMaxSec), `systemd.kill` and `systemd.resource-control` options; `PrivateNetwork=` and `BindReadOnlyPaths=` are `systemd.exec` options, valid for service units only. `MemoryMax` and `RuntimeMaxSec` on a scope are fine. The integration plan `docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md` §5 gets this right — it spawns a transient *service* (`systemd-run --user --collect --unit=sentinel-scan-<hash8> -p MemoryMax=... -p RuntimeMaxSec=...`, no `--scope`). Recommend dropping `--scope`. Secondary caveat: `man systemd.exec` says `PrivateNetwork=` under a per-user manager implicitly enables `PrivateUsers=` and needs unprivileged userns (present here: `kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone = 1`, `user.max_user_namespaces = 30967`), so it should still be treated as best-effort — which the packet's `sandbox="none"` fallback already anticipates and should also cover partial application.
- FIELD-NAME COLLISION with shipped code: `ScanResult(... score, severity, recommendation, ..., report_path ...)` does not line up with `investigate._render_new_agent` and `SentinelModel.riskValue/riskVerdict`, which require `risk_score`, `verdict` and `report` inside `evidence.scan`. Nothing in W6-01a converts between them and the packet does not name a serializer, so W6-01b will either duplicate the mapping or the panel silently shows no risk bar. Worth adding an explicit `to_evidence()` (or renaming the dataclass fields) in this packet.
- VERDICT VOCABULARY is unresolved across three documents: claim 3 says use the tool's own `SAFE`/`CAUTION`/`DO_NOT_INSTALL` and that the plan's mapping is allow/review/block; the plan's alert example writes `"verdict": "DO NOT INSTALL"`; `tests/test_investigate.py` asserts the string `"review"` renders; `Panel.qml` prints the verdict verbatim next to bands labelled SAFE / CAUTION / DO NOT INSTALL. Whichever string `ScanResult` carries needs to be decided here rather than in W6-01b.
- D-006 CONTRADICTION: the packet asserts "D-006 is open; default off, so network off", but `docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md` §7 ships `osv_lookup = true` as the default and §12 recommends "on". `docs/collab/LEDGER.md` lists D-006 as open with no decision. The packet is quietly flipping a plan default; that is a Chief call to make explicitly, not an implementation detail.
- SCANNER RESOLUTION AND TEST SEAM MISSING: `sentinel-scan <root> [--json] [--timeout N]` has no `--scanner`/path flag, there is no `[scan] path` config support in `daemon.load_config` or `packaging/config.toml`, and `skillspector` is not on PATH on this machine. The required test "the runner builds an argv array and honours the timeout" and "a missing scanner exits non-zero with the instruction line" both need a documented seam (env var, config key, or explicit flag) that the packet does not specify. The plan's stub-in-`tests/bin/` approach also implies a resolution order that must be written down.
- BASELINE NOT WIRED: claim 4 establishes that baselines exist and matter, but the Changes section never passes `--baseline`/`-b` to the runner, while the plan's runner line does (`[-b baseline]`). Either the packet should say baselines land in W6-01d, or the argv builder should take an optional baseline path now so the flag does not become a breaking argv change later.
- PACKET FORMAT: W6-01a is missing the `## Fact-check`, `## Non-goals` and `## Report` sections required by `docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md` §4. Grok is required by §5 to deliver a fact-check table (a report without one is RETURNed) and the report path (`docs/collab/reports/W6-01a-report.md`) is never named. The numbered Context is checkable, but the packet as written does not tell Grok what to verify or where to write.
- INSTALL-SCRIPT CONVENTION MISMATCH plus a live-machine hazard: the packet asks for `--yes` while every existing script in `scripts/` defaults to dry-run and gates on `--apply`/`--dry-run`, and they resolve the plugin id from `manifest.json` rather than hardcoding `tav.sentinel`. Separately, `scripts/install-scanner.sh` writes under `~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/scanner`, which this session is forbidden to touch: it must be authored but never executed, and no test may invoke it. It also installs from the network, so it can never run inside the suite.


### W6-01a · integration

W6-01a is implementable but is NOT safe to hand to an implementer as written. Three blocking problems: (1) the packet is missing the mandatory `## Fact-check`, `## Non-goals` and `## Report` sections that PROTOCOL.md section 4 requires, so it is "wrong" by PROTOCOL's own tie-break rule and Grok has no report target; (2) writing the raw report to `state_dir()/scans/<tree_hash>.json` makes Sentinel alert on itself — the daemon watches `state_dir()` as a self path, inotify supplies no writer PID, so `rules.evaluate_write` raises a **R-SELF high** alert on every scan, and the daemon is running live on the Owner's machine right now (pid 18419, since Sep 5 20:03); (3) W3-07 is rewriting exactly that write-classification path and turns the same event into a **sticky critical toast that never clears**, while W6-01a never mentions W3-07's `control.sock` announcement protocol. Also: the packet's `ScanResult` field names (`score`, `recommendation`) do not match the already-shipped Panel contract (`evidence.scan.risk_score`, `evidence.scan.verdict`), and the `systemd-run --user --scope` sandbox both collides with the standing "never start a systemd unit" rule and rests on an unverified claim that `PrivateNetwork=yes` works in a user scope. W3-03 and W3-04 are low-collision by comparison. All file paths below are under /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel/.

**Findings**

- PACKET IS INCOMPLETE (blocking, protocol-level). docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md section 4 mandates Goal, Context, Changes, Tests, Acceptance, Fact-check, Non-goals, Forbidden, Report. docs/collab/packets/W6-01a-scanner-core.md has only six of the nine: no `## Fact-check`, no `## Non-goals`, no `## Report`. Every sibling packet (W3-03, W3-04, W3-07, UX-02b) has all three. Consequences are concrete, not pedantic: AGENTS.md says "A report without the fact-check table is incomplete" and PROTOCOL section 5 says such a report gets RETURNed, yet the packet never tells the implementer which claims to verify; and with no `## Report` line there is no target path (the convention is docs/collab/reports/W6-01a-report.md). PROTOCOL section 2: "If a task packet and this file disagree, this file wins and the packet is wrong." Fix the packet before launching.
- SELF-ALERT: the scan report location makes Sentinel alert on itself (blocking, highest severity). Full verified chain: `Daemon._self_paths()` returns `[config_dir(), state_dir()]` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:427-429); `_dirs_to_watch()` adds both as inotify roots (daemon.py:456-471); `_drain_inotify` auto-adds a watch for any newly created directory, so `mkdir state_dir()/scans` is watched immediately (daemon.py:719-723); `_on_fs_event` filters only on `path.name in OPERATIONAL_NAMES` (daemon.py:701-703) and `<tree_hash>.json` is not in that frozenset (daemon.py:45-59) and can never be, because OPERATIONAL_NAMES matches fixed basenames while tree hashes are dynamic; `handle_write(path)` is then called with `writer_pid`, `writer_exe` and `writer_cmdline` all defaulted to None (daemon.py:524-544); `rules.evaluate_write` finds the path under `self_paths` and calls `_is_sentinel_writer(None, None)`, which returns False because `_basename(None)` is `""` and `"".startswith("sentinel")` is False (src/sentinel/rules.py:170-189, 211-226). Result: **R-SELF, severity high, "write to sentinel path by unknown"** on every single scan.
- The sentinel-writer escape hatch is dead code in production, so there is no accidental rescue. docs/collab/LEDGER.md, "Known limits carried into wave 3": "inotify gives no writer PID; the editor allowlist is unreachable in production until W6-04." `_is_sentinel_writer` can only ever fire when a caller passes writer metadata explicitly, which the inotify path never does. Do not design around it.
- Deduplication does not suppress the storm either. `Daemon._seen_key` returns None unless `alert.rule in PROCESS_RULES`, and `PROCESS_RULES = frozenset({"R-BYPASS", "R-CHILD-SHELL"})` (daemon.py:61, 496-499) — R-SELF is excluded, so the one-alert-per-instance registry from W3-02 does not apply. The coalescer is the only remaining guard and `coalesce_key` falls back to `f"{rule}|{basename}|{loc}"` where `loc` is the alert path (daemon.py:262-270). Every distinct `tree_hash` is a distinct path, hence a distinct key, hence a fresh high alert per scanned tree.
- LIVE MACHINE: the daemon is running and cannot be fixed by merging. `ps` shows pid 18419 = `/home/tav/.local/share/tav.sentinel/venv/bin/python3 .../sentinel daemon`, started Sat Sep 5 20:03:19, i.e. it is watching `~/.local/state/sentinel` (0700, currently alerts.jsonl 133 rows, allowlist.json, health.json, watchlist.json, alerts.lock — no `scans/` yet). That daemon runs from an *installed copy* of the package, not this worktree, so a fix landed on feature/omarchy-sentinel changes nothing until the Owner reruns scripts/install-daemon.sh --apply and restarts. Practical rule for this packet: any manual smoke of `sentinel-scan` must set `XDG_STATE_HOME` to a temp dir (as W3-03's and W3-07's Acceptance blocks do, e.g. `XDG_STATE_HOME=$(mktemp -d)`), because W6-01a's Acceptance block has no XDG override and a bare `sentinel-scan <root>` writes into the live state dir under the live daemon's watch.
- AFTER W3-07 THE SAME WRITE BECOMES AN UNCLEARABLE CRITICAL TOAST. docs/collab/packets/W3-07-self-defense-tamper-evidence.md sets `evidence={"event":"foreign-write", "path":...}` for foreign writes. `notify.is_sticky` returns True for `rule == "R-SELF"` when `evidence["event"]` is in `STICKY_EVENTS = frozenset({"foreign-write", "alert-log-truncated"})` (src/sentinel/notify.py:40, 78-80), and sticky selects `STICKY = SeverityPolicy("critical", 0, True)` (notify.py:74), which omits `-t` from the toast argv (notify.py:231-232). Today's R-SELF write alert carries no `event` key so it is a normal 30 s high toast; under W3-07 each scan would leave a critical toast on the Owner's screen until clicked. This is a regression that gets worse over time, not better.
- DIRECT COLLISION WITH W3-07 ON daemon.py's WRITE PATH. W3-07 deletes the OPERATIONAL_NAMES bypass outright ("writes to any operational file are no longer ignored; they are classified with the ledger") and replaces it with `src/sentinel/statewatch.py` `StateLedger` plus an announcement protocol: `sentinel-action` sends `{"path":..., "sha256":...}` to a unix datagram socket `state_dir()/control.sock` before and after writing, with a `state_dir()/.cli-writes` nonce fallback when the daemon is down. `sentinel-scan` is a *second* out-of-daemon writer into state_dir and must speak the identical protocol — W6-01a does not mention W3-07 at all and does not list it under "Parallel-safe with" (it lists only W3-05, W3-06, UX-02b). Both packets edit `src/sentinel/daemon.py` (`OPERATIONAL_NAMES`, `handle_write`, `_on_fs_event`) and `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py`. Whichever merges second gets rewritten. Recommendation: sequence W3-07 first and make W6-01a depend on it, or explicitly scope W6-01a to write nothing under `state_dir()` in this packet.
- SANDBOX: `systemd-run` collides with the standing rules, and its key property is unverified. `/usr/bin/systemd-run` does exist here. But PROTOCOL.md section 9.6 says "Nothing in a packet run enables, starts, or edits a systemd unit", and `systemd-run --user --scope` starts a transient unit through the same user manager that AGENTS.md invariant 6 forbids driving with `systemctl`. Separately, the load-bearing claim is unproven: a `--scope` registers an externally-started process and systemd applies almost no sandboxing to scopes, and `PrivateNetwork=` in the user manager generally needs privileges the user manager lacks; `MemoryMax=` needs the memory controller delegated. If `PrivateNetwork=yes` is silently refused or ignored, the packet ships something that reports `sandbox="scope"` while providing no network isolation at all — the exact opposite of the guarantee. Required: write the runner behind an injected callable, exercise it in tests with a fake that only asserts the argv array, never invoke systemd-run in tests or in the Acceptance block, have the Owner verify the property at gate G2, and make the runner degrade to `sandbox="none"` on refusal rather than claiming a sandbox it did not get. `RuntimeMaxSec` on a scope is also not a reliable kill — the subprocess timeout the packet already specifies is the real guard.
- DO NOT LET THE SCANNER WRITE THE REPORT ITSELF. docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md records the real argv as `skillspector scan <export> --no-llm --format json -o <file>` — the scanner process writes the output. If the runner passes `-o state_dir()/scans/<hash>.json`, the sandboxed third-party scanner is handed a write handle inside Sentinel's own state directory, which is precisely what R-SELF exists to detect, and it fights the read-only bind of the scanned root. Correct shape: scanner writes to a private temp file outside state_dir; `sentinel-scan` reads it, parses it, and writes the 0600 copy itself via `fsutil.write_private_atomic` (src/sentinel/fsutil.py:44) — which gives 0600 for free and lands as a single atomic rename (one `IN_MOVED_TO`) instead of a stream of `IN_MODIFY` events.
- `tree_hash(root)` will walk `.venv/`, `.git/` and `.worktrees/` as specified. The packet says only "skipping anything over the size cap" — no directory exclusions. docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md documents this exact trap for the scanner itself: scanning the working tree "walks `.venv/` and every `.worktrees/grok-*` copy, which multiplied 57 findings into 271", which is why the self-scan targets a clean `git archive HEAD` export. A tree_hash over this repo with no exclusions hashes the entire venv, is slow, and is not stable across a `pip install` — defeating the packet's own "stable across mtime changes" test intent. It needs the same exclusion list W3-03 is introducing (`.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `.worktrees`). That makes three packets inventing an exclusion list; share one helper (in the new `src/sentinel/vendors.py` W3-03/W3-05 create, or a small `sentinel/walk.py`) rather than writing it twice.
- PANEL CONTRACT MISMATCH WITH ALREADY-SHIPPED CODE. SentinelModel.js:111-120 reads `alert.evidence.scan.risk_score` (a number it divides by 100) and `alert.evidence.scan.verdict` (a string). Panel.qml:180-195 renders the D-008 bands from those, and 448/532-533 bind `riskValue`/`riskVerdict` into the card — this is committed (d4173cd) and D-008 is DECIDED per the LEDGER. W6-01a names the same data `score`, `severity`, `recommendation` on `ScanResult` and says nothing about attaching a `scan` block to an alert. As written the risk bar never lights up. Decide now, in the packet: either `ScanResult` serializes to `evidence["scan"] = {"risk_score": ..., "verdict": ...}` matching the shipped QML, or the QML is renamed — do not create two names for one field.
- UX-02b (declared parallel-safe — mostly true, one real overlap). docs/collab/packets/UX-02b-evidence-v2-capture.md adds `evidence["schema"] = 2`, `alert.hashes[path]` for kinds hooks/settings/mcp/**self**, and a new `[investigate] hash_max_bytes = 1048576` size cap. W6-01a independently introduces a size cap inside `tree_hash`. That is two hashing helpers and two caps, and UX-02b's `self` kind would hash the scan reports under state_dir. Reuse one helper and one config key. Also, if W6-01a attaches `evidence["scan"]`, it must be covered by UX-02b's `schema = 2` contract or `investigate` prints the stale "recorded before evidence v2" limit on scan alerts.
- W3-04 (not listed as parallel-safe; interaction is real but cheap to handle). W3-04 rewrites `sample_proc` to walk every pid with ppid and then runs `evaluate_child_shell(snapshots, agent_basenames=self._known)`, with a new `net-helper` kind at medium severity. A scan launched from a terminal that is a descendant of an agent produces a `python .../skillspector` child, and `scripts/install-scanner.sh` runs `pip install git+https://github.com/NVIDIA/skillspector.git@v2.9.6`, i.e. `git`/`pip` doing network — a textbook `net-helper` false positive. Two mitigations already exist: `systemd-run --user --scope` reparents the scanner out of the agent tree (a second, better argument for the scope than the sandbox claim), and the installer is Owner-run in a plain shell. Add an explicit note so W3-04's implementer is not surprised. Note `src/sentinel/procinfo.py` already provides `read_ppid` (line 36) and `instance_key` (line 46), so W3-04 has no need to touch anything W6-01a writes. Keep W6-01a's tests confined to `tests/test_scan.py` and out of `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py`, which W3-04 and W3-07 both extend.
- W3-03 (not listed as parallel-safe; collision is low). W3-03 rewrites `src/sentinel/scout.py` `classify_kind`, adds `vendors.py`, bumps `watchlist.json` to version 2, and adds a debounced hot reload on watchlist writes. W6-01a reads no watchlist and touches no scout code, so there is no direct conflict. One edge worth stating: W3-03's `[scout] project_roots` defaults to `["~/Work"]` and this repository lives under `~/Work`, so after W3-03 more project `.claude/` dirs become watch roots — a `tree_hash` walk over them is read-only and harmless, but any scan artifact cached *inside* a scanned project would raise R-HOOK-WRITE. Keep every scan artifact in `state_dir()/scans/` and nowhere near a watched root.
- PACKAGING AND CONFIG DRIFT (four packets, one file each). Adding `sentinel-scan = "sentinel.cli:scan_main"` to `[project.scripts]` (pyproject.toml:12-15 currently lists exactly three) leaves the live install stale until the Owner reruns scripts/install-daemon.sh --apply; that script hardcodes `sentinel{,-action,-scout}` (its header, line 7) and W3-05 adds a `scripts/install-cli.sh` with the same hardcoded triple — both need `sentinel-scan`. The committed `src/omarchy_sentinel.egg-info/entry_points.txt` will also drift. Separately, if W6-01a adds a `[scan]` config block (timeout, size cap, sandbox toggle, OSV toggle) it must also land in `packaging/config.toml`, which W3-03 (`[scout] project_roots`), W3-04 (`[rules] net_helper_ignore`, `[daemon] child_shell`) and UX-02b (`[investigate]`) are all appending to as well. `packaging/config.toml` is a guaranteed merge conflict across four in-flight packets — have the Chief write that file once, centrally, rather than letting each packet append.
- OPEN OWNER DECISIONS THIS PACKET LEANS ON. D-006 (SkillSpector OSV lookups on by default) is open and the packet keys `PrivateNetwork=yes` off it; building the toggle is fine only if the default is off and enabling it requires an explicit config edit, and the report must cite D-006 rather than silently assume. D-007 (pinning and update policy) is open while the packet hardcodes `v2.9.6` — which is at least consistent with docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md and .skillspector-baseline.yaml (`scanner_version: 2.9.6`), so it is defensible, but note it. Packet Context claims 1-4 are otherwise well grounded: the self-scan review and the committed baseline confirm the git+https install, the `--no-llm` default, the SAFE/CAUTION/DO_NOT_INSTALL verdicts, and the fingerprint+glob baseline format.

**Risks**

- MUST NOT (hard rules, restated for the implementer): no `systemctl`, no `omarchy`, no `sudo`; do not execute `systemd-run` at any point during the packet run — it starts a transient systemd unit, which PROTOCOL section 9.6 forbids — write it behind an injected runner and test it with a fake that asserts the argv array only; do not run `scripts/install-scanner.sh` and make no network call from the packet run (no `pip install git+https://...` of SkillSpector); no writes under `~/.config` or `~/.local`, which includes the live `~/.local/state/sentinel` — keep tests/conftest.py:12-15's autouse XDG isolation intact and never let a manual smoke run without `XDG_STATE_HOME` set to a temp dir; do not edit `tests/joint/` (test_adversarial.py, cases/, SCHEMA.md — Chief owns it); do not import SkillSpector into `sentinel` or the daemon; never put `code_snippet` or any file body into ScanResult, alerts, logs or state; no new dependencies; no push, merge or rebase; do not edit docs/collab/LEDGER.md, docs/collab/PROTOCOL.md, or any packet file (AGENTS.md — file an ask instead).
- HIGHEST LIVE RISK: an R-SELF high alert storm on the Owner's running daemon (pid 18419) the first time `sentinel-scan` writes to `state_dir()/scans/`. With `toast_high = true` and `high_timeout = 30` in packaging/config.toml, that is a 30 s toast per scan today. The Owner has already lived through one storm — the LEDGER's live evidence log records 132 alerts in the daemon's first 81 minutes, 127 of them high, and 95 files approved one by one. Do not repeat it. Decide the scan report location before any code is written.
- SECOND-ORDER LIVE RISK: once W3-07 merges, the same write becomes a critical sticky toast (notify.py:74, 78-80) that does not auto-dismiss and must be clicked away, one per scan. This lands silently if W6-01a merges first and nobody rechecks.
- The sandbox may be a false guarantee. If `PrivateNetwork=yes` on a `--user --scope` is refused or silently ignored, the runner reports a sandbox it does not have while the packet's whole premise is that the network is off. Verify before shipping the claim; degrade to `sandbox="none"` rather than over-report. This is the one claim in the packet that, if wrong, breaks a security invariant (PROTOCOL section 9.3) rather than merely a feature.
- Merge-order hazard: W6-01a, W3-07 and W3-04 all edit `src/sentinel/daemon.py` and `tests/test_daemon_coalesce.py`; W6-01a, W3-03, W3-04 and UX-02b all append to `packaging/config.toml`; W3-06 rewrites README.md, AGENTS.md, PROTOCOL.md and LEDGER.md while W6-01a needs README and config documentation for `sentinel-scan`. Landing these in an arbitrary order guarantees conflicts and, worse, silent semantic regressions where a later merge reintroduces the write path an earlier one fixed.
- `tree_hash` over a real project with no directory exclusions is a performance and stability trap (walks `.venv/`, `.git/`, `.worktrees/`), and the resulting hash is not stable across a `pip install` — which would break the allowlist pinning the packet says tree_hash exists to support.
- Process risk: the packet is missing its Fact-check section, so the implementer has no instruction to verify the two claims most likely to be wrong (the systemd-run sandbox properties and the scanner's exact argv/-o behavior), and PROTOCOL section 5 would then RETURN the resulting report for lacking a fact-check table. This is a self-inflicted round trip and, given the metered Grok budget noted in the LEDGER usage log, an expensive one.


### W6-01a · security

Security analysis for packet W6-01a (scanner core) in /home/tav/Work/sentinel/.worktrees/omarchy-sentinel. W6-01a is the first packet that (a) opens arbitrary files under an agent-controlled root (`tree_hash`), (b) stores third-party output containing file bodies in the state dir, and (c) spawns an external process that parses hostile input. Every one of the eight invariants has a concrete, non-theoretical violation path in this design. The three sharpest are: `tree_hash` opening auth-kind files and symlinks (breaks the UX-02b invariant that an auth file is never opened for any reason); the scanner writing its own report via `-o PATH` under the user's 0022 umask (0644 file containing `code_snippet` bodies, breaking 0600-in-0700); and the runner writing into `state_dir()/scans/` which lies inside `Daemon._self_paths()`, so W3-07's tripwire will read every scan as a `foreign-write` R-SELF high — a sticky-alert storm that trains the user to ignore the one alert class designed to be unmissable. Read-only stage: I edited nothing.

**Findings**

- INVARIANT 1 — runs as the user, never root. How W6-01a breaks it: (a) `systemd-run` without `--user` targets the system manager and prompts polkit for root; (b) `scripts/install-scanner.sh` reaching for `sudo pip`/`pipx` or a system Python; (c) `pip install git+https://github.com/NVIDIA/skillspector.git@v2.9.6` executes the upstream `setup.py`/build backend as whoever runs it. What the code must do: the sandbox argv builder in src/sentinel/scan.py must emit `--user` unconditionally and a test must assert it is present in the list; `cli.scan_main` should refuse to run when `os.geteuid() == 0` with a one-line message and non-zero exit. `scripts/install-scanner.sh` must contain no `sudo` (grep it in acceptance), must create the venv under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/tav.sentinel/scanner` exactly the way scripts/install-daemon.sh derives `DATA_DIR` from manifest.json, must print the plan first and require `--yes` (the packet says so; install-daemon.sh's `--apply` dry-run default is the precedent), and must never be invoked from Python — no `subprocess` call anywhere in src/sentinel/ may name it. Branch hard rule: the implementer writes the script but never executes it, `systemctl` is never run, and nothing under ~/.config or ~/.local is touched; the sandbox path is proven by asserting the built argv, not by spawning.
- INVARIANT 2 — metadata only, never file bodies or credentials. This packet is where bodies enter the system for the first time: SkillSpector's `issues[].code_snippet` is literal source text from a hostile file. How it breaks: `parse_report` doing `ScanResult(**data)`, `dict(data)`, or a blocklist-style `data.pop('code_snippet')` on a copy — the copy still carries `explanation`, `remediation`, `suppressed`, and `metadata`, any of which can quote file content. What the code must do: allowlist extraction only, field by field — `top` entries built from `issue['finding']`, `issue['location']['file']`, `issue['location']['start_line']` and nothing else, exactly the three keys the packet names; no catch-all `extra`/`raw` field on the frozen dataclass; a test that asserts the string `code_snippet` does not appear in `repr(result)` nor in `json.dumps(asdict(result))` for a fixture whose snippets contain a unique sentinel string. `finding` is attacker-influenceable text (it can echo the matched pattern), so strip newlines and cap it before it reaches a summary or a toast body — src/sentinel/notify.py:36 already sets the precedent with `SUMMARY_MAX_CHARS = 80`. `ScanResult.error` must be a fixed token (`not-installed` | `timeout` | `bad-json` | `exit-2` | `no-sandbox`), never a stderr tail: plan section 8 says the stderr tail lives in the on-disk report and never in the alert. `--no-llm` must be a hardcoded constant in the argv builder in this packet — the LLM stage sends file contents to a provider and is W6-01c's per-click path only. Finally, the raw report on disk is now the one place in state_dir() holding bodies: it must never be read back into an alert, into the W3-07 journal mirror, or into `build_redacted_bundle` (src/sentinel/summarize.py:56, `ALLOWED_KEYS` at :16) — `report_path` may travel as a path string, its content never.
- INVARIANT 3 — auth-kind files are never opened. This is the invariant W6-01a is most likely to break silently, because `tree_hash(root)` computes 'content hashes' and therefore opens every file under a root that an agent controls. `classify_kind` (src/sentinel/scout.py:27-38) returns 'auth' for any name containing 'auth' or 'credentials', and UX-02b line 30 states flatly: an `auth` kind file is never opened for any reason, with `evidence['hash_skipped']` set to `"auth"`, `"too-large"` or `"gone"` instead. A skill or plugin root can perfectly well contain `credentials.json`, and `~/.claude/plugins/...` sits next to real auth files. What the code must do: `tree_hash` calls `sentinel.scout.classify_kind(p)` per file and, for `kind == 'auth'`, folds only the relative path plus a literal marker (e.g. the bytes `auth-skipped`) into the digest so presence still changes the hash without a read; it applies the size cap via `os.lstat()` BEFORE any open; and — the part a name-based classifier cannot cover — it uses `os.walk(root, followlinks=False)`, skips anything that is not `stat.S_ISREG` on `lstat`, and skips symlinks outright. Without that, a file named `notes.txt` symlinked to `~/.claude/.credentials.json` gets opened and hashed, and the classifier never sees an auth-looking name. `tree_hash` must also refuse to leave the root (containment check in the shape of `_path_under`, src/sentinel/rules.py:148-159). Second, restate the invariant at the process boundary: the scanner subprocess reads everything including auth files and puts matched bytes in `code_snippet`, so `scan_main` must refuse a root whose own `classify_kind` is `auth`, must refuse a root that is not a directory, and the sandbox must expose only that root read-only with the report directory as the sole `ReadWritePaths=` (plan section 3 rule 4). `ProtectHome=read-only` alone is not containment when the scanned root is itself under $HOME.
- INVARIANT 4 — state files 0600 inside 0700 dirs. Direct break: passing `-o state_dir()/scans/<hash>.json` lets the SCANNER create the file under the user's 0022 umask, producing a 0644 file full of `code_snippet` bodies — the exact regression the security review's S3 fixed and src/sentinel/fsutil.py was written to prevent. What the code must do: capture the report on stdout (`--format json`, no `-o`) and write it with `sentinel.fsutil.write_private_atomic` (fsutil.py:44), or pre-create the target with `open_private` (fsutil.py:31) and re-verify the mode with `os.stat` after the child exits. `state_dir()/scans` and `state_dir()/scans/queue` must be created with `ensure_private_dir` (fsutil.py:19), not `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700)` — the mode argument is masked by umask, which is precisely why `ensure_private_dir` re-chmods at fsutil.py:22-26. Additionally pass `-p UMask=0077` to `systemd-run` so anything the child writes on its own is private. Validate the tree hash matches `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` before joining it into the report filename, or a caller-supplied hash traverses out of the scans dir. Note that `write_private_atomic` uses a fixed `path.name + '.tmp'` sibling (fsutil.py:47), which is safe only because report names are per-tree-hash and the runner is one-at-a-time (plan section 5); keep it that way.
- INVARIANT 5 — argv arrays only, never shell strings. Beyond the packet's own `grep -rn "shell=True" src/sentinel/scan.py`, the real exposure here is argument injection rather than shell injection, plus systemd property injection. What the code must do: build `[scanner, 'scan', '--no-llm', '--format', 'json', '--', str(resolved_root)]` with an explicit `--` before the positional, so a root beginning with `-` cannot become a flag; resolve the root with `Path.resolve()` and reject non-directories before building anything; same treatment for `-b <baseline>`. Every systemd property is its own pair of list elements (`['-p', f'MemoryMax={mem}']`) and — critically — `memory_max`, `timeout_sec` and the unit name come from `~/.config/sentinel/config.toml`, which is writable by the very agent being watched: validate `memory_max` against `^\\d+[KMG]?$` and `timeout_sec` as an int in a bounded range before interpolation, because a `-p` value is a systemd unit directive and an unvalidated one buys the attacker `Environment=`, `ExecStartPost=` or `PrivateNetwork=no`. Build `--unit=sentinel-scan-<hash8>` from hex only. Never `/bin/sh -c`, never `os.system`, never `shlex.join` into a string — extend the acceptance grep to `grep -rn 'shell=True\|os.system\|/bin/sh' src/sentinel/scan.py`. Environment scrubbing belongs here too: the child would otherwise inherit `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `XAI_API_KEY` and friends from the user's session into hostile-input-parsing code, which is finding S4's class moved to the process boundary. Pass an explicit minimal `env=` (PATH, HOME, LANG, the XDG vars actually needed) and drop everything matching `*_API_KEY` / `*_TOKEN` / `ANTHROPIC_*` / `AWS_*`, and assert that in a test. The existing argv discipline to match is src/sentinel/health.py:70-90 and src/sentinel/notify.py:189 — argv array, explicit timeout, no shell.
- INVARIANT 6 — no network from the daemon. How W6-01a breaks it: any `from sentinel import scan` or `import skillspector` reaching daemon.py; or `scan.py` importing `urllib`/`http`/`socket` at module level, which puts a network-capable module one import away from the daemon. What the code must do: `sentinel-scan` is its own console script — add `sentinel-scan = "sentinel.cli:scan_main"` to `[project.scripts]` in pyproject.toml:12-15 — and the daemon reaches it only through `subprocess.Popen` with an argv array, exactly as summarize.py stays out of the daemon's import graph today. `scan.py` must import nothing network-capable; add a test that asserts `sys.modules` gains no `urllib.request` after importing `sentinel.daemon`. On the sandbox itself: D-006 is OPEN, so `PrivateNetwork=yes` is the default and only an explicit `[scan] osv_lookup = true` may remove it — never the reverse. The packet's degraded path is the hole: when `systemd-run` is unavailable the runner records `sandbox="none"`, and in that state there is no `PrivateNetwork` at all, so a hostile root is scanned by an unconfined, unlimited, network-capable process. `sandbox="none"` must therefore be surfaced in `ScanResult` (it is, per the packet) AND be a decision point rather than a silent degrade — my recommendation to the Chief is that an unsandboxed scan is allowed only for an explicit manual `sentinel-scan <root>` invocation and never for a daemon-queued one. Also do not assert isolation you have not proven: `PrivateNetwork=` on a `--user` transient scope depends on unprivileged user namespaces and can fail to apply; a test can only assert the property is in the argv, so the Owner-run smoke must confirm it actually took before any doc claims the scan is network-isolated.
- INVARIANT 7 — no silent auto-kill, and more broadly no silent automatic action. W6-01a produces a `DO_NOT_INSTALL` verdict, and the tempting next step is to act on it. What the code must do: nothing. No quarantine, no rename, no delete, no chmod of the scanned root anywhere in scan.py or scan_main — quarantine is W6-01c and is confirm-gated and reversible by design (plan section 5). The only writes this packet performs are under `state_dir()/scans/`. The timeout does kill a process, but it must kill only the child the runner spawned: `subprocess.run(argv, timeout=...)` plus `proc.kill()` on that Popen object, never a name-based `pkill`, never `os.killpg` on a group that could contain the caller, and nothing in scan.py may import `sentinel.kill` — the project's kill discipline (uid check at src/sentinel/kill.py:131, `confirm_kill` at kill.py:71, TERM-then-KILL in `execute_kill` at kill.py:168) exists for monitored agents and must not be reused here. Use `--collect` and a unique `--unit=` per scan so a stale unit is never adopted or stopped.
- INVARIANT 8 — sticky self-tamper alerts bypass pause and prefs. The mechanism to protect: `STICKY_EVENTS = frozenset({'foreign-write', 'alert-log-truncated'})` at src/sentinel/notify.py:40, `is_sticky()` keyed on `rule == 'R-SELF'` at notify.py:78-80, `STICKY = SeverityPolicy('critical', 0, True)` at notify.py:75, and — the load-bearing detail — the ordering inside `Notifier` where the sticky check at notify.py:421 runs BEFORE the pause check at notify.py:424 and before the burst policy. Four ways W6-01a can break it. (a) Do not touch notify.py's ordering or `STICKY_EVENTS` in this packet at all; a marketplace refresh queuing dozens of scans will make noise, and the correct levers are `toast_low` and the one-at-a-time queue, never a policy reorder or an early return placed above line 421. (b) Scan outcomes must be `R-NEW-AGENT` (plan section 6), never `R-SELF` — not for 'scanner missing', not for 'report malformed'. Giving a failure mode an R-SELF rule with a sticky event name hands an attacker who can reliably make scans fail an unlimited supply of critical, never-expiring toasts, until the user turns notifications off. That is a denial-of-attention attack on the exact alert class this invariant protects. (c) The runner must never call `write_pause` / `is_paused` (src/sentinel/action.py:70, :96) or read or write `notify-prefs.json`. (d) The state-dir collision described in the risks below is the same denial-of-attention outcome arriving by accident.
- Cross-packet mechanics worth stating in the packet before Grok starts: the scans directory sits inside `Daemon._self_paths()` (src/sentinel/daemon.py:426-429 returns `[config_dir(), state_dir()]`), and `evaluate_write` raises R-SELF high for any write there by a non-Sentinel writer (src/sentinel/rules.py:191-215). W3-07 replaces the `OPERATIONAL_NAMES` ignore-list (daemon.py:45-60, checked at :532 and :701) with a `StateLedger` classification, so the runner's report writes will classify as `foreign` unless the runner announces them. Fix by announcement over `state_dir()/control.sock` the way `sentinel-action` will, or by having the daemon record the expected write when it spawns the runner — do NOT fix it by adding `scans/*` to `OPERATIONAL_NAMES`, because that list is precisely the blind spot finding S1 was written about.

**Risks**

- Highest-risk interaction: R-SELF storm from Sentinel's own scan writes. `state_dir()/scans/<tree_hash>.json` is inside `_self_paths()`; once W3-07's ledger lands, every scan looks like a `foreign-write` and every one is a sticky, critical, never-expiring toast that bypasses pause. The user learns to dismiss the sticky class. Resolve before implementation: the runner announces its writes (W3-07 `control.sock`) or the daemon pre-registers them. Never by suppression.
- Spec conflict on tree hash identity, with a real allowlist bypass. The packet (line 39-40) defines `tree_hash` over sorted relative paths plus CONTENT hashes, stable across mtime changes. Plan section 4 defines root identity as sha256 over `(relative path, size, mtime_ns)` plus the marker hash. If the plan's version wins, an agent can rewrite a file's contents while preserving size and restoring mtime (`touch -r`), and an approved root stays approved with different code inside. The packet's content-hash version is the secure one and must be the one implemented; the plan needs correcting.
- TOCTOU between hashing and scanning. The root is agent-writable and can change between `tree_hash` and the scan, so the allowlist would pin a tree that was never the tree scanned. Mitigation: hash, scan, re-hash, and record a `stable` flag (or set `ok=False` with error `tree-changed`) when they differ; W6-01c must refuse to approve an unstable result.
- Config as an injection surface. `[scan] memory_max`, `timeout_sec`, `path` and `roots_extra` in `~/.config/sentinel/config.toml` are writable by the monitored agent and flow into `systemd-run -p` values and the scanner binary path. Unvalidated, `-p` gives an attacker arbitrary unit directives (`Environment=`, `ExecStartPost=`, `PrivateNetwork=no`); an unvalidated `[scan] path` makes Sentinel execute an attacker-chosen binary on a timer. Validate against strict patterns, and require the scanner path to be an existing regular file owned by the user and not group/world-writable — the same check finding S11 asks for on `sentinel-action` in BarWidget.qml.
- Environment inheritance leaks provider keys into hostile-input-parsing code. Without an explicit scrubbed `env=`, the SkillSpector child inherits `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `XAI_API_KEY` from the user's session even in `--no-llm` mode. This is finding S4's class at a new boundary and is cheap to close now.
- `sandbox="none"` is a silent security downgrade, not just a display string. With no `systemd-run` there is no `PrivateNetwork`, no `MemoryMax`, no `RuntimeMaxSec` beyond the Python-side timeout, and no read-only bind. Recommend the Chief decide: unsandboxed scans allowed for explicit manual invocation only, refused for daemon-queued roots. Related: `PrivateNetwork=` on a `--user` scope can fail to apply depending on unprivileged user namespaces, so no document may claim network isolation until the Owner-run smoke confirms the property took.
- Committed fixtures are a self-scan and safety hazard. Plan section 10's fixture roots deliberately contain `curl | bash` and prompt-injection strings; they must be inert text with no executable bit, the suite must never execute them, and they will trip this repository's own SkillSpector gate — they need `.skillspector-baseline.yaml` entries with the reasoning recorded, the way the tests/** row is handled in docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md. The recorded real report committed under `tests/fixtures/` must also be scrubbed of absolute home paths and of any snippet captured from the Owner's machine.
- Scanner supply chain. `pip install git+https://github.com/NVIDIA/skillspector.git@v2.9.6` runs upstream build code as the user and a tag is mutable. D-007 should pin a commit SHA, or the install script should verify the resolved SHA after clone; either way `scripts/install-scanner.sh` prints exactly what it will fetch before `--yes`.
- Denial-of-attention via forced scan failures. Any design that maps scanner failure to a sticky alert class lets an attacker who can crash or time out the scanner generate unlimited critical toasts. Keep failures at `R-NEW-AGENT` medium with `verdict: UNSCANNED` per plan section 8, and rate-limit repeated failures for the same tree hash.


### W6-01a · spec

Read W6-01a in full plus everything it cites (PROTOCOL.md, AGENTS.md, SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md, plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md, the recorded report at docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json, .skillspector-baseline.yaml, LEDGER.md, and the existing src/sentinel modules). The packet demands 39 distinct testable behaviours across five deliverables: sentinel.scan (frozen ScanResult, parse_report, severity_for, tree_hash), the sentinel-scan entry point in sentinel.cli, a systemd-run sandbox around the scanner, scripts/install-scanner.sh, and tests/test_scan.py with a committed fixture. Baseline in this worktree is 210 collected tests (.venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --collect-only). Four items are blocking and need a packet revision before Grok starts: (1) systemd-run --user --scope cannot accept PrivateNetwork= or a read-only bind (systemd.scope(5) on systemd 261 allows only [Unit]/[Scope]/kill/resource-control options) and the integration plan uses a transient service instead; (2) ScanResult has no sandbox field yet the runner is told to record sandbox="none"; (3) building top[].title from the issue's finding ships a raw source line into the alert (real report: "finding": "        sp = getattr(eff, name)"), which the packet's own no-file-bodies rule and PROTOCOL section 9 invariant 2 forbid; (4) parse_report(data) cannot populate root, tree_hash or report_path from the report JSON. The packet also contradicts the integration plan's tree-hash definition, which W6-01b depends on, and omits the PROTOCOL section 4 mandatory Fact-check, Non-goals and Report sections. I edited nothing.

**Findings**

- R1 MODULE: `src/sentinel/scan.py` exists, imports only the standard library plus existing `sentinel.*` helpers, and never imports SkillSpector (packet Changes bullet 1; Forbidden line 56).
- R2 DATACLASS: `sentinel.scan.ScanResult` is declared `@dataclass(frozen=True)` and has exactly the twelve fields `tool, version, root, tree_hash, score, severity, recommendation, counts, top, report_path, ok, error` (packet line 24-25); `dataclasses.fields(ScanResult)` names them and mutating an instance raises `FrozenInstanceError`.
- R3 PARSER SURFACE: `parse_report(data)` reads only the top-level keys `skill`, `risk_assessment` (`score`, `severity`, `recommendation`), `components`, `issues`, `suppressed_count`, `suppressed`, `metadata`, `execution_successful`, `analysis_completeness` and, per issue, `category`, `severity`, `confidence`, `location.{file,start_line,end_line}`, `finding`, `explanation`, `remediation`, `code_snippet` (packet Context claim 2, verified against `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json`).
- R4 EXTRA KEYS TOLERATED: `parse_report` accepts real reports that also carry `id`, `finding_id`, `pattern`, `intent`, `tags` per issue and `metadata.skillspector_version` (present in `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json`), i.e. unknown keys are ignored rather than treated as malformed.
- R5 NO BODIES: no field of the returned `ScanResult` (recursively, after `dataclasses.asdict`) contains any issue's `code_snippet` value, and the serialized result contains no `code_snippet` key — assert by scanning the JSON dump of a result parsed from a fixture whose issues all carry `code_snippet` (packet lines 26-27, 42-47; PROTOCOL §9 invariant 2).
- R6 TOP LIST: `ScanResult.top` is a list of at most three mappings, each with exactly the keys `title`, `file`, `line`, built from each issue's `finding` plus `location` (`location.file` → `file`, `location.start_line` → `line`) (packet line 27).
- R7 TOP ORDERING: the three entries chosen are deterministic for a given report (the packet does not state the ordering rule; the only defensible one is by issue severity then input order — see risks).
- R8 MALFORMED INPUT: a malformed, truncated, non-dict, or empty report yields `ScanResult(ok=False, error=<non-empty str>)` and `parse_report` raises no exception — tested for at least truncated JSON text, `{}`, `None`, and a report whose `risk_assessment` is missing or not a mapping (packet line 44).
- R9 OK FLAG: a well-formed report yields `ok=True` and `error` empty/None, and `ScanResult.recommendation` equals `data["risk_assessment"]["recommendation"]` verbatim (packet Context claim 3: use the tool's verdict, never derive one from the score).
- R10 MAPPING: `severity_for("SAFE") == "low"`, `severity_for("CAUTION") == "medium"`, `severity_for("DO_NOT_INSTALL") == "high"` (packet line 28); an unknown or missing recommendation must have a defined behaviour (packet is silent — see risks).
- R11 VERSION AND TOOL: `ScanResult.tool` is the constant `"skillspector"` and `ScanResult.version` comes from `metadata.skillspector_version` (value `"2.9.6"` in the recorded report) — the packet names the fields but not their source.
- R12 COUNTS: `ScanResult.counts` is a per-severity issue count derived from `issues[*].severity`; the plan (`docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md` §6) fixes the shape as `{"critical": n, "high": n, "medium": n, "low": n}` — the packet defines neither keys nor case (see risks).
- R13 ENTRY POINT FUNCTION: `sentinel.cli.scan_main(argv: list[str] | None) -> int` exists alongside `main`, `scout_main`, `action_main` in `src/sentinel/cli.py`.
- R14 CONSOLE SCRIPT: `pyproject.toml` gains `sentinel-scan = "sentinel.cli:scan_main"` under `[project.scripts]` (currently only `sentinel`, `sentinel-scout`, `sentinel-action` are listed) so the name `sentinel-scan` in packet line 29 actually exists.
- R15 CLI GRAMMAR: `sentinel-scan <root> [--json] [--timeout N]` parses — `root` positional and required, `--json` a boolean flag, `--timeout` numeric (packet line 29).
- R16 ARGV ARRAY: the runner builds the scanner invocation as a Python list and passes it to `subprocess` with no `shell=True` and no shell string, matching the house rule already asserted for `health.py`/`notify.py` (packet line 46; acceptance grep).
- R17 TIMEOUT HONOURED: the runner passes the `--timeout` value to `subprocess` as `timeout=` and a `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` is caught and turned into an `ok=False` result rather than a traceback (packet lines 29-30, 47).
- R18 STATIC MODE: the scanner argv includes `--no-llm` and `--format json` and writes/reads JSON, so no API key and no LLM stage is ever used (Context claim 1; plan §3 rule 2).
- R19 REPORT ON DISK: the raw report is written to `state_dir()/scans/<tree_hash>.json` with file mode 0600 inside a 0700 directory — use `sentinel.fsutil.write_private_atomic` / `ensure_private_dir` (`DIR_MODE=0o700`, `FILE_MODE=0o600`); assert `stat.S_IMODE(...) == 0o600` under a temp `XDG_STATE_HOME` (packet lines 30-31; `src/sentinel/paths.py:state_dir`).
- R20 REPORT PATH FIELD: `ScanResult.report_path` equals the path the raw report was written to.
- R21 PRINTS RESULT: `scan_main` prints the parsed `ScanResult` on success — human-readable by default and machine-readable JSON under `--json` (packet line 31; exact formats unspecified, see risks).
- R22 SCANNER MISSING: when the scanner executable cannot be found, `scan_main` returns/exits non-zero, prints exactly one line telling the user how to install it (pointing at `scripts/install-scanner.sh`), and prints no Python traceback (packet lines 31-32, 47).
- R23 SANDBOX PRESENT: when `systemd-run` is on PATH, the built argv starts with `systemd-run --user --scope` and carries `PrivateNetwork=yes` (D-006 open, default off so network off), a `MemoryMax` property, a `RuntimeMaxSec` property, and a read-only bind of the scanned root, before the scanner argv (packet lines 33-36) — note this combination is not actually supported by scope units, see risks.
- R24 SANDBOX ABSENT: when `systemd-run` is not available the runner still runs the scanner directly and the result records `sandbox="none"` so the panel can say so (packet lines 35-36) — `ScanResult` as specified has no `sandbox` field, see risks.
- R25 NO NETWORK FROM SENTINEL: neither `sentinel.scan` nor any daemon/CLI code path opens a socket or imports `urllib.request`; the only process that may touch the network is the spawned scanner, and only if OSV lookups are enabled (Context claim 5; Forbidden line 56-57).
- R26 INSTALL SCRIPT: `scripts/install-scanner.sh` exists, is executable, and creates `~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/scanner` as its own venv (id `tav.sentinel` from `manifest.json`, matching `scripts/install-daemon.sh`'s `DATA_DIR` convention) (packet lines 37-38).
- R27 PINNED TAG: the install script installs `git+https://github.com/NVIDIA/skillspector.git@v2.9.6` — the exact pinned tag from `docs/collab/reviews/SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md` — and never `pip install skillspector` (not on PyPI, Context claim 1).
- R28 DRY RUN DEFAULT: run without `--yes` the script prints every step it would take and exits without creating the venv or installing anything; only `--yes` performs the work (packet line 38); it uses no `sudo`, touches nothing under `~/.config`, and enables no systemd unit (packet Forbidden; AGENTS.md rule 6).
- R29 NEVER AUTOMATIC: no code path under `src/` or in the daemon invokes `install-scanner.sh` (packet line 38; Forbidden 'installing anything automatically').
- R30 TREE HASH DEFINITION: `tree_hash(root)` returns a stable sha256 hex digest computed over the sorted relative paths and the content hash of each file, skipping any file over the size cap (packet lines 39-40).
- R31 TREE HASH MTIME-STABLE: `tree_hash` is unchanged after `os.utime` on files in the tree (touch-only change) (packet line 46).
- R32 TREE HASH CONTENT-SENSITIVE: `tree_hash` differs when a single byte of any in-scope file changes (packet line 46), and also when a file is added, removed, or renamed.
- R33 SIZE CAP: files larger than a named module-level cap constant are skipped from content hashing; the cap value, and whether the skipped file's path still contributes to the digest, are unspecified in the packet (see risks).
- R34 TEST FILE: `tests/test_scan.py` is added and covers, at minimum: fixture report → `ScanResult`; malformed/truncated → `ok=False`; no `code_snippet` in the result; the SAFE/CAUTION/DO_NOT_INSTALL mapping; `tree_hash` mtime-stability and byte-sensitivity; runner argv is a list and the timeout is honoured; missing scanner exits non-zero with the instruction line (packet lines 42-47).
- R35 FIXTURE COMMITTED: a recorded SkillSpector report is committed under `tests/fixtures/` (e.g. derived from `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json`, which is a real v2.9.6 `--no-llm` report) and the parser test reads it rather than running the tool (packet line 43).
- R36 TESTS ARE HERMETIC: no test in `tests/test_scan.py` executes the real `skillspector` binary, `systemd-run`, or the network; the runner is exercised through an argv-capturing double, and the autouse `_isolated_xdg` fixture in `tests/conftest.py` keeps writes off the live `~/.local/state/sentinel` (AGENTS.md engineering standard).
- R37 SUITE STAYS GREEN: the full suite is green after the change; the current baseline in this worktree is 210 collected tests (`.venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --collect-only` → `210 tests collected`), so the new file only adds.
- R38 FORBIDDEN, VERIFIABLE: the diff imports SkillSpector nowhere in the daemon or CLI, makes no network call from `sentinel`, stores no file bodies or code snippets in alerts or state, installs nothing automatically, uses no `sudo`, and touches no file under `tests/joint/` (packet lines 55-58; PROTOCOL §9).
- R39 PROTOCOL DELIVERABLE: Grok must still deliver `docs/collab/reports/W6-01a-report.md` with a Fact-check table covering Context claims 1 to 5, per PROTOCOL §5 (a report without the table is RETURNed), even though this packet omits the `## Report` and `## Fact-check` sections.
- ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 1 (verbatim): `.venv/bin/pytest -q`
- ACCEPTANCE COMMAND 2 (verbatim): `grep -rn "shell=True" src/sentinel/scan.py    # no output`

**Risks**

- CONTRADICTION (blocking, sandbox): `systemd-run --user --scope` cannot carry `PrivateNetwork=yes` or a read-only bind. `man 5 systemd.scope` on this machine (systemd 261) states scope units accept only `[Unit]`, `[Scope]` (`OOMPolicy`, `RuntimeMaxSec`, `RuntimeRandomizedExtraSec`), `systemd.kill(5)` and `systemd.resource-control(5)` options; `PrivateNetwork=` and `BindReadOnlyPaths=` are `systemd.exec(5)` options that exist only for units that fork their own process. `MemoryMax` and `RuntimeMaxSec` are fine. `docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md` §5 uses `systemd-run --user --collect --unit=sentinel-scan-<hash8> -p ...` — a transient *service*, which does support all four. Packet line 33 should say transient service (add `--pipe`/`--wait` to capture stdout and the exit code), or drop PrivateNetwork and the bind. Needs a packet revision before Grok implements.
- CONTRADICTION (internal): packet line 36 requires the runner to record `sandbox="none"` in the result, but the `ScanResult` field list on lines 24-25 has no `sandbox` field. Either add a 13th field `sandbox` (values e.g. `"systemd-run"` / `"none"`) or state where it lives. As written the two sentences cannot both be satisfied.
- CONTRADICTION (privacy, sharp): `top` titles are to be built from each issue's `finding` (line 27), but in real reports `finding` IS a raw source line — e.g. `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json` issue 0 has `"finding": "        sp = getattr(eff, name)"`. Putting that in `top.title` ships a file body fragment into the alert, which the same packet forbids ('never carries `code_snippet` or any file body') and PROTOCOL §9 invariant 2 forbids. Use `pattern` ("Dynamic attribute access via getattr()") or `category` as the title, or state explicitly that `finding` is exempt.
- AMBIGUITY (signature): `parse_report(data) -> ScanResult` cannot populate `root`, `tree_hash` or `report_path` — none are in the report JSON (`skill.source` is the scanned path, and in the recorded report it points at a scratch export dir, not the root Sentinel scanned). The signature needs optional keyword arguments (`root=`, `tree_hash=`, `report_path=`) or defaults, otherwise the dataclass and the parser are incompatible.
- AMBIGUITY (`severity` field): `ScanResult.severity` could be the tool's `risk_assessment.severity` (`CRITICAL`/`LOW`/...) or the Sentinel severity produced by `severity_for()` (`low`/`medium`/`high`). Both are supported by the text; the plan's alert schema (§6) uses low/medium/high at the alert level. Decide, and name the other one explicitly if both are needed.
- AMBIGUITY (`counts`): shape, keys and case are undefined; the plan says `{"critical": 1, "high": 2, "medium": 3, "low": 5}`. Also unstated: whether `suppressed` issues are counted, and whether the count comes from `issues` or from `analysis_completeness.findings_after_filtering` (57 in the recorded report).
- AMBIGUITY (mapping edge case): `severity_for()` behaviour for an unknown, lower-case, missing, or `None` recommendation is unspecified. The plan says scanner absent or exit 2 → medium with `verdict: "UNSCANNED"`; the packet says nothing, so an `ok=False` result has no defined severity.
- AMBIGUITY (`top` ordering): 'at most three' does not say which three. Severity-then-order is the only sensible rule; without stating it the test will encode whatever the implementation does.
- AMBIGUITY (CLI output and exit codes): `--json` output shape is unspecified (JSON of the whole `ScanResult`?), and the only defined non-zero exit is 'scanner not installed'. SkillSpector itself exits 1 for score > 50 and 2 on error (plan §2) — the packet does not say whether `sentinel-scan` propagates that, so a DO_NOT_INSTALL verdict currently exits 0.
- AMBIGUITY (`--timeout` default): unspecified. The plan's `[scan] timeout_sec = 120` is the obvious default; state it, since the same number must also feed `RuntimeMaxSec`.
- AMBIGUITY (scanner discovery): the packet never says how the runner finds the scanner — `PATH`, `~/.local/share/tav.sentinel/scanner/bin/skillspector`, or config `[scan] path` (plan §7). Since `packaging/config.toml` has no `[scan]` section today and `tests/test_config_template.py` asserts on that file, decide whether this packet adds the section or defers it to W6-01b/c.
- AMBIGUITY (baseline): Context claim 4 documents baselines and this repo ships `.skillspector-baseline.yaml`, but no requirement in Changes uses it — the CLI has no `--baseline`/`-b` flag, though the plan's runner line does (`[-b baseline]`). Say whether baselines are in scope here or in W6-01d. (Trap for later: the baseline is YAML and there is no stdlib YAML parser — reading it would need a dependency, which is banned.)
- CONTRADICTION (cross-document, tree hash): the packet defines `tree_hash` over relative paths plus *content* hashes and demands mtime-stability; `docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md` §4 defines root identity as sha256 over `(relative path, size, mtime_ns)` plus the marker hash, 'metadata only'. Those produce different digests, and the plan's version fails the packet's own mtime test. W6-01b builds the watchlist roots on the plan's definition, so unless the plan is corrected the daemon's key and the scanner's key will not match and the allowlist pin will never hit. W6-01a should be declared the owner of `tree_hash`.
- GAP (size cap): no value is given, and the semantics of 'skipping' are undefined — if an over-cap file is omitted entirely, its content can be swapped without changing the hash (an obvious evasion for a payload). Recommend: cap the *content hash* only, but always fold the path and size into the digest, and state the constant (e.g. 1 MiB) in the module.
- GAP (traversal rules): symlinks (follow? record as link?), `.git/`, hidden files, unreadable files, and non-regular files are all unspecified for `tree_hash`; SkillSpector itself excludes hidden files (`analysis_completeness.scope_exclusions` shows `reason_code: hidden_file`), so Sentinel's hash and the scanner's scope will disagree unless stated.
- PROCESS (packet is non-conforming): PROTOCOL §4 requires `## Fact-check`, `## Non-goals` and `## Report` sections; W6-01a has none of them. PROTOCOL §2 says the protocol file wins and the packet is wrong. Grok's report is still required to carry a fact-check table for Context claims 1-5, and the report path (`docs/collab/reports/W6-01a-report.md`) should be stated in the packet.
- PROCESS (acceptance command 2): `grep -rn "shell=True" src/sentinel/scan.py` exits 1 when it matches nothing, so a `set -e` harness will read the passing case as a failure. Read the expectation as 'no matching lines', or write it as `! grep -rn ...`.
- PROCESS (entry point plumbing): the `sentinel-scan` console script does not exist until `pyproject.toml` is edited and the venv re-installed (`pip install -e .`); the packet asks for the entry point but not the pyproject change. Tests should call `sentinel.cli.scan_main` directly so they do not depend on a re-install.
- SCOPE (packet vs plan): plan §11 assigns W6-01a a queue runner, a stub scanner in `tests/bin/` and fixture roots under `tests/fixtures/roots/`; the packet body requires none of these (no `--from-queue`, no stub). `tests/bin/` does not exist. Confirm the narrower scope is intended, otherwise W6-01b will have no runner to spawn.
- CROSS-PACKET RISK: `sentinel-scan` writes into `state_dir()/scans/` from a separate process. W3-07 (self-defense tamper evidence, still 'written' in the LEDGER) is meant to alert on foreign writes to the state dir — the scan runner will look foreign unless it is whitelisted by W3-07. Flag now so the two packets do not fight.
- OPEN DECISION D-006: the packet hard-codes network off (`PrivateNetwork=yes`) while D-006 is undecided (LEDGER 'Open decisions', line 45). That is a defensible default, but it silently disables OSV CVE lookups, so scan results will differ from the Owner's expectation if D-006 later lands as 'on'; the result should record which mode ran.
- MINOR (Context claim 2 incompleteness): the claim's issue-key list is correct but partial — real issues also carry `id`, `finding_id`, `pattern`, `intent`, `tags`, and `metadata` carries `skillspector_version`, `llm_requested`, `filtering_mode` (verified in `docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json`). Grok should mark claim 2 CONFIRMED-with-additions, and the parser must tolerate the extra keys rather than treat them as malformed.
- MINOR (parallel-safety line): the packet says 'Parallel-safe with: W3-05, W3-06, UX-02b'; the plan says W6-01a is parallel-safe with W6-01b. Since both packets define root identity, they are only parallel-safe once `tree_hash` ownership is settled (see the tree-hash contradiction above).


### W6-01a · tests

Test design for W6-01a, read-only stage: 58 tests in one new file (tests/test_scan.py) plus six fixtures under tests/fixtures/skillspector/, four of them trimmed from the two SkillSpector reports already recorded in this repo. No existing test breaks (baseline verified: 210 passed in 1.92s), but one invariant the packet calls central - the daemon never importing the scanner - is currently unguarded by any test. Three conflicts must be resolved before implementation, the sharpest being that the packet's instruction to build `top` titles from the `finding` field would inject verbatim source code into alerts, contradicting the packet's own no-file-bodies rule; verified against real output.

**Findings**

Do not copy them whole: 106 KB each, and their code_snippet fields carry verbatim src/sentinel source.

Keep 6 issues: 1 CRITICAL, 2 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 1 LOW. Keep the real per-issue key set, which is larger than packet claim 2: ['category','code_snippet','confidence','explanation','finding','finding_id','id','intent','location','pattern','remediation','severity','tags']. - BREAKAGE ANALYSIS - no existing test breaks. Baseline confirmed: .venv/bin/pytest -q gives 210 passed in 1.92s on this worktree. The four plausible suspects and why each is safe: (1) tests/test_config_template.py asserts 'notify' in data and 'kill' in data - membership, not equality - so a new [scan] section in packaging/config.toml would not break it, and the packet adds no config anyway. (2) tests/test_systemd_units.py reads only scripts/install-systemd.sh, so a new scripts/install-scanner.sh is invisible to it. (3) tests/test_scout.py walks only tests/fixtures/fake_home, so a sibling tests/fixtures/skillspector/ directory does not perturb it. (4) tests/joint/test_adversarial.py globs cases/*.json and gates on IMPLEMENTED = {'ux-01','ux-02a'}; W6-01a adds no alert rule and no case, so it is unaffected - and it must not be edited (Chief owns it).
- THE ONE REAL GAP, not a breakage: tests/test_summarize_redaction.py::test_daemon_does_not_import_summarize_or_cloud_sdk AST-parses daemon.py but bans only summarize/openai/anthropic/httpx/requests/groq. If an implementer adds 'from sentinel.scan import ...' to daemon.py, that test still passes and the packet's central Forbidden rule goes unnoticed. Cover it with G3 in the new file rather than editing the existing one.

**Risks**

- BLOCKING CONTRADICTION IN THE PACKET. It says top is built 'from finding plus location' AND that the result 'never carries code_snippet or any file body'. In real SkillSpector 2.9.6 output, 'finding' IS a verbatim source excerpt. Verified over docs/collab/reviews/skillspector-self.json: finding values include "        sp = getattr(eff, name)" (src/sentinel/action.py:327) and "    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30) as resp:" (summarize.py:126); 5 of 57 span multiple lines with embedded newlines. Building a title from 'finding' therefore puts file bodies into ScanResult, into evidence.scan, into alerts.jsonl and into the notify body - breaking security invariant 2. Recommendation: title comes from the tool-authored label fields 'pattern' (e.g. 'Dynamic attribute access via getattr()') falling back to 'category'. Test B2 encodes this. This needs an ask to the Chief before implementation, because the packet as written cannot be satisfied.
- tree_hash DEFINITION CONFLICT. The packet says 'sha256 over the sorted relative paths and their content hashes' and requires the test 'stable across mtime changes'. docs/collab/plans/SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md section 4 says the opposite: 'sha256 over the sorted list of (relative path, size, mtime_ns) ... Metadata only, consistent with the scout.' The plan's definition fails the packet's own test. The packet should win, but the plan doc then needs a Chief-side correction, and the shift from metadata-only to content hashing means tree_hash reads file bytes - acceptable (only the digest is emitted, same as Alert.hashes) but it is a change of posture worth stating explicitly.
- SEVERITY MAPPING CONFLICT, three ways. Packet: severity_for(recommendation), SAFE->low / CAUTION->medium / DO_NOT_INSTALL->high. SKILLSPECTOR-INTEGRATION.md section 6: score thresholds, <=20 low / 21-50 medium / >50 high. Panel.qml RiskMeter (landed in d4173cd) hardcodes cautionAt 0.20 and dangerAt 0.50 against the score. On fixture 1 all three can disagree. Packet claim 3 ('use the tool's own verdict rather than deriving one') is the right rule; D4 pins it. The plan doc and the QML band thresholds should be reconciled by the Chief, not by this packet.
- FIELD-NAME MISMATCH WITH THE ALREADY-MERGED PANEL. SentinelModel.js:111-120 reads a.evidence.scan.risk_score and a.evidence.scan.verdict. ScanResult uses score and recommendation. The plan's evidence.scan also carries mode, report, duration_ms and per-entry top[].id / top[].severity, which the packet's three-key top entries do not have. Serialization is W6-01c's job, but if W6-01a ships no to_evidence() the seam is undefined. Suggest the packet add it, or an ask records that W6-01c owns the rename.
- ok MUST NOT BE DERIVED FROM analysis_completeness.is_complete. Both recorded reports have is_complete: false with execution_successful: true, because --no-llm disables four analyzers. An implementer who wires ok to is_complete marks every successful scan as failed. Test C6 exists solely to catch this.
- UNDEFINED IN THE PACKET, each of which a test has to pin, so each is an ask candidate: the exact shape and key casing of counts (real severities are uppercase CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW; the plan's evidence uses lowercase, and whether 'suppressed' belongs in counts is unstated); the ordering rule that picks WHICH three findings land in top (proposed: severity rank, then confidence descending, then file/line, stable); the numeric value of the tree_hash size cap and whether an over-cap file is dropped entirely or recorded by path+size (E6 assumes path+size, so a swapped-out huge file is not invisible); symlink policy; whether .git is excluded (E8 assumes yes - without it every commit in a scanned root re-triggers a scan); the ok/error/recommendation triple for the UNSCANNED case; and the exact literal values of the sandbox field ('none' vs 'systemd-run').
- location.end_line is null in 48 of the 57 real issues, and location itself can be a bare string in hostile input. Any top-building code that does int(location['end_line']) or assumes a dict raises on real data. A5 and C3 cover it.
- FIXTURE PROVENANCE. Copying the recorded reports verbatim would commit 212 KB and re-introduce real src/sentinel source (in code_snippet) into tests/. The self-scan baseline suppresses tests/** by glob, so it would not fail the gate, but it is exactly the leak this packet exists to prevent. Trim to 6 issues and replace every snippet with a CANARY string - that also makes B1/B2/B4 decisive rather than incidental.
- EXECUTION SAFETY OF THE TEST SUITE ITSELF. No test may run systemd-run, skillspector, or scripts/install-scanner.sh: the hard rules forbid writes under ~/.local, and CI/dev machines have no scanner installed (SKILLSPECTOR-SELF-SCAN.md records it is not on PyPI and lives in a venv outside the repo). Every runner test must go through an injected runner and an argv-building function; every script test must read text only, like tests/test_systemd_units.py. If scan.py exposes no pure build_argv(), the sandbox tests F1-F8 cannot be written at all - flag that as a design requirement on the implementation, not a test detail.
- tests/joint/ is Chief-owned and off limits. If a future joint case wants a scan gate, tests/joint/test_adversarial.py's IMPLEMENTED = {'ux-01','ux-02a'} set needs a Chief-side edit; the tests agent must not add it.
- PARALLEL-PACKET COLLISION. docs/collab/packets/TEST-02-blackbox-acceptance.md claim 1 enumerates the console scripts as exactly sentinel / sentinel-scout / sentinel-action. If TEST-02 lands a test asserting that exact set while W6-01a adds sentinel-scan to [project.scripts], the two packets break each other. Worth a one-line ask to the Chief to sequence them.
